The admin's editor starts a translation from the Default language's Blocks, keeping their ids; a save in another language works out each Block's `source` (see "AI keys and AI translation").
- **Edit mode** shows a page's body from its Draft and tags it for the block editor's editing-bar part. It keeps each image Block's image as an image region of its own (`data-wc-block`, `RenderBodyOptions.imageRegion`), whose image the image popover changes in that Block alone (see "Body editing, paste and Markdown exchange" and "Edit mode").
- **Markdown**: `bodyMarkdown` (`body/markdown.ts`) writes a body Block by Block for Markdown copies, the Content API and Management API exchange. `blocksFromMarkdown` reads the same dialect back. Text is escaped and formatted marks, links and Blocks keep their structure; callouts, toggles, videos and Components use the extensions documented below. The caller supplies image addresses and resolves links, so public copies use absolute addresses while exchange uses Media ids.
- **Seams**: `?body=html|blocks|markdown` in the Content API (#108, `renderBody`, `readBody` and `bodyMarkdown`), Module Block types (#110, the registry), translation one Block at a time (#80 and AI translation, `source` on each Block).

### Relationships

A Relationship value is a list of Entry ids inside a version. `entry_links` also keeps every link per Entry, version (`draft`, `published`) and Field path (`categories`, `credits.person` inside a Repeater), written by `changeDraft` and `publish` (`content/relationships.ts`), so what points at an Entry is one indexed lookup. Categories and tags are ordinary Post Types joined by Relationships.

- **Two-way** (the default): the Entries pointed at show the link back, as `linkedFrom` (`{ entryId, postTypeId, field, title }`, from the linking Entries' Drafts). **One-way**: nothing shows on them. Loops (a query's Relationship filters) and the Content API (#108) read the same links.
- **Rendering** shows only published Entries pointed at (see "What a binding reads").
- **Deleting an Entry** (`entries.delete`, Permission `entry.delete`: Staff, Owners and Editors) takes two calls. The first deletes nothing and answers `{ deleted: false, links, confirmation: { id, expiresAt } }`, `links` being how many other Entries link to it; the second, with `confirmation`, deletes it, removes the links to it from those Entries' Drafts and published versions (their status doesn't change), clears the page cache and logs `entry.delete`. While another Entry must point at it through a required Relationship, both calls answer `ENTRY_LINKED` with `data.blockedBy`.
- **Globals** can't hold Relationships.

### Two-step confirmation

`askConfirmation` and `useConfirmation` (`packages/core/src/confirmations.ts`, table `confirmations`) are how destructive procedures confirm (spec › Management API): the first call works out a preview and asks; the second works it out again inside the transaction that goes ahead and uses the id. A confirmation is for one person, one action and one target, lasts 5 minutes (`CONFIRMATION_LIFETIME_MS`), works once, and only while the preview is unchanged. Otherwise the API answers `CONFIRMATION_INVALID` with `data.problem`: `unknown`, `expired` or `changed`. They take a Site scope or a Workspace scope (a Workspace's confirmations have no Site), such as removing a Workspace-level Module's data. A Module's procedures ask through `context.confirmations` (SDK 1.6), their actions named under the Module's id (`forms.submissions.delete`). Changing the Default language and removing a language use them too, and so does Publish all (its preview is the ticked items and their Drafts' versions, so a Draft saved in between asks for a new confirmation).

**API clients** (`isApiClient(access)`: an API token, or an AI client's OAuth grant) confirm in two steps what the admin does in one click, since there a person pressed the button on what they see: every publish (`confirmedPublish`, previewed by `publishing.preview`: the item, its name, `firstPublish`, `changed` and the Draft's `version`), deleting a Class and removing a Library (`confirmedForApiClients`). Their first call changes nothing and answers `{ confirmation: { id, expiresAt }, preview }`; the second passes `confirmation`. The middleware opens the Site with the Permission the change needs first, so nobody is shown a preview of what they may not do. Procedures that take a `confirmation` input are marked `x-webcraft-confirmation` in the OpenAPI document. The spec's destructive procedures, all two steps for API clients: every publish, `publishing.publishAll` (two steps for everyone), `entries.delete`, `components.delete`, `forms.delete`, `designSystem.deleteClass`, `libraries.remove`, `media.delete`, `media.deleteForever`, `media.emptyTrash`, `languages.remove`, `languages.setDefault`, `domains.remove`, `webhooks.remove`, `imports.undo`, `modules.removeSiteData` and `modules.removeWorkspaceData` (`tests/api/openapi.test.ts` holds the list).

### Body editing, paste and Markdown exchange

The admin and Edit mode share the block editor's model (`apps/server/src/body-editor/model.ts`), inline text and caret operations (`inline-dom.ts`), Block registry and converters. Edit mode loads its body part (`edit-tools.ts`) as a hashed, same-origin module through the editing bar's kit, only on pages showing a Body field. Blocks are edited where they render; Alt+F10 reaches their tools, Escape returns to the Block, and phones offer Move up/down instead of drag. Body images keep the image popover. Entry Drafts are live kinds: each edit enters the shared document immediately, whose client batches persistence; merged changes update even a focused Block while keeping its caret. The ordinary `save` endpoint is the fallback before that document opens. Rendering an inserted Component waits for an acknowledged save; refusal, read-only state or a timeout must not be treated as success.

- **Paste**: `entries.pasteBody` (admin, internal) and Edit mode's `body/paste` convert clipboard HTML, Markdown or plain text to existing Blocks. `blocksFromHtml` has a separate `paste` option, leaving Import leftovers and import image handling unchanged. Document formatting keeps text marks, lists and tables, discards styling and scripts, demotes H1 to H2, and degrades unknown content to text. Pasted image addresses become empty image Blocks, never outgoing requests. Clipboard image files use the existing Media upload endpoint, with its Permissions, file checks and Workspace quota.
- **Components**: `BodyField.components` is a list of Site Component ids, empty by default, set through `postTypes.update` (`structure.edit`). `entries.bodyComponents` and `body/get` list only allowed, published Components with their published Props. The `/` menu asks for those Props before insertion. A `component` Block holds `{ id, type: "component", component, props: [{ name, value }] }`; `blockPlacement` turns it into the existing Placement node, rendered by `PlacementRenderer`. A new unallowed Placement or invalid Prop is refused. An existing Component removed from the allow-list stays editable but raises a problem and blocks publishing until corrected. Authors save Drafts and follow the ordinary Review rules. The Content API renders Component Blocks as published Placements in HTML too, limiting their Globals and Relationship bindings to the API’s exposed Fields.
- **Exchange**: public `entries.get`/`list` accept `bodyFormat: "markdown"`; `entries.create`/`saveDraft` accept the same flag with the Body field as a string. Omit the flag (or use `"blocks"`) for JSON Blocks. The public catalogue generates the corresponding MCP tools and CLI options. `blocksFromMarkdown` and `bodyMarkdown` are the single conversion pair; Markdown copies and the Content API use the same writer with public image URLs, while Management API exchange uses `media:<id>` and keeps links as written. Rewriting Markdown preserves matching Block ids for translations. Invalid Markdown is refused atomically as `ENTRY_INVALID`, with each `data.problems` item naming the Field and its one-based `line`.

The Markdown dialect supports paragraphs and inline marks/links, H2–H4 (an optional `{#anchor}`), nested lists, quotes, fenced code, pipe tables and dividers. Its extensions are:

| Block | Markdown |
| --- | --- |
| Image | `![alt](media:<id>)`; `::image` when no Media is chosen |
| Video | `::video[Title]{url="https://…"}` |
| Callout | `:::callout{tone="info"}`, text on following lines, closing `:::` |
| Toggle | `:::toggle[Summary]`, text on following lines, closing `:::` |
| Component | `<Note text="Hello" />`, using the Component's name and fixed Prop text |

Raw HTML, unknown Components, unsupported syntax and invalid Props are refused in exchange Markdown, with line numbers. Paste is deliberately forgiving instead. JSON Blocks remain the exact stored representation, including stable ids and empty placeholders.

### Components

Components (`content/components.ts`, table `components`) are reusable element trees with Props: Structure (`structure.edit`, Staff only) with a Draft and a published version. Each version is `{ props, tree }`, published together as one item through `publish` with `{ kind: "component", id }`.

- **Name**: the Component's tag in Template markup, PascalCase (`PostCard`: a capital letter, then letters and digits, at least one lower-case, at most 64), unique on the Site and never the name of one of the Install's Modules' Components (`COMPONENT_NAME_TAKEN`, `data.by`). Trees place Components by id, so renaming (`components.rename`, logged as `component.rename`) only changes how markup writes them, everywhere at once.
- **Props** are declared with the Field types (`parseFields` with `listName: "props"`, so problems say `props[1].options` and "Prop"); every Field type works, a Prop keeps its type (in the Draft and the published version), and only the words Conditions use are kept from keys.
- **What its tree reads** (`componentKnownValues`): its Props as `props.x`, with their parts as for Fields (`props.link.url`, `props.category.title`), and the Globals as `site.x`. Nothing of the Entry it is placed on: that comes in through Props. It holds neither `<main>` nor `{{@outlet}}`. Its elements name the Site's Classes and token utilities as Templates' do (an inline style becomes a generated Class, which goes live when the Component is published); the stylesheet holds those its published version uses, and a Class it uses can't be deleted (`CLASS_IN_USE`, a use of `kind: "component"`).
- **Placements** are a node of their own (`PlacementNode` in `packages/renderer/src/components.ts`): `{ type: "component", id, component, props: [{ name, value }] }`, `component` being the Component's id and each value fixed text and values, as an attribute's. A Placement holds nothing else: a stored one with any other key (`children`, `tree`) is refused, since a copy's internals are never edited on their own, and Components have no slots. Its id shares the tree's element ids.
- **In Template markup** a Placement is the Component's tag with its Props as attributes, written as declared (`heroImage`), closing itself: `<PostCard heading="{{ title }}" featured data-wc-id="…" />`. A Prop with an empty value prints as its name alone. Content between `<PostCard>` and `</PostCard>` is refused, as are unknown Components and Props, a Prop given twice and a required Prop (one without a default) left out.
- **Prop values** (`content/props.ts`): fixed text is read through the Prop's Field type and checked against its rules on save: text, rich text, email, dates and a select's value as they are; numbers (`3`, `2.5`); true/false as `true`, `false` or the Prop's name alone; media as `media:<id>`; several choices or Entries separated by commas; a link as its address or JSON `{"url", "text"}`; Groups and Repeaters as JSON of their Fields' values. A value that is one binding alone (`heading="{{ title }}"`) passes the value on as it is, parts and all (`props.heading.url` reads `title.url` where it is placed); text with values in it is text. A Prop left out has its default.
- **Drafts and publishing**: Drafts of Templates and Components place Components' Drafts (`siteComponents(scope, "draft", …)`); a publish checks against the published versions, so publishing a Template (or Component) that places a Component never published is refused ("isn't published yet"). Publishing a Component changes every copy at once and clears the page cache.
- **Placing Components in Components**: never in a loop back to itself, in Drafts or what is published; at most `COMPONENT_LIMITS.depth` (10) deep; a tree shows at most `COMPONENT_LIMITS.expandedNodes` (100,000) nodes through its Components (refused on save; the renderer stops past it). What a Component shows must be HTML a browser keeps as it is where it is placed (`boundaryProblem`: its `<div>` can't go inside a `<p>`), checked on save, on publish, and when a new version of the Component is published (`checkPublishedPlacements`).
- **Rendering**: `RenderOptions.component` (a `PlacementRenderer`) renders a Placement's Component; on Site pages `pageComponents` (`site-pages/index.ts`) loads the published Components the page shows (Relationship Props written as Entry ids included) and reads Props with `placementValues`. A Component that isn't published, or a Module's that doesn't run on the Site, shows nothing.
- **Usage** (`usage` on `components.list`, `get`, `getMarkup` and `saveMarkup`): `usedIn` lists every Template and Component whose Draft or published version places it directly, and `pages` how many published pages show it: every page (published Entries with a published Entry template, and published Pages) when the published Site template places it, directly or through published Components, else the published Entries of the Post Types whose published Entry templates do. Code mode shows it as the "used on N pages" warning.
- **Deleting** (`components.delete`) takes two steps (`askConfirmation`) and is refused while anything places it, in its Draft or published version (`COMPONENT_IN_USE`, `data.usedIn`). Logged as `component.delete`; creating as `component.create`.
- **Locking**: Component saves, renames, deletes and publishes, and Template saves and publishes, take `lockComponents(scope)` (one advisory lock per Site) before any row lock, so what places what is never read half-changed.
- **API**: `components.list`, `get`, `create`, `saveDraft` (Props and a tree), `getMarkup`, `saveMarkup` (markup and, optionally, new Props), `rename`, `publish`, `delete`, each taking `siteId`; `componentId` is a UUID, or `<module id>:<Name>` for a Module's (whose changes answer `COMPONENT_FROM_MODULE`).
- **Seams**: Component custom CSS (#64) is published with the Component, beside its Props and tree in `ComponentVersion`. Page layouts (#75, which Pages render empty until then) and Components in a Body (#79) place Components with the same node and render them through `PlacementRenderer`; their Prop values, Content edited by Members, may be stored per language. Allowed components (#75) choose among the Site's Components.

### Loops

Loops (`content/loops.ts`, `content/loop-queries.ts`, table `loops`; `GLOSSARY.md`) are Structure (`structure.edit`, Staff only) with a Draft and a published version, published as one item through `publish` with `{ kind: "loop", id }` (logged `loop.publish`; creating `loop.create`). Templates and Components name them; pages show their published versions.

- **Name**: lower-case letters and digits in words joined by dashes (`latest-posts`, at most 64), unique on the Site (`LOOP_NAME_TAKEN`), never a name bindings read from (`site`, `props`, `item`, `loop`, a built-in Field) or a word Conditions use. It never changes, as a Post Type's key doesn't, since markup names the Loop by it.
- **Where its items come from** (`source`): a **query** over Entries, or its own **Loop items**. A Repeater or Relationship on the current Entry needs no Loop: markup loops over it by its path (`{{@loop gallery as photo}}`).
- **A query** (`LoopQuery`): the Post Types it lists (by key), filters matching all or any, sorts, `limit` (1–100), `offset` (0–1,000), `paginate` and `excludeCurrent`. Filters (`QueryFilter`): a Field's value (`equals`, `notEquals`, `contains`, `lessThan`, `atMost`, `greaterThan`, `atLeast`, `empty`, `notEmpty`, by the Field's type; a Group's Field by its path, `address.city`); a Relationship (by its definition path, Repeaters' too) pointing at the current Entry, at what the current Entry points at through the same Field (`sharedWithCurrent`), or at chosen Entries; a date (a built-in date or a date Field) `past`, `future`, within the `last` or `next` N days, weeks, months or years, or `between` two days; and the status (`draft`, `published`, `changed`). Sorts: the title, a built-in date, or a text, email, number, date, true/false or single select Field; newest published first without one. Everything is checked against the Site's Post Types on save and again on publish (`LOOP_INVALID`, each problem at its place, `query.filters[1].value`); a query Loop with no Post Type can't be published. `QUERY_LIMITS` caps every list. Filters and Site search narrow the same query at render time (`Narrowing`, see "Filters" and "Site search"); the name `search` is kept for Site search's built-in Loop.
- **Loop items**: Staff define their Fields (`fields`, owner `loop`: no Body field and no Relationships); their values are stored like a Repeater's rows under `values.items` in each version, per Field and per language (ADR 0003), at most `LOOP_ITEMS_MAX` (200). `loops.saveItems` (`entry.edit`: Loop items are Content) takes the whole list in one language, as a Repeater's value is saved; values of the wrong kind are refused, broken rules kept and listed in `problems`, and publishing checks every language (`LOOP_INVALID`, `LOOP_INCOMPLETE`). Edit mode changes them in place (see "Edit mode" › Loop items in place): their values through the same save, and which items there are and their order through `rearrangeLoopItems` (every language at once, the items as stored); `undoLoopItems` puts a Loop's items back as published, keeping the rest of its Draft.
- **API**: `loops.list`, `get` (its Loop items in a `language`), `create`, `saveDraft` (`source`, `query`, `fields`; each given replaces that part, a query's settings left out taking their defaults), `saveItems`, `publish`, `data` (the Data tab).
- **On pages** (`site-pages/loops.ts`, `pageLoops`): a page runs each Loop of the Site its templates and the Components they place name (`loopNamesIn`) once, however often or deeply placed, so a page never runs one query per item, and at most `LOOP_LIMITS.perPage` (20) of them (the rest show no items). A query is one parameterised SQL query (`queryRows`: every key and value a parameter, `count(*) over ()` for pagination) over published Entries shown in the page's language (`publishedVisibleIn`), their values in that language with the Default language's as fallback one Field at a time, read through each listed Post Type's own definition; then one query per Post Type for addresses, one for authors and one for what the items' Relationships point at (one level deep). In Edit mode Loops show the same published Entries, and a Loop's own items its published version shows with their values from the Loop's Draft (`LoopPlace.items: "draft"`: Loop items are Content); `PageLoops.info` and `byId` say which Loop a name is, for Edit mode's marks and regions. Loops nest at most `LOOP_LIMITS.depth` (4) deep, inside Components too (refused on save, deeper ones render nothing), and a page shows at most `LOOP_LIMITS.items` (2,000) items through them.
- **Pagination**: on Archive pages and Pages, the first query Loop with `paginate` that the page shows takes the page number (`?page=N`): it skips `offset + (N − 1) × limit` Entries and links its pages through `{{@pagination}}` (`pageHref`: the page itself for the first, `?page=N` after). Past the first page, the page's title says which (`About · Page 2`), and its `hreflang` alternates are the same page in each language; the Language switcher still goes to the first. On an Entry's page Loops show their first page.
- **The Data tab** (`site-pages/data-samples.ts`; `loops.data`, `postTypes.data`, `globals.data`, all `structure.edit`): a real item's values from the Draft, as JSON shaped the way bindings read them (a select as `{ value, label }`, a Relationship as the Entries it points at, a body as its excerpt, image, reading time and headings, a media Field as `media:<id>`), and the bindings that read each (`{{ item.title }}`, `{{@loop item.steps as step}}`, `{{ step.text }}`). For a query Loop, the first Entry its Draft query finds among the Drafts of the Site's Entries (never-published ones too); for a Loop of its own items, its first item; for an Entry template, the Entry asked for or the one saved last; for the Globals, their Draft values with `site.language` and `site.name`.

### Loops in Template markup

- `{{@loop latest-posts as post}} … {{@empty}} … {{/loop}}` (`LoopNode`): the content renders once per item, reading the item as `post.x` (`item` when `as` is left out) and its position as `loop.x`; `{{@empty}}`'s content renders when there are none and reads only what is around the Loop. The source is a Loop of the Site by name or a list the place reads by its path (`gallery`, `post.tags`, `props.lines`; each value of a list is an item, a single value one item). A Loop's name is resolved before a value of the same name.
- What a binding inside may read is checked where it is (`loopBodyScope`, `packages/renderer/src/loops.ts`): an item's values as its Loop has them (`loopItemKnownValues`: a query's Entries' built-in values and their Post Types' Fields, Relationships one level deep; Loop items' Fields), or for a list, the list's own parts. The items' name can't be `site`, `props`, `loop` or a word Conditions use, nor hide a value or the items of a Loop around it. Saves check against the Loops' Drafts, publishes against their published versions (a Loop never published is refused, as a Component is).
- `<name>.count` (the items shown), `.empty`, `.total` (every matching item, across pages), `.page` and `.pages` read a Loop of the Site anywhere.
- `{{@pagination latest-posts}}` (`PaginationNode`): a `<nav class="wc-pagination">` named in the page's language, with the previous page, the first and last pages and those next to the current one (gaps as …), the current page marked `aria-current="page"`, and the next page; nothing unless the Loop paginates on the page with two pages or more. It goes where a `<nav>` can.

### Archive templates

An Archive template (`templates` row of kind `archive`, one per Post Type; `content/archives.ts`) lists a Post Type's Entries through Loops. API: `templates.getArchiveTemplate`, `saveArchiveTemplate`, `getArchiveTemplateMarkup`, `saveArchiveTemplateMarkup` (`structure.edit`), published with `templates.publish`.

- **What it reads**: the Globals (`site.x`) and the Loops of the Site; no Entry's values. It holds neither `<main>` nor `{{@outlet}}`.
- **Its address**: the URL pattern's fixed parts before `{slug}` (`/blog` for `/blog/{slug}`, `/shop/products` for `/shop/products/{slug}`), in every language under its prefix (`/fr/blog`), answered before Entries and Pages. The Page Post Type has none, nor has a pattern starting with `{slug}` (`ARCHIVE_UNAVAILABLE`, `data.reason`: `pages` or `address`). Its page is titled with the Post Type's name (and `· Page N` past the first), lists `hreflang` alternates to the same page in every language, and its Language switcher links go to its first page in each.
- **One path, one page**: a published Archive template owns its address as a URL pattern owns its paths. Publishing one where another Post Type's pattern owns the address or a published Page is, in any language, is refused with `PATH_TAKEN` (`by` the Post Type or the Page); publishing a Page there, creating a pattern that would own it, or changing a Post Type's pattern so its published Archive moves onto a Page or another pattern's path, likewise (`by: { kind: "archive", postTypeId, name }`). Each takes `lockSitePaths` before any row lock (a Template publish always does).

### Globals

The Globals (`content/globals.ts`, one `globals` row per Site, made the first time it is needed) are one set of Fields per Site, read by every Template as `site.x`.

- `globals.get` (`site.view`): the Fields, the Draft and published values, `status`, `problems` and dates.
- `globals.setFields` (`structure.edit`): the full list of Fields, in order. Like a Post Type's, it applies at once, keeps each Field's type, clears the page cache and logs `globals.update`. Keys can't be `name`, `url`, `language` or `languages`, kept for the Site's own values under `site.`.
- `globals.saveDraft` (`entry.edit`, so Authors too) and `globals.publish` (`entry.publish`), which goes through `publish` with `{ kind: "globals" }`.

### Scripts

The Site script and page scripts (`content/scripts.ts`, table `scripts`) are the JavaScript Staff write in Code mode's JS panel: Structure (`structure.edit`, Staff only) with a Draft and a published version, as text, published through `publish` with `{ kind: "script", id }` (logged as `script.publish`). The Site script (`kind: "site"`, one per Site) runs on every page; a page script (`kind: "page"`, one per Entry, gone with it) runs on its Entry's page only, after the Site script.

- **Never inline** (ADR 0023): each version is a file named by the first 16 hexadecimal digits of its text's SHA-256 (`draft_hash`, `published_hash`). `PageToRender.scripts` lists them, the Site script first, and the renderer writes each as `<script src defer>` at the end of `<head>`. Published ones are `/_wc/js/<hash>.js` (`pageScripts`, served by the Site pipeline beside the theme toggle's, `public, max-age=31536000, immutable`); empty ones aren't loaded.
- **Who runs what**: visitors run the published versions, and so does Edit mode (ADR 0022: the Site's own scripts run there, for Members too); Code mode runs the Drafts, from `/_webcraft/edit/js/<hash>.js`, served only to Staff in Code mode (`no-store`). Safe mode runs none of them (see "Site script safety"). In Edit mode each `<script>` says which it is (`data-wc-script="site"` or `"page"`), so the editing bar can name one that fails.
- **API** (all `structure.edit`): `scripts.get({ siteId, entryId })` (`entryId` null for the Site script; null while there is none), `scripts.saveDraft({ siteId, entryId, code, basedOn? })` (makes it the first time; an `entryId` that isn't the Site's is `ENTRY_NOT_FOUND`) and `scripts.publish({ siteId, scriptId })`. A script's `version` is its Draft's hash.
- A published script a publish replaces is kept, like stylesheets, in `retired_script_files` and still served under its name for 30 days, so a page loaded just before the publish finds it; `site-files.purge` forgets it after that.

### Publishing

`services.publishing.publish(scope, item)` (`content/publishing.ts`) is the one way anything is published. `item` is `{ kind: "entry" | "template" | "component" | "loop" | "filter" | "script" | "form" | "formEmails", id }`, `{ kind: "globals" }`, `{ kind: "consentBanner" }` (the Consent banner's words) or `{ kind: "designSystem" }`. Open the Site with `publishPermission(item)` (`entry.publish` for Content: Entries, the Globals and the Consent banner's words; `formEmails.edit` for a Form's emails; `structure.edit` for Structure: Templates, Components, Loops, Filters, scripts, Forms and the Design system); `publish` checks it again, so the worker's Scheduled publishes hold it too. In one transaction it:

1. locks the item and checks it can be published: an Entry's (or the Globals') values must keep their rules (`EntryInvalidError`, `GlobalsInvalidError`) and every required value must be there, the title and slug included (`EntryIncompleteError`, `GlobalsIncompleteError`); an Entry's slug must be free among published Entries of its Post Type (`EntrySlugTakenError`; for a Page, among the Pages under its parent); a Page's parent must be published (`PageParentUnpublishedError`) and its new path, and those of the Pages under it, free of URL patterns (`PathTakenError`); a Template must still fit what it renders: its Post Type, the Globals, what its Relationships point at and the published Components it places (`ElementTreeInvalidError`); a Component must still fit what it reads and places, and every published Template and Component placing it (`checkPublishedPlacements`);
2. copies the Draft over the published version and sets `publishedAt` (first time) and `lastPublishedAt` (an Entry's published links too);
3. clears the Site's page cache (`clearPageCache`: raises `sites.page_cache_generation`), and indexes a published Entry for Site search (`indexPublishedEntry`, behind a savepoint, so indexing never fails the publish; see "Site search");
4. writes the Audit log (`entry.publish`, `template.publish`, `component.publish`, `script.publish`, `globals.publish`). `entry.publish` holds the Entry's `path` and `pathChanges`: every published path the publish changed (`{ entryId, language, from, to }`: an Entry's new slug, a Page that moved and every Page under it). The result (`Published`) carries the same `pathChanges`: Redirect rules (#94) follow them so old links keep working;
5. saves a Revision of each item it published (`saveRevision`, see "Revisions and Checkpoints"), unless Revisions are off for the Install or the Entry's Post Type.

Once a publish commits, its Audit log entry fires the Site's Webhooks (see "Webhooks"); Scheduled publish (below), Review (below: `reviewsOnPublish` ends the Review of each item published, in the publish's transaction) and the CDN purge hook in here, and save their Revisions through `publishItems` without more work.

`publishItems(scope, items, sources, refusal?, frozen?)` takes a fifth argument, `frozen`: the version to publish of each item instead of its Draft, as the item's table stores its Draft (a Scheduled publish's). Every publisher takes it (`publishEntry`, `publishLoop`, `publishGlobals`, `publishScriptDraft`, …) and checks it exactly as a Draft. Without it (publishing now), each item published leaves the Scheduled publish of its Draft it waited in (`leaveScheduledPublishes`, `content/scheduled-items.ts`): its newer version is live already, so the older one must not follow it. A one-item Scheduled publish is then cancelled, a Publish all set goes on with the rest (`scheduledPublish.cancel` or `scheduledPublish.change`, `reason: "published"`); a scheduled unpublish stays.

`publish` is one item through `publishItems(scope, items, sources, refusal?)`, which publishes several in the order given, all or none: one transaction, the locks every publish needs taken once and always in the same order, before any row lock (`lockSitePaths` for Entries and Templates, then `lockComponents` for Templates and Components, then the Design system's row for anything changing the stylesheet), so no two publishes wait for each other in a circle; one page cache clear and one replaced stylesheet kept; each item its own Audit log entry. A transaction Postgres gives up on (a deadlock, a serialization failure) answers `CHANGED_MEANWHILE`.

### Publish all

Publish all (ADR 0014, `content/publish-all.ts`, procedures `publishing.*`, the admin's top bar) publishes the Drafts of a Site the person ticks, together.

- **The Drafts** (`siteDrafts`, `publishing.drafts`, `site.view`): every item whose Draft differs from what is published (`draft is distinct from published`): Entries, never-published ones too; the Globals and the Consent banner's words (against an empty version while never published); the Design system (section by section, against the default one while never published); Components, Loops, Filters, Forms and Templates; scripts (`draft_hash`, an empty never-published one left out). Content first (Entries, newest change first; the Globals; the Consent banner's words), then Structure (the Design system, Components, Loops, Filters, Forms, Templates, scripts). Each says `status` (`new` or `changed`), `changes` (an Entry's, the Globals' or the Consent banner's values that differ, by key with the Field's label, and the Design system's sections), who changed it last (`changedBy`, the Draft tables' `updated_by`, which every Draft save writes; null for a Module or an Import's background work) and when (`updated_at`), `canPublish` (`publishPermission(item)`: Members see Structure, never theirs to tick), and for an Entry its Draft address on the Staging address (`path`). `count` (and `publishing.count`, what the top bar shows) is how many the person may publish. Redirect rules and Site settings have no Draft and are never listed.
- **Publishing** (`publishing.publishAll`, two calls): the first, with the ticked `items`, publishes nothing and answers `preview.items` in the order they would go live and a `confirmation`; the second, with the same items and the id, publishes them through `publishItems` in the order they need each other: the Design system, Loops, Filters, Forms, Components (each after those its Draft places), Templates, scripts, the Globals, the Consent banner's words, then Entries (Pages first, each after its Draft parent). All or none: an item that can't be published stops them all with `PUBLISH_ALL_REFUSED` (409; `data`: the `item`, its `name` and its own refusal's `code` and `data`), so a redesign never goes live half done. Refused before anything: an item that is no Draft of the Site (`PUBLISH_ALL_INVALID`, `data.items`) and one the person may not publish (`FORBIDDEN`). Once committed, each item's Audit log entry fires its Webhooks (`publish`, one delivery per item) and Module events, as publishing it alone does, and `site.publishAll` records the items and their count.
- **API clients** call the same procedures at `/api/v1/publishing/drafts` (a read) and `/api/v1/publishing/publishAll` (a write, marked `x-webcraft-confirmation`): Publish all takes two calls for everyone, so a script confirms just as the admin does. `publishing.count` is the admin's own.

### Scheduled publish

A Scheduled publish (`GLOSSARY.md`, spec story 46; `content/scheduled-publishes.ts`, `content/scheduled-items.ts`, `content/unpublishing.ts`; procedures `publishing.scheduled`, `schedule`, `scheduleAll`, `scheduleUnpublish`, `reschedule`, `updateScheduled`, `cancelScheduled`; tables `scheduled_publishes`, `scheduled_publish_items`, `gone_paths`, Site tables) publishes at a later time the version that existed when it was scheduled, of one item (`kind: "item"`) or a Publish all set (`kind: "set"`), or unpublishes an Entry (`action: "unpublish"`).

- **A frozen version**: scheduling reads each item's Draft as its table stores it (`itemState(…).draft`, every language) and keeps it with its version (`draftVersion`) and name in `scheduled_publish_items`. At the time, exactly that is published through `publishItems(…, frozen)`, so Revisions, Webhooks, the page cache, Module events and Automatic translation follow as for any publish. Later edits stay in the Draft: an item whose Draft's version differs from the frozen one is "Changed since scheduled" (`changedSinceScheduled` in `publishing.scheduled`, and on Entries `scheduled.publish.changedSinceScheduled`). With Co-editing the Draft row is current as each batch of changes is saved (ADR 0025), so what is frozen is what people see.
- **Checked when scheduled**: the frozen versions are published once in a transaction of their own that is rolled back (`rolledBack`), so what couldn't be published now is refused now, with the reason publishing gives (`ENTRY_INCOMPLETE`, `PAGE_PARENT_UNPUBLISHED`, `PUBLISH_ALL_REFUSED` for a set, …). What it needs must be published first or scheduled with it. Open the Site outside a transaction for these calls; nested `scope.transaction` calls share the outer one, and nothing waiting for the commit (Webhooks, Module events, AI translation) hears of a rolled-back one.
- **One at a time**: an item waits in at most one Scheduled publish of each action (a unique index; `ALREADY_SCHEDULED`, 409, `data.scheduleId`), so an Entry can have a launch and an expiry. Times are UTC instants after now and at most ten years ahead (`SCHEDULE_INVALID`, `data.reason`: `past`, `tooFar`; `SCHEDULE_LIMITS`).
- **Who**: whoever may publish an item may schedule it (`publishPermission`; Authors can't), change its time, take the current Drafts into it or cancel it (`canChange`: the person may publish every item of it); an unpublish takes `entry.publish`. Anyone who sees the Site sees them (`site.view`). At the time WebCraft acts on behalf of the person who scheduled it, with their access as it is then: the data layer opens the Site as that person with `via: { kind: "scheduledPublish" }`, so the Audit log, Revisions, presence and Webhooks show "<person> (Scheduled publish)". Scheduling an item In review ends its Review (`reviewsOnSchedule`, in the transaction that saves the Scheduled publish, and when the current Drafts are taken into one): the run publishes on behalf of whoever scheduled it (see "Review"). Not a Sensitive action: it publishes nothing a publish now couldn't.
- **API clients**: `schedule`, `scheduleUnpublish` and `updateScheduled` take two steps for API clients (`confirmedForApiClients`), `scheduleAll` two calls for everyone, as Publish all; every scheduling procedure but listing and cancelling is marked `publishes` in `TOOL_PLACES` (Toolset Publishing), so an AI client needs "May publish".
- **The worker**: `publishing.scheduled-due` runs every minute (`* * * * *`), so a Scheduled publish runs within a minute of its time; when the worker was down at a due time, the job runs once when it is back and runs every Scheduled publish missed meanwhile (`runDueScheduledPublishes`), each in its own transaction. A run holds its row (`for update skip locked`) and deletes it in the same transaction that publishes, so two workers never run one twice. More than two minutes after its time it ran late: `scheduledPublish.run` says `late: true` and `lateByMinutes`, and the log has a warning.
- **All or none**: a run that can't publish every item (an item deleted, a Template's Component deleted, a Field changed so a frozen value breaks its rules, a path taken since, the person's access gone) publishes none of them, is removed, is logged `scheduledPublish.fail` (`reason`: `deleted`, `invalid`, `pathTaken`, `parentUnpublished`, `structureChanged`, `access`, `homePage`, `other`; the `item` that stopped it), and the person who scheduled it gets the `scheduled-publish-failed` email (a work email, in their Admin language, under Install branding) naming the item and why, linking to the Entry or to Publish all. A run Postgres gave up on (a deadlock) is tried again the next minute, up to 5 times (`attempts`). The Drafts stay as they are.
- **Unpublishing** (`unpublishEntry`): the Entry's published version is cleared (`published` null, `unpublishedAt` set; its status is `draft` again, its Draft and Revisions kept), its published slugs and links go, Site search forgets it, and each path it had, in each language it was shown in, is kept in `gone_paths` (the path within the language, without its prefix). A Page takes every published Page under it along (their paths start with its own). The Site's home page and a Page above it can't be unpublished (`HOME_PAGE_UNPUBLISH`, 409). Each Entry is logged `entry.unpublish` (with its `paths`; a Page under it names it as `with`), which sends the Webhooks' `unpublish` event; the page cache is cleared. Publishing it again deletes its gone paths.
- **410 Gone**: the Site pipeline answers a path with no page that is in `gone_paths` (`sitePages.gone`, read as visitors read, in the language the hostname and path name) with 410, the Site's 404 page as its body, `Cache-Control: no-store` and `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`; the 404 log doesn't hear of it. Loops, Relationships on pages, sitemaps (#92) and the Content API read published Entries only, so it leaves them without more work. The Staging address shows Drafts, so an unpublished Entry still shows there.
- **Cancelling, or a run that fails, reopens its Reviews**: an item a Scheduled publish put In review by scheduling it (`reviewsOnSchedule`) holds that Scheduled publish's id (`reviews.schedule_id`). When the Scheduled publish is removed without ever publishing it — `cancelScheduled`, or a run that `fail`s — `reopenReviewsOfSchedule` finds the Reviews holding its id and still `scheduled`, and puts each back to `inReview` (clearing `decidedBy`, `decidedAt`, `scheduledAt` and `schedule_id`) in the same transaction as the Scheduled publish's row leaving, logged `review.reopen`; not emailed (nothing was decided: whoever removed it, or the `scheduled-publish-failed` email, already says why). It is looked up and reopened *before* the row is deleted: `reviews.schedule_id`'s foreign key is `ON DELETE SET NULL`, so deleting it first would already have cleared the column `reopenReviewsOfSchedule` searches by. An item taken out of a Publish all set because it was published now needs none of this: that publish ends its Review itself (`reviewsOnPublish`), whatever had scheduled it. "Update to the current Drafts" (`updateScheduled`) keeps the Scheduled publish (and so the Review) as they are; only `reviewsOnSchedule`'s own update sets `schedule_id` (unchanged, since it is the same id).
- **Audit log**: `scheduledPublish.create` (action, kind, at, items), `scheduledPublish.reschedule` (`from`, `to`), `scheduledPublish.update` (the current Drafts taken), `scheduledPublish.change` (an item removed because it was published now), `scheduledPublish.cancel` (`reason`: `cancelled` or `published`), and at the time, as done by WebCraft on behalf of the person who scheduled it (`via: scheduledPublish`): each item's own `<item>.publish` or `entry.unpublish`, then `scheduledPublish.run` (`late`, `lateByMinutes`), or `scheduledPublish.fail` (`review.reopen` beside it, for each Review it puts back).
- **The admin**: the Entry editor's Status card (`ui/publishing/EntrySchedule.tsx`) schedules a publish ("Schedule…") or, once published, an unpublish, and shows each schedule with its time in UTC (`formatUtc`) and the person's own local time beside it (`LocalTime`, drawn only in the browser, so hydration agrees), "Changed since scheduled", Change time, Update to the current Draft and Cancel schedule; a set it belongs to is changed in the Publish all panel. The Publish all panel (`ui/publishing/PublishAll.tsx`) lists the Site's Scheduled publishes in a Scheduled section with the same actions, marks Drafts waiting in one, and schedules the ticked set with "Publish N items at…". The Entries list marks scheduled Entries (`ScheduleBadges`). The date and time are chosen in the browser's time zone (`ScheduleDialog`, native date and time boxes, the zone named under them, the UTC time said as it changes) and sent as a UTC instant.
- **Tests**: `tests/publishing/scheduled-publish.test.ts` (the fake clock moves past the time, `drainJobs()` runs the minute's look; `drainJobs({ only, batchSize: 1 })` twice at once shares the queue's jobs as two workers do); `tests/browser/scheduled-publish.browser.ts`.

### Review

Review (`GLOSSARY.md` › Review, spec stories 23, 49 and 134; `content/reviews.ts`, `content/review-items.ts`; procedures `reviews.*`; table `reviews`, a Site table) is the step where an Author submits a Draft and someone who may publish it publishes it, schedules it or sends it back with a comment. While it waits, the item is In review.

- **What is reviewed** (`isReviewable`): the Content Authors change and can't publish, whose publish takes `entry.publish`: Entries (Pages too), the Globals and the Consent banner's words. A Form's emails are never Authors' (`formEmails.edit`); Loop items are saved to the Loop's Draft, which is Structure, so Staff publish them.
- **Submitting** (`reviews.submit`, `entry.edit`; Edit mode's `review/submit`): one or more items with an optional note (2,000 characters), one Review row per item, in one transaction, logged once as `review.submit`. Refused: a Draft that is what visitors see (`REVIEW_INVALID`, `nothingToReview`), one whose values publishing would refuse, with publishing's own error (`checkEntryReady`, `checkGlobalsReady`, `checkConsentTextReady`: `ENTRY_INCOMPLETE`, `ENTRY_INVALID`, …; where it would be published is checked when it is), and an item In review already (`ALREADY_IN_REVIEW`, `data.reviewId`; a partial unique index keeps one waiting Review per item). Anyone who edits may submit (an Editor's AI client without "May publish" can ask a person to check its work); the admin offers it to those who can't publish. No new Permission: submitting and withdrawing take `entry.edit`, deciding takes what publishing the item takes. A submission saves a Checkpoint of the Draft (reason `review`, "Submitted for review"), so the item's history shows what was submitted and compares it with the Draft.
- **The Draft stays shared** (ADR 0025): nothing is locked or frozen while it waits; the Author (or anyone) goes on editing, and whoever acts on it publishes the Draft as it is then, which they see live in the editor. The Review keeps the submitted Draft's version (`draftVersion`), so it says `changedSinceSubmitted`. A frozen copy, as Scheduled publish keeps, would have the reviewer publish something other than what the shared Draft shows.
- **Acting on it**: publishing (each item's `publish`, Publish all, Edit mode's Publish page, an API client, a Scheduled publish's run) ends the item's waiting Review as `published` inside `publishItems` (`reviewsOnPublish`, logged `review.publish`) and stops a sent-back comment showing (`cleared_at`); a Scheduled publish's frozen version does so only when it is the Draft as it is then. Scheduling it (`publishing.schedule`, `scheduleAll`, or taking the current Drafts into a Scheduled publish) ends it as `scheduled` with the time and the Scheduled publish's id (`reviewsOnSchedule`, `review.schedule`); the run publishes on behalf of the person who scheduled it. If that Scheduled publish is instead cancelled, or its run fails, without ever publishing it, the Review is put back to `inReview` in the same transaction (`reopenReviewsOfSchedule`, `review.reopen`; see "Scheduled publish"), rather than waiting forever for a time that will never come. Sending back (`reviews.sendBack`, a required comment of up to 5,000 characters, needing what publishing the item needs) makes it `sentBack`: the item's answer (`Entry.review`, `Globals.review`, the Consent banner words' `review`) carries it, with the comment, until the item is submitted again or published. A Review decided already answers `REVIEW_DECIDED` (409, `data.status`). Editors, Owners, Staff and the Superadmin act on Reviews; Authors never publish by any path (they hold no `entry.publish`: the admin, `/api/v1`, MCP, Publish all, scheduling and Edit mode all refuse them).
- **Withdrawing** (`reviews.withdraw`, `entry.edit`; Edit mode's `review/withdraw`): only the person who submitted it (`REVIEW_NOT_YOURS`, 403), while it waits; nobody is emailed. Statuses: `inReview`, `withdrawn`, `sentBack`, `published`, `scheduled`. Rows stay (ordered by `serial`); deleting an Entry deletes its Reviews.
- **Who hears** (`createReviews`, an `afterAudit` listener, so nothing goes for a change rolled back): `review.submit` emails `review-submitted` (a work email, in the Admin language under Install branding, with the note and a link to the item, or to the Site overview for several) once per submission to each Member who may publish on the Site (its Owners and Editors, found by their role's Permissions, never role names); when the Site has none, to the Staff who reach it, and failing them the Superadmin, so a Review never waits unheard. Otherwise Staff and the Superadmin see what waits in the admin. The submitter is never emailed. `review.publish`, `review.schedule` and `review.sendBack` email the Author `review-result` (`published`, `scheduled` with the time in UTC, or `sent-back` with the comment), unless they acted themselves. `review.reopen` emails nobody: nothing was decided, and whoever removed the Scheduled publish already knows why.
- **Reading** (`site.view`): `reviews.list` (the Site's waiting Reviews, oldest first, or one item's), `reviews.get` (any state) and `reviews.count` (internal: how many the person may act on). Each Review says `canAct` (they may publish the item) and `canWithdraw`.
- **API clients and Toolsets**: `reviews.submit` and `withdraw` are Content writes; `reviews.sendBack` is Publishing and publishes nothing, so an AI client needs no "May publish" for it; publishing and scheduling from a Review go through those procedures, which need it.
- **The admin**: the Entry, Globals and Consent banner editors' Status card offers "Submit for review" (a dialog with the note) where Publish would be, to people who may not publish, and shows the Review (`ui/review/Review.tsx`: `ReviewStatus` with Withdraw and Send back…, `SubmitForReview`, `SendBackDialog`); a sent-back comment shows there until submitted again. Site › Overview lists what waits (`WaitingForReview`), the sidebar's Overview item says how many wait for the person (`place.reviews`), and the Entries list and the Publish all panel mark Drafts In review (`ReviewBadge`). Edit mode's Publish menu gives Authors "Submit for review" with a note, then "Withdraw from review", and shows everyone the page's Reviews (the page state's `reviews`).
- **Tests**: `tests/publishing/review.test.ts`, `tests/edit-mode/review.test.ts`, `tests/browser/review.browser.ts`.

### Revisions and Checkpoints

Every publishable item keeps a history (`GLOSSARY.md` › Revision, Checkpoint; `packages/core/src/history/`, behind `services.history`; procedures `history.*`; tables `revisions`, `checkpoints`, `draft_changes`, Site tables like the content store's): Entries of every Post Type (Pages too), the Globals, the Consent banner's words, a Form's emails, Templates, Components, Loops, Filters, scripts, Forms and the Design system, named as publishing names them (`{ kind, id }`, the kind alone for the items a Site has one of). Post Types, Languages and Site settings have no Draft, so no history.

- **Revisions**: `publishItems` saves one for each item it publishes (`saveRevision`, in the publish's transaction), so publishing one item, every item of Publish all and Scheduled publishes all save one; so do translated values AI translation publishes (Automatic mode, an approval) through `writeEntryTranslations`, `writeGlobalsTranslations` and `writeFormEmailsTranslations`. A Revision holds the whole published version as the item's table stores it (every language; a script's text as a JSON string), who published it and how they came in (`via`, the `AuditVia`), and when. None is saved while Revisions are off: for the Install (Install settings › Revisions, `revisionsOn`) or, for an Entry, for its Post Type (`postTypes.update({ revisions: false })`, `structure.edit`; answered as `revisions` on each Post Type). Revisions saved before stay and follow the usual retention; Checkpoints are still saved.
- **Checkpoints** save the Draft while it is edited (`checkpoints.ts`). Every place that writes a Draft calls `noteDraftChange(scope, item, before, after)` in its transaction (`changeDraft` for Entries; the Globals', Consent banner words', Forms', Form emails', Filters', Loops', Components', scripts', Templates' and the Design system's own writers): the first change once the item's newest Revision or Checkpoint (or, with none, the item itself) is 10 minutes old or more (`CHECKPOINT_EVERY_MS`) saves the Draft as it was just before that change (`reason: "activity"`). `history.leave` (internal) saves the Draft as it is (`left`): the web process calls it when the last participant of a shared Draft leaves (see "Co-editing"), and editors that don't co-edit yet (the Consent banner's words) when they close (`useLeaveOnClose`, with `keepalive` as the tab closes), a restore saves it first (`restore`), and so does a submission for Review (`review`). None is saved when the Draft is what the newest Revision or Checkpoint holds, or holds nothing yet. A Checkpoint records who made the last change it holds and when. Co-editing saves live changes through the items' own writers, so they call `noteDraftChange` like any save.
- **Who changed what**: `noteDraftChange` also records, per part, who changed the Draft last, how and when (`draft_changes`, one row per item, part and language): an Entry's (or the Globals', the Consent banner words') stored values by key and language, as their own values in that language (`parts.ts`; a Body's Blocks without their translation `source`), and a Structure item's (or a Form's emails') sections, kept once for every language (`sectionsOf`: a Template's `tree`, a script's `code`, the top-level keys of the rest).
- **Attribution**: history shows a person and the client they came through ("<person> via <client>": an API token's name, an AI client's). `attribution()` (`history/attribution.ts`) reads the client through `clientNameOf(via)` (`audit.ts`), the one seam every way in shares, so a new kind of client shows once that function names it. `via` is stored whole (`jsonb`), whatever kind it is.
- **Reading** (`history.list`, `history.get`, `history.compare`, public reads): whatever reading the item needs (`historyReadPermission`: `site.view` for Entries, the Globals and the Consent banner's words, `formEmails.edit` for a Form's emails, `structure.edit` for Structure). `list` pages newest first (50, at most 100, `cursor`), with whether publishing it saves Revisions now (`revisions: { on, off: "install" | "postType" | null }`), the retention and `canRestore`; `get` answers a copy with its whole `content`. Copies are named `{ type: "revision" | "checkpoint", id }`; an unknown one is `REVISION_NOT_FOUND` / `CHECKPOINT_NOT_FOUND` (404), an unknown item its own not-found error.
- **Compare** (`compare.ts`, `history.compare({ siteId, item, copy, language? })`): the copy next to the Draft, part by part. For Entries, the Globals and the Consent banner's words: the built-in values (`title`, `slug`, `parent` for Pages, `hiddenLanguages`, `seo`), then the Fields in order (with their labels), then values of keys no Field has now, each in one language (the Default language unless given; `languages` counts the differences in each): a value with nothing of its own there reads `fallback`, a shared one is `shared` and the same in every language. Values read as people read them (`Shown`): text, a choice's label, an Entry's title, a Media file's name, a link's text and address, true/false (unset reads false), a Group's Fields and a Repeater's rows each marked `changed` (rows by id, `added`/`removed`), a Body Block by Block by id (`changed`, `added`, `removed`). Structure sections read as text: Template markup (without `data-wc-id`, names from the Draft's Classes and the Site's Components), CSS and code as they are, the rest as indented JSON, with `lines` marking each line `same`, `removed` or `added` (`lineDiff`: common start and end, then a longest common subsequence up to 4 million cells). Each part that differs carries who changed the Draft's value last and when, from `draft_changes`.
- **Restore** (`restore.ts`, `history.restore`, a public write; not two-step, since nothing is lost): anyone who may change the Draft (`historyEditPermission`: `entry.edit`, Authors too, for Entries, the Globals and the Consent banner's words; `formEmails.edit`; `structure.edit`). In one transaction: a Checkpoint of the Draft (`restore`), then the copy's version written through the item's own save, checked as any change is (`restoreEntryDraft` leaves out links to Entries deleted since; Templates, Components, Filters, Forms, Form emails, scripts and the Design system go through their normal saves, so a Template placing a Component deleted since is refused), and `draft.restore` in the Audit log. It never publishes. With Co-editing it is one more change to the shared Draft (ADR 0025).
- **Retention** (Install settings › Revisions, `install.history.get` / `update`, internal, `install.manage`; logged `historySettings.change`): Revisions are kept `revisionDays` (90) and each item's newest `revisionsKept` (50) however old, whichever keeps more; Checkpoints `checkpointDays` (30) (`HISTORY_LIMITS`). The daily `history.prune` job (03:47) deletes what is past them, the history of items that no longer exist (an Entry, a Component, a Form deleted), and Co-editing's shared documents unused for 30 days or whose item is gone, in batches of 5,000. A Site's history goes with the Site (foreign keys).
- **Size**: a Revision stores a whole version, every language included; Postgres compresses large `jsonb` values itself (TOAST), and retention bounds how many are kept. Backups hold the three tables like any other (`tests/backups/round-trip.test.ts` restores a Revision); a Site export (#109) can carry them as they are: rows name their item and hold its version in its own table's shape.
- **The admin**: the Entry and Globals editors show a History card (`ui/history/HistoryCard.tsx`, the newest 5 copies, loaded with the editor); Site › History (`routes/_admin/sites.$siteId.history.tsx`, `?item=<kind>&itemId=<id>&copy=<type>:<id>&language=…&all=true`) lists the copies, compares the chosen one (the newest unless given) with the Draft, only the parts that differ unless "Show every part", changes marked with words and icons (never colour alone), long markup folded around its changes, and restores after a dialog saying nothing is published. Loops, Forms (and their emails), the Consent banner, Templates and Components link to it (`HistoryLink`); a Post Type has a Revisions tab; Install settings has Revisions.
- **Known gaps**: changing a Site's Default language moves shared values to the new language's key in Drafts and published versions, not in saved copies, so comparing or restoring a copy from before shows shared values where they were then. The generated Classes a Template's publish brings into the published Design system don't save a Design system Revision of their own.

### Co-editing

Several people, AI clients and AI translation change the same Draft at once, with no locks (ADR 0025, `GLOSSARY.md` › Co-editing). Core: `packages/core/src/co-editing/` (`document.ts` and `protocol.ts` and `client.ts` are shared with the browser as `@webcraft/core/draft-document`; `store.ts`, `items.ts`, `hub.ts` are the server's); the web side: `apps/server/src/co-editing/sockets.ts`; tables `draft_documents` and `draft_document_changes` (Site tables).

- **The shared document**: each Draft people open live is a Yjs document (`yjs`, MIT) holding the Draft as its table stores it, less what only the server writes: values kept by key and language (an Entry's, the Globals') keep each value without its `by` and `source` (and a Body's Blocks without theirs), which the save works out as the person (`view.ts`). Objects are maps, lists arrays (items with an `id` keep their place by it: rows, Blocks, tree nodes), text is Yjs text, so two people typing in the same value merge character by character. `changeDocument(doc, next)` makes the smallest change that turns the document into `next`; editors and the server change it only that way. Kinds opened live so far: `LIVE_KINDS` (`entry`, `globals`, `template`, `component`).
- **Postgres keeps it**: `draft_documents` holds each document's merged state (`state`, through `state_through`), the item's Draft as its table stored it after the document's last change (`draft`), and when it was last used; `draft_document_changes` the changes since (each a Yjs update, with `person_id` and `via`), merged into the state once there are 200. A document is made from the Draft the first time someone opens it (`ensureDocument`, holding the item first); one the history prune removes (unused for 30 days, `DOCUMENT_KEPT_DAYS`, or its item deleted) is made again with a new id, and an editor holding the old one starts again from the new one, keeping what it hadn't had saved.
- **Every change an editor sends is checked and saved before anyone else sees it** (`hub.ts`, `applyChange`): the person's session and access are read again (`can()` through the data layer with the item's Permission: `entry.edit` for Content, `structure.edit` for Structure, `formEmails.edit`), then, in one transaction, the item and its document are held (`lockItem`, then the document's row), changes other processes made are brought in, the change is tried on a copy of the document, and what it changed is saved through the item's own save as that person (`saveLiveChange`: an Entry's values language by language through `saveDraft`, the Globals' through `saveGlobalsDraft`, a Template's or Component's whole version through its save, as a restore writes one), then kept as a change. Values of the wrong kind, a slug another Entry has, a person who may only read: the save refuses, the change is dropped for everyone, and its editor gets `refused` (the API's code, message and data) and the whole document to start again from. Broken rules are kept and listed, as everywhere. So the Draft row the renderer, the Staging address and the API read is written as each batch of changes is accepted (editors send at most one batch every 400 ms, `SEND_EVERY_MS`) and is never behind its document when anything publishes it; normalising (trimming, cleaning rich text) happens in the row, never back into the document under someone's cursor. Who changed what (`draft_changes`), `updated_by`/`updated_via` and Checkpoints after 10 minutes of activity work as for any save.
- **Every other Draft writer goes through the document**: `noteDraftChange` (which every writer calls in its transaction) calls `mirrorDraftChange`, which brings the write into the item's document when it has one: only the values the write changed change there (`mergeChange`), so an API token or AI client saving one Field never undoes what someone typed in another meanwhile, and a whole value they set replaces it. API tokens, AI clients (`/api/v1`, `/mcp`), AI translation (Review mode's values, Automatic mode's Draft values), restores, Edit mode's `save`, deleting a linked Entry, Undo of an Import run, changing the Default language (`setDefaultLanguage` calls it itself) all come in that way, as the person who made them (`person_id`, `via`). A write that bypassed it anyway shows as a difference between `draft_documents.draft` and the Draft when the document is opened, and comes in then.
- **Between web processes**: changes are announced on the `webcraft_drafts` channel with `pg_notify` in the transaction that keeps them (delivered on commit), with ids only (`{ t: "change", d, c, p, v }`; a notification holds at most 8,000 bytes); each web process holding the document LISTENs (`listen()` in `@webcraft/db`, a connection of its own outside the pool, opened again a second after it drops, when every document catches up) and reads the changes it lacks from Postgres. Presence goes the same way (`t: "presence"`, each process's own editors, again every 30 seconds), and so does a publish (`t: "published"`, from `publishItems`). Nothing else is needed: no other service.
- **Presence** (`Participant`): who has a Draft open, by name (never colour alone: one of 8 colours by person, `colourOf`), and where (`at`: a value's key, an element's id), here and in other processes; an API client, an AI client or AI translation that changed it shows for 15 seconds as "<person> via <client>" (`via`, from `clientNameOf`) or "<person> via AI translation" (`translation`); a person impersonated by the Superadmin or Staff shows as "<Staff> as <Member>" (`impersonator`, from `impersonatorNameOf`). When someone publishes, the others get `published` with who did ("Published by X"); the publish takes the Draft as it is (the row is current). When the last participant (in every process) leaves, the Draft is kept as a Checkpoint (`services.history.leave`, as that person).
- **The socket**: the admin's editors open `GET /api/drafts/<site id>/<kind>[/<id>]` on the admin host with the session cookie; Edit mode opens `GET /_webcraft/edit/drafts/<kind>[/<id>]` on the Site's own domain with the edit cookie and its header token as a subprotocol (`webcraft-edit-token.<token>`: a browser can't send a header there). Both speak the `webcraft-drafts` subprotocol and must come from their own pages (`Origin` the request's own host; `Sec-Fetch-Site` same-origin when sent), else 403; signed out 401; an item the person may not read 404 or 403 through the data layer, so another Workspace's Draft never opens. `createWebHandler` answers upgrades with `handler.upgrade(request)`: a refusal `Response`, or a `SocketUpgrade` the server opens (`serve.ts` hands it to `Bun.serve`'s WebSockets); the hub is made on the first upgrade (a web process nobody co-edits on never LISTENs) and `handler.stop()` closes it. In development, `vite dev` hands those upgrades to a Bun server on the loopback interface running the same code (`draftSockets` in `apps/server/vite.config.ts`, `src/socket-serving.ts`), so `bun run dev` co-edits as production does.
- **Limits** (`DRAFT_LIMITS`): a frame of at most 1 MiB (`MESSAGE_TOO_LARGE`, close 1009), 600 frames a minute per connection by the Install's clock (`TOO_MANY_MESSAGES`, 1008), 20 Drafts open at once per person in one web process (the upgrade answers 429). A session that ended closes with `SESSION_ENDED` (4401), lost access with `ACCESS_LOST` (4403); every connection is checked again every 30 seconds too.
- **The protocol** (`protocol.ts`): binary frames carry Yjs data (`sync` with a state vector, `state` answering one, `change` from an editor with its number, `changed` from the server), text frames JSON (`welcome`, `presence`, `saved`, `refused`, `published`, `ended`; an editor sends `presence`). An editor connects, asks for what it lacks, and sends what the server lacks (its changes made offline), which merge like any change.
- **Editors** (`createDraftClient`, `client.ts`): their own copy, changed at once, batches sent every 400 ms, `saving`/`saved` from the server's answers; without the server they go on editing (`offline`) and send their changes when it is back; after 5 minutes without it (`OFFLINE_EDITING_MS`) they turn read-only until it is back. Nothing can be changed before the first sync. The admin's Entry and Globals editors (`ui/co-editing/`: `useSharedDraft` over a WebSocket opened again after 1, 2, 4, 8, then every 15 seconds; `useCoEditedValues`; `values.ts` reads a language's values from the document and writes a Field's back as a save takes them) show who else is editing (`PresenceList`), an outline in their colour and their name on the Field each is in (`PresenceOutline`), the state in a live region (`SharedDraftStatus`) and a banner when offline or read-only (`SharedDraftBanner`); Edit mode's bar loads the client as a hashed module on the Site's domain (`/_webcraft/edit/assets/drafts-<hash>.js`, bundled by `Bun.build` from `@webcraft/core/draft-document/edit-mode`, which hands it to the bar in a `webcraft:drafts` event), opens the page's Drafts, shows others' changes in the regions nobody is typing in (rich text cleaned first), lists who else is editing and outlines the regions they are in. What is typed in place still saves through `save`, which goes through the same documents.
- **Not live yet** (their writes come into the documents all the same, so nothing is lost): the visual builder and Code mode keep saving whole versions with the conflict prompt (`CHANGED_MEANWHILE`); the Loops, Forms, Filters, Consent banner, Design system and scripts screens keep `useAutosave` and the saves they had.

### Languages

A Site's languages (ADRs 0003, 0010; `content/languages.ts`, `content/site-languages.ts`, table `site_languages`, procedures `languages.*`) are Structure: everyone who sees the Site reads them (`languages.list`), Staff change them (`structure.edit`), each change applies at once, clears the page cache and is logged. They aren't part of Publish all.

- **A language** is a BCP 47 tag kept in its canonical form (`canonicalLanguage`: a language, an optional script and region: `fr`, `pt-BR`, `zh-Hant`, `es-419`; `LANGUAGE_INVALID` otherwise), a name (its own name, `Français`, unless given), a direction (`ltr`/`rtl`, from the code unless given) and an optional domain of its own (in lower case and its ASCII form, unique on the Install among every Site's domains; never the admin host or under the sites domain; `LANGUAGE_TAKEN` with `data.field`). A language's domain is also on the Site's domain list (`site_domains`, role `language`), where it is verified like any other domain; once verified, it serves that language without a prefix (see "Domains"). Every Site has a row for its Default language (`sites.default_language`, `en` for a new Site); `siteLanguages(scope)` answers them, the Default language first.
- **Addresses**: the Default language is unprefixed; every other language's paths start with its code in lower case (`/fr/blog/pain-frais`, `/pt-br/…`; its home page at `/fr`). `languageOfPath` reads a path's language, `languagePath` writes one. A prefix belongs to its language: a top-level Page, an Entry under a `{slug}`-first URL pattern, or a URL pattern whose first part is a prefix is refused with `PATH_TAKEN` (`by: { kind: "language", code, name }`), and adding a language whose prefix the Site's paths already start with is refused naming the Page or Post Type there (`checkPrefixFree`). These take `lockSitePaths` first. A path starting with the Default language's own code goes on (301) to the path without it where nothing else answers, so a new Default language's old addresses keep working.
- **Adding** (`languages.add`) makes every published Entry answer in the new language at once, at its Default language slug (`publishSlugsIn`), and found by Site search there once the index is rebuilt (adding a language and changing the Default language ask for a rebuild); **removing** (`languages.remove`, two steps: the first answers `preview.pages`) deletes its published slugs, its 404 page and its Site search documents; values written in it stay in the content store, so adding it again brings them back. The Default language can't be removed.
- **Changing the Default language** (`languages.setDefault`, two steps; the first answers `preview: { from, to, changedPaths, withoutOwnTitle }`, the warning the admin shows) moves every shared value (shared Fields, `parent`, `hiddenLanguages`, in Entries' Drafts and published versions and the Globals) to the new Default language's key, so it keeps its value; Translatable fields now fall back to the new one. Every published address that changes is answered and logged as `pathChanges` (`defaultLanguage.change` logs them and their count, `changedPaths`); Redirect rules follow them (see "Redirects and the 404 log").
- **Slugs** are translatable and never change on their own once published: publishing gives each language the slug of its own, else the one it was published with before, else (the first time) the Default language's (`published_slugs` has a row per Entry and language). An Entry's `path` and `published.slug` are its address in the language asked for. Slugs clash per language (`ENTRY_SLUG_TAKEN` with `data.language`).
- **Publishing** checks every language the Entry (or the Globals) is shown in; a problem of another language's own value carries `language`.
- **Language visibility** (Content, in the Draft): `hiddenLanguages` on `entries.create` / `saveDraft` lists the languages an Entry is hidden in, stored beside `parent` as one shared value. Once published, the Entry answers 404 there, has no `hreflang` alternate there, and is left out where Entries are listed in that language: Relationships on pages now; Loops (#62) and sitemaps (#92) call the same rule, `isVisibleIn(version, languages)` or, in a query, `publishedVisibleIn(languages)` (`content/visibility.ts`).
- **Pages**: `<html lang dir>` are the page's language's; every page of a Site with more than one language lists `<link rel="alternate" hreflang>` for each language it is shown in, at that language's address (absolute, from `SiteHost.addresses`: on the Staging address every language under its prefix there; on the Site's domains each language on its own verified domain, else under its prefix on the Primary domain), then `x-default` at the Default language's; the 404 page lists none.
- **The Language switcher** is the Template command `{{@languageSwitcher}}`: a `<nav class="wc-language-switcher">` named in the page's language, a list of links to the page in each language (that language's home page where the Entry is hidden, and on the 404 page), each named in its own language with `lang`/`hreflang` (and `dir` when it differs), the page's own marked `aria-current`. It renders nothing on a Site with one language, and goes only where a `<nav>` can. A link to a language on another of the Site's domains goes straight there (its address); the others go through `/_wc/lang/<path>` (`LANGUAGE_CHOICE_PATH`): the pipeline keeps the language of the path in the `webcraft.language` cookie (`__Host-` on HTTPS, a year) and sends the visitor on (303, `no-store`), without JavaScript; any other path there is a plain 404.
- **The browser-language redirect** (`languages.setBrowserRedirect`, off by default, `sites.browser_language_redirect`): a request to `/` with no language cookie, from a browser (not a robot by its user agent, not without one), not coming from the Site itself, goes on (302, `no-store`) to the home page in the language its `Accept-Language` prefers among those the home page is shown in, which is then remembered in the cookie. Deep links are never redirected, nor anything on a language's own domain (its `/` is already that language's). A language on its own domain is sent to its address there. While it is on, `/` answers with `Vary: Accept-Language, Cookie`.
- **Fixed text** in Templates (and Components, Forms) has a value per language: a text node keeps its text as written and, optionally, `translations` by language tag; pages in those languages show theirs, every other page the text as written (`textIn`, `packages/renderer/src/languages.ts`). Template markup writes it as `{{ "Read more" fr: "Lire la suite" }}`, in text and in attribute values (`\"` for a quote, `\\` for a backslash, `\r` for a carriage return); text with other languages is never joined with the text beside it.
- **Edit mode** shows a page in its path's language: its Drafts in that language, regions saved in it (`save` takes `language`), and the editing bar lists the page in every language (`state.languages`).
- **AI translation** builds on this: each stored value keeps who wrote it (`by`) and what it translates (`source`); see "AI keys and AI translation".

## AI keys and AI translation

WebCraft calls AI providers directly (ADR 0011) with the Workspace's AI key, records the tokens and estimated cost of every call, and keeps monthly caps itself. AI translation (`GLOSSARY.md` › Translation) builds on that. Code: `packages/core/src/ai/` (`services.aiKeys`, the provider port) and `packages/core/src/translation/` (`services.translations`); tables `install_ai_keys`, `workspace_ai`, `ai_calls`, `ai_budgets`, `protected_terms`, `translation_cache`, `translation_tasks`, `translation_counts`, and `site_languages.translation_mode`.

### AI keys

- **A Workspace's own key** (Workspace › AI keys, `aiKey.manage`: Staff and Owners; procedures `aiKeys.*`): a provider (`AI_PROVIDERS`: `openai`, `anthropic`, `google`, `openrouter`, `openaiCompatible`), a model, an endpoint (an OpenAI-compatible provider only), an optional price and an optional monthly cap. The key is the Workspace secret `ai.key`. Setting, replacing and removing it are a Sensitive action (`aiKey.change`). **A stored key is never sent anywhere new**: changing the provider or the endpoint without typing the key again is refused (`AI_KEY_INVALID`, `field: "key"`, `reason: "needed"`), as for SMTP passwords.
- **Shared Install keys** (Install settings › AI, the Superadmin; procedures `sharedAiKeys.*`, internal): each with a name, provider, model, endpoint, price; its key the Install secret `ai.shared:<id>`. Assigned to a Workspace (`sharedAiKeys.assign({ workspace, keyId, monthlyCap })`) with a **required** monthly cap; a shared key needs a price (a cap means nothing without one).
- **Which key**: the Workspace's own key wins; the shared one is used only without it (`keyInUse`). `aiKeys.get` shows the own key (only "set" and its last 4 characters), the shared one assigned (never its key or last characters), which is in use, and this month's usage per key and per Site.
- **Every call** goes through `services.aiKeys.call(place, purpose, estimate, run)`: it picks the key, reserves the call's estimated cost against the cap, fills the key in for that call only (`secrets.useWith`) and records what the provider reported (`ai_calls`: tokens, estimated cost, the Site, the purpose: `translation`, `altText`, `test`). Suggest alt text uses it too. Keys and request bodies are never logged: the adapters log nothing, and errors name the provider's status, never the key or the text.
- **Prices** (`ai/models.ts`, `@webcraft/core/ai-models` for the admin): a small table of each provider's suggested models with their price in dollars per million input and output tokens, written down as estimates; any other model id may be typed, and a key's own `price` wins over the table. Costs are kept in micro-dollars. A model without a price records its tokens at no cost, so a cap needs a price.
- **Caps** are per Workspace, per key (`own`, `shared`) and per calendar month (UTC), counted in `ai_budgets`. **Race-safe**: a call reserves its estimate with one conditional update (`spent + reserved < cap`), so two calls at once see each other's reservation and can't both pass the cap; a call may pass it by what it costs beyond its estimate, never more. A reservation left by a process that stopped is given back after 30 minutes (`translations.resume`, hourly). At **80%** the Workspace's Owners and Staff get the `ai-key-cap` email (once a month) and the screens show a notice; at **100%** calls are refused (`AI_CAP_REACHED`, with `resumesAt`) and the 100% email goes once. Raising the cap or the month ending lets calls start again.

### The providers

`InstallPorts.ai` (`AiProviders`: `translate`, `describeImage`) is the edge. The processes use `createHttpAiProviders` (`ai/http-providers.ts`): OpenAI's chat completions (OpenAI, OpenRouter, OpenAI-compatible endpoints, the key as a bearer token), Anthropic's Messages API (`x-api-key`, `anthropic-version: 2026-10-21`, no sampling parameters; models that take them ask for server-side fallbacks) and Google's `generateContent` (the key in `x-goog-api-key`, never in the address). Every call goes through the guarded fetcher: only to the provider's host (or the key's endpoint), checked and pinned, no redirect followed, 120 seconds. A translation sends a JSON array of texts and expects one back, the same length, every marker kept (`AiProviderError` `answer` otherwise; `refused` for a refused key or request, `unavailable` for the rest).

**Self-hosted models**: a Workspace's own endpoint must be `https` on the public internet, checked when saved and again by the guarded fetcher on every call.
