A shared Install key may reach an endpoint inside the Install's own network (and use `http`) only while the Superadmin allows it (Install settings › AI, `aiPrivateEndpoints`, a Sensitive action): the guarded fetcher's `privateNetwork` option, which nothing else passes. Clients can never point a key inside the network.

### Translation modes and the work

- **Modes** (Site settings › AI translation and Site › Translations, `translation.manage`; `translations.setMode`, logged `translationMode.change`): each language other than the Default language is `automatic`, `review` or `manual` (the default).
- **After a publish**: `translations.afterAudit` hears `entry.publish`, `globals.publish` and `formEmails.publish` (whatever published them: one item, Publish all, a Scheduled publish), and queues a task per Automatic and Review language (`translation_tasks`, one per item and language) and a `translations.run` job. The publish never waits for it and never fails because of it.
- **Automatic** translates the item's published Default-language values into the language and publishes them (`publish: true`): the published version and, where the Draft's value in that language is still the one published, the Draft. Then the page cache, Site search and the Audit log (`translation.publish`, which Webhooks turn into a `publish` event). A translation breaking its Field's rules is written to the Draft only.
- **Review** translates the Draft's Default-language values into the Draft. The Review queue (`translations.queue`) lists AI values in the Draft that aren't published, and Outdated translations; **approving** (`translations.approve`, `entry.publish`) publishes the AI's values waiting in one language, for some items or all (`translation.publish`).
- **Translate now** (`translations.translateNow`) queues every Entry, the Globals and every Form's emails for the Automatic and Review languages (or one of them).
- **The work** runs as the person who asked for it (who published, or pressed Translate now), with their access as it is when it runs (AuditVia `translation`), and is **all or nothing per item and language**: when a call fails, nothing of it is written. Without a key the task is `held` (`noKey`), at the cap `held` (`cap`); a held task starts again when a key or cap changes (`aiKeys.onChange`) or, hourly, when a new month can pay for it. A provider failure or refused answer is `failed` with its reason; Translate now or the item's next publish queue it again. `translations.overview` shows each language's progress, to review, Outdated, missing, queued and waiting work with why, the key in use, and this Site's usage this month (cost only for `aiUsage.view`).

### What is sent

Only text (`translation/units.ts`, `translation/engine.ts`): an Entry's title, SEO title and description, and its Translatable `text`, `richText`, `link` (its text, never its address) and `body` Fields, through Groups and Repeater rows; the Globals' likewise; a Form's Auto-reply subject and text (Notifications go out in the Default language). Never numbers, dates, choices, Media, addresses, code, slugs, ids or shared values. Formatted text is one piece per paragraph with numbered markers where its formatting goes (`Bake at ⟦1⟧220 °C⟦/1⟧`, `⟦B1⟧` for a line break): Blocks through each type's `translatable`/`translated` in the Block-type registry, rich text through `richTextTranslation` (`@webcraft/renderer`). Links, attributes and code stay behind and come back where they were.

- **Protected terms** (`translation/terms.ts`; Workspace › Protected terms and the Site's settings; `protectedTerms.*`, `protectedTerms.edit`; logged `protectedTerm.add`, `protectedTerm.remove`): the Workspace's apply to every Site, a Site's to that Site. Each occurrence (a whole word; any case unless `matchCase`; longer terms first) is swapped for `⟦T1⟧`, `⟦T2⟧`… and put back exactly as written there. An answer that drops, adds or changes any marker is refused.
- **The cache**: per Site, language and set of terms (`glossary`, a hash of the terms that applied), by the SHA-256 of each piece sent. Unchanged text is never sent twice; batches already paid for stay cached when a later one fails. `translation_counts` keeps how many pieces came from the cache and how many were sent, per month (the screens' cache hits).

### Who wrote what, and Outdated translations

Every value in a language other than the Default language keeps who wrote it (`by`) and the Default-language value it translates (`source`, `content/translation-sources.ts`). A person's save records the source as it is then (`personValue`); confirming (`translations.confirm`) records it again without changing the value. **A value a person wrote is never overwritten**: once its source changes it is an Outdated translation, until someone updates, confirms or re-translates it (`translations.retranslate`, which replaces it on purpose). A value the AI wrote from a source that changed since is translated again.

A Body field keeps this **per Block** (ADR 0018): each Block of a translation has `source: { id, by, was }`, the Block it translates, who wrote it and what that Block held. A save in another language keeps an unchanged Block's source, gives a changed Block `by: "person"` and the source Block as it is now, treats a Block still holding exactly its source's content as a copy waiting for translation (no source), and leaves a person's own Blocks without one. AI translation rebuilds the list in the Default language's order: a person's Blocks stay (Outdated on their own), current AI Blocks stay, the rest are translated (or copied, with no text), AI Blocks whose source is gone are dropped, and a person's own Blocks stay where they were.

Each Entry the API answers in another language carries `translation` (`ItemTranslation`): its Outdated translations (`at`, `block`, `labels`, the translation, the old and the new source), the values the AI wrote, those waiting to be published and those missing. The Entry editor shows the Outdated ones above the Fields, with Confirm and Re-translate. Form emails keep the AI's translations apart (`forms.emails_ai_translations`, by language and place, with the text it wrote and its source): a translation is the AI's only while its text is still that one.

### Tests

`install.ai` answers translations with `translateWith((text, request) => …, usage?)` (`taggedTranslation` writes `[it] Fresh bread`, keeping every marker), images with `answer(…)`, and fails with `fail(problem)`; `install.ai.translations()` lists what was sent. `createTestInstall({ aiProviders: "http" })` uses the real providers on the fake outgoing HTTP (`tests/ai/providers.test.ts`). Keys, usage and caps are in `tests/ai/`, translation in `tests/translation/` (`support.ts` makes a translated Site), the screens in `tests/browser/translations.browser.ts`.

## The Site pipeline and the page cache

`apps/server/src/site/pipeline.ts` answers every hostname other than the admin host (ADR 0004):

0. **Domain verification** comes first: `/_webcraft/verify/<token>` on any hostname answers the Install's own answer for one of its domains' tokens (`services.domains.verificationAnswer`), else the plain 404, before the hostname step, as a domain waiting for DNS belongs to no Site yet (see "Domains").
0b. **The Share link address** (`share.<sites domain>`) is the Install's own, never a Site's: `shareAnswer` (`apps/server/src/site/share.ts`) answers it before the hostname step (see "Reports and Share links").
1. **Hostname → Site** (`services.sitePages.resolveHost`), compared in lower case without a trailing dot: a Staging address, `<site slug>.<WEBCRAFT_SITES_DOMAIN>`, or one of the Site's **verified** domains (see "Domains"). The answer (`SiteHost`) says how the hostname reaches the Site (`address`: `staging`, `primary`, `redirect` or `language`, with the `language` a language's domain serves) and where the Site's pages are (`addresses`). A **Redirect domain** answers every request with a 301 to the same path and query on the Primary domain (`Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, so a later change of Primary domain can't loop in a browser), or the plain 404 while the Site has no verified Primary domain. Anything else gets the unbranded 404. The admin host never resolves to a Site, and no Site slug can make a Staging address equal to it (`reservedSlugs`).
1a. **The Content API** (`/_content/v1/…`, `apps/server/src/site/content-api.ts`): answered right after the hostname step, before the Staging address's checks and Redirect rules, with a Content key (see "The Content API").
1b. **Redirect rules** (`services.sitePages.redirect`), for `GET` and `HEAD` on every path but the Install's own (`/_…`), where the Module keeping them runs (SDK 1.5, see "Modules" and "Redirects and the 404 log"): a 301 or 302 to where the Site's rules send the path, the request's query parameters added to the target's own, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, never kept as a page. They come before everything else the Site answers (its pages, its files, Edit mode), as the spec orders them; a rule WebCraft added for a changed path steps aside where a page answers. Answers are kept per web process with the Site's page cache generation.
2. **Only `GET` and `HEAD`** for now (405 otherwise), except where a reserved path's feature takes more: a Module's Site routes, Form posts (`POST /_webcraft/forms/<id>`, see "Forms") and Consent records (`POST /_wc/consent`).
3. **Paths the Install keeps** (`isReservedPath`): every path whose first part starts with `_` (`/_webcraft/…`, `/_media/…`, `/_content/v1/…`, `/_wc/m/<module>/…`) and every path naming a file, with a dot in a part (`/sitemap.xml`, `/robots.txt`, `/llms.txt`, Markdown copies such as `/about.md`). No slug or URL pattern part can start with `_` or hold a dot, so no Page or Entry is ever there. Each feature answers its own (add its step here), before Entry routing and never kept as pages: the discovery files (`/robots.txt`, `/sitemap.xml` and `/sitemaps/<key>-<n>.xml`, each language's `llms.txt`, Markdown copies at a page's address plus `.md`; see "Discovery files"), kept in the page cache like pages; Media files at `/_media/<hash>-<name>.<ext>` (see "Media"); the Site's stylesheet at `/_wc/css/<hash>.css` and scripts at `/_wc/js/<hash>.js`: WebCraft's own (the theme toggle's and the embed script of a body's videos, see "The Design system" and "The Body field"), the Site's published Site script and page scripts (see "Scripts"), the code of its outside scripts' snippets, and any of them replaced in the last 30 days; its Libraries at `/_wc/lib/<hash>-<file name>` (see "Site script safety"); Edit mode and Code mode at `/_webcraft/edit/…` (see "Edit mode"); Form posts at `/_webcraft/forms/<id>` (see "Forms"); Consent records at `POST /_wc/consent` (see "The Consent banner"); the Language switcher's links at `/_wc/lang/…`, which remember the visitor's language and send them on (see "Languages"); a Filter's fragments at `/_wc/filter/<page path>?<choices>` (see "Filters"); Modules' Site routes at `/_wc/m/<route prefix>/…`, which go to the Module host (`services.modules.answerSiteRoute`, with how the hostname reaches the Site, the client address and whether an edit cookie holds) and are answered where the Module runs, with any method it declares (see "Modules"), Site analytics' collection path among them (`POST /_wc/m/analytics/event`, see "Site analytics"). The rest get the plain 404 until their features come.
4. **Edit mode**: a request carrying an edit cookie that still holds gets the page rendered afresh for Edit mode, never from or into the page cache (see "Edit mode"); an edit cookie that no longer holds is cleared and the request goes on as a visitor's.
5. **The browser-language redirect**, when the Site has it on: a first request to `/` may go on to the home page in the browser's language (see "Languages").
6. **Page cache lookup** (after a Form just sent, `?wc-sent=<n>`, is answered for its visitor alone, never from or into the cache; see "Forms") by the address's origin and normalised URL (`canonicalPath`): decoded path segments, no trailing slash, and of the query parameters only `page`, the page of a paginated Loop (`/blog?page=2`: each page its own entry), and what the page's Filters allow, in their normal form (see "Filters"). `page` is a whole number from 2 to `MAX_PAGE_NUMBER` (500) written without leading zeros; `?page=1` answers a 301 to the page itself (keeping a search and Filters' choices), and anything else (`0`, `02`, `abc`, the parameter twice) the 404 page. Every other parameter is dropped. The Site's search results page is never looked up or kept: it answers each search afresh (`no-store`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`, at most `SEARCH_RATE_LIMIT` searches a minute per client address; see "Site search"), and `?q=…&page=1` goes on to `?q=…`. The same path is another page on each of the Site's addresses (its language, its absolute links), so each address keeps its own.
7. **Render on a miss** (`services.sitePages.render`): in the language the hostname and the path name (`pathOnHost`: on the Staging address, and in Edit mode, the language the path's prefix names, the Default language without one; on a language's own domain, that language, without a prefix, a path starting with any language's prefix going on (301, keeping the query) to where that language's path is; on the Primary domain, as on the Staging address, except that the prefix of a language with a verified domain of its own goes on (301) there), the published Archive template whose address the path is (see "Archive templates"); else the published Entry whose Post Type's URL pattern and published slug match, through its published Entry template (an Entry whose Entry template isn't published has no page yet); else the published Page at that path; at `/`, the home page. Every page renders inside the Site template. The page is kept in the cache: it is rendered with `sitePages.render(host, path, undefined, { forms: { cached: true }, page })` (one options value: what its Forms show, see "Forms", and the page number), so its Forms hold the token mark that every answer, HIT or MISS, fills in. The home page's own path answers a 301 to `/`. A page number on a page where no Loop paginates (an Entry's page) answers a 301 to the page itself, and one past the paginated Loop's last page the 404 page.
8. **Answer** with `Content-Type: text/html`, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate` (browsers and proxies revalidate every time, since a publish must show at once), a strong `ETag` (SHA-256 of the HTML; `If-None-Match` gets 304), `X-Cache: HIT` or `MISS`, the page's Content Security Policy (kept with the page in the cache, see "Site script safety"), and on a Staging address `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` (never on the Site's domains).

Every answer on a Site's hostname, whatever it is (a page, a 404, a file, a redirect, a Redirect domain's, a domain verification, a refusal, a Module's route, Edit mode's), leaves the pipeline through `withSecurityHeaders`, which adds the fixed security headers it doesn't carry already (see "Site script safety").
9. **A path with no page** where an Entry was until it was unpublished (`sitePages.gone`) gets **410 Gone** with the 404 page as its body (see "Scheduled publish"); any other gets the Site's **404 page** (`services.sitePages.notFound(host, pathname)`), in the path's language: the Page Staff chose for it or for the Site, once published, else the built-in one (`builtInNotFoundPage`: a heading and one sentence in the page's language), inside the Site template, with status 404 and `Cache-Control: no-store`. It is never kept in the page cache and has no ETag, so every miss reaches the Site and a page published at the path shows at once. A visitor's miss goes to the Site's 404 log (`services.sitePages.noteMiss`, see "Redirects and the 404 log") without the answer waiting for it: only `GET` from a browser (not a robot by its user agent, not a prefetch), never in Edit mode, never a file or the Install's own paths (those get the plain 404), never past a paginated Loop's last page.

**The page cache** (`apps/server/src/site/page-cache.ts`) is in the web process's memory, 64 MiB by default, least recently used pages going first. Every page is kept under the Site's page cache generation, read with the hostname; `publish` raises the generation in Postgres in the publish's own transaction, so the whole Site's cache is cleared at once, in every web process, whichever process published (the worker too). A request that read an older generation never stores its page. A page that shows something time alone changes is kept only until then (`SitePage.expiresAt`, read by the Install's clock): a Condition comparing `now` with a later date until that date, a Loop with a date window relative to now (the last 12 months, upcoming) for `RELATIVE_WINDOW_REFRESH_MS` (5 minutes). The Content API keeps its answers under the same generation (see "The Content API"), and a CDN purge can key on it too.

### The Staging address

A Site's Staging address (ADR 0014, `<site slug>.<WEBCRAFT_SITES_DOMAIN>`) shows the Site as it would be with every Draft published, to the people reviewing it and nobody else. Core: `packages/core/src/staging/` (`services.staging`: links, sessions, the staging password and the page shown to strangers); the web side: `apps/server/src/site/staging.ts`, before step 1b above.

- **Who may open it**: someone signed in to the admin who sees the Site (`site.view`: its Members reaching it, Staff, the Superadmin), through a hand-off like Edit mode's: "Staging" in the admin's top bar (and View in Publish all, and Site › Domains & Staging) calls `staging.open({ siteId, path })`, which answers `…/_webcraft/staging/enter?token=…`, working once, within 60 seconds, for that person, Site and admin session (`staging_links`); it is the admin's own, never served to API tokens. Following it (rate-limited like sign-in) starts a Staging session (`staging_sessions`, at most 8 hours, gone with the admin session) and sets `__Host-webcraft.staging` (`webcraft.staging` on `.localhost`; `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`), then answers 303 to the page. Every request checks the session again: not run out, its admin session still signed in, the person still seeing the Site. An edit cookie that holds lets the browser in too (Edit mode lives on the Staging address while the Site has no domain), and `/_webcraft/edit/…` keeps its own checks.
- **The staging password** (optional, `staging.get`, `staging.setPassword`: `staging.manage`, Owners and Staff; a Sensitive action, so never through an API token; logged `stagingPassword.change` with `set`): 8 to 200 characters, kept as the same scrypt hash people's passwords get (`sites.staging_password_hash`). Given on the Staging address's page (`POST /_webcraft/staging/password`, from the Staging address's own page only, at most `STAGING_PASSWORD_RATE_LIMIT`, 10 a minute per client address and Site, then 429), it sets `__Host-webcraft.staging-password` for a week (`STAGING_PASSWORD_ACCESS_MS`): an expiry signed with HMAC-SHA256 under a key derived from the Master key (`stagingPasswordKey`) over the Site and the password's hash, so setting it again, even to the same password, or removing it shuts out every browser it let in.
- **Everyone else**, for every path (pages, files, Modules' routes, Form posts): a page asking them to sign in (`stagingPage`: in the path's language, the seven pages have words in; a link to the admin's `/staging/<site id>?path=…`, which signs them in and opens the address at that page; and, with a password, its form), with status 403, `private, no-store`, `noindex` and its own policy (`STAGING_PAGE_POLICY`: its own stylesheet at `/_wc/staging/page-<hash>.css`, no script). Anything other than `GET` or `HEAD` gets a plain 403. Cookies that no longer open anything are cleared.
- **What it shows** (`showsDrafts(host)`, read by `site-pages/index.ts`'s `viewFor`): the same renderer as the Site's domains, through a `View` with every Draft: the Entries' and the Globals' Drafts, the Templates' and Components' Drafts, the Design system's Draft (its stylesheet served as `/_wc/css/<hash>.css` on the Staging address only, built by `draftStyles`), the Site and page scripts' Drafts (served as `/_wc/js/<hash>.js` there only), the Loops' Drafts over every Entry's Draft, Forms' Drafts and the Consent banner's Draft words. It reads through `services.data.staging(site)`.
- **Draft addresses** (`content/draft-paths.ts`): a path finds the Entry whose Draft would be published there (`publishedSlug`'s rule, in SQL: its own slug in the language, else the one it was published with there, else the Default language's), a Page by its Draft parents, an Archive template by its Draft, never-published ones too; Loops, Relationships and the page's languages link Entries at their Draft addresses. Edit mode routes the published page first, then such an Entry (`publishedThenDraft`), so a Page never published can be edited in place, and its page switcher lists it.
- **Never indexed, never kept**: every answer says `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`; pages also `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` and `Cache-Control: private, no-store` (no ETag, never in the page cache, so a CDN in front never keeps one either); their canonical URL points at the Primary domain, and while the Site has none they carry none, so the Staging address is never a page's canonical address; the 404 log hears nothing from it. Its `robots.txt` keeps every crawler out (`STAGING_ROBOTS`), answered to everyone before the access check so crawlers read it; it has no sitemaps, `llms.txt` or Markdown copies (a plain 404, even for the people reviewing it).
- **Seams**: Form posts on the Staging address are still checked against the published Form (a Form never published shows there, but its post answers 404); Forms' messages join Content Drafts with #89.

### Element trees and the renderer

Templates are stored as element trees (`packages/renderer/src/tree.ts`, ADR 0002), the one canonical format the builder, Template markup and the renderer share:

```ts
{ version: 1, nodes: [
  { type: "element", id: "Xb3…", tag: "article", attributes: [], classes: ["<Class id>"], children: [
    { type: "element", id: "Q9z…", tag: "h1", attributes: [], classes: [], children: [{ type: "value", path: "title" }] },
    { type: "value", path: "publishedAt", format: { type: "date", style: "long" } },
    { type: "comment", text: " kept for Staff, never sent to visitors " },
    { type: "condition", branches: [{ test: { type: "value", path: "summary" }, children: [/* … */] }], otherwise: null },
    { type: "element", id: "k2P…", tag: "a", classes: [],
      attributes: [{ name: "href", value: [{ type: "text", text: "/blog/" }, { type: "value", path: "slug" }] }],
      children: [{ type: "text", text: "Read more" }] },
  ] },
] }
```

- Elements have a stable `id` (filled in when missing, kept when given), a lower-case `tag` from `TEMPLATE_ELEMENTS`, ordered `attributes` (a list: Postgres `jsonb` doesn't keep key order, and a round trip must lose nothing), `classes` (the ids of the Site's Classes, ADR 0009), `children` and an optional layer `label` (what the visual builder's Layers panel calls it, one line of at most 100 characters, never rendered; `labelProblem`). Text nodes are fixed text, as written and optionally in other languages (`translations`, see "Languages"); value nodes and attribute value parts are **bindings**: a `path` (`title`, `address.city`, `category.url`, `site.phone`; later `props.x`, `item.x`) and an optional `format`, which Edit mode (#72) reads to tag editable regions. Comments are kept and never rendered. Template commands' nodes (a Condition so far) come from the command registry (see "Template markup").
- `parseElementTree(input, options)` checks a tree and answers its canonical form (text next to text joined, empty text dropped, line breaks in comments as `\n`), or throws `ElementTreeInvalidError` with every problem and where (`nodes[0].children[2].attributes[1]`): scripts, elements outside `TEMPLATE_ELEMENTS`, `on…`, `style`, `class`, `srcdoc` and `data-wc-…` attributes, unknown value names (`isKnownValue`), Class ids the Site doesn't have (`classIds`), nesting a browser would change (a block inside `<p>`, a link inside a link, table parts out of place, `<li>` inside `<li>`), duplicate ids, children of void elements, NUL and broken characters, more than 64 levels or 10,000 nodes. The API answers it as `TEMPLATE_INVALID` with `data.problems`. The element and attribute rules are functions (`elementProblem`, `attributeProblem`, `nestingProblem`) the markup parser calls too, so both refuse the same things with the same words.
- New node types join the `TreeNode` union; a change old trees can't be read as raises `ELEMENT_TREE_VERSION`.
- `renderPage` (`packages/renderer/src/page.ts`) writes a whole document: `<html lang dir>` (the direction from the language), the title (`PageToRender.seo`'s, with the page's SEO tags and structured data, see "SEO"; without it `<title> · <Site name>`), a skip link as the first thing in `<body>`, then the Site template (the built-in one when the Site has none published) with the page's content (an Entry template, a Page's layout, the 404 page's content) rendered at its `{{@outlet}}`. The Site template's one `<main>` gets the id the skip link points at and `tabindex="-1"`, so following the link moves focus there; WebCraft adds them through `extraAttributes`. With `editMode` (`EditModePage`), `renderPage` writes the page for Edit mode instead: `noindex`, WebCraft's own files first in `<head>`, and editable regions tagged (`RenderOptions.editRegion`; see "Edit mode"). Landmarks (`<header>`, `<nav>`, `<footer>`) are the Site template's. The words WebCraft writes into pages (the skip link, the built-in 404 page) come from `pageWords(language)`: English, Italian, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Dutch, by the language's primary subtag, English for any other. **Accessibility hooks** the Design system (#64) styles from its base layer are named in `PAGE_HOOKS`: the skip link's class `wc-skip-link` (out of sight until focused), the main id `wc-main`, and the custom properties the focus style reads (`--wc-focus-color`, `--wc-focus-width`, `--wc-focus-offset`), set from Design tokens; it can be restyled but not removed (see "The Design system"). A body's hooks are there too: `wc-callout` (with the tones' custom properties `--wc-callout-info`, `--wc-callout-success`, `--wc-callout-warning`, `--wc-callout-danger`) and `wc-embed`. Values are typed (`TemplateValue`: text, numbers, true/false, dates, rich text, a body's Blocks, a table of contents, or a list of them); the Site pipeline hands dates over as dates and rich text marked with `richText(html)` (see "What a binding reads"). A list renders as its values joined with commas, each through the value's formatter. Text and values are escaped for text or double-quoted attributes; `href`, `src` and other URL attributes (each `srcset` candidate, each `ping` URL) keep only `http`, `https`, `mailto`, `tel`, relative paths and `#anchors`, anything else becoming `#`. A date without a formatter renders as `2026-03-02`. Rich text renders as its cleaned HTML in text and as its plain text in attributes and formatters. Comments are left out. A placed Form (`{{@form <key>}}`, a `FormNode`) renders through `RenderOptions.form` (see "Forms"); a Form's CAPTCHA joins the page's outside scripts through `PageToRender.formScripts`, asked once the page has rendered. No inline script, inline style or event handler is ever written: the `<head>` links the Site's stylesheet, and a page showing a theme toggle (or a body's video) links its script file, as a page asking for consent links the consent script (see "The Consent banner").

## Site search

Site search (ADR 0021, `GLOSSARY.md`; `packages/core/src/search/`, behind `services.search` for the worker) is a built-in Filter with only a search box, on a query Loop over the searchable Post Types, on the Site's search results page. Filters (#97) build on the same pieces: the index, `searchQueryIn` (the `q` parameter and its checks), `Narrowing` on a Loop's query, `pageHref`'s state and `searchSnippets`.

- **The index** (table `search_documents`, a Site table): one row per published Entry and language it is shown in, `(entry_id, language)`, holding a `tsvector` (`document`, GIN-indexed) and the text besides the title (`text`, for snippets). Queries always name the Site and the language, so each Site and language has an index of its own. Every Post Type is indexed; `searchable` only decides what Site search lists.
- **Searched fields** (`search/searched-fields.ts`, also read by the admin as `@webcraft/core/search-fields`): each Post Type's `searchedFields`, `[{ field, weight }]`, `field` being `title` or a Field's path (`summary`, `origin.city` in a Group, `steps.text` across a Repeater's rows) of a type search reads as text (`SEARCHED_TYPES`: text, rich text, Body, select (its labels), link (its text), email), `weight` `A` (counts most) to `D`; at most `SEARCHED_FIELDS_MAX` (50). A new Post Type searches its title (A) and its Body field (B); Pages their title. A Field removed from the Post Type leaves its Searched fields. Refusals are `POST_TYPE_INVALID` at `searchedFields[1].field` (`searchedFieldUnknown`, `searchedFieldType`, `searchedFieldTwice`, `searchedFieldsCount`). The admin sets them on the Post Type's **Site search** tab.
- **Languages** (`search/text-search.ts`): documents and queries use Postgres's text search configuration for the language's primary subtag where Postgres has one (`textSearchConfiguration`: `en` English, `fr` French, `pt-BR` Portuguese, `nb`/`nn`/`no` Norwegian, 30 in all), `simple` (words as written, lower-cased, nothing stemmed) for every other language. Values are read in the language with the Default language's as fallback, one Field at a time; a body gives its Blocks' text (`bodyText`), rich text its text. Each weight's text is cut at 100,000 characters, and control characters become spaces.
- **Kept up to date**: publishing an Entry rewrites its documents in the publish's transaction (`indexPublishedEntry`, inside a savepoint: if indexing fails it is undone and the Site's index is marked stale, never failing the publish); deleting an Entry deletes them (foreign key). An Entry hidden in a language has none there, and searches filter with `publishedVisibleIn` too.
- **Rebuilds**: a change to what documents are built from (a Post Type's Searched fields, their Fields' definitions, adding a language, changing the Default language) marks the Site's index stale (`markSearchIndexStale`: `sites.search_index_version = 0`); `search.rebuild-due` (every minute) claims Sites behind `SEARCH_INDEX_VERSION` and queues one `search.rebuild` each, which reindexes every Entry 100 at a time with their rows locked (so a publish never races it) and drops documents of removed languages. Raise `SEARCH_INDEX_VERSION` whenever what documents hold changes (a Field type's text, the configuration list): every Site's index is then rebuilt. Until a rebuild runs, search answers from the index as it was.
- **The query** (`searchQueryIn`): the first `q`, control characters as spaces, spaces collapsed, cut at `SEARCH_QUERY_MAX_LENGTH` (200) characters (a longer one is cut, not refused); empty searches for nothing. It reaches SQL only as a parameter of `websearch_to_tsquery` (words, `"a phrase"`, `or`, `-word`), which never fails on its input; it is shown back escaped.
- **The search Loop** (`site-pages/loops.ts`): on the search results page, the Loop named `search` (`SEARCH_LOOP`, a name Staff Loops can't take) runs `searchLoopQuery`: the searchable Post Types whose Entries have pages (the Page Post Type, and Post Types with a published Entry template), `SEARCH_PAGE_SIZE` (10) a page, paginated, the search results page itself left out, narrowed by the query (`queryRows` joins `search_documents` in the page's language and orders by `ts_rank`, then the newest). Each hit reads as a query Loop's item, plus `snippet`: `ts_headline` around the best match (about 15 to 35 words), all of it escaped, the matched words in WebCraft's own `<mark>` (`searchSnippets`; text Postgres's parser reads as a tag is left out of headlines). Its pages keep the query (`?q=bread&page=2`, `LoopPlace.state`).
- **The search results page**: the Page Staff choose with `pages.setSearchPage` (`structure.edit`, logged `searchPage.change`), at its own path in each language it is shown in (translated slugs, language domains), titled `Results for “bread”` with a query. Its content is the **Search results template** (`templates.getSearchTemplate`, `saveSearchTemplate`, `getSearchTemplateMarkup`, `saveSearchTemplateMarkup`, published with `templates.publish`), or the built-in one until Staff publish theirs (`builtInSearchTemplate`: a heading, the Search element, the hit count, the hits with their snippets and pagination, or a "no results" message saying what to try, in the page's language). It reads the Globals, the Loops of the Site, the Loop `search` (`hit.title`, `hit.url`, `hit.snippet`, the searchable Post Types' Fields), `search.query` and `search.total`; no Entry's values; neither `<main>` nor `{{@outlet}}`. Answers: never cached (`no-store`, no ETag, never in the page cache), `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`, a page past the last answers 404. Edit mode shows it with its hits too.
- **The Search element** (`{{@search}}`, `SearchNode`): `<form class="wc-search" method="get" action="<search results page>">` with a visually hidden label, a `type="search"` box named `q` (`maxlength="200"`, the query on the results page) and a button, in the page's language; the first on a page carries `role="search"`, so a page has one search landmark. No script: it works without JavaScript. Styled from the Design system's base layer (`SEARCH_HOOKS`).
- **Rate limits**: a search (a non-empty `q` on the search results page) counts against `SEARCH_RATE_LIMIT`, 30 a minute per client address and Site (`rateLimits.count`, key `search:<site id>:<address>`); past it the answer is a plain 429 with `Retry-After: 60`. Every other page, and the search results page without a query, never counts.
- **Filters** attach a search box to any query Loop: the same `q`, `searchQueryIn`, `Narrowing.search` and snippets (`item.snippet`), with their own controls narrowing beside it (see "Filters"). On the search results page a Filter's keys take its name as a prefix, as Site search keeps `q`.
- **Seams**: Noindex Entries stay out of Site search (`publishedIndexable`, see "SEO"). The Content API (#108) searches through `queryRows` with `Narrowing`. Loop items' `url` carries the language prefix on a language's own domain (it redirects there): a general Loop gap, not search's.

## Filters

A Filter (`GLOSSARY.md` › Filter, Filter control; the decision of ticket #28) is the controls visitors use to narrow, search and sort the items of one query Loop. Core: `packages/core/src/filters/` (`services.filters`; the definition, `definition.ts`; storage and publishing, `filters.ts`; the address's allow-list, `state.ts`) and the Site side, `packages/core/src/site-pages/filters.ts`; the renderer: `packages/renderer/src/filters/` (the stored shape, `version.ts`, which the admin reads as `@webcraft/renderer/filters`; the words WebCraft writes, `words.ts`; the form, `render.ts`; the script, `script.ts`); the web side: the fragments in `apps/server/src/site/pipeline.ts` and `filter-fragments.ts`. Procedures `filters.*` (`packages/api/src/procedures/filters.ts`, all `structure.edit`); where a Filter is placed, `filters/usage.ts`; the builder's live preview, `filters/preview.ts`. The Filter builder screen (#98) is Site › Filters (see "The admin").

- **Structure** (table `filters`, a Site table): a name (lower-case words joined by dashes, its own on the Site, `FILTER_NAME_TAKEN`; it never changes), the query Loop it narrows (`loop`, by name, chosen when it is made: a query Loop of the Site, one Filter per Loop, never changed), and a Draft and a published version, published through `publish` with `{ kind: "filter", id }` (logged `filter.publish`; creating `filter.create`). Publishing needs its Loop published and every control to fit the Loop's published Post Types. Publish all lists it with Structure and publishes it after its Loop. A refused Filter is `FILTER_INVALID` with `data.problems`, each where it is (`controls[2].source.field`); a missing one `FILTER_NOT_FOUND`.
- **A version** (`FilterVersion`): `mode` (`live`, the default, or `submit`), its layout preset `layout` (`sidebar`, the default, `topbar` or `drawer`) and what it becomes on phones, `phone` (`drawer`, the default, or `same`), `label` (the form's name for screen readers, also the drawer's title and button) and `applyLabel`, and `controls`, in order, at most `FILTER_LIMITS.controls` (12). Every control has an `id` (made when missing, kept when given: the builder selects controls by it) and a `type`:
  - `search` (one at most): `label`, `placeholder`; its key is always `q` (Site search's, ADR 0021).
  - `checkboxes`: `key`, `label`, `source`, `match` (`any`, OR, or `all`, AND), `counts` (true), `empty` (`hide`, `disable`, the default, or `show`), `order` (`source`, the default, `label` or `count`), `showFirst` (10; the rest behind "Show more", a `<details>`).
  - `choice` (radio buttons or a select, `display`): the same as checkboxes without `match` and `showFirst`, with `allLabel` (the option choosing none).
  - `toggle`: `key`, `label`, `field` (a true/false Field); on keeps the items whose value is true.
  - `range`: `key`, `label`, `field` (a number Field, or a date Field with a day in it), `kind` (read from the Field), `minLabel`, `maxLabel`; for numbers a `slider` (`{ min, max, step }` or null), for days `presets` (`FILTER_DATE_PRESETS`: `today`, `next-7-days`, `next-30-days`, `this-month`, `last-7-days`, `last-30-days`, `last-12-months`, `this-year`, each once, in order).
  - `sort` (one at most): `key`, `label`, `defaultLabel` (the Loop's own order), `options` (1 to 10: `value`, a slug; `label`; `by`, what a Loop query sorts by or `relevance`, which needs a search box; `direction`).
  - `chips` (one at most): `label`, `resetLabel`: the active choices, each a link removing itself, and Reset.
  - `count` (one at most): `one`, `other` (`{count}` is the number) and `none` (the no-results words), chosen with the language's plural rules.
  A source is `{ kind: "field", field }` (a select Field's options, by their values as slugs; or a Relationship Field's: the Entries of the Post Types it may point at, by their slugs in the page's language and their titles; at most `FILTER_LIMITS.options`, 100) or `{ kind: "postType" }` (the Loop's Post Types, by key). Keys are lower-case words joined by dashes, never `q` or `page`, each its own in the Filter. Words (labels, placeholders, the no-results words) are a string or `{ text, translations }` by language tag, as a Form's are; one left out shows WebCraft's in the page's language (`filterWords`).
- **On pages** (`pageFilters`): a page shows the Filters its templates and the Components they place name, at most `FILTER_LIMITS.perPage` (4), each in the version its Structure shows (published; the Drafts on the Staging address and in Code mode). A Filter never published shows nothing and narrows nothing. When two Filters (or Site search and a Filter) share a page, each key takes its Filter's name as a prefix (`shop.category`).
- **The address** (`state.ts`): one parameter per control, under its key (the same in every language): checkboxes' slugs joined with commas (`category=bread,pastries`), one slug for a choice or a sort, `1` for a toggle, `2-10` (`2-`, `-10`) for a range of numbers and `2026-01-01..2026-03-31` for days, cleaned text for the search box (`searchQueryIn`'s rules). Only what the page's Filters allow is read (`StateReader`, an allow-list): unknown keys are left alone and never shown back; unknown values, numbers or days that don't parse and more than `FILTER_LIMITS.values` (50) values are dropped. The same choices always write the same query (`writeQuery`: keys sorted, an option list's slugs sorted, numbers in their shortest form). A request whose choices aren't in that form (a plain form sends `category=bread&category=pastries`, `price.min=2`, empty boxes) answers a 302 (`no-store`) to the normal address, dropping what isn't allowed.
- **Narrowing** (`Narrowing.where`, `.search`, `.sort` on the Loop's query, `site-pages/loops.ts`): inside a control its options match any (OR; checkboxes may match all), and controls combine (AND); everything is parameterised SQL through `QuerySql` (`selectHas`, `linksTo`, `isTrue`, `numberWithin`, `dayWithin`, `postTypeIn`). A search box narrows through Site search's index in the page's language, best match first unless a sort is chosen, and the Loop's items read `snippet`. **Counts** (`facetCounts`): for each control with options, one query grouped by option over the Loop's query narrowed by every other control's choice (OR-matching controls ignore their own, so ticking Bread never zeroes Pastries; an `all` control keeps its own). They are worked out when a control shows counts or hides or disables empty options. The result count is how many items match across the Loop's pages.
- **The form** (`renderFilter`): `<form class="wc-filter" method="get" action="<the page>">` named for screen readers, each control labelled (fieldsets and legends for groups of options, a toggle as `role="switch"`, a range as two boxes, `price.min` and `price.max`), its choice shown back, the other Filters' choices on the page kept as hidden fields, then an Apply button: it works without JavaScript. `data-wc-announce` says the result count; `data-wc-layout` and `data-wc-phone` its preset. Each control's wrapper has the id `<form id>-<n>-control`. Styled from the Design system's base layer (`FILTER_HOOKS`), never inline styles.
- **Layout presets**: the preset only changes WebCraft's markup and classes around the controls; the page around them is the Template's. A `sidebar` stacks them. A `topbar` is one row: the search box and the sort menu as they are, each control with options (checkboxes, a choice, a toggle, a range) behind a dropdown button (`popovertarget`, saying how many are chosen: "Category 2 chosen") opening a popover with the control (and its own Apply in On submit mode); popovers open below their button where browsers anchor them. A `drawer` holds the controls with options in a modal `<dialog class="wc-filter-drawer">` opened by a "Filters" button (`command="show-modal"`, so no script is needed where browsers have invoker commands; the Filter script handles them where they don't), with Close, Reset (the page without this Filter's choices) and a Show button applying the choices, which says how many items they list ("Show 14 products", from the count control's words). With `phone: "drawer"` a sidebar or top bar becomes that drawer below the Design system's smallest breakpoint (768 px by default, `phoneWidthOf`); at and above it, the dialog's content shows in place (`display: contents`). The drawer always waits for its Show button, whatever the mode.
- **Controls shown on their own** (`renderFilterPart`, `{{@filter … control="<id>"}}`): beside the Filter's form on the page, the control's fields name that form (the `form` attribute), the form leaves the control out, and the part is `<div class="wc-filter-part" id="<form id>-<n>-part">…<!--/wc-filter-part--></div>`; without the form, a control with fields is a small form of its own (id `…-part`) sending the page's other choices on as hidden fields; chips and the count never need a form. The fragments answer each part by its id, as they do forms.
- **Ranges' extras**: a slider is two `<input type="range">` (no name: the boxes are what the form sends), hidden until the Filter script shows them and keeps each in step with its box. Date presets are links to the page with the period chosen (`baked=2026-05-01..2026-05-07`, the page's other choices kept, the chosen one `aria-current`), counted from today in UTC (`presetDays`); a page showing them leaves the page cache at the next midnight (`PageFilters.changesAt`).
- **Without reloading** (the Filter script, `FILTER_SCRIPT`, a hashed file at `/_wc/js/<hash>.js` linked only from pages showing a Filter, under the strict policy): Live mode applies a choice as it changes (text and ranges after 400 ms), On submit mode when Apply is pressed (Live mode hides Apply). It asks `GET /_wc/filter/<page path>?<the form's fields>`, which answers the page at that path as a request for it would (the page cache, the Staging address with its access check, Edit mode) as JSON: `url` (the normal address), `title`, `filters` (each form by id) and `regions` (by name). Regions are what a filtered Loop shows and its pagination, marked by the renderer with comments (`<!--wc-region loop products 1-->` … `<!--/wc-region loop products 1-->`, `FILTER_REGION_MARK`); a filtered Loop's page links and the chips carry `data-wc-filter-link`. The script swaps them (inserting only what the server rendered; words by `textContent`), puts the address in history (Back and Forward swap again), keeps focus on the same control (and a search box's own text and caret), and announces the result count in a live region of its own. Pagination scrolls to the Loop, smoothly only without reduced motion. When the parts don't line up (a Condition around a Loop changed), or the request fails, it loads the page. In Edit mode it runs as on visitors' pages (ADR 0022: WebCraft's own scripts run there), its fragments showing Edit mode's page. A field belongs to its Filter by `field.form` (so parts work). An open drawer holds its choices: each change asks the fragments for the pending address only to put the new count on its Show button (and in the live region); Show applies and closes it (focus back on its button); its Reset empties its fields without applying. In Live mode a top bar's dropdown opens again after its swap, from its button. Sliders show once the script runs; a date preset fills its range's boxes where choices wait (On submit mode, an open drawer) instead of applying.
- **Caching** (`apps/server/src/site/page-cache.ts`): a filtered page is kept under its normal address (`pageHref(path, n, state)`), so the same choices in any order, with any unknown parameter, find it. The cache remembers what each path's Filters read (`reader`, per page cache generation), so a request is looked up before anything renders. Filtered pages are variants: a Site keeps at most `DEFAULT_FILTER_VARIANTS` (1,000) at a time; past it a filtered page is answered with `no-store` and not kept. A search (`q`) is never kept (`no-store`) and counts against `SEARCH_RATE_LIMIT` like Site search.
- **Search engines**: a filtered or sorted page carries `<meta name="robots" content="noindex, follow">` and `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, follow`, and its canonical URL is the page without its choices (a paginated filtered page's too); plain `?page=N` pages stay indexable with their own canonical URL. Sitemaps list pages without Filters' choices only.
- **The builder's API**: `filters.get` also answers `usage` (where the Filter is placed: each Template by kind, with its Post Type, and each Component, from their Drafts and published versions, `filterUsages`) and `sources` (what its controls can use from its Loop's Draft: select and Relationship Fields for options, true/false Fields for toggles, number and date Fields for ranges, and what sorts can be by, `filterSources`, read by the same rules saves keep); `filters.list` answers each Filter's `usage`. `filters.preview` (internal) renders the Draft with changes not saved yet (checked as `saveDraft` checks them, `FILTER_INVALID`) where it is placed (an Archive template first, then the Search results template, an Entry template, the Site template, a Component; its Draft tree, rendered as the visual builder's canvas renders it, `renderPreview` with `filter`, the other Filters as placeholders) or, placed nowhere, beside a list of its Loop's items; with the choices of `query`, in `language`, over published Entries with real counts (`pageFilters` with `only`); with the Design system's Draft stylesheet, the page's address with its choices, the result count and `phoneWidth`.
- **Seams**: words are kept per language in the Structure version (the builder's Translations tab, Staff only); editing them as Content (Members) can split them out as Forms' emails are. A sitemap lists pages without Filters' choices only.

## SEO

Every page carries good metadata out of the box (the SEO decision; `GLOSSARY.md`: SEO fields, Social card, Schema type). Core: `packages/core/src/seo/` (`services.seo` for the Site's settings); the renderer writes the tags (`packages/renderer/src/seo.ts`); the admin: the Entry editor's SEO & sharing card, a Post Type's SEO tab and Site › SEO (`apps/server/src/ui/seo/`).

- **SEO fields** (`seo/entry-seo.ts`): every Entry, Pages included, has an SEO title (the page's whole title), description, canonical URL, social image and noindex. They are part of its Draft and publish with it, stored as Fields are (ADR 0003) as a Group under the key `seo` in each version (`SEO_GROUP`): the first four per language, falling back to the Default language one at a time; noindex one value for every language, under the Default language (a Default language change moves it, `site-languages.ts`). No Field may take the key `seo` (`RESERVED_FIELD_KEYS`). `entries.create` and `saveDraft` take `seo: { title, description, canonical, image, noindex }` (only those given change; null or "" clears one), Authors included (Content, `entry.edit`); the Entry answers them as `draft.seo` and `published.seo`. Refused at their path (`ENTRY_INVALID`, `seo.canonical`): a canonical URL that isn't an http(s) address or a Site path (`isCanonical`), a social image that isn't a JPEG, PNG, WebP, AVIF or GIF of the Site's library, or is in the Trash (`isShareableImage`: never an SVG, which other sites can't show). They have no rules: what search engines prefer is the SEO check's, never a reason not to publish.
- **A Post Type's SEO settings** (`seo/seo-rules.ts`, which the admin reads as `@webcraft/core/seo-rules`; column `post_types.seo`, `{}` until Staff set them, when `defaultPostTypeSeo` applies): `titlePattern` (tokens `{title}`, `{site}`, `{postType}`, `{separator}`; `{title} {separator} {site}` by default; `applyTitlePattern` drops a token without a value with the separator next to it, never touching the title's own text), `descriptionField` (the Post Type's Summary field, which the API also answers as the Post Type's `summaryField`, its newer name: one setting; a text, rich text or Body field's path, null for none), `imageField` (a media Field, or a Body field's first image; the first media Field, else the Body field, by default) and `schema` (`type` from `SCHEMA_TYPES` and `properties`, each schema.org property mapped to a source: `title`, `author`, `publishedAt`, `lastPublishedAt`, `createdAt`, `seo.description`, `seo.image` or a Field's path, a Repeater's Field across its rows for lists). `postTypes.create`/`update` take `seo` (each part given replaces that part); a Schema type given without `properties` takes `guessSchemaProperties`: built-in sources first, then Fields of the right kind (`PropertyKind`) whose key or label names the property (`words`), and for an FAQ page the first Repeater with two text Fields as questions and answers. Refusals are `POST_TYPE_INVALID` at `seo.titlePattern`, `seo.descriptionField`, `seo.imageField`, `seo.schema.type`, `seo.schema.properties.<name>`, with `SETTING_RULES` codes (`seoTitleToken`, `seoTitlePatternLength`, `seoDescriptionField`, `seoImageField`, `schemaTypeUnknown`, `schemaPropertyUnknown`, `schemaSource`, `schemaFaqRepeater`). `keptPostTypeSeo` leaves out what a Field change made impossible. Pages, the home page, the 404 page and search results take the Page Post Type's title pattern; Archive pages their Post Type's, with its name as `{title}`.
- **A Site's SEO settings** (`seo/site-settings.ts`, table `seo_settings`, a Site setting: `seoSettings.get` with `site.view`, `seoSettings.update` with `structure.edit`, applied at once, clearing the page cache, logged `seoSettings.change` with what `changed`): `siteName` (null for the Site's own name), `separator` (1 to 3 characters, `·` by default), `defaultImage` (a Media image), `robotsLines` (Staff's own robots.txt lines; see "Discovery files") and `organization` (`kind` `organization` or `localBusiness`, `name`, `logo`, `sameAs` https addresses, and the Globals its `telephone`, `email` and `address` come from, by path). Refusals are `SEO_SETTINGS_INVALID` with `data.problems` (`seoSeparator`, `seoImage`, `seoSameAs`, `seoGlobal`, …).
- **Fallbacks** (`seo/page-seo.ts`, worked out with the published values a page renders, in its language): the title is the SEO title, else the pattern filled in (`Recipes · Page 2` past a paginated Loop's first page); the description the SEO description, else the Summary (the Summary field's text), else the start of the Body field, as plain text (`textOf`), cut at a word to about 155 characters (`cutText`, `DESCRIPTION_CUT`); the social image the Entry's own, else the image Field's, then the Social card (#93, the seam is marked in `entryPageSeo` and `seoReports`), then the Site's default image (`shareableImages`: ready, not in the Trash, not an SVG; the narrowest WebP variant at least 1,200 pixels wide, else the original, which is then narrower). The server never crops (ADR 0027), so the Focal point plays no part in shared images; the Social card will compose with it.
- **The head** (`PageToRender.seo`, `PageSeo`; `seoTags` and `sharingTags` in the renderer, every value escaped for where it lands): `<title>`, `<meta name="description">`, `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` for a noindex Entry, the Site's 404 page (at its own path too, where it answers 200), a page of search results and a Form sent back with errors; `<link rel="canonical">` (none on a 404 answer): the Entry's canonical URL (an address as written, a Site path at its language's address, `canonicalOf`), else the page's own address where the Site's pages are for visitors (`SiteHost.visitorAddresses`, from `siteAddresses`: the Primary domain and languages' domains, never the Staging address, whose pages say `noindex` and carry no canonical URL of their own) with `?page=N` past the first page; the `hreflang` alternates (#80) agree with it on the Site's domains. Then Open Graph (`og:type` `article` for an Article or Blog post, else `website`; title, description, URL, Site name, `og:locale` and `og:locale:alternate` as `pt_BR`, the image with its absolute address, type, size and alt text in the page's language; `article:published_time` and `article:modified_time`) and the social tags (`twitter:card` `summary_large_image` with an image, else `summary`, its title, description, image and alt). Edit mode pages carry the title, description and canonical URL only.
- **Structured data** (`seo/structured-data.ts`): one JSON-LD graph in `<head>`, a `<script type="application/ld+json">` data block, which a strict policy doesn't block since it is never run (ADR 0023). `jsonLdText` writes `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028 and U+2029 as `\u` escapes, so no value can end the block or read as markup, and every value is plain text (rich text and bodies without markup, ISO dates, numbers, absolute addresses). Every page has the `WebSite` (`<main address>/#website`, its name, home page in the page's language, and a `SearchAction` when the Site has a search results page) and the organisation (`/#organization`, an `Organization` or `LocalBusiness` from the Site's settings and Globals). An Entry's page adds its `BreadcrumbList` (the home page by its title, else the Site's name; then the Post Type's Archive page when it has one published, or a Page's parent Pages; then the page) and the page itself: a `WebPage` (or `FAQPage`, whose `mainEntity` lists a Repeater's questions and answers) with the mapped properties, or a `WebPage` and a node of the Schema type (`<address>#<type>`, `mainEntityOfPage`, Articles naming the organisation as publisher) holding them; an author is a `Person`, linked to the Entry's page when the author is a Relationship. An Archive page adds its breadcrumbs and a `CollectionPage`; the 404 page, search results and Form error pages only the `WebSite` and the organisation.
- **Site search** leaves noindex Entries out of its hits (`publishedIndexable`, through `Narrowing.indexableOnly` on its built-in Loop); sitemaps and `llms.txt` take the same rule (see "Discovery files").
- **The SEO check** (`seo/checks.ts`, a Check: warnings only): an Entry answers `checks` (after its body's) with `seoTitleLong` (over 60 characters), `seoDescriptionMissing`, `seoDescriptionShort` (under 50), `seoDescriptionLong` (over 160), `seoImageMissing` and `seoImageAlt` (the social image without alt text in the language), each at `seo.title`, `seo.description` or `seo.image` with `params` (`length`, `max`, `min`); the codes and limits are `SEO_CHECKS` and `SEO_CHECK_LIMITS` in `seo-rules.ts`, where the Site's list of open Checks (#95) can collect them. It measures what the Draft gives through the same fallbacks pages use; `seoPreview` (`{ title, description, image: { mediaId, from } }`) is that for the SEO & sharing card.
- **Media**: an Entry's social image is in its versions, so Used in names the Entry (`fields: ["seo"]`); the Site's default image and logo are the `seoSettings` `MediaUseSource` (`settings: ["defaultImage", "organizationLogo"]`), shown before a file goes to the Trash. A file in the Trash stops being shared at once.
- **Seams**: the Social card (#93) joins the image fallbacks between the image Field and the Site's default image (and `SeoPreview.image.from`); the Page details drawer in Edit mode edits the same `seo` values through `saveDraft` (see "Edit mode"); the Content API (#108) can answer `seo`. A filtered or sorted page is `noindex, follow` (`PageSeo.follow`), its canonical URL the page without its choices (see "Filters").

## Social cards

Social cards (#93) are generated fallback images, distinct from Media. Site › SEO holds `socialCards: { enabled, layout }` (on by default, `editorial`); a Post Type's SEO settings hold `socialCardLayout` (null inherits the Site). These settings take effect when saved and use the existing `structure.edit` Permission. Owners and Editors can read them. The 20 original layouts and their stable keys are in `core/social-cards/rules.ts`; the controls and layout names are in English and Italian. Turning the Site switch off also disables Post Type overrides.

- **Inputs**: the published Entry title in each visible Site language (with the normal per-Field language fallback), the SEO Site name or Site name, the organisation logo from Site › SEO, and the published Design system's light colour tokens: `primary` and `accent` at step 60, `neutral` at steps 10 and 95. Missing named tokens use the first colours or safe defaults. The renderer selects a contrasting text colour when needed. The logo is optional, uses its whole image, and must be a ready raster image of this Site, outside the Trash. No remote assets or fonts are fetched.
- **Rendering**: `socialCards.render` reads current published inputs in a background Site scope. It runs after a publish commits, through the same Audit listener for individual publishes, Publish all, frozen Scheduled publishes, Automatic translation and translation approval. SEO settings, Post Type settings, language settings, published colour changes and logo changes refresh cards too; a replacement logo refreshes again after the Media worker cleans it. An hourly `socialCards.reconcile` enqueues all Sites, recovering a lost notification, failed render or renderer upgrade. Unexpected rendering failures retry with backoff. Expected storage outages are logged at warn, keep the existing social fallback, and recover through the hourly reconciliation. No rasterisation happens in the web process.
- **Artwork**: `apps/worker/src/social-cards.ts` uses the existing Sharp dependency for original geometric compositions and Pango for escaped text, Unicode shaping, wrapping and fitting. It makes 1200 × 630 PNGs, bounded to 2 MB each. Titles over 240 graphemes are shortened with an ellipsis; a missing title falls back to the Site name, then a dash. A missing logo leaves a small geometric mark. The worker image includes Liberation and Noto fonts (OFL) for language coverage.
- **Cache**: `social_cards` keeps PNG bytes in Postgres, as Reports keep PDFs, keyed by Site, Entry, language and a SHA-256 of the renderer version and actual inputs (including the logo's content-addressed original name). It is derived data, never a Media item, and uses no additional disk or S3 files. Identical inputs reuse a render. Keys are immutable: a late job cannot overwrite a newer publication. Both metadata and the file handler require the current input hash. A successful render clears the Site page cache; obsolete renders are removed after 30 days, current ones retained. Entry and Site deletion cascade through the cache.
- **Serving and fallback**: on the Site's own or language domain at `/_wc/social/<entry>/<language>/<hash>.png`, after normal hostname and Staging checks, with an ETag, GET/HEAD and conditional 304 support. Browser/CDN caches must revalidate so disabling, hiding, deleting or unpublishing also revokes a known image URL at the origin. SEO uses the explicit Entry image, then the Post Type's image Field, then a ready Social card, then the Site default. Pending or failed cards keep the last fallback; obsolete cards never masquerade as current. JSON-LD uses the same image. Draft/Staging metadata never substitutes a published card for Draft text. The Entry editor and Page details describe a Draft's future card as `{ from: "socialCard", layout, state: "onPublish" }`, without a fictitious Media id; Checks account for that planned fallback.
- **Export/import (#109)**: export the Site's two `seo_settings` columns and Post Type `seo.socialCardLayout` with the other SEO settings; remap `organization.logo` as an ordinary Media reference. Omit `social_cards`: they are a disposable cache. After importing published content, enqueue `socialCards.render` for the new Site (hourly reconciliation is the recovery path). Its new ids produce its own addresses; no card storage files or secrets need moving. Whole-Install Backups include the table normally.
- **Tests**: `tests/social-cards/` drives real Postgres through the request handler, including the real worker renderer's output, settings permissions/isolation, metadata and PNG serving, multilingual and Draft behaviour, publish routes, stale renders, logo replacement, switch-off, and recovery. Most tests use `install.socialCards`, a controllable rendering port; the real renderer test checks all 20 outputs. SEO browser checks cover the new keyboard controls and axe in both themes.

## Discovery files

What a Site tells search engines and AI engines about itself, on each of its domains (spec stories 112, 117, 118, 119): `robots.txt`, sitemaps, `llms.txt` and Markdown copies. Core: `packages/core/src/discovery/` (`services.discovery`); the web side: `apps/server/src/site/discovery.ts`, at the Site pipeline's reserved-path step (after Redirect domains, Redirect rules and Edit mode's paths; before Entries).

- **Built on request, kept in the page cache**: each file is built from what is published when it is first asked for on a domain, kept in the page cache under the Site's page cache generation (by the domain's origin and the file's path, with a strong ETag and `X-Cache`, `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`), so every publish (one item, Publish all, AI translation's publishes) and every change to a setting (a Post Type, a language, a domain, the SEO settings, the AI crawler setting, a Redirect rule) clears them at once in every web process. Restoring a Revision publishes nothing, so they stay. A Redirect domain sends them on to the Primary domain like any path; the Staging address offers none (see "The Staging address").
- **What is listed** (`listed.ts`, one rule for sitemaps and `llms.txt`, so they never disagree): published Entries of a Post Type whose Entries have pages (the Page Post Type, or one with a published Entry template), at their published address in each language whose pages are on the domain (on the Primary domain the Default language and the prefixed languages without a domain of their own; on a language's domain that language), and each Post Type's Archive page while its Archive template is published (its first page: its date is the newest of its template's publish and its Entries'). Left out: noindex Entries (`publishedIndexable`), an Entry in a language it is hidden in (`isVisibleIn`), Drafts, an Entry whose canonical URL (`canonicalOf`) is another address in that language, the Site's 404 pages and its search results page, a path a Redirect rule sends elsewhere (a batch at a time through the Module keeping them, SDK 1.10 `findMany`, then each answer followed as a request would be; a rule WebCraft added when a path changed steps aside where a page answers, so it never leaves one out), and Post Types Staff leave out (`postTypes.update({ sitemap: false })`, `structure.edit`; their pages stay indexable). Filtered, sorted, searched and paginated pages are never listed, only each page's own address. Entries are read `LISTING_BATCH` (500) at a time, by id or newest first, so a big Site is never read in one query.
- **Sitemaps** (`sitemaps.ts`, the sitemaps protocol 0.9): `/sitemap.xml` is an index of one sitemap per listed Post Type with something to list (Pages first, then by name), each part at `/sitemaps/<Post Type key>-<n>.xml`, numbered from 1, with its newest `lastmod`. A part lists `<url>` elements: `loc` (absolute, percent-encoded), `lastmod` (the Entry's last publish, a W3C datetime to the second) and its `hreflang` alternates as `xhtml:link rel="alternate"`, exactly as the page's head lists them (every language it is shown in, at that language's address on this domain or another of the Site's, then `x-default`; none on a Site with one language). A Post Type's addresses are split into parts past `SITEMAP_LIMITS` (50,000 addresses or 50 MB a file): the index walks them once and notes where each part starts (kept per page cache generation, domain and Post Type, `createPartStarts`), so a part reads only its own Entries; an Entry's languages may fall into two parts. Each domain lists its own addresses only.
- **robots.txt** (`robots.ts`): WebCraft's groups first (the AI crawlers the setting keeps out, `Disallow: /`; then `User-agent: *`, `Allow: /`), Staff's own lines, then `Sitemap:` with the domain's sitemap's absolute address on a line of its own. On the Staging address, `User-agent: *` / `Disallow: /` (`STAGING_ROBOTS`), answered before its access check.
- **The AI crawler setting** (`ai-crawlers.ts`, read by the admin as `@webcraft/core/ai-crawlers`; column `seo_settings.ai_crawlers`): `allowAll` (the default), `allowSearch` (AI search and answer engines in, crawlers collecting pages for training out) or `blockAll` (every AI crawler out); search engines' own crawlers are never named. `AI_CRAWLERS` is the one list of AI crawlers, each a user-agent token with its purpose (`training` or `search`); its comment says how it is kept up to date (checked against each operator's crawler documentation before a release; a token never moves between purposes without a note in the release notes). `seoSettings.setAiCrawlers({ siteId, aiCrawlers })` (a public write, `aiCrawlers.manage`: Staff and Owners), applied at once, logged `aiCrawlers.change` with `from` and `to`; `seoSettings.get` answers it as `aiCrawlers`.
- **Staff's own lines** (`seoSettings.update({ robotsLines })`, `structure.edit`, column `seo_settings.robots_lines`; `readRobotsLines`): at most 200 lines and 5,000 characters (`ROBOTS_LINES_MAX`); each line blank, a `#` comment, or `User-agent`, `Allow`, `Disallow` (a path starting with `/` or `*`), `Crawl-delay` (seconds) or `Sitemap` (an absolute http(s) address), a trailing comment allowed, no control characters; a rule follows a `User-agent` line; a group never names one of `AI_CRAWLERS` (the setting decides those). Line breaks become `\n` and lines are trimmed, so nothing reaches the sitemap line written after them. Refusals are `SEO_SETTINGS_INVALID` at `robotsLines` with `params.line`: `robotsLine`, `robotsRuleWithoutAgent`, `robotsAiCrawler`, `robotsLinesLength`.
- **`llms.txt`** (`llms.ts`, after the llms.txt proposal): one per language at its root (`/llms.txt` for the Default language, `/fr/llms.txt` under a prefix, `/llms.txt` on a language's domain; a language living on another of the Site's domains goes there, 301): `# <the Site's name>` (the SEO settings' name, else its own), `> <its home page's description>` in the language when it has one, then a `## <Post Type>` section per listed Post Type with its listed Entries, newest first, at most `LLMS_ENTRIES_PER_POST_TYPE` (500) each: `- [Title](<its Markdown copy>): <description>`, the description being the page's own (its SEO description, else its Summary, else the start of its Body field, cut at a word). Every text is escaped as Markdown.
- **The Summary** (GLOSSARY.md): a Post Type's Summary field (`summaryField`, `structure.edit`) is the text, rich text or Body field (by path) that says in short what each Entry is about. It is the SEO settings' `descriptionField` under its newer name, so the two can never disagree: either spelling sets it, the API answers both, and a call giving two different Fields is refused (`POST_TYPE_INVALID` at `summaryField`, `summaryFieldTwice`; a Field of another kind `summaryField`). Descriptions fall back to it, then to the start of the Body field (`descriptionFrom`); `llms.txt` uses those descriptions and Markdown copies show it in full. The admin sets it on a Post Type's SEO tab, with the sitemap switch.
- **Markdown copies** (`markdown-copy.ts`): every indexable Entry's page has one at its address plus `.md` (`/blog/fresh-bread.md`, `/fr/blog/pain-frais.md`); an address ending in `/` (the home page) takes `index.html.md`, as the llms.txt proposal writes it, so the home page's copy is `/index.html.md` (and `/fr.md` for a prefixed language's home). It answers `text/markdown; charset=utf-8` with `Link: <canonical URL>; rel="canonical"` (the page's own address, or the canonical URL the Entry gives): `# <title>`, `> <Summary>` (else its SEO description), the Fields a reader needs as a list (every Field but the Summary, the Body field and true/false ones: `- **Label:** value`, choices by their labels, links, email addresses, Relationships and Media files as absolute Markdown links and images, a Group's Fields nested, a Repeater's rows numbered), then its Body field (`bodyMarkdown`). Every text is escaped and nothing passes through as HTML; rich text is its plain text. No copy (a plain 404): a noindex Entry, one hidden in the language, a Draft, an Archive page, the 404 and search results pages, a path a Redirect rule sends elsewhere. A copy whose page moved goes where the page went (301): to a language's domain, or the home page's own path to the root's copy. Post Types left out of the sitemaps keep their copies (their pages are still indexable).
- **Tests** (`tests/discovery/`): every file against a Site's live domain, read as crawlers read it (`tests/harness/xml.ts` reads XML strictly and checks sitemaps against the protocol); `createTestInstall({ sitemapLimits: { urls, bytes } })` lowers the limits to test splitting with a few Entries.

## Domains

A Site's domains (ADR 0015; `packages/core/src/domains/`, behind `services.domains`; table `site_domains`, procedures `domains.*`; the admin's Site › Domains & Staging) are Site settings: everyone who sees the Site sees them (`domains.list`, `site.view`), Staff change them (`structure.edit`), each change applies at once and is logged (`domain.add`, `domain.makePrimary`, `domain.remove`), and none is part of Publish all.

- **Names** (`domains/names.ts`): `domainName` keeps a domain in lower case, without the trailing dot, international names in their ASCII form (`Bäckerei.example` is `xn--bckerei-…`; `displayName` shows them as written); it refuses an address with a scheme, a path or a port, an IP address, a wildcard and a single name (`DOMAIN_INVALID`, `reason: "format"`), and `isInstallName` the Install's own names: the admin host, the sites domain and every name under it (`reason: "install"`). A name is unique on the Install, whichever Site or Workspace has it, languages' domains included (`DOMAIN_TAKEN`, `data.name`).
- **Roles**: one **Primary domain** per Site, which serves it; any number of **Redirect domains**, which answer 301 to the same path and query on the Primary domain; and the **languages' domains** (`role: "language"`, `language` set), which serve that language without a prefix. A language's domain is set in Languages (`languages.add`/`update` write both `site_languages.domain` and its `site_domains` row; removing the language removes the row); here it is only listed, verified and checked (`DOMAIN_CHANGE_REFUSED`, `problem: "language"`, for the rest).
- **Adding** (`domains.add`): the first domain of a Site without a Primary domain becomes it, the rest are Redirect domains. With `withPartner`, the apex or `www` partner comes too (`partnerOf`: `www.bakery.ie` for `bakery.ie` and back), as a Redirect domain; one the Site has already is left as it is, one another Site has refuses the whole addition.
- **The DNS records** each domain lists (`records`): A and AAAA records to `WEBCRAFT_PUBLIC_ADDRESSES` (`address`, none without it), or for a subdomain a CNAME record to the admin host (`alias`). The screen shows them for the Install's `edge` first (`domains.list` answers it), with the steps for Coolify and Railway, where the domain is also added in the platform's panel.
- **Verification**: a domain serves nothing until the Install has seen it reach it. `checkReaches` (`domains/checks.ts`) resolves the domain, fetches `http://<domain>/_webcraft/verify/<token>` (on the web process's port for a development name under `.localhost`) and accepts only the answer this Install makes for that token. Token and answer are HMACs under a key derived from the Master key (`domainVerificationKey`): the token from the domain row's id and name (a domain added again gets a new one), the answer from the name and token, so neither is stored and no other Install, nor anything echoing the request, can make the answer. The Site pipeline answers the path before its hostname step (step 0 above), only for a domain's own token on that domain, `no-store`. A domain that doesn't resolve, or answers wrongly while pointing elsewhere than the Install's addresses, has the problem `dns`; one pointing at them that doesn't answer, `unreachable`; one answering something else, `response`.
- **What the checks call** (the SSRF line): every request goes through the guarded fetcher, `GET` only, no redirect followed (a redirect is a wrong answer) except the one to the same address over HTTPS (see "Behind a platform's proxy" below), 1 KB, 10 seconds. Besides public addresses it may call **only the Install's own addresses** (`installAddresses`: `WEBCRAFT_PUBLIC_ADDRESSES` and what the admin host resolves to), since a domain must point at the Install, which may be on a private network or, in development, on this machine. A domain resolving to any other internal address is never called (`dns`), and nothing it answers reaches anyone: only whether it was the Install's own answer. Staff can't choose the path, the method or the port.
- **When**: `domains.verify-due` runs every minute (`coreSchedules`), claims each domain waiting for DNS whose `next_check_at` came (moving it on first, so no check runs twice) and queues one `domains.verify` job each: every 5 minutes for the first hour after the domain was added, hourly until 7 days, then only when Staff press **Check now** (`domains.check`), which checks at once and starts the round again. A verified domain clears the Site's page cache (its addresses changed) and has its certificate looked at, which also starts an on-demand one.
- **The Domain check**: `domains.check-all` runs hourly and queues one `domains.check` job per verified domain: it checks the domain as verification does, then its certificate over TLS (`checkCertificate`, through the `tls` port: `systemTls` in the processes, faked in tests; skipped for `.localhost` names): one that is missing or not trusted is `certificate-missing`, one running out within 14 days `certificate-expiring`. A failing domain is **Unreachable** (`unreachable_since`; it keeps serving, and Caddy keeps renewing its certificate) and active again once a check passes. The check that turns it Unreachable, and only that one, sends the `domain-unreachable` System email to the **Site's Owners**: the Members reaching the Site whose role manages the Workspace's Members (`member.manage`, which only Owners hold in v1), linking to `/sites/<id>/domains`. Check now on a verified domain runs the Domain check at once.
- **Making a domain the Primary domain** (`domains.makePrimary`): only a verified Redirect domain (`DOMAIN_CHANGE_REFUSED`, `problem: "not-verified"`); the Primary domain before it becomes a Redirect domain, so its links keep working. Clears the page cache.
- **Removing** (`domains.remove`, two steps: the first answers `preview: { name, role, live }`): the domain stops at once (no page, no certificate); to move it to another Site, remove it, add it there and verify it again. Without its Primary domain a Site is on its Staging address only, and its Redirect domains answer the plain 404 until another domain is made Primary.
- **Caddy's ask** (`GET /caddy/ask?domain=<name>` on the admin host, `apps/server/src/admin/caddy-ask.ts`; point Caddy's `on_demand_tls { ask … }` there): 200 for the admin host, the Staging address of an existing Site and every verified domain (Primary, Redirect and language, Unreachable ones too), 404 for any other name, 400 for what isn't a hostname, 405 for any method but `GET`. It reads at most one row and writes nothing: no rate limit, no log line. The ask comes from inside the deployment: `deploy/compose/` sends it to `http://<admin host>:3000/caddy/ask`, the admin host being a network alias of the web service on a network only Caddy and the web process share, and its Caddyfile answers `/_webcraft/verify/…` over plain HTTP without redirecting to HTTPS, since a domain has no certificate until it is verified.
- **Behind a platform's proxy** (Coolify, Railway), plain HTTP is answered with a redirect to HTTPS. The verification request follows exactly that one redirect, to the same verify address over HTTPS on the same host (through the guarded fetcher again, the certificate checked against the name); any other redirect is still a wrong answer.
- **A CDN in front of a Site** (spec story 43; `packages/core/src/cdns/`, behind `services.cdns`; column `sites.cdn`, table `cdn_ranges`): a Site setting on the Domains & Staging screen, `domains.setCdn` (`structure.edit`, logged `cdn.change` with `from` and `to`), read by `domains.list` as `cdn`.
