Rollups hold each dimension on its own, so a filtered count reads the raw events only, as far back as they are kept (`countedFrom` on a Widget says where its counts start when the range reaches further).
- **Goals** (`goals.ts`; table `goals`; Structure: `goals.list` for `analytics.view`, `goals.create`, `update`, `delete` for `structure.edit`): a name (unique on the Site whatever its case, at most 100 Goals) and what it counts: a Form's Submissions (`form`), a page view of a path (`page`, `exact` or `prefix`), a click on an outbound, download, phone or email link (`link`, optionally whose target matches a pattern, `*` for any characters, upper and lower case alike) or a custom event by name (`event`). Only what visitors did after accepting Analytics counts: page views before consent count towards totals, never Goals. `stats.goals` answers each Goal's conversions and the visitors who reached it, with the range's visitors (who accepted Analytics), the whole a conversion rate is a share of. Deleting takes two steps (`context.confirmations`) and is refused while a Funnel uses the Goal (`GOAL_IN_FUNNEL`). Logged `site-analytics.goal.create`, `.update`, `.delete`.
- **Funnels** (`funnels.ts`; table `funnels`; the same Permissions; `funnel.create`, `.update`, `.delete` logged): two to five steps, each a Goal (by id) or a page visit, counted per visitor within one day (the daily id): a visitor reaches a step when one of their events that day reaches it after the step before (`stats.funnel`; the events of the visitors who reached the first step, walked in order in memory with `goalMatches`, the mirror of `goalWhere`). A Form Submission step counts Submissions linked to the sender's visit (above).
- **Widgets** (`widgets.ts`, SDK 1.8): the catalog Dashboards are built from: `totals`, `trend`, `top-pages`, `sources`, `countries`, `devices`, `browsers`, `operating-systems`, `links`, `custom-events`, `goals`, `goal` and `funnel`, each reading through the query interface; Goal and Funnel settings name them by name. `widgetGoals` offers the Site's Goals to Widgets' Goal filter. See "Dashboards".
- **Admin pages**: Site › Goals and Site › Funnels (`structure.edit`; screens in `apps/server/src/ui/analytics/`).
- **Settings**: `settings.get` and `settings.update` (`structure.edit`, Staff): the Site's excluded address ranges (addresses or CIDR blocks, at most 50), logged `site-analytics.settings.update`.
- **Countries** come from the countries port: `WEBCRAFT_COUNTRY_FILE`, a list of address ranges an agency downloads (no list ships with WebCraft); without one every country is unknown.

## Dashboards

Grids of Widgets showing a Site's analytics, or a Workspace's Sites added together (spec story 131, `GLOSSARY.md`). Dashboards are WebCraft's (core: `packages/core/src/dashboards/`, `services.dashboards`; tables `dashboards`, a Site table, `workspace_dashboards` and `dashboard_templates`, Workspace tables; procedures `dashboards.*` and `dashboardTemplates.*` in `packages/api/src/procedures/dashboards.ts`); the Widgets on them come from the catalog Modules add (SDK 1.8, so far Site analytics') and WebCraft's own note. Core keeps them because only core reaches across a Workspace's Sites (Workspace Dashboards) and across Workspaces (templates), while each Widget counts through its Module on one Site at a time, so ADR 0024's boundary holds: no Module reads another place, and no Module code runs in the admin. Their tests are in `tests/dashboards/`.

- **A place**: every procedure names a Site (`siteId`) or, for a Workspace Dashboard, a Workspace (`workspaceId`), exactly one (`BAD_REQUEST` otherwise). A Workspace Dashboard's Widgets add up the Workspace's Sites the person sees (`sites.counted`/`of` in each answer): counts add, a rate's two counts add, tables add by row key then keep the biggest (each Site answers up to 1,000 rows first), and visitors are added Site by Site and day by day, as Site analytics counts them (the screen says so). A Funnel is matched across Sites by name only, and each Site builds its own steps, so two Sites' Funnel of the same name can have different steps (a step's label is its Goal's name or its path): when every Site's steps are the same, they add up step by step; when they differ, the Widget answers no data with a problem (`FUNNEL_STEPS_DIFFER`) instead of mixing one Site's step into another's.
- **Who**: `dashboard.manage` builds (Staff), `dashboard.view` sees all (Staff and Owners); a Dashboard Staff show to everyone (`sharedWithEveryone`) is seen by everyone who sees the place, and exported by them too; any other answers `DASHBOARD_NOT_FOUND` to them. API tokens read and export like their person (a read-only token never changes one).
- **A Dashboard**: a name, a range (`last30Days` by default; `today`, `yesterday`, `last7Days`, `last30Days`, `last90Days`, `thisMonth`, `lastMonth`, `last12Months`, or `custom` days, both counted, at most 3,660; UTC days, by the Install's clock; two days or less count by hour), and its Widgets, kept as JSON with it (`StoredWidget`), at most 40, read and written whole under a row lock, so two people moving Widgets never lose a move.
- **Widgets**: `kind` (`note`, or `<module id>:<kind>`), `title` (null: the kind's name), a place and size on a 12-column grid (2 to 12 cells wide and tall), `range` (null: the Dashboard's), `filters` (those the kind takes), `settings` (the kind's choices; fixed options are checked when saved, options worked out per Site, such as Goals and Funnels by name, when counted) and `rows` (5, 10, 20 or 50, for tables). `widgets.add` puts a new one in the first free place at its shape's size; `widgets.move` moves and resizes one and moves down only what it would cover (`placeWidget`); a refused kind, filter, setting or range is `DASHBOARD_INVALID` with `data.problem`.
- **Data** (`widgets.data`): worked out when asked, one Widget at a time, for the Widget's own range, else the `range` given (exploring, which changes nothing), else the Dashboard's: the Module's `query` runs as the Module acting by itself on the Site (`ModuleHost.runWidget`), after the person's access was checked. Counts come as counts, a rate as two (`{ count, of }`), labels in the person's Admin language. A Widget that can't count answers its `problem` instead: the Module's own refusal (`GOAL_NOT_FOUND`, `FUNNEL_NOT_FOUND`, `WIDGET_SETTING_MISSING`), `MODULE_NOT_RUNNING`, `WIDGET_UNKNOWN`, or `WIDGET_FAILED` (written to the Module log).
- **CSV exports** (`widgets.export`, `export`, reads): UTF-8 with a byte order mark, every cell quoted, CRLF, a cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return made text with a `'` (`csvCell`); each Widget headed by its title and its days (and the Sites counted, and where counts start, when they say so), rates as percentages, sources, countries and devices in words; a Dashboard's file holds each Widget in turn. Logged `dashboard.export`.
- **Dashboard templates** (`saveAsTemplate`, `dashboardTemplates.list`, `.delete`): a Dashboard's Widgets and range kept in its Workspace (at most 50 a Workspace), offered by New Dashboard on any Site or Workspace of a Workspace where the person builds Dashboards. Widgets name Goals and Funnels by name, so a template finds the Goals of the same names where it is used; `create` answers `unmatched`, the names the place lacks, whose Widgets say so until they are added.
- **The admin**: Site › Dashboards and Workspace › Dashboards (`ui/dashboards/`; the sidebar offers them where the person sees a Dashboard, `place.dashboards`), a Dashboard at `…/dashboards/<id>` with the days explored in `?range=`. Every chart has the same numbers as a table, and lines differ by dash and marker as well as colour. Staff move a Widget by dragging its handle (or resize by its corner), or from the keyboard on the handle: Alt with the arrows moves it, Alt and Shift resize it, Space picks it up so the arrows move it (Shift resizes), Space puts it down and Escape puts it back; every change is said in the screen's live region and saved in order.
- **For Reports and Share links (#104)**: `services.dashboards.widgetAnswers(access, place, dashboardId, { range, language })` works out every Widget of a Dashboard as plain data for a person; `services.dashboards.shown(place, dashboardId, { range, filters, language })` does the same with nobody signed in (a background scope: `data.background`, or `data.backgroundWorkspace` for a Workspace Dashboard, whose Widgets then add up every Site of the Workspace), with `filters` narrowing every Widget that takes them and has none of its own. Only Reports' jobs and a Share link's page, which checked who may see it, call it. See "Reports and Share links".

## Reports and Share links

A Dashboard (a Site's, or a Workspace's adding up its Sites) sent as a PDF on a schedule, or explored by anyone holding a link (spec stories 132, 133 and 135, `GLOSSARY.md` › Report, Share link, Services list, Report branding). Core: `packages/core/src/reports/` (`services.reports`, `services.shareLinks`; tables `reports`, `report_sends`, `share_links`, `report_services`, `install_report_branding`, `workspace_report_branding`, `site_report_branding`); procedures `reports.*`, `shareLinks.*`, `reportBranding.*` and `servicesList.*` in `packages/api/src/procedures/reports.ts`; the Share link address in `apps/server/src/site/share.ts`; the worker's renderer in `apps/worker/src/pdf.ts`. Tests: `tests/reports/`.

- **A Report** (`reports.create`, `update`, `delete`; `report.manage`): a Dashboard's, with a name, a schedule (`weekly`: each Monday at 06:00 UTC, covering the week, Monday to Sunday, that ended; `monthly`: on the 1st at 06:00 UTC, covering the calendar month that ended), each period compared with the one before (the week before, the month before), its recipients (at most 20 addresses; Owners change them too, `reports.setRecipients`, `reportRecipients.edit`, as Content), its language (`en` or `it`: the Report's words and the AI text in it), whether it waits for review, and whether it goes out. At most 10 a Dashboard. `nextRunAfter` and `periodBefore` (`model.ts`) work out when and what.
- **The worker** (`jobs.ts`): `reports.due` (every quarter of an hour) claims each Report whose time came (one row lock, the next time set at once) and starts an entry of its history (`report_sends`) with the recipients as they are then; a Report going to nobody is skipped. `reports.prepare` works out every Widget for the period and the period before (`dashboards.shown`), writes the AI text, keeps both with the entry (`content`), and holds it for review (`review`, telling the Workspace's Staff with the `report-review` System email) or goes on. `reports.render` builds the page (below) and turns it into a PDF through the `pdf` port, kept with the entry (`pdf`), then emails it. A PDF that can't be made is tried again after 2 and 4 minutes (`RENDER_ATTEMPTS`, 3 in all), then the entry is `failed` with the problem in words; a Report is never sent without its PDF. `reports.purge` (daily, 03:41) deletes history older than 400 days (`REPORT_HISTORY_DAYS`), PDFs with it.
- **Email**: one email per recipient, from the Site's name through the Site's own mail (`siteMail.route`, Site SMTP rules: retried for about 24 hours, never sent another way, the Site's Owners and Staff told when its server becomes Failing), with the PDF attached. A Workspace Dashboard's Reports go through the Install SMTP under the Workspace's client name. The queued email names its attachment (`EmailAttachmentRef`, `kind: "reportPdf"`), never holds it: `email.send` reads the PDF from the entry when it sends (`services.reports.attachment`), and an email whose attachment is gone fails rather than going out without it. Each is in the Email log related to the entry (`reportSend`), which the history reads for each recipient's state; an email already queued for an entry isn't queued again when a job runs twice.
- **The history** (`reports.history`, `reports.sends.get`, `reports.sends.pdf` as base64; `report.view`): each entry's kind (`scheduled`, or `test`: Send a test to me, `reports.sendTest`, internal, made now for the last whole period and sent to the person alone, never held for review), state (`preparing`, `review`, `rendering`, `sent`, `failed`, `discarded`), its days, its AI text's state, each recipient's email state and its PDF's size. `reports.sends.resend` (`report.manage`) sends a sent (or failed) entry's PDF to the Report's recipients now (a test: to its person), or makes its PDF again when that failed.
- **AI text** (`ai.ts`): 1–3 sentences per Widget, "What happened" and up to 3 suggestions that may name a service of the Services list by id, in the Report's language. Only totals are sent (`reportFacts`): each Widget's counts, a table's 5 biggest rows, a series' sums and a funnel's steps, for the period and the one before; never a note's words, never anything about one visitor. Paid by the Workspace's AI key through `aiKeys.call` (purpose `report`), so its key choice, caps and usage apply (`AiProviders.writeReport`; the HTTP providers ask for a JSON object). No key (`noKey`), the cap reached (`cap`) or a provider that failed or answered nonsense (`failed`): the Report goes without AI text and says so. The answer is untrusted text: read as JSON, cut to `AI_TEXT_LIMITS`, stripped of control characters and direction marks (`plain`), and always shown escaped, never as HTML.
- **Review** (`review` on a Report): an entry with AI text waits (`review`) until Staff edit its text (`reports.sends.editText`: plain text within the same limits, services by id from the list), approve it (`reports.sends.approve`, which makes the PDF and sends it) or discard it (`reports.sends.discard`). Logged `report.approve`, `report.discard`.
- **One page** (`page.ts`, `reportPage`): what a Share link shows and what the worker turns into the PDF, from the same function. WebCraft's own HTML: every value escaped, no script, its styles in one `<style>` element allowed by its hash (`REPORT_PAGE_POLICY`: `default-src 'none'`, the style by hash, `img-src data:`, the form to itself), images only as `data:` addresses (logos), light and dark by the device, printed light. Number Widgets say how each count changed on the period before in words (and ▲/▼), never by colour alone; series are SVG charts with the same numbers in a table; tables and funnels are tables. A Widget with its own days shows them and compares with nothing.
- **The PDF** (`pdf.ts`, `PdfRenderer` in `InstallPorts.pdf`): only the worker has a real one, `createChromiumPdf()` (headless Chromium, `--print-to-pdf`, one at a time, per PDF a temporary folder removed afterwards, stopped with its helpers after 60 seconds, page and PDF size limited by `PDF_LIMITS`); the web process and commands have `pdfOnlyInWorker`. Chromium gets the page as a file, with its policy set by a `<meta>` element (no script, `data:` images only), every host name mapped to nothing and every request sent to a proxy that isn't there, a throwaway profile and a capped JavaScript heap; its sandbox is off (it can't start in an unprivileged container), which is safe because it prints only WebCraft's own escaped page. No route serves a Report's page: the worker builds it itself. The worker image installs Debian's `chromium` (BSD-licensed, run as a program, not linked) and `fonts-liberation` (OFL).
- **Share links** (`share-links.ts`; `shareLinks.list` for `shareLink.view`, `create`, `update`, `revoke` for `shareLink.manage`): a name, the page's language, an optional password (8 to 200 characters, kept as the scrypt hash people's passwords get) and an optional expiry (within five years). Making one, or changing its password or expiry, is the Sensitive action `shareLink.change`, so no API token or AI client makes one; revoking isn't. The token (32 random bytes) is answered once, in its address; only its SHA-256 hash is kept, with its last 4 characters to tell links apart. At most 20 a Dashboard. People with accounts keep seeing Dashboards by Dashboards' own rules.
- **The Share link address** is `share.<WEBCRAFT_SITES_DOMAIN>` (`SHARE_LINK_LABEL`; no Site may take the slug `share`, and Caddy's ask approves it), never a Site's own domain; without a sites domain there are no Share links (`SHARE_LINKS_UNAVAILABLE`). The Site pipeline answers it before any Site (`shareAnswer`): `GET /<token>` shows the page, exploring with `?range=` (the Dashboard's presets) and `country`, `device`, `source` and `page` (a path and the pages under it), anything else left out; a Widget with its own days keeps them. A link unknown, revoked or expired answers the same page (404), so nobody learns which links exist. With a password, the page is a form (401, naming nothing of the Dashboard) posting to the link itself, from its own page only; the right password sets `__Host-webcraft.share-<link id>` for a week (or until the link expires): an expiry signed with HMAC-SHA256 under a key derived from the Master key (`shareLinkPasswordKey`) over the link and its password's hash, so a new password, removing it, or a new Master key shuts out every browser it let in. Limits: 120 pages a minute per client address (`SHARE_PAGE_RATE_LIMIT`), and 10 password guesses a minute per address and link, 50 an hour per link from every address (`SHARE_PASSWORD_RATE_LIMIT`, `SHARE_PASSWORD_LINK_LIMIT`). Every answer is `private, no-store`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` (the token is in the address), with the page's policy and the fixed security headers; pages also say `noindex` themselves.
- **Opens**: a link followed as it was sent (`GET` without a query) counts one open and the time of the last; exploring counts nothing more. Nothing about who opened it is kept: no address, no browser, no cookie but the password's (the rate limits hold a keyed hash of the address for a minute, as everywhere).
- **Branding** (`branding.ts`; the admin's Install settings › Report branding, Workspace › Report branding, Site › Report branding): the agency brand (a name, a logo, an accent colour shown as a bar) is the Superadmin's for the Install (`reportBranding.install.*`, `install.manage`), which a Workspace may replace with its own (`ownAgency`); the client brand is the Site's name and logo (`reportBranding.site.*`), else the Site's own name and the Workspace's logo, and for a Workspace Dashboard the Workspace's (`reportBranding.workspace.*`; `reportBranding.manage`, Staff). Three switches, the Install's with a Workspace's own choice over them: the agency brand, the client brand, "Made with WebCraft". Logos are PNG, JPEG or WebP of at most 256 KB, checked by their bytes (never SVG), kept in the database and shown as `data:` addresses. Report branding is kept apart from Install branding (System emails): its own tables, so neither changes the other.
- **The Services list** (`services-list.ts`; `servicesList.get`, `update`, internal; `servicesList.edit`, the Superadmin and Staff; the admin's Install › Services list): up to 30 services, each a name and a short description, written once for the Install (one row); a service keeps its id while it stays on the list, which is how suggestions name it.
- **The admin**: a Dashboard's header has Reports (its Reports page, `…/dashboards/<id>/reports`: the Reports, a Report's history with its PDFs, resend, and the review dialog at `?review=<entry id>`, which the `report-review` email links to) and Share (the Share links dialog: each link's state and opens, a new link made through "Confirm it's you" and shown once). Screens in `apps/server/src/ui/reports/`.

## Media

A Site's Media library (`packages/core/src/media/`, behind `services.media` and `services.storage`; ADRs 0026, 0027). Its tables (`media_folders`, `media`, `media_files`) are Site tables; `media_quotas` is a Workspace table; `install_storage` and `install_settings.media_max_file_bytes` hold the Install settings.

### Storage

- **One interface, two kinds** (`media/storage.ts`): `put`, `putStream`, `read`, `stream(key, range?)`, `delete`. `putStream` writes atomically from a stream: to a temporary file then a rename on disk, or 5 MiB multipart uploads in a bucket (aborted on failure). **Local disk** keeps files under `WEBCRAFT_STORAGE_DIR` (written beside the final name, then renamed; each read opens and closes the file, so a visitor who goes away never keeps one open). An **S3-compatible bucket** (`media/s3-storage.ts`: AWS S3, Cloudflare R2, MinIO, …) is spoken to through `services.http`, every request signed with AWS Signature Version 4 (`aws4fetch`) by `services.clock`'s time, with a SHA-256 of the body. It goes to the endpoint the Superadmin set, not through the guarded fetcher: a self-hosted store is usually on an internal address.
- **Keys** have one folder per Workspace and Site: `<workspace id>/<site id>/media/<name>` for files visitors get, `<workspace id>/<site id>/uploads/<name>` for raw uploads the worker hasn't cleaned (never served), `<workspace id>/<site id>/imports/<id>.csv` for CSV files uploaded for an Import and `<workspace id>/<site id>/imports/<id>/part-000001`, `…-000002`, … for the parts of a Module source's file (private, never served, see "Imports"); Form uploads use `…/form-uploads/<upload id>` (private, see “Form uploads” below). A bucket's `prefix` goes in front, so one bucket can hold several Installs. A file served as uploaded is named by its content hash, so identical bytes uploaded twice under the same name share one stored file (two `media_files` rows, one key); a stored file is removed only once no row names its key (`removeUnusedFiles`, `media/trash.ts`).
- **The Install setting** (Install settings › Storage, `install.storage.get` / `update`): local disk (the default) or a bucket. Changing it is a Sensitive action (`storage.change`): the bucket's secret access key is the Install secret `storage.s3.secretAccessKey`, write-only like every secret; leaving it out keeps the stored one. A bucket is checked before it is saved: WebCraft writes, reads back and removes `webcraft-check/<random>`, refusing the change with `STORAGE_UNREACHABLE` when it can't. Recorded as `storage.change`, never with the secret.
- **Each stored file records which storage holds it** (`media_files.storage`), and is always read from there: switching storage changes only where new files go, and never breaks a URL. The S3 settings are kept when the Install switches back to disk, so files in the bucket stay readable.
- **Moving files between them** is `webcraft admin media move --to s3|disk [--keep-source]` (`moveMediaFiles`, `media/move.ts`): refused (`MediaMoveRefusedError`) while Install settings › Storage sends new files elsewhere or no bucket is set; then one stored file (key) at a time it copies the file, reads the copy back and compares its SHA-256, points every `media_files` row naming the key at the new storage, and deletes the old copy (unless `--keep-source`). Addresses never change. A move that stopped is run again and goes on; a key in neither storage is reported (`missing`) and its rows stay. Import files (`import_files`) stay where they are: they are short-lived. Tests run it as the command does with `install.moveMedia({ to })`.

### Limits and quotas

- **The largest file** is an Install setting (Install settings › Storage, `install.media.get` / `update`, `install.manage`): 50 MB unless the Superadmin sets another, from 1 MB to 1 GB (`MEDIA_MAX_FILE_BYTES`), logged as `mediaMaxFileSize.change`. Uploads, replacements and imports over it are refused (`MEDIA_TOO_LARGE`, 413, `data.maxBytes`). Before the procedure reads a multipart body into memory, `/api/rpc` refuses one whose `Content-Length` passes the limit (plus 64 KiB for the form's other parts) with the same answer, and stops reading one that doesn't say its length once it passes it (`bodyWithinLimit`, `packages/api/src/rpc.ts`).
- **A Workspace's quota** (`media_quotas`; `workspaces.storage` with `workspace.view`, `workspaces.setQuota` with `quota.manage`, at least 1 MB, null removes it; logged as `storageQuota.change`) is optional. Usage is counted from stored bytes (`workspaceMediaBytes`): every stored file of the Workspace's Sites (originals, variants, uploads waiting for the worker, files in the Trash, replaced files still served), a key shared by several items counted once. An upload, replacement or import that would take usage past the quota is refused with `MEDIA_QUOTA_FULL` (413, `data: { usedBytes, quotaBytes }`), checked before the file is stored and again, with the quota row locked, in the transaction that records it, so two uploads at once can't both squeeze past. Uploads and quota changes also take turns on a per-Workspace advisory lock (`lockQuota`: uploads hold it shared, a quota change alone), since a Workspace with no quota yet has no row to lock. Once usage passes 80% (`QUOTA_WARNING_SHARE`), the Workspace's Owners and the Staff assigned to it get the `media-quota-warning` alert email once (`noticeQuotaUse`); it is sent again only after usage went back under 80%. `media.overview` and the Workspace page show `{ usedBytes, quotaBytes, warning, full }`; the Media library and the Workspace page show the notice.

### Uploads and the worker

- **Uploading** (`media.upload`, a File in the oRPC input). What a file is comes from its first bytes (`media/sniff.ts`), never its name or the type the browser claims: JPEG, PNG, WebP, AVIF, HEIC/HEIF, GIF and SVG images, PDF, MP4/MOV/WebM video, MP3/M4A/OGG/WAV/FLAC audio; anything else is `MEDIA_TYPE_REFUSED` (`reason: "unknown"`).
- **Import from URL** (`media.importUrl`, `media.upload`): fetches the file at an address through the guarded fetcher (see "Outgoing calls"), allowed any host on the public internet (`allowedHosts: "any"`), pinned to the address checked, at most 3 redirects each checked again, 30 seconds (`MEDIA_IMPORT_TIMEOUT_MS`) and the Install's largest file, then takes it exactly as an upload, named by the server's `Content-Disposition` or the last part of the address it finally came from. Refusals are `MEDIA_IMPORT_REFUSED` with `data.problem` (`scheme`, `host`, `address`, `redirects`, `timeout`, `size`, `unreachable`, or `status` with `data.status` for an error answer). Logged as `media.import` with the host (never the path: an address can carry a token).
- **Images are cleaned before anyone sees them**: they may carry GPS data. The upload is stored apart (`uploads/`), the item is `processing`, and the `media.process` job runs in the worker: it strips every piece of metadata (EXIF with GPS and camera data, XMP, IPTC), turns the pixels upright by the EXIF orientation, converts to sRGB, converts HEIC to JPEG (other formats keep theirs), stores the result as the **original** visitors get, deletes the raw upload, marks the item `ready`, clears the Site's page cache and queues `media.variants`. A file that can't be read as an image is `failed` (`failure: { reason: "unreadable" }`) and never served.
- **SVG files** go through the same job, cleaned instead of resized (`images.cleanSvg`, `apps/worker/src/svg.ts`): read as XML with jsdom, left without what only drawing programs read (comments, processing instructions, `<metadata>`, editors' own namespaces), then passed through DOMPurify (its SVG and SVG-filter profiles, plus `<use>` within the file). A file is refused rather than changed (`failed`, `failure: { reason: "svgUnsafe", found }`) when it holds a script, event handlers, `foreignObject`, anything outside the SVG namespace, outside references (an `href` that isn't `#id` or an inline PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP, a `url()` that isn't `url(#id)` in an attribute, a `style` or a style sheet, `@import`, `image-set()`, an `xml-stylesheet` instruction), entities (any document type with declarations) or anything else DOMPurify would remove; one that isn't well-formed SVG is `svgInvalid`. SVGs are never resized (no variants); their size comes from their `width`/`height` in pixels, else their `viewBox`.
- **Variants** (`media.variants`): every width of `VARIANT_WIDTHS` (320, 640, 960, 1280, 1920, 2560) up to the original's (equal counts), each in AVIF and WebP, stored together in one transaction, then the page cache is cleared. Until then pages show the original alone. Adding a width later means running the job over the library again.
- GIFs (kept animated), documents, video and audio are stored as uploaded and `ready` at once.
- **Image processing is a port** (`InstallPorts.images`, `media/images.ts`): `prepare` (the cleaned original), `variant` (one width) and `cleanSvg`. Only the worker hands in the real one (`apps/worker/src/images.ts`: sharp on libvips, libheif in WebAssembly for HEIC, which sharp's own libvips doesn't decode, and DOMPurify on jsdom for SVG); the web process and server commands get `imagesOnlyInWorker`, so the Bun web process never loads them (ADR 0027). The test harness uses the real one, since it drains the worker's jobs.

### Serving

`/_media/<name>` on the Site's own domain (its Staging address for now) answers the Site's originals and variants by name, never a raw upload, never a file in the Trash, and never another Site's file. A name is the first 16 hexadecimal characters of the file's SHA-256 and a readable name: `3f9a1c2b7d4e5f60-sourdough-loaf.jpg`, `…-sourdough-loaf-640.avif`. So a name always holds the same bytes: `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, a strong `ETag` (the hash; `If-None-Match` gets 304), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, the stored `Content-Type`, `Accept-Ranges: bytes` with single byte ranges (206, 416), and `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` on a Staging address. Media files get `MEDIA_POLICY`, SVGs `SVG_POLICY` (`default-src 'none'; script-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; img-src data:; font-src data:; sandbox; …`), so an SVG opened by itself runs and loads nothing whatever the cleaning missed (`mediaPolicy(contentType)`). The page cache never keeps them. The admin shows the same files at `/api/media/<site id>/<name>` on the admin host, only to signed-in people who reach the Site (`private` caching, the same policies), files in the Trash too, so previews work whether or not the Site has an address.

### The library

- `media.list` (newest first; a folder, `none` for files in no folder; a search over file name, title and alt text in any language; a kind; `trash` for the files in the Trash, most recently deleted first; `limit`, `offset`), `media.get`, `media.update`, `media.overview` (the largest file, the Workspace's storage, how many files are in the Trash, whether alt text can be suggested) and `media.folders.list`, `create`, `rename`, `delete`. Folder names are unique on the Site whatever their case; deleting a folder keeps its files, in no folder.
- **Title, alt text and caption** are stored per language like Entry values (`{ values: { alt: { en: { value, by } } } }`, ADR 0003), in any of the Site's languages (`language` on `media.get`, `list` and `update`), falling back to the Default language one text at a time. They change at once, with no Draft, and clear the page cache: published pages show new alt text immediately.
- **The Focal point** (`focalPoint: { x, y }` on `media.update`, whole percentages of the image's width and height from its top left; null clears it; images only, else `MEDIA_INVALID` at `focalPoint`) is set once per image and shared by every language. It changes at once and clears the page cache (see "Media on pages"). Edit mode's image popover sets it on the Media item too (and alt text per language), never per use.
- **Used in** (`media.usage`, `media/usage.ts`): every place a file is used on its Site, each with the versions using it (`draft`, `published`): Entries (a media Field, a Body image Block, a Component's Prop on them, in any language, with the Fields by key), the Globals, Templates and Components (`media:<id>` written in them), Loops (their Loop items), and the Design system (its CSS names the file's address). Each place is found by the item's id (or, for CSS, its served names) in the stored versions, so a new kind of Field or Block needs nothing here; a feature that keeps references in a table of its own adds a `MediaUseSource` (the Site's SEO settings, `seoSettings`: its default image and logo). Forms hold no Media.
- **Deleting** (`media.delete`) takes two steps (see "Two-step confirmation"): the first answers `{ trashed: false, usedIn, confirmation }`, the admin's warning; the second puts the file in the **Trash** (`media.trashed_at`, logged `media.trash`). From then on it isn't served, pages stop showing it (the page cache is cleared), it can't be chosen for a media Field (`ENTRY_INVALID`; Entries already pointing at it show the `media` rule in their `problems`), and other changes answer `MEDIA_IN_TRASH`. `media.restore` puts it back as it was (`media.restore`); `media.deleteForever` (two steps, files in the Trash only, else `MEDIA_NOT_IN_TRASH`) and `media.emptyTrash` (two steps, `media.manage`; the first answers how many) delete files for good (`media.deleteForever`, `mediaTrash.empty`). The daily `media.purge` (03:47 UTC) deletes files 30 days after they went to the Trash (`TRASH_LIFETIME_MS`); a stored file goes only once no other item shares its bytes.
- **Replace file** (`media.replace`) gives an item a new file of the same kind (an image for an image; `MEDIA_REPLACE_REFUSED`, `reason: "kind"`), keeping its id, texts, Focal point and every use; logged as `media.replace`. A document is swapped at once; an image waits for the worker (`replacing: true`) while the current file is served, then is swapped and the page cache cleared. The files it replaced (`media_files.retired_at`) are still served under their names for 30 days, for pages loaded before, then `media.purge` deletes them. A replacement the worker refuses leaves the item as it was, with `failure: { reason, replacement: true }`.
- **Suggest alt text** (`media.suggestAltText`): an AI provider describes an image in a language, with the Workspace's AI key (the Workspace secret `ai.key`, `WORKSPACE_AI_KEY`), and nothing is saved. Without a key `media.overview` answers `altTextSuggestions: false` (the admin hides the button) and the procedure answers `AI_KEY_MISSING` (409); a document is `ALT_TEXT_UNAVAILABLE` (`reason: "kind"`), an image with no file providers read (an SVG) `reason: "format"`; a provider that fails `AI_PROVIDER_FAILED` (424, `data.problem`). The image sent is the smallest WebP width of at least 640 pixels (else the original as a JPEG, PNG, WebP or GIF). Providers are a port (`InstallPorts.ai`, `packages/core/src/ai.ts`, ADR 0011: called directly): AI keys and providers (#81) fill it in; until then every process gets `aiProvidersToCome`, which refuses every call as unavailable.
- **Who changes what**: everyone who reaches the Site sees the library, uploads to it and imports into it; Authors change, replace, delete and restore only their own uploads (`FORBIDDEN` otherwise), Editors, Owners and Staff every file, the folders and the Trash. Each item says `canEdit` for the person asking.

### Media on pages

An `<img>` whose `src` reads `media:<id>`, written as fixed text in a Template or read from a media Field (Templates read a media Field as `media:<id>`), renders as a Media image (`packages/renderer/src/media.ts`):

```html
<picture>
  <source type="image/avif" srcset="/_media/…-320.avif 320w, /_media/…-640.avif 640w" sizes="(min-width: 800px) 800px, 100vw">
  <source type="image/webp" srcset="…" sizes="…">
  <img src="/_media/…-croissants.jpg" alt="…" width="800" height="400" loading="lazy">
</picture>
```

- `alt` is the Template's own when it gives one (`alt=""` makes it Decorative), else the Media library's in the page's language, else empty. `width` and `height` are the original's, or the Template's (one of them scales the other). `sizes` is the Template's, or made from the width shown. The Template's other attributes and Classes stay on the `<img>`. An SVG has no variants: its `<img>` comes alone.
- **The Focal point** places the image through a fixed set of WebCraft's own classes, after the Template's: `wc-focus wc-fx-<n> wc-fy-<n>`, to the nearest 5% (`FOCAL_POINT_STEP`, `focalPointClasses`). Every Site stylesheet holds their rules in its base layer (`focalPointRules`: `.wc-focus{object-position:var(--wc-fx,50%) var(--wc-fy,50%)}` and one custom property per step), so pages keep their strict policy (no inline styles, ADR 0023) and a Class Staff give the image, in a later layer, can still position it. It matters where a design crops the image (`object-fit: cover`).
- The first Media image of the page's content (inside the Site template's `<main>`, at its outlet) loads with `loading="eager" fetchpriority="high"`; every other one, the Site template's own included, `loading="lazy"`. A Template's own `loading` or `fetchpriority` wins, and a first image the Template makes lazy gets no high priority.
- Before variants exist, the `<img>` comes alone; before the original is cleaned, for a file in the Trash, or for an id that isn't one of the Site's ready images, nothing renders. In any other URL attribute (`href`, a video's `src`), `media:<id>` becomes the file's address.
- Template markup writes it as it is stored, `<img src="media:<id>">`: the parser checks the id's form only (a file can be deleted later, so it isn't looked up), and `{{ photo }}` in an `src` reads a media Field the same way.

## Imports

Imports (GLOSSARY.md › Importing; `packages/core/src/imports/`, behind `services.imports`; tables `imports`, `import_files`, `import_runs`, `import_items`, `import_run_changes`, `import_run_problems`; procedures `imports.*`; the admin's Site › Imports and Import CSV on a Post Type's Entries) bring outside content into one Site as Drafts, through one pipeline whatever the source: a **source** reads items as a stream, a **mapping** turns them into Entries, an **Import run** writes them as Drafts with their **Source id**, and its **Import report** records what happened. CSV files are WebCraft's own source; Modules add theirs through the SDK ("Import sources" below): the WordPress import (`modules/wordpress-import`) reads an export file (SDK 1.7) or a live site's REST API (SDK 1.13).

- **The seam** (`imports/items.ts`, `imports/pipeline.ts`): a source turns what it reads into `EntryItem`s (a position, a Source id, the value it matches Entries by, values as saving a Draft takes them (SEO fields and a Page's parent included), what must be found first, problems) each for an `ItemDestination` (a Post Type, a language, what items match by; a CSV file gives every row the same one, a Module source's items each their type's). The pipeline then decides where each goes (`decide`), finds what its values point at (`resolveFinds`: Entries by slug or title, or by the Source id this Import gave them; Media files by id; files to fetch by address, with the texts the source gives them) and writes it (`writeItem`), whatever the source. A **Source id is looked up within the Post Type its item goes to** (`import_items.post_type_id`, `itemKey`): an item whose type a later run sends to another Post Type comes in anew there, and the Entry it made before stays where it is. A source that brings Media items fetches them with `fetchFile` (Import from URL, recorded as the run's, so Undo puts them in the Trash, and kept by address so a later run reuses them, once per address per Import; a fetched file gets the source's title, alt text and caption where it has none). Redirect rules from a run belong to the Redirects Module, which the run reaches through the SDK (`redirectRules.add`), recorded among the run's changes (`import_run_changes.kind` `redirect`, with the rule's id, path and target) so Undo removes them; Import leftovers are problems with the rule `leftover` (their text, the markup they were and their Block), which the report lists apart.
- **Imports** (`imports.create`, `update`, `list`, `get`): a name, a source (`csv`, or a Module's import source such as `wordpress-import.export`) and its settings, checked against the Site (`IMPORT_INVALID`, each problem at its place with its rule; settings of another source's kind are `settingsKind`). An Import counts its runs, so run numbers keep rising after reports are purged, and the Entries it keeps track of (`entries`: those it brought in that still exist). A CSV Import that keeps track of any keeps its Post Type (`IMPORT_POST_TYPE_FIXED`, 409; undo its runs, or start a new Import), and the admin shows the Post Type fixed; Source ids are looked up within each item's Post Type either way, so a Module source's Import, whose types go into several Post Types, maps them anew freely.
- **CSV files** (`imports.uploadCsv`, `imports/csv.ts`): read whole when uploaded, then kept privately (`<workspace id>/<site id>/imports/<id>.csv`: never in the Media library, never served, like a Form upload) for the person who uploaded it, until a run takes it (the run deletes it when it ends) or a day passes (`imports.purge`). UTF-8 only, with or without a byte order mark (UTF-16, UTF-32 and bytes that aren't UTF-8 are refused, never guessed); a comma, semicolon or tab, whichever the header holds most of; quoted fields with separators, `""` and line breaks; `\n`, `\r\n` or `\r` line ends; empty lines skipped. At most 10 MB (refused by `/api/rpc` before it is read, `OWN_UPLOAD_LIMITS` in `packages/api/src/rpc.ts`), 10,000 rows, 200 columns and 100,000 characters a field (`CSV_LIMITS`); the header names every column once. Refusals are `IMPORT_FILE_REFUSED` with `data.reason` (`size`, `encoding`, `empty`, `header`, `quote`, `cell`, `rows`, `columns`) and what fills in its wording. Rows are numbered as a spreadsheet numbers them: the header is row 1.
- **CSV settings** (`imports/csv-source.ts`, `csvSettingsSchema`): one Post Type, a language (null: the Default language), what rows match existing Entries by (`title`, `slug`, or a top-level text, email or number Field) and where each column goes (`title`, `slug`, `fields.<key>`, `fields.<group>.<key>` inside Groups; never a Repeater) or `null` to skip it. Columns are matched by name, whatever their case, so Run again with a new file keeps the mapping; file columns the settings don't map are skipped and listed (`skippedColumns`).
- **Reading a cell** goes through its Field type (`fromText` in `FIELD_TYPE_SPECS`): text, email and rich text as written (rich text is HTML, cleaned like any rich text); numbers with a dot or a comma as the decimal mark and no thousands separators (`5.50`, `5,50`); true/false as `true`/`false`, `yes`/`no`, `1`/`0`, `y`/`n`, `on`/`off` and the Italian words; dates in their stored forms, a moment without an offset (`2026-03-02 09:30`) read as UTC; a choice by its value or label in any case; a link as its address; a media Field as an `https` address (fetched into the Media library through Import from URL and the guarded fetcher, once per address per Import) or the id of a file already there; a Relationship by slug, else title, in any language and case, among its Post Types' Entries (as they are when the row is written); a Body as plain paragraphs (a blank line starts one, a single line break stays one, nothing is read as markup). Several choices or Entries are separated by semicolons, or by commas when there is no semicolon. **Empty cells change nothing**: a new Entry keeps the Field's default, an updated one its value.
- **Problems**: a cell its Field can't take is left out and listed at its row and column with its rule (`number`, `boolean`, `date`, `choice`, `mediaAddress`, `mediaNotFound`, `entryNotFound`, `entryAmbiguous`, `notFetched` with the fetch's `problem`, `tooLong`, `slug`, `link`); the rest of the row is imported. A **problem row** isn't imported at all: no value to match by (`matchMissing`), the same value as an earlier row (`matchedTwice`), matching several Entries (`matchesSeveral`), more cells than columns (`columnCount`), a slug another Entry has (`slugTaken`), or, in another language, no Entry to translate (`translationWithoutEntry`). Values that break a Field's rules are kept, as in any Draft, and show in the Entry's `problems`.
- **Matching and Source ids**: a row's Source id is its matching value (`fields.sku:pk-700`; text compared without case). A row goes to the Entry this Import brought in with that Source id (`import_items`, kept as long as the Import and the Entry), else to the one Entry of the Post Type whose Draft has that value (in the run's language, falling back to the Default language), else it creates one. An Entry this Import brought in that someone **edited** since it last wrote it is skipped and reported (`skipped`, `reason: "edited"`), never overwritten; Entries made by hand that a row matches are updated, since the person chose to match them. "Edited" is any change to the Draft the run left (its hash and when it was saved): any Draft save by a person, even one changing nothing, or a later run. Publishing isn't an edit: Run again updates a published Entry's Draft (it shows `changed`; visitors keep the published version).
- **Language**: values are written in the Import's language. Translations come in by running a file in that language matched by the same column: Translatable fields get its values, shared Fields (one value for every language) are written too, so map only what is translated, and a row matching no Entry isn't imported.
- **Preview** (`imports.preview`): the same pipeline without writing or fetching anything: how many rows create, update or skip Drafts, how many have problems, the files it would fetch, the first 200 problems. With `importId`, rows match what that Import brought in (Run again).
- **Runs** (`imports.start`, `imports.stop`, `imports.report`): starting a run (logged `import.run`) queues `imports.run`, which runs in the worker as the person who started it (`actors.person`, their access checked again; a run whose person lost `import.run` fails with `failure.reason: "access"`). It goes through the rows from where it got to, 500 per job, each row's write in its own transaction, counting `done` of `total` (rows, not lines), so a big file is gone through by several jobs and a restarted worker takes up where it stopped. One run of an Import at a time (`IMPORT_RUNNING`). **Stop** (logged `import.stop`) ends a waiting run at once; a running one finishes the row it is on, keeps what's done and ends `stopped`. A run ends `done`, `stopped` or `failed` (`access`, `settings`, `file`, `error`), and its file is deleted.
- **The Import report** (`imports.report`): status, progress, counts (`created`, `updated`, `unchanged`, `skipped`, `problemRows`), skipped columns, problems by row and column with their rules, the first 200 Entries created, updated and skipped, files fetched, Import leftovers, and whether Undo is available (`undo.available`, `undo.reason`). It is kept 90 days after the run ends (`expiresAt`), then `imports.purge` deletes the run, its changes and its problems; the Import and its Source ids stay.
- **Undo** (`imports.undo`, two steps through `askConfirmation`; logged `import.undo`): deletes the Drafts the run created (removing links other Entries make to them, as deleting an Entry does), puts back the Drafts it updated exactly as they were (`replaceDraft`), and moves the files it fetched to the Trash unless something outside the run uses them (Used in). Refused with `IMPORT_UNDO_REFUSED` (`data.reason`, `data.entries`): `status` (the run hasn't ended, or was undone), `published` (something it brought in was published since), `edited` (edited since, as above; a later run counts, so undo the later run first) or `linked` (another Entry must point at one it created, through a required Relationship; `removalBlocks` in `content/entries.ts` works it out without changing anything). Every refusal comes at the first step already, and in the report's `undo.reason`. After Undo, the Import runs again as if the run had never been.
- **A Module source's file** (`imports/uploads.ts`) comes in parts, never in one body: `imports.startUpload` (its source, name and size, at most what the source takes: 1 GiB, `IMPORT_FILE_MAX_BYTES`) answers the file's id and `partBytes` (8 MiB, or a smaller size asked for, 1 KiB at least, 10,000 parts at most); `imports.uploadPart` takes each part at its `offset` (a multiple of `partBytes`) with the SHA-256 of its bytes, refusing a part that doesn't have that hash (`IMPORT_UPLOAD_REFUSED`, `reason: "hash"`: send it again), one at another offset (`offset`, with `receivedBytes`: where to go on) or of the wrong size (`part`), and taking a part sent again as it was; `imports.finishUpload` checks every byte came and that the SHA-256 of the parts' hashes (each part's 32 bytes, in order) is the one sent (`hash`). So an upload that stopped goes on where it got to (`imports.file` answers `receivedBytes`). `/api/rpc` takes a part of at most `partBytes` (`OWN_UPLOAD_LIMITS`); its other body limits are unchanged. Each part is its own private object (see "Storage"), read back as one stream; the file is deleted when the run that took it ends, or a day after it was last used (`imports.purge`).
- **The survey** (`imports.survey`, `imports/survey.ts`): once a file is whole, the worker reads it through its Module, as a stream, and keeps what it holds on the file (`import_files.details`): each type (its key, name, whether it holds Entries or files, how many items), each type's keys (their kind of value, `title`, `slug`, `text`, `html`, `date`, `file` or `items`, how many items have them, and a value from one), and each type's first 20 items as samples. A file the Module refuses is `refused` with its code (the WordPress import's: `doctype`, `notExport`, `xml`, `itemTooLarge`, `valueTooLarge`; WebCraft's own `encoding` for bytes that aren't UTF-8). `imports.file` answers the survey with `suggested`, the mapping the Map step starts from (`suggestedSettings`, by generic patterns only: names that match, each value's kind, `seo`/`meta` with `title` or `desc` in a key's name for the SEO fields, keys starting with `_` skipped).
- **A Module source's settings** (`imports/source-settings.ts`, `sourceSettingsSchema`): a language, and for each type where it goes (`{ postTypeId }`, `{ newPostType: { name } }`, `"media"` for files, or `null` to skip it) and where each key goes (`title`, `slug`, `seo.title`, `seo.description`, `seo.canonical`, `seo.noindex`, `seo.image`, `fields.<key>`, `{ newField: { key, label, type, to? } }`, or `null`). A key's kind of value must suit where it goes (`takes`, `@webcraft/core/import-rules`, which the admin reads too); Pages take no Body field (a new rich text Field takes their content). Types and keys the settings send nowhere are skipped and listed in the report.
- **A Module source's run** (`imports/source-runs.ts`) first makes the Structure its settings ask for, once: the Post Types it creates (`/<key>/{slug}`, with their new Fields; a new Relationship's Post Type first) and the Fields it adds to Post Types the Site has, each recorded among its changes, then written back into the Import's settings (`{ postTypeId }`, `fields.<key>`) so Run again finds them. Then it goes through the items from where it got to (2,000 a job, each job reading the file again from the start): each type's items become Drafts as above; a body's HTML becomes core Blocks (`blocksFromHtml`, `packages/renderer/src/body/from-html.ts`, with parse5: scripts, styles, event handlers and every attribute left behind; a shortcode, page-builder layout (containers holding containers) or an embed with no Block becomes an Import leftover, kept as a plain paragraph where it stood); each image becomes an image Block pointing at its file once fetched; rich text is cleaned as rich text always is; a source's slug is kept (`-2`, `-3`… when taken, said in the report). Where an item's path changed, and for each file whose address was on the old site, it adds a Redirect rule (from the old path to the Draft's path once published, or to the Media file, `media:<id>`), through the Redirects Module when it runs on the Site; the rules step aside where a page answers (they never hide a live page) and never take a path that has a rule.
- **The Preview of a Module source** (`imports.previewSource`, the `imports.preview` job) is worked out in the worker over the whole file, without writing or fetching anything, and kept on the file for its settings (a new mapping asks again): `working`, then the Entries each type would create, update or skip, the new Post Types, the files it would fetch, the Redirect rules it would make (none, with `redirectsOn: false`, where the Redirects Module is off), its leftovers and problems, and the first 20 Entries of each type with their Blocks, leftovers and rule. **The live sample** (`imports.sample`) reads one of a type's samples with the settings as they are, at once.
- **The report of a Module source's run** adds `leftovers` (with the Entry and Post Type each is in), `notFetched`, `redirects` (`on`, `count`, `rules`), `structure` (the Post Types and Fields it made), `skippedTypes`, `skippedKeys` and `skippedItems`; **Undo** also removes the Redirect rules it added (each while still as added) and deletes the Post Types it made once they hold no Entry and nothing else names them (a Template, a Relationship of a Post Type that stays), putting the Import's settings back as they were; Fields it added to a Post Type the Site had stay.
- **The admin** (`apps/server/src/ui/imports/`): Site › Imports (Content, `import.run`) lists the Imports with their last runs and the ways to start one (a Module's sources for those who may use them); New Import and Run again go through Source, Map and Preview (`ImportFlow`, which offers the sources: `CsvImportFlow` for a CSV file, `SourceImportFlow` (`SourceFlow.tsx`) for a Module's: the upload in parts with its progress, the survey, Map variant B (the types, each key's card, the live sample Entry) and the Preview's real Entries), then the run's page shows progress with Stop (polled every 2 seconds) and becomes its Import report with Undo and Run again (`RunReportPage`). Import CSV on a Post Type's Entries opens the same mapping and preview in a side sheet (`CsvImportSheet`).

### Live WordPress sources

`wordpress-import.live` uses the same Map, sample, Preview, run, Media, Redirect rules, report, Undo and Source id matching as the export-file source. The Source step takes the site's HTTPS address, username and application password. **Test connection** (`imports.connect`, Staff Structure permission, sensitive action `import.connect`) shows the account's roles and the count of each exposed REST type and taxonomy. Login inputs are cleared when submitted and never read back. A later Test connection can reuse the saved login; `imports.refresh` does that for API clients with the Structure Toolset. The source address cannot change on an existing Import, so credentials and Source ids cannot move silently to another site.

- **Visibility** is stored on the connection, false by default. With it off, posts use `context=view&status=publish`, and Media REST requests are anonymous so the login cannot expose attachments belonging to private posts. With it on, posts use `context=edit` and `publish,draft,pending,future,private`; WordPress must permit all requested content. Returned post statuses are checked, including parents fetched separately. Media bytes always use the existing guarded Media fetcher without the source's login.
- **Reading**: `/wp/v2/users/me?context=edit` supplies roles; `/wp/v2/types` and `/wp/v2/taxonomies` supply REST routes. Counts use `X-WP-Total` and `X-WP-TotalPages`; pages contain at most 100 items, ordered by id. At most 100 post types and 100 taxonomies, one million items per type, 5 MiB per REST response, and 1 GiB per private snapshot. Only ids and the current page/ancestor chain are held across items. Parents are yielded before children, with cycles and depth over 100 refused. These requests follow the official [authentication](https://developer.wordpress.org/rest-api/using-the-rest-api/authentication/), [pagination](https://developer.wordpress.org/rest-api/using-the-rest-api/pagination/), [users](https://developer.wordpress.org/rest-api/reference/users/) and [posts](https://developer.wordpress.org/rest-api/reference/posts/) contracts.
- **Incomplete sources**: authentication refusal, inaccessible routes, rate limits, invalid responses, changed totals, repeated ids or incomplete pages refuse the capture. Nothing is imported from a partial capture. Test connection again starts a fresh capture; there is no automatic request retry that could disguise a changed source. The worker rechecks the initiating person's permissions and that the Module still runs. Captures stream into private NDJSON parts tracked by `import_files`, ready only when complete. Map, Preview and the run then read that same immutable snapshot, so a retry resumes its exact item order without rereading a changing remote site. Normal file expiry and run cleanup apply.
- **Credentials**: the existing Site secret service stores one encrypted `import:<Import id>:credentials` value; no password or username lives in `imports.connection`, job payloads, report data or readbacks. Core fills the Authorization header through its secret-backed outgoing-request mechanism. The Module gets only a GET port bound to the authorized HTTPS origin and its `wp-json/` root, with guarded DNS/pinned addresses, size and time limits, and no redirects. Response headers are restricted and echoed credentials redacted before reaching the Module. Each authenticated call records `import.credentials.use` with the Module and source host. The capability `importSourceHttp` requires Superadmin approval; an update adding it waits for fresh consent.
- **Retention**: a successful initial connection keeps its login for 30 days; starting a run renews that period. Testing/refreshing alone does not prolong it. Expired credentials cannot be used and the daily `imports.purge` removes them, including while the Module is off. `imports.remove` requires two-step confirmation, removes the Import's snapshots and login, and leaves imported content in place. Staff with Structure permission can remove a live connection even when its source Module is off or missing; the unavailable-source screen keeps that removal action. An active run prevents removal or reconnection. Reports carry `restCustomFields`, shown in English and Italian as a warning that some custom fields may be available only in the export file.
- **Site transfer (#109)**: `imports.connection` contains only the canonical source address, visibility, connection revision and absolute expiry; it travels with the Import. Imported Entries, Source ids, matching history and reports retain the existing transfer rules. Snapshot files, surveys and unfinished capture jobs are operational data and are not exported. The named credential travels only in the passphrase-encrypted secret envelope, is re-encrypted under the destination Install's Master key, and its name is remapped to the destination Import id. The original expiry remains; transfer does not extend retention. Without a secret envelope the saved Import needs its login entered again. A destination must install and approve the live source capability before refreshing or running it. Temporary credentials used while testing a replacement are never needed for transfer.

## Template markup

Template markup (`packages/renderer/src/markup/`, ADR 0017) is the one written form of element trees, for Code mode, the builder's code view, files, MCP, the CLI and imports. It is never stored: `parseTemplateMarkup(markup, options)` reads it into a canonical tree and `printTemplateMarkup(tree, options)` writes a tree as markup. Printing then parsing gives the same tree, for every setting (`tests/markup/round-trip.test.ts` checks it by hand and over generated trees).

```html
<article class="card" aria-label="Post" data-wc-id="Xb3kP9a2Lm4Q">
  <h1 data-wc-id="Q9zT2b7Rk1Wd">{{ title | truncate: 80 }}</h1>
  <!-- Comments stay in the markup and the tree; visitors never get them. -->
  {{@if summary and status != "draft"}}<p data-wc-id="r5Hs8Kq0Zx3N">{{ summary }}</p>
  {{@else if author}}<p data-wc-id="m2Lp7Vd4Ty9C">By {{ author }}</p>
  {{@else}}<p data-wc-id="w8Bn1Fj6Gu5E">Write \{{ summary }} to show the summary.</p>{{/if}}
  <time datetime="{{ publishedAt | date: datetime }}" data-wc-id="c3Xr9Ma1Pq7S">{{ publishedAt | date: long }}</time>
</article>
```

- **Element ids** are written as `data-wc-id`, always last. An element written without one gets a new id; when two elements carry the same id (a copied element), the first keeps it and the copy gets a new one, with a notice. An element's layer label is written as `data-wc-label`, just before it (no values in it). Other `data-wc-` attributes are WebCraft's.
- **Classes** are written by name in `class` (printed first) and stored by id; a name the Site doesn't have is refused. An inline `style` is read strictly (declarations only, safe `url()`s) and becomes a **generated Class** (`style-` and a hash of the canonical declarations, so the same style finds the same Class), with a notice; pages never carry inline styles. Names come from the Draft of the Design system: its Classes and its token utilities (`classCatalogue`, `packages/core/src/design-system/classes.ts`). The inline style is checked like any CSS of the Design system (no addresses on other hosts, no `!important`) and the generated Class joins the Design system's Draft; it goes live with the Template that made it (see "The Design system").
- **Values**: `{{ path }}` or `{{ path | formatter }}`, in text and inside attribute values. Formatters are a fixed set (`packages/renderer/src/formatters.ts`): `date` (`short`, `medium` (the default), `long`, `full`, `time`, `iso`, `datetime`; in the page's language, in UTC until Sites have a time zone), `number` (optionally `number: 2` decimals) and `truncate: <length>` (cut at a word, then `…`). Which paths are known depends on the kind of Template (`isKnownValue`): an Entry template reads the built-in Fields, its Post Type's Fields and their parts, and the Globals (`site.x`; see "What a binding reads"); inside a Component its Props (`props.x`) and the Globals; anywhere, the Loops of the Site's `<name>.count` and the rest of `LOOP_STATS`; inside a Loop, its items (`post.x`) and their position (`loop.x`). See "Loops in Template markup".
- **Fixed text in other languages**: `{{ "Read more" fr: "Lire la suite" pt-BR: "Leia mais" }}`, in text and inside attribute values: the text as written in double quotes, then each language's (`\"` for a quote, `\\` for a backslash, `\r` for a carriage return). It is a text node with `translations` (see "Languages"); the printer writes the languages in alphabetical order, and plain text without other languages as plain text. A language tag WebCraft doesn't keep, or one given twice, is refused where it is.
- **Escaping**: text prints `&`, `<` and `>` as references; `\{{` is a literal `{{` (the printer writes `{{` in text that way), and a `{` or `\` at the end of text prints as `&#123;` / `&#92;` so it can't run into a following `{{`. Whitespace is kept exactly: the printer adds none, so a file pulled, edited and saved shows only your changes.
- **Strict HTML**: every element needs its end tag (`<br>` and other void elements have none, `<br/>` is fine), nothing a browser would repair is accepted, and doctypes, `<script>`, `<style>`, `<meta>`, `on…` attributes and values in tag names, attribute names, `style`, `class`, `srcdoc` and `on…` are refused. A `media:` address must be `media:<id>` (see "Media on pages"). PascalCase tags place Components (see "Components"); `{{@popup <key>}}` attaches a Popup and renders its Click link (see "Popups").
- **Rules by kind of Template**: besides `isKnownValue` and `refuseElement`, a kind of Template gives `refuseCommand` (a command it can't hold: Entry templates refuse `{{@outlet}}`) and `checkTree` (rules about the whole tree once it is read, such as the Site template's one `<main>` and one outlet). Both parsers take them and report each problem where its node is.
- **Problems and notices**: `TemplateMarkupInvalidError` lists up to 20 problems, each `{ line, column, message }`; the API answers it as `TEMPLATE_MARKUP_INVALID` with `data.problems`. A save that works answers `notices` in the same shape.
- **API**: `templates.getEntryTemplateMarkup({ siteId, postTypeId })` answers `{ template, markup: { draft, published }, version }` (all null without a template); `templates.saveEntryTemplateMarkup({ siteId, postTypeId, markup, basedOn? })` saves the Draft (creating the template) and answers `{ template, markup, version, notices }`, the markup as stored now. With `basedOn` (the version it was made from, null for none yet), a save over a Draft someone changed meanwhile is refused with `CHANGED_MEANWHILE` and the version now in `data.version`. Both need `structure.edit`; `getSiteTemplateMarkup` and `saveSiteTemplateMarkup` do the same for the Site template. In core, `services.templates.entryTemplateMarkup` and `saveEntryTemplateMarkup` (`content/templates.ts`).

### Template commands and Conditions

`{{@name …}} … {{/name}}` blocks are **Template commands**, each one entry in `TEMPLATE_COMMANDS` (`packages/renderer/src/commands.ts`): its name, its node type, whether it is a block, the branch keywords allowed inside it (`else if`, `else`; a Loop's `empty`), the `snippet` Code mode's `{{@` menu inserts (`$0` where the caret goes), and how it reads from markup, prints, is checked as a stored node, lists its children and renders. The parser and printer handle every command through the registry, so **adding a command** is: a node type in the `TreeNode` union, an entry in `TEMPLATE_COMMANDS`, and removing its name from `RESERVED_COMMANDS` (currently empty). A command that names something of the Site (`{{@form <key>}}`, `{{@popup <key>}}`, `{{@filter <name> for="…"}}`) reads it through the tools the parser hands it (`FromMarkupTools.form`, `.popup` and `.filter`, `CheckTools.form`, `.popup` and `.filter`, `ToMarkupTools.formKey`, `.popupKey` and `.filterName`), filled from the parse and print options (`forms`, `popups`, `filters`, `formKey`, `popupKey`, `filterName`; `SiteComponents.forms`, `.popups` and `.filters` give them). A command whose content reads more than what is around it (a Loop's items) gives `bodyScope`, which both parsers use for its first section. A kind of Template that can't hold a command refuses it with `refuseCommand`.

`{{@outlet}}` is the content outlet: where the Site template shows each page's content. It takes nothing, holds nothing, and renders the page's content, already rendered (`RenderOptions.outlet`); only the Site template holds it, once (see "The Site template").

`{{@themeToggle}}` is the theme toggle: a button that switches the page between the Design system's light and dark values (see "The Design system"). It takes nothing, can't sit inside a link, a button or another control (`placementProblem`, which both parsers call), and renders only while the Site's published Design system has dark mode.

`{{@languageSwitcher}}` is the Language switcher: links to the page in each of the Site's languages (see "Languages"). It takes nothing, renders a `<nav>` and so can't sit inside a link, a control, a paragraph, a heading or inline text, nor directly in a list, and renders nothing on a Site with one language.

`{{@filter <name> for="<loop>"}}` places one of the Site's Filters (see "Filters"): written by the Filter's name, stored by its id with the Loop it narrows (`{ type: "filter", filter: <id>, loop: <name> }`). The Loop must be the Filter's own, shown in the same Template or Component (`{{@loop <loop>}}`; `filtersWithoutTheirLoop`, which both parsers check). A `<form>` of its own, it goes where a Form can, never in a Loop's items; a name the Site doesn't have, or a Filter never published in a tree being published, is refused where it is. A Module's Component can't place one. `{{@filter <name> for="<loop>" control="<id>"}}` shows one of its controls on its own (`control` in the node), by the control's id in the Filter's version (Draft for Drafts, published for publishing; an id it doesn't have is refused, naming the ids it has); it may sit in any tree of the page, such as the Site template's header, and shows only on pages that show its Loop.

`{{@search}}` is the Search element (see "Site search"): a search box sending visitors to the Site's search results page in the page's language with a plain GET. It takes nothing, is a `<form>` of its own (so it goes where a Form can, Loops' items included), and renders nothing while the Site has no search results page there.

`{{@form <key>}}` places one of the Site's Forms (see "Forms"): written by the Form's key, stored by its id (`{ type: "form", form: <id> }`), so changing the key changes only how markup writes it. A `<form>` of its own, it can't sit inside a link, a control, a paragraph, inline text, another form, nor directly in a list; nor in a Loop's items, as a page shows a placed Form once, never once per item (`{{@empty}}`, which renders once, may hold one; one a Component brings into a Loop's items renders nothing there); a key the Site doesn't have, or a Form never published in a tree being published, is refused where it is. A Module's Component can't place one (each Site has its own Forms).

`{{@consentSettings}}` is "Cookie settings": a button that opens the Consent banner again (see "The Consent banner"). It takes nothing, can't sit inside a link, a button or another control, nor directly in a list, and renders (hidden until the consent script shows it) only where the Site asks with a banner of WebCraft's, never in Edit mode; a page showing it carries the banner.

`{{@if}}` / `{{@else if}}` / `{{@else}}` is a Condition. Its tests are stored as data (`Expression` in `packages/renderer/src/expressions.ts`) and run by WebCraft's own interpreter, never `eval`:

- **empty**: a value on its own passes when it isn't empty (blank text, false, empty rich text, a body without content, an empty list are empty; 0 is a value); `not summary` tests that it is empty;
- **equals**: `==` and `!=` (a number as a number, true/false as yes or no, otherwise as text; empty equals `""`);
- **compare**: `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`: dates as dates (a date, or text written `2026-03-02…`), numbers as numbers, other text alphabetically; an empty side, a list, or a date against a number never passes;
- **contains**: a list holding a value (`tags contains "vegan"`), or text holding text whatever its case;
- **language**: `site.language`, the page's language (`site.language == "fr"`);
- **date window**: `now`, the time the page is rendered, compared with a date (`now >= "2026-12-01" and now < "2027-01-01"`); `now` alone or with `contains` is refused, and a page reading it is kept in the page cache only until the next date it compares with;
- **Loop position**: `loop.first`, `loop.last`, `loop.odd`, `loop.even`, `loop.index` (from 1) and `loop.count`, the innermost Loop's;
- **and/or/not** and brackets, with literals `"text"`, numbers, `true` and `false`.

`now` and `contains` are words of their own, so no Field can be named after them. A test nests at most 32 deep; markup is read by the same bounded parser.

`{{@loop}}`, `{{@empty}}` and `{{@pagination}}` are described in "Loops in Template markup".

### Rich text

`cleanRichText(html)` (`packages/renderer/src/rich-text.ts`) parses with parse5 (the HTML standard's algorithm, in a `<div>`'s context, as a browser would) and writes back only the allowlist: `p`, `br`, `strong`, `b`, `em`, `i`, `u`, `s`, `sub`, `sup`, `code`, `mark`, `small`, `ul`, `ol`, `li`, `blockquote`, and `a` with `href` (safe schemes only) and `title`. Scripts, styles, embeds, SVG, MathML, forms and comments go with their content; other elements keep their text. Nothing is matched with regular expressions, and the output is the cleaner's own, so cleaning clean rich text changes nothing. The rich text Field type (`content/field-types.ts`) cleans values on save (rich text with no text left is empty; at most 20,000 characters of cleaned HTML), and the renderer cleans them again on render.

### What a binding reads

`content/render-values.ts` works out what a binding's path names on a page (`entryParts`, `resolvePath`), and which paths a template may hold (`knownValues`, checked on save and on publish):

- a built-in Field or a Field of the Post Type: `summary`, `price`;
- a part of a Field: a Group's Field (`address.city`), a Repeater's Field across its rows (`team.name`: each row's name, a list), a link's `url` or `text`, a select's `value` or `label`, a body's `excerpt`, `image`, `reading_time` and `toc` (see "The Body field");
- what a Relationship points at, one level deep: the published Entries' `title`, `slug`, `url` (from their Post Type's URL pattern; a Page's path for a Page), dates (as dates, for the `date` formatter) and Fields (`category.title`, `category.url`, `authors.bio`); unpublished Entries are left out;
- `site.x`: the published Globals (`site.phone`, `site.address.city`).

On its own a select shows its label, a link its text (or its address), a Relationship its Entries' titles, rich text its cleaned HTML, a body its Blocks as HTML, true/false `true` or `false` (Conditions test them as they are); Groups and Repeater rows show nothing, and a media Field reads as `media:<id>` (see "Media on pages"). A list (a multi-select, a Repeater's Field, a multi-Relationship) shows its values joined with commas; a Loop over it (`{{@loop tags as tag}}`) shows them one by one. A path through a list keeps one value per item (null where an item has no such part), so a Loop finds each item's value at its place.

- `site.language` is the page's language and `site.name` the Site's name (the Globals can't take those keys);
- `<name>.count`, `.empty`, `.total`, `.page` and `.pages` read a Loop of the Site (see "Loops in Template markup").

## The visual builder

Staff build Templates and Components in a full-screen visual builder (#66; ADRs 0002, 0009, 0017). Core: `packages/core/src/builder/` (`services.builder`); procedures `builder.*` (`packages/api/src/procedures/builder.ts`, all `structure.edit`); the screens: `ui/builder/` and the route `routes/builder.$siteId.$.tsx`, outside the admin shell. Staff open it from Site › Templates (`templates.list`: every Template the Site can have, made or not; an Archive template whose URL pattern starts with `{slug}` has none) and Site › Components (which also creates them).

- **What it opens** (`BuilderTarget`): `siteTemplate`, `searchTemplate`, a Post Type's `entryTemplate` or `archiveTemplate`, or one of the Site's own `component`s (a Module's changes with its Module). Its address is `/builder/<site>/site-template`, `…/entry-template/<post type>`, `…/component/<id>` and so on. `builder.open` answers the Draft's tree (empty while the Site hasn't made the Template: the first save makes it), its `version`, a Component's Props, Dark mode and the Default language, and the **catalogue**: the elements and Template commands this kind of tree may hold (from the same `refuseElement` and `refuseCommand` its saves use), the Draft's Classes (with their styles as CSS), token utilities and token variables, the Components it may place (never one placing it), the Loops, the Forms and the values bindings may read.
- **One Draft, two views** (ADR 0017): the builder edits the tree in the browser, and saves by printing it (`builder.markup`) and handing the markup to the same saves Code mode, the CLI and AI clients use (`templates.save…Markup`, `components.saveMarkup` with the Props), based on the version it opened (`CHANGED_MEANWHILE` opens the conflict prompt: reload theirs, or save over it). The Template markup view shows the same print; switching back reads it (`builder.read`), so markup typed with its own line breaks comes back exactly as typed (whitespace-only text is kept in the tree, hidden from the Layers panel, and moves step over it). `ui/builder/save.ts` is the one place the builder writes its Draft; its saves reach the Draft's shared document like any write (see "Co-editing"), and binding the builder to the document live is still to come.
- **One-off styles** stay pending until the save: the builder keeps them by element id, `builder.markup` prints them as inline `style` attributes, and the markup save turns each into a generated Class in its transaction, exactly as a style typed in markup does (`builder.read` keeps a style that would make a new Class pending, so switching views makes nothing). An element's one-off style is its pending declarations, or its generated Classes' styles; changing it replaces them. Named Classes are made (`designSystem.createClass`) and restyled (`designSystem.updateClass`) in the Design system's Draft at once, as Code mode's CSS panel does: pages show those once the Design system is published.
- **The canvas** is an iframe of `/api/builder/canvas` on the admin host: an empty page whose own policy allows no script (`default-src 'none'`, images and media as Site pages may show them, `frame-ancestors 'self'`), framed `sandbox="allow-same-origin"` without scripts and out of the tab order. The builder fills it itself: `builder.preview` renders the tree as the Site renders it (the same renderer, escaping and URL rules) with real values where the Site has them (an Entry template shows the Entry saved last, from its Draft; the Globals' Draft; a Component's Props' defaults; Loops over published Entries), a binding with no value shown as written (`{{ summary }}`), Media from the admin's `/api/media/<site>/…`, every element tagged `data-wc-code="b#<id>"` (inside a Placement, `b/<placement id>#<id>`), and what only a page shows (`{{@outlet}}`, a Form, the Search element, the theme toggle, the Language switcher, Cookie settings) as placeholders the builder words; with the Design system's Draft stylesheet (`compileStylesheet` over the Draft trees and this one, pending one-off styles as the generated Classes they will become), applied as a constructed stylesheet. The light/dark switch sets `data-wc-theme` on the canvas's `<html>`. Outlines (selection, hover, fixed text) and drop targets are drawn by the admin over the iframe, never into the Site's HTML. A tree its saves would refuse is refused by the preview too (`TEMPLATE_INVALID`, each problem with where, `styles.<element id>` for a one-off style), and the canvas lists the problems.
- **The Layers panel** is an ARIA tree (one tab stop): the arrow keys move between layers and open and close them; Alt with the arrows moves the selected layer up, down, into the layer before it and out of what holds it; Space picks it up so the arrows move it, Space puts it down and Escape puts it back (as the component-arranging prototype's layout drawer); Ctrl/⌘ with X, C, V and D cut, copy, paste and duplicate (copies get new ids); Delete removes; F2 renames the layer label in place. Every change is announced (`role="status"`) with where the layer is now (`Moved li: 3 of 3 in ul.`), undone with Ctrl/⌘ Z and redone with Shift. A toolbar offers the same on the selected layer, rows drag, and the selected element drags on the canvas; both drop the same way (`dropPositionAt`: inside the middle of something that can hold children, else before or after). Moves are checked in the browser with the rules stored trees keep (`@webcraft/renderer/builder`: elements, nesting a browser would change, text placement, Template commands' places), so a refused move says why in the tree's own words.
