- **Attribute and Prop fields** show and read the text between an attribute's quotes exactly as Template markup writes it (`printAttributeText` and `parseAttributeText`, `@webcraft/renderer/builder`: the renderer's own printer and parser), so `{{ path }}` is a value, `\{{` a literal `{{` and `&amp;` an `&`, and what a field shows reads back as the same parts.
- **The insert menu** (Ctrl/⌘ I) offers semantic elements first (sections, headings, paragraphs, lists, links, a link styled as a button, since Templates hold no `<button>` and run no scripts, quotes, figures, tables, disclosure), Media images (chosen in the Media library, written `media:<id>`), values, every Template command of the registry the tree may hold (a Filter asks which, and whether its whole form or one of its controls on its own: the catalogue's `filters` list each with its Loop and its Draft's controls), the Components it may place (asking for required Props), text, comments and any other element by its tag; inside the selected layer, after or before it.
- **Opening with a Filter selected**: `?filter=<id>` on the builder's address selects where that Filter is placed (its form, else one of its controls); the Filter builder's Layout tab links there.
- **Fixed text**: a text node that shows text is flagged as "Fixed text" in the Layers panel (a badge) and on the canvas (a dashed amber outline around the element holding it, shown or hidden from the top bar, with a count). Its settings offer to make it editable: "Make it a Prop" in a Component (a new text Prop whose default is the text, saved with the Component, or one it has), "Make it a Field" in an Entry template (a new text Field of the Post Type whose default is the text, added at once, or one it has) and "Make it a Global" in the other Templates (a new Global whose default is the text, added with `globals.setFields` in one call, which starts the Globals' Draft with it, so a refusal, such as text longer than a text Field holds, adds nothing; or one the Site has); the text becomes the binding that reads it. The dialog names the text by its start.
- **Seams**: binding the builder to the shared Draft live (Co-editing) replaces `saveDraft` in `ui/builder/save.ts` with `useSharedDraft` over `/api/drafts/<site>/template|component/<id>`, which the server already serves; Checks (#95) can read the same trees; Conditions' and Loops' settings are edited as text (`readExpression`), so a richer Condition builder can replace those fields.

## The Design system

Each Site's Design system (ADR 0009) is its Design tokens, Classes, base styles, custom CSS and dark mode setting. It is Structure (`structure.edit`, Staff only), has a Draft and a published version, and is published as one item. The pure parts (the document, tokens, colour ramps, CSS checking, the compiler, DTCG files, the theme toggle's script) are in `packages/renderer/src/design-system/`; storage, usage, publishing and the Site's stylesheet in `packages/core/src/design-system/`.

- **Storage**: one `design_systems` row per Site holds the Draft and the published version as documents (`DesignSystem`), made the first time the Site changes it. Until then the Draft is the default one (`defaultDesignSystem()`), and until the first publish visitors see the default one. Every change locks the row (`lockDesignSystem`). Sections a stored document lacks read as the default ones (`readDesignSystem`), which is how the Classes #58 made were moved in by a custom migration.
- **Tokens** (`tokens.ts`, `colour.ts`): colours, the space and text scales, fonts, radius, shadows, breakpoints, content widths and the focus style. Each has an id that never changes and a name, and becomes custom properties in the stylesheet's `tokens` layer: `--<colour>-<step>`, `--space-<step>`, `--text-<step>`, `--font-<name>`, `--radius-<name>`, `--shadow-<name>`, `--width-<name>` (breakpoints are used by Classes instead). A colour is one base colour; its shades (10, the lightest, to 95; the base is 60) are worked out in OKLCH and its transparencies (`a10` to `a90`) from the base, and any shade but 60 can be given its own value. Dark values run along the same steps the other way (10 stays the page's background end) from a dark base colour, the light one unless set, with dark overrides of their own: Light/Dark is one set of ramps. Scale steps are `base × ratio^exponent` at the smallest and the largest viewport (360 and 1440 px by default), written as `clamp()` in between; any step can be given its own sizes. The smallest named breakpoint is where phones end (768 px by default).
- **Classes**: `{ id, name, generated, styles, breakpoints, css }`. `styles` are declarations for every width, `breakpoints` declarations from a breakpoint's width up (by the breakpoint's id), `css` the Class's own custom CSS, declarations and rules nested with `&`. Names are unique on the Site, never the name of a token utility, and never start with `wc-` (`isReservedName`: WebCraft's own classes, `wc-skip-link`, `wc-theme-toggle`). Elements store Classes by id, so a rename changes every page once published.
- **Token utilities**: one-declaration classes the tokens make (`bg-primary-10`, `p-m`, `text-xl`, `radius-m`, `shadow-s`, `max-width-content`, …, `UTILITIES`). Elements name them like Classes and store them by an id made from the token's id (`u:bg:colour-primary:10`), so renaming a token renames its utilities everywhere.
- **CSS as stored** (`css.ts`): every piece of CSS (Class styles, breakpoint styles, a Class's own CSS, base styles, the Site's custom CSS, later a Component's) is read with css-tree before it is saved, and each problem has its line and column: syntax, unknown properties, values a property doesn't take (values with `var()` are left to the browser), at-rules out of place, `!important` (layers make it unnecessary), `@import`, `@layer`, `@charset`, `@namespace`, properties that only ever ran script, and any address outside the Site (`url()`, `image-set()`, `src()`, in custom property values too, escaped names read as CSS reads them): only paths such as `/_media/…` and `#ids`. It is stored as written, with each `--token` and each `.class` in a selector kept by id (`CssText`); printing writes today's names, so a rename changes every use. When a save sends CSS text unchanged from what it read, the stored references are kept, even if a name changed in the same save.
- **In use**: deleting a Class, or removing a token, that an element tree (any Template's or Component's Draft or published version) or the Design system's own CSS, focus style or breakpoint styles uses is refused with `CLASS_IN_USE` / `TOKEN_IN_USE` and `data.usedBy`, each use saying where (`usage.ts`: `treeSources` reads Templates and Components, including Popups’ backing Components).
- **Template markup** reads Class and utility names from the Draft (`classCatalogue`). Saving and publishing a Template check its Class ids against the Draft. Visitors see a Class's name and styles once the Design system is published, except generated Classes, which go live with the Template that made them (`publishGeneratedClasses`): a one-off style shows with its Template; a Class Staff made or changed waits for the Design system.
- **The stylesheet** (`compileStylesheet`, `core/design-system/stylesheet.ts`): one per Site, built from what it has published: the published Design system (the default one before) and the Classes and utilities its published trees use, nothing else. Its cascade layers are declared first, `reset, tokens, base, classes, utilities, custom`, so later layers win whatever their selectors: `reset` (box sizing, media, `[hidden]` always hidden, reduced motion), `tokens` (`:root` custom properties, and dark values when dark mode is on), `base` (the focus style's custom properties from the focus token, base styles of plain elements, the skip link, the theme toggle, the Focal point classes of Media images, a body's callouts and videos: the info tone follows the primary colour token by id, the others have their own colours until custom CSS sets the `--wc-callout-*` properties; Forms; Filters, see "Filters"; the Consent banner, see "The Consent banner"), `classes` (Class styles, then each breakpoint's from the smallest up), `utilities`, `custom` (Classes' own CSS, Components' (#63, `StylesheetUse.componentCss`), then the Site's). Everything is written out by the parser from the stored CSS, never pasted, so nothing can end a layer early; each piece is checked again as it is built, and one that fails the checks is left out (`buildCss`), so CSS stored before the checks existed (Classes moved from the earlier `classes` table) never reaches a stylesheet unchecked. It is served at `/_wc/css/<16 hex digits of its SHA-256>.css` from the Site's domain with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, an ETag and `nosniff`; its name changes whenever what it holds changes. The same published data always builds the same file, so every web process agrees on it; each process keeps a Site's built styles for the Site's page cache generation. Publishing a Template or the Design system keeps the stylesheet it replaced in `stylesheets` for 30 days (`RETIRED_STYLESHEET_LIFETIME_MS`), so a page loaded just before still finds its file; such publishes take the Design system's row lock before reading the current stylesheet, so two at once take turns and neither loses the one the other made. Paths are `/_wc/…`, never naming WebCraft, since Site output stays unbranded.
- **Base styles** (`BASE_TARGETS`): `body`, headings, paragraphs, links, lists, tables and the rest, from tokens; links in `<nav>` are at least 24 px so lists of links are big enough to tap (WCAG 2.5.8). Staff can change them; the defaults pass axe in light and dark.
- **Dark mode** (`darkMode`): off by default. With it on, pages say `<meta name="color-scheme" content="light dark">` and the dark values apply under `prefers-color-scheme: dark`, unless the visitor chose light with the theme toggle (`data-wc-theme` on `<html>`), and whenever they chose dark.
- **The theme toggle** (`{{@themeToggle}}`): a `<button class="wc-theme-toggle" aria-pressed hidden>` in the page's language. Pages never carry inline scripts (ADR 0023), so its script is a file, `/_wc/js/<hash>.js` (`THEME_TOGGLE_SCRIPT`), linked in `<head>` only from pages that show a toggle; it applies the stored choice (`localStorage` `wc-theme`) before the page shows, unhides the toggles and keeps `aria-pressed` true to the theme on screen. Without dark mode the toggle renders nothing and no script is linked.
- **DTCG files** (`dtcg.ts`): `designSystem.exportTokens` writes the tokens as DTCG 2025.10 JSON (every shade and transparency, each step's size at the smallest viewport, `$type` per group, the focus style as aliases such as `{color.primary.70}`), WebCraft's own details (ids, base colours, overrides, dark values, ratios, fluid ranges) under `$extensions.webcraft`; importing an export gives back the same tokens. `designSystem.importTokens` also reads files from other tools (plain colour tokens, hex strings or DTCG colour objects, `"16px"` or `{ value, unit }`): each top-level group it holds replaces that kind of token, kinds it leaves out stay, and a token keeps its id by `$extensions.webcraft.id`, else by name. A file it can't read is `TOKENS_FILE_INVALID` with `data.problems`.
- **API** (all `structure.edit`): `designSystem.get` (Draft and published, CSS as text with today's names, `status`), `saveDraft` (sections `tokens`, `classes`, `baseStyles`, `customCss`, `darkMode`; each given replaces that section, `classes` being every Class in order; tokens without an id are new), `createClass`, `updateClass` (rename or restyle), `deleteClass`, `exportTokens`, `importTokens` and `publish` (through `publishing.publish` with `{ kind: "designSystem" }`, clearing the page cache, logged as `designSystem.publish`). A Draft that can't be saved is `DESIGN_SYSTEM_INVALID` with `data.problems`, each `{ at, message, line?, column? }` (`at` such as `customCss`, `classes[2].styles`, `tokens.colours[0].light.base`).
- **The Design system file** (`core/design-system/file.ts`, spec story 63): the whole Design system as one versioned JSON file, `{ format: "webcraft-design-system", version: 1, darkMode, tokens, classes, baseStyles, customCss }`: the tokens as `exportTokens` writes them (DTCG), each Class as `{ name, styles, breakpoints, css }` with its breakpoint styles by the breakpoint token's name, the same pieces `structure pull` writes as separate files. `designSystem.exportFile` (a public read, the Draft's or the published one, logged `designSystem.export`) writes it; `designSystem.importFile` (a public write, two steps for everyone, as Publish all) reads it over the Draft, never the published version: the first call answers `preview.changes` (per kind of token, Classes, base styles, custom CSS, Dark mode: what it adds, removes and changes) and a confirmation, the second (same file, that confirmation, while the Draft's content hash is unchanged) saves it through the Draft's own writer, logged `designSystem.import` with the changes. Tokens and Classes keep their ids by name (a token id from another Site is dropped first, `withKnownIds`), so elements stay linked; leaving out a Class or token in use is `CLASS_IN_USE` / `TOKEN_IN_USE`. A file it can't read is `DESIGN_SYSTEM_FILE_INVALID` with every problem (`format`, `version` (a newer file says so), `darkMode`, `classes[1].name`, `classes[0].breakpoints.tablet`, `baseStyles.marquee`, `tokens.<DTCG path>`); CSS it can't hold, `DESIGN_SYSTEM_INVALID` as any save.
- **The contrast Check** (`core/design-system/contrast.ts`, pure; spec story 123): `contrastChecks(designSystem)` works out the WCAG contrast of the colour pairs the Design system puts together, in light and, with Dark mode on, dark: each base style setting a text or background colour token (as that text on that background, the other one taken from `body`; `h1` to `h5` are large text), the focus colour on the body's background (a UI part), and in each colour's ramp step 95 on 10 (text on its tint), 10 on 60 (a label on the solid colour) and 60 on 10 (the solid colour as a UI part). Text needs 4.5:1, large text and UI parts 3:1 (`REQUIRED_CONTRAST`); ratios are rounded down to two decimals; transparencies are laid over the page (white, or black in dark). It warns and never stops a save or a publish. **Seam for the Checks list (#95)**: `designSystemChecks(designSystem)` gives the failing pairs as `{ check: "contrast", … }`, ready to collect into the Site's list.
- **The screen's preview** (`core/design-system/preview.ts`, `designSystem.preview`, internal): the Draft with the change not yet saved (`withChange`, refused as saving it would be), answering its compiled stylesheet (`compileStylesheet` with every Class, and with the dark values whether Dark mode is on or not, so the screen can show them), each colour's steps in light and dark with the generated values beneath overrides, each scale step's sizes, how many places use each Class and token (`useCounts`: elements of the Drafts' trees and pieces of the Design system's own CSS), the contrast results and the base style targets. Nothing is saved.
- **The screen** (#65, Site › Design system, `routes/_admin/sites.$siteId.design-system.tsx`, `ui/design-system/`; Structure, `structure.edit`): tabs Tokens (`?tab=` in the address), Classes (`?class=<id>`), Base styles and Custom CSS. Tokens: the colour ramps as one table with the Light/Dark switch (`Segmented`; the switch shows each colour's dark values and sets `data-wc-theme` on the previews), each swatch a button opening its editor (the base colour at 60, the dark base, or an override, with "Reset to the worked-out value"), Dark mode, then the contrast Check, the spacing and type scales (base, ratio and viewport at each end, any step given its own sizes), fonts, radius, shadows (names only: their layers come from a tokens file), breakpoints and content widths. Classes: a search over the list (generated Classes hidden unless asked), new Classes, and the style panel: the name, the styles for every width and from each breakpoint up as declaration rows (`DeclarationsEditor`, whose values offer the fitting tokens first, `tokenValuesFor`; any value can be typed) and the Class's own CSS. Every preview (`StylePreview`) is sample markup in the visual builder's canvas frame (sandboxed, no script, out of the tab order) styled by the preview's stylesheet as a constructed stylesheet, samples styled by data attributes, never by a class a Site could have. Edits go to the Draft through `useAutosave` (sections through `saveDraft`, Classes through `updateClass`; new tokens get their ids in the browser), adding and removing (a token, a Class) at once, so one in use is refused with where; leaving saves a Checkpoint (`useLeaveOnClose`). The header has Import (the dialog shows `preview.changes`, then "Replace the Draft"), Export (the file, the published file, or the DTCG tokens alone), Preview in Edit mode (Edit mode shows the Draft's stylesheet), History and Publish Design system. The builder's Class editor and this screen share `ui/design-system/declarations.ts`.
- **Seams for later tickets**: Components' trees are in `publishedTrees` (`stylesheet.ts`) and `treeSources` (`usage.ts`), and a Component's generated Classes go live with it; Component custom CSS (stored with the Component's version, published with it) goes into `StylesheetUse.componentCss`; Code mode (#76) shows the Draft with `draftStyles` (`compileStylesheet(draft, { classIds })` over the Draft trees) and saves Class CSS and the Site CSS through `changeDraft`; the Checks list (#95) collects `designSystemChecks`; binding the screen to a shared Draft (Co-editing) replaces its `useAutosave` once the Design system is a live kind.

## Migrations

Migrations are drizzle-kit files in `packages/db/src/migrations`, applied by their own `migrate` step (ADR 0031), never by web or worker at start-up.

- Change the schema in `packages/db/src/schema/` (one file per area, re-exported from `index.ts`), then `bun run db:generate`. Commit the generated folder.
- Hand-written SQL (functions, triggers, data fixes) goes in its own custom migration: `bun run db:generate --custom --name=<what-it-does>`. Keeping it separate means a regenerated schema migration never loses it.
- Every change keeps the previous version working on the new schema: add first, remove in a later release. There are no down-migrations.
- **The migrate step** (`webcraft admin migrate`, `migrateStep` in `packages/core/src/migrate-step.ts`, on top of `runMigrations` in `packages/db/src/migrations.ts`):
  1. When anything is pending (WebCraft's migrations, a Module's, the job queue's schema) on a database that already holds an Install, it **takes a Backup first** (reason `before-migrations`, where Install settings › Backups says), reading the database through its catalog, since this build's tables may not exist yet. `--no-backup` skips it, and the output says so. A new database has nothing to back up.
  2. **Each migration runs in its own transaction**, WebCraft's first (oldest first), then each Module's in the order the build lists its Modules (`FIRST_PARTY_MODULES`), each Module's oldest first. Module migrations come after all of WebCraft's because they may point at WebCraft's tables, never the other way round. A migration that fails is rolled back and stops the step; the ones before it stay applied, which the previous version runs on (add first). WebCraft's are recorded in Drizzle's own table (`drizzle.__drizzle_migrations`), as drizzle-kit records them, so its tools still read it.
  3. It installs or upgrades the job queue's schema, then **makes every job queue** of `coreJobs` and brings the existing ones' retry and expiry settings up to date (`jobQueues(coreJobs)`), so a job sent before the worker ever started waits in its queue.
  4. It records this build's version (`WEBCRAFT_VERSION`) in `install_versions`: the newest is the version the data was written by, which a Backup's manifest carries.
  Only one migrate step changes a database at a time (an advisory lock). Web and worker check the result at start-up and refuse to run on a database that is behind; `/readyz` reports the same check.
- **Going back a version**: a database ahead of the build is fine: WebCraft's applied migrations the build doesn't have are left alone, and a Module's applied migrations that sort after every one the build has are accepted (with the tables they made). A migration the build has lost from the middle, or one that changed after it was applied, is still refused. The job queue is the exception: pg-boss refuses a schema newer than its own, so `checkSchema` answers `job-queue-newer` (and `/readyz` 503) and start-up says to restore the Backup the migrate step took before that update.
- **Module migrations** follow the same steps (see "Modules › Tables and migrations"): each in its own transaction, with its rules checked inside it (nothing outside the Module's schema), and the Module's tables checked against what it declares with its last pending migration (and on every run), recorded in `module_migrations` with a checksum. The schema check counts a pending Module migration as behind. `runMigrations`, `checkSchema` and `assertSchemaCurrent` take the build's Modules as `{ modules: moduleSchemas(FIRST_PARTY_MODULES) }`; `services.modules.schemas` holds the Install's.
- **A restore** rebuilds a Backup's schema with `applyMigrationLevel`: exactly the migrations the Backup's database had, each in its own transaction (see "Backups").
- When two branches both add migrations, keep the integration branch's and regenerate yours on top with `bun run db:generate`. Git often merges two such migrations without a conflict, but both were generated from the same parent, so the newer snapshot misses the other's tables and the next `db:generate` would write them again. `tests/db/migration-chain.test.ts` fails when that happens: delete your migration's folder and generate it again (a custom one: recreate it with `--custom` and paste its SQL back).

## Backups

A Backup (GLOSSARY.md, ADR 0032) is a whole Install in one archive; `packages/core/src/backups/`, behind `services.backups`; tables `backups` (the Backup log), `backup_settings` and `install_versions`.

- **The archive** is a plain tar file (`tar.ts`: POSIX ustar, pax headers for long paths and large sizes, written and read as streams, never held in memory), `webcraft-backup-<yyyymmdd>-<hhmmss>-<reason>-<4 hex>.tar`: `manifest.json` first (`manifest.ts`, `backupManifestSchema`: format and version, the archive's name, when, why, the WebCraft version the data was written by (`install_versions`) and the one that took it, the SDK version, the Modules and their versions, the migration level, the Master key's fingerprint, the tables in load order with their columns and row counts, sequences, partitions, and what the files are), then `database/<n>.copy.gz`, then `files/<storage key>`. `tar -tf` and `tar -xf` read it.
- **The database dump** (`dump.ts`) is written by WebCraft itself through `pg` and `pg-copy-streams` (Postgres's COPY text format, gzip-compressed per table), in one `REPEATABLE READ` snapshot, so web, worker and the CLI need no `pg_dump` and any Postgres server works. It holds data only: the schema is the migrations. It covers `public` and every Module schema (`m_*`), partitioned tables through their parent (the partitions' bounds are in the manifest), and every sequence's value, in foreign-key order. Left out (`LEFT_OUT_TABLES`): the job queue (`pgboss`), the migration records (rebuilt by the restore's migrations) and `visitor_salts` (a day's salt must not outlive its day).
- **Files** (`files.ts`): every file under local storage (`WEBCRAFT_STORAGE_DIR`) except Media variants on disk (`media_files.role = 'variant'`, read in the dump's snapshot), half-written `.partial` files and `webcraft-check/`. A file deleted while the Backup runs is left out. Files in an S3-compatible bucket aren't copied; the manifest counts them and names the bucket.
- **Taking one** (`takeBackup`, `take.ts`): one at a time on the Install (an advisory lock; `BackupBusyError`), recorded in the Backup log as `running`, then `done` with its size or `failed` with why (`BackupFailedError`, logged as an error). `services.backups.take({ reason, to? })`; the server commands build their context with `openBackupForCommand` (it works on a database that is behind, and refuses a Master key that isn't the Install's).
- **Targets** (`targets.ts`): the Backups folder (`WEBCRAFT_BACKUP_DIR`; written beside its name, then renamed), a file or folder `--to` names, or an S3-compatible bucket (`<prefix>/backups/<name>`, a multipart upload of 32 MB parts through `s3Client` from `media/s3-storage.ts`, aborted on failure; the bucket's secret is the Install secret `backups.s3.secretAccessKey`). Like Media storage, the bucket is spoken to through `services.http` directly, not the guarded fetcher: a self-hosted store is usually on an internal address.
- **Install settings › Backups** (`install.backups.get`, `install.backups.update`; `install.manage`; changing it is the Sensitive action `backups.change`, logged as `backups.change`, never with the secret): scheduled on or off (on unless changed), the target, the bucket (checked like Media storage's before it is saved: `STORAGE_UNREACHABLE`). `get` also answers the Backup log (newest 30) and `status` (`{ last, lastDone, failing }`), which Install health's Backups part reads. The admin shows `shell.context`'s `banners.backupFailing` on every page for the Superadmin until Install health's own banner says the Backups part is Failing.
- **Scheduled Backups**: `backups.scheduled` (01:11 UTC) takes one while the setting is on, then `prune()` removes the scheduled Backups the keep rules (`keep.ts`, `backupsToRemove`) no longer keep: the newest of each of the last 7 days and of each of the last 4 weeks (Monday to Sunday, UTC; `BACKUP_KEEP`). Backups taken by command or before migrations are never removed. A failed one isn't retried before the next day.
- **Restoring** (`restoreBackup`, `restore.ts`; `webcraft admin restore`): refuses (`RestoreRefusedError` with `refusal.reason`) a file that isn't a Backup (`not-a-backup`; that includes a manifest naming anything but tables, columns and sequences of WebCraft's and the Modules' schemas as plain names, or a partition bound other than the monthly ranges WebCraft makes, `PARTITION_BOUND`: the manifest is read before the archive can be trusted, and the partition statement is built again from the bound's two timestamps), a newer WebCraft version (`newer-version`, by semver) or migrations this build doesn't have (`newer-migrations`), another Master key's fingerprint (`master-key`), a database web or worker is connected to (`in-use`, by `application_name`), and a database that holds any table (`not-empty`) unless `replace: { confirm }` names the database (`confirmation`); then it empties it. Then `applyMigrationLevel` rebuilds the schema at the Backup's level, the partitions are made, the rows load in one transaction and the sequences are set, the files go back into local storage, the migrate step runs (an older Backup is brought up to this version; the job queue is made), and `afterRestore` deletes the variants' rows of local files and queues `media.variants` for every image without variants, `media.process` for uploads waiting, `imports.run` for runs under way, raises every Site's page cache generation and marks every search index for a rebuild, and settles the Backup log's `running` line. Waiting jobs (emails being retried, Webhook deliveries) aren't in a Backup.
- **Left out of the dump** as well: `worker_heartbeats` (which workers run says nothing about a restored Install).
- **Tests** (`tests/backups/`): `install.backup({ to? })`, `install.restore(archive, { replace? })` and `install.migrate({ backup: true })` run what the commands run; `tests/harness/backups.ts` reads an archive's manifest and writes a copy with a rewritten one (a Backup "from a newer version"); `tests/backups/commands.test.ts` runs the real commands.

## Site export and import

A Site export (GLOSSARY.md, ADR 0033) is one Site in one archive, to copy or move it into a Workspace on the same or another Install; a Site import reads one into a Workspace as a new Site, never over an existing one, and keeps its Site import report. It is not a Backup (a whole Install) and not an Import (outside content into a Site). `packages/core/src/site-transfer/`, behind `services.siteTransfer`; tables `site_exports` (a Site table), `site_imports` (a Workspace table) and `paused_domains` (a Site table); procedures `siteExports.*`, `siteImports.*` and `domains.paused`, `domains.switchOnPaused`, `domains.discardPaused`, all internal.

- **The archive** (`format.ts`) is a plain tar file written and read with the Backups' streaming `tar.ts`, `webcraft-site-<slug>-<yyyymmdd>-<hhmmss>.tar`: `manifest.json` first (`siteExportManifestSchema`: format and format version, the WebCraft version that wrote the data and the one that took it, the SDK version, WebCraft's migrations, the Site's own row, the Modules whose data it holds with their versions and tables, every file with its row count and SHA-256, the people, the files, the secrets' names, what was left out and why, and counts), then `secrets.json` when it carries secrets, `people.jsonl.gz`, one `data/<n>-<schema>.<table>.jsonl.gz` per table (each row as `row_to_json` writes it, one a line, gzip-compressed, in load order: a table after the tables its foreign keys point at), `files.jsonl.gz` (each stored file's key, size and SHA-256) and the files themselves under `files/<storage key>`. JSON Lines rather than COPY text keep the archive readable by any tool and let the import read each row into the target table's own types (`json_populate_record`), whichever version wrote it.
- **What goes in** is read from Postgres's catalog (`tables.ts`, `siteTables`): every table, WebCraft's or a Module's (`m_*`), with a `workspace_id` and a `site_id`, so a later feature's Site table goes in by itself. What must not is listed in `LEFT_OUT_TABLES` with its reason, and the manifest names both (`tests/site-transfer/round-trip.test.ts` checks every table holding a Site's rows is one or the other): credentials (API tokens' and grants' Sites, Content keys and their usage, Share links), secrets (carried apart), sessions and links (Edit mode, Staging, confirmations), Co-editing's live documents (the Drafts are in their items), Members (by email instead), what publishing makes (published slugs, search documents, gone paths, stylesheets), Scheduled publishes (dropped and listed), work under way (Import files, retired files, translation tasks and cache), the AI spend of the Workspace that paid, delivery logs and Reports' sends, the Module log, links into the Install-wide Error log, and Site transfer jobs and reports. Media variants are left out (made again on import, as after a restore) and replaced files on their way out too (`ROW_FILTERS`).
- **Exporting** (`siteExports.start`, Staff and the Superadmin: `site.export`; a Sensitive action, `site.export`; logged `site.export`) queues `siteExports.run`, one export of a Site at a time (`SITE_EXPORT_RUNNING`). The worker writes it as the person who asked, their access checked again (`failure.reason: "access"`), from one `REPEATABLE READ` snapshot: each table through a cursor, a batch of rows at a time (`batchRows`, 500), into gzip files in a temporary folder; the stored files it names (Media originals and uploads, Libraries' files, claimed private Form uploads) are hashed in a first pass, then the archive is streamed into storage as parts of 32 MiB (`<workspace id>/<site id>/exports/<id>/part-000001`, …, `parts.ts`), never held whole. `createTestInstall({ siteTransfer: { batchRows, partBytes } })` lowers both, to test the streaming with a small Site.
- **Submissions and private Form uploads**: included by default; `siteExports.start({ includeSubmissions: false })`, the admin checkbox, or the command's `--without-submissions` excludes `m_forms.submissions` and `form_uploads` rows and their private bytes. The manifest and import report record `leftOut.submissions` (false for older archives). Only uploads claimed by a Submission are exported; abandoned and detached cleanup rows are excluded. Each file is read from its row's recorded disk/S3 backend, even if the Install now writes elsewhere. A missing required file fails the export. Import matches each upload to exactly one `SubmissionFile.uploadId`, Submission, Form, field and file metadata; missing, repeated, oversized or inconsistent claims are refused even if Forms is missing and its rows are kept aside. Workspace/Site/Form/Submission/upload ids are replaced together, private keys become `<workspace>/<site>/form-uploads/<upload>`, and stored file URLs are removed: the admin download API generates them for the destination. Files go to the destination's current backend, remain behind the Forms Module's download permission checks, and never count against the Media quota.
- **Social cards**: the Site's enabled/layout settings and each Post Type's layout override transfer unchanged, with the organization's logo remapped to its new Media id. Derived `social_cards` rows and their PNG bytes are left out (`published`); an archive carrying that cache is read and validated but its rows are ignored. There are no extra Social card storage files or secrets. Both API and command imports enqueue `socialCards.render` alongside Media work; the worker reads only published Content, so imported Drafts produce no cards until first publish. Normal publish notifications and hourly reconciliation regenerate cards using the destination's ids and settings.
- **The download link** (`siteExports.list` and `get` answer it while it works) is `/api/site-exports/<site id>/<export id>?expires=…&signature=…` on the admin host: an HMAC-SHA256 of the Site, the export and the expiry under a key derived from the Master key (`siteExportLinkKey`), for 24 hours after the export was written (`SITE_EXPORT_LINK_LIFETIME_MS`), only to a signed-in person who may export that Site (anyone else, or a link that doesn't hold, gets the plain 404). It streams the parts as `application/x-tar`, `private, no-store`, and is logged `siteExport.download`. The hourly `siteTransfers.purge` (:13) deletes the parts once the link has expired (status `expired`).
- **Secrets** (`passphrase.ts`, ADR 0032): with an export passphrase (12 characters at least, never stored), the Site's secrets (its SMTP password, CDN token, Webhook and CAPTCHA secrets, its Modules' secrets) go into `secrets.json`, each encrypted with AES-256-GCM under a key derived from the passphrase with PBKDF2-SHA256 (600,000 rounds, a random salt), bound to its name, beside a known value that tells a wrong passphrase. Between the person typing it and the worker, only the derived key is kept, as an Install secret (`siteExport:<id>:key`, `siteImport:<id>:key`) removed when the job ends (and by the purge). Without a passphrase they are left out, and the manifest lists their names. A Workspace's AI key isn't a Site's and never goes in. Neither the staging password nor its hash or change timestamp is included in Site settings. The encrypted inventory may contain each listed name only once; unlisted names are refused. Secrets that cannot be decrypted, are empty or exceed the secret store's 65,536-character limit are dropped and named in the report.
- **Uploading** (`siteImports.startUpload`, `uploadPart`, `finishUpload`; `site.import`) works as an Import's source file does: parts of 8 MiB at most, each with its SHA-256, kept privately under `<workspace id>/site-imports/<id>/`, going on where an upload stopped; an upload is its uploader's alone, and is deleted a day after it was last used if nobody started it. Finishing it reads only the archive's head (its manifest and the passphrase check) and refuses (`SITE_IMPORT_REFUSED`, `data.reason`) a file that isn't a Site export (`not-an-export`), a newer layout (`newer-format`) or a newer WebCraft (`newer-version`): the import then shows what the archive holds and the Modules it needs, and how each stands here (`approved`, `waiting`, `update-waiting`, `cannot-run`, `missing`).
- **Starting** (`siteImports.start`; a Sensitive action, `site.import`) takes the new Site's name and slug (checked as a new Site's: `SLUG_INVALID`, `SLUG_TAKEN`), checks the Workspace's Media quota (`MEDIA_QUOTA_FULL`, from the manifest's Media bytes), and checks the passphrase at once (`SITE_IMPORT_PASSPHRASE_WRONG`); then `siteImports.run` reads it in, as the person who started it, their access checked again.
- **Reading it in** (`import.ts`, `importSite`) runs in one transaction: the new Site's row (its own settings from the export; never the staging password, which is left out and listed), then each table, streamed (unzipped, COPY into a staging table, one `INSERT … SELECT` from `json_populate_record` per table, so rows pointing at rows of the same table load together), every file's checksum and count checked as it goes, then the stored files copied into the storage new files go to now. File contents stream into storage, never through a whole-file buffer. A file that doesn't hold what the manifest says fails the import (`damaged`); nothing stays: the transaction rolls back and the files copied are removed.
  - **Archive validation and resource limits**: tar extension headers are at most 64 KiB, the manifest and encrypted secrets at most 4 MiB each, each decompressed JSON row at most 16 MiB and 128 nesting levels, and each people/file-index metadata file at most 16 MiB decompressed. Metadata is decompressed incrementally; table rows stream into Postgres. Invalid UTF-8, duplicate identities, invalid Member roles, mismatched Module/schema names, repeated or missing files, wrong checksums and sizes, and file references outside this Site are refused. The file inventory must match Media/Library/private Form upload rows and its byte totals. The worker checks actual Media usage again under the Workspace's quota lock, so a changed quota or another upload cannot slip past the check (`failure.reason: "quota"`).
  - **Identifiers and literal Content** (`remap.ts`, `references.ts`): UUID-typed SQL columns and the explicit reference slots of each stored contract are remapped. Field definitions distinguish text from Media and Relationships, including Groups, Repeaters, Body Component Props, translations and history. Template trees, Classes, token references, first-party Module references, canonical storage keys and Webhook secret names have their own rules. Text, slugs, external URLs, Form answers, filenames and unknown Module JSON remain literal, even when they contain an existing item's exact UUID. Person discovery follows these same contracts, so Content cannot accidentally export an unrelated account. Original rows and Structure are staged in temporary Postgres tables; the visitor reads one bounded row and looks up original definitions, without a Site-sized in-memory ID map. IDs use the old UUID XOR a random mask, preserving valid version and variant bits. Invalid UUID identifiers are refused; the destination Workspace/Site always override row tenancy.
  - **Archive compatibility**: format 3 is the release baseline. It records each table's UUID columns, person columns and primary key, plus source Field declarations and versions for reusable Module Components and Block types. Formats 1 and 2 were unpublished development layouts; the explicit 1 → 2 → 3 chain makes a best-effort upgrade using installed SQL schemas and exact-version Module declarations when available. Missing or incomplete source contracts for stored Module Props, or legacy tables of an unavailable Module, are refused with a useful message instead of guessing which text is an ID. A missing column takes its default, a missing table stays empty, and sequenced columns take new values in their original order. Later layouts add `FORMAT_UPGRADES` steps; newer layouts or WebCraft versions are refused.
  - **Module Component provenance**: `components.allModuleComponents()` supplies compiled declarations without running a Module. Exports include declaration-only Modules too. Imports use the archive's declarations ahead of the destination build's, retaining them in the Site-scoped `site_module_components` inventory in the same transaction. Re-export uses these source contracts even after the import report expires or the Module disappears or changes version. An imported contract stays authoritative for that stored data; installing a different Module version does not silently reinterpret it. Unknown Prop paths are refused, including nested Group/Repeater keys. This metadata contains no Module code or Template markup and is carried in the manifest, not as a table dump.
  - **Content arrives as Drafts**: every item with a published version (Entries, the Globals, Templates, Components, Loops, Filters, Forms and their emails, the Design system, scripts, the Consent banner) keeps its Draft (the published version where it had none) and loses its published version and times, so nothing is published until someone publishes (Publish all once puts it all live). Revisions and Checkpoints come as they were. If the former published version has no Revision (Revisions were off or pruned), import preserves it as a Revision attributed to the `site import` command, without claiming a publisher; the existing Draft stays. Thus each item's history shows what was published where it came from, by whom and when, and the next publish here adds a Revision. The report counts the items that were published.
  - **Domains arrive paused** (`paused_domains`): the Site's domains and its languages' domains are kept apart, off the domain list, where a name is unique on the Install; Staff switch each on (`domains.switchOnPaused`: added, or set as its language's, waiting for DNS like any new domain; `DOMAIN_TAKEN` while another Site has it) or discard it (`domains.discardPaused`, logged `pausedDomain.discard`), on Site › Domains.
  - **People** (`people.jsonl.gz`: the people the rows name, the Site's Members with their role and its pending invites, by email only; never anything they sign in with): matched to accounts here by email. A Member of the Workspace keeps their role there and reaches the new Site; anyone else (an account elsewhere on the Install, or none) becomes a pending invitation to the new Site with the role they had, emailed only when someone sends them from the report (`siteImports.sendInvitations`, a Sensitive action, `member.invite`, logged `member.resendInvite` each). What people without an account here did is attributed to the person importing (columns naming a person), and listed (`reassigned`); the Audit log keeps the Site's entries with their times, naming such a person in the entry (`details.importedPerson`) instead.
  - **What else changes**: Scheduled publishes are dropped (listed; a Review they ended goes back In review), Reports come paused, Webhooks whose signing secret didn't come are switched off, Import runs under way stop, and Media is queued for the worker (`media.process`, `media.variants`).
  - **Modules**: installed Modules load their tables whether approved or waiting for approval. Missing Modules' validated, remapped rows are kept durably in `site_retained_rows`, one JSON row per record, with `site_retained_tables` recording Module identity/version, source column contracts and load order. Both are tenant-scoped and deleted with the Site; neither depends on the 90-day report. Subsequent exports stream these rows under their original table names with fresh counts/checksums, preserving table order. If the Module is now installed, export combines live and retained rows, preferring live primary keys (or subtracting exact matching rows as a multiset without a key). A subsequent import on an Install with the Module loads the combined data normally. Submissions omission applies equally to live and retained rows and their private bytes. Partitioned tables get the months their rows require.
  - **Atomic completion**: the same Postgres transaction writes the Site, secret ciphertext under the receiving Master key, Media/Social card jobs, report/status and `site.import`/invitation Audit entries. Secret writes share the key-rotation lock; job sends use the import's SQL connection. A finalization failure rolls the Site and all of those records back and removes copied files. Both the worker and the real CLI use this finalizer. The report lasts 90 days (`SITE_IMPORT_REPORT_LIFETIME_MS`); deleting it leaves the Site and retained Module data intact.
- **The server commands** (`webcraft site export <site id> [--to …] [--passphrase] [--without-submissions]`, `webcraft site import <archive> --workspace <id> --slug <slug> [--name …] [--passphrase]`, `apps/cli/src/admin/site-transfer.ts`) do the same on the server, at once (`services.siteTransfer.exportByCommand`, `importByCommand`), the passphrase asked on a terminal or read from standard input, never from the command line; no person acts, so the Audit log records them as the command, and what people without an account here did is attributed to nobody. An import by command keeps its report on the Workspace's Site imports too.
- **The admin**: Site › Export (Site settings, `site.export`: the passphrase form, the exports with their progress, download links and what was left out), Workspace › Site imports (`site.import`: the upload, the imports), each import's page (what the archive holds and the start form; its progress; the Site import report with its numbers, tabs for the invitations (Send invitations), Members kept, people reassigned, Paused domains, secrets, what was left out and the Modules, and the next steps), and the Paused domains card on Site › Domains.
- **Social cards (#93)**: Site and Post Type settings transfer, including the organization logo's Media reference. Derived `social_cards` rows/PNGs are omitted. Both API and CLI imports enqueue `socialCards.render` atomically; imported Content arrives as Drafts, so ordinary first publish and hourly reconciliation regenerate cards when applicable.
- **Tests** (`tests/site-transfer/`): `tests/harness/site-transfer.ts` exports and downloads (`exportSite`, `exportedArchive`), uploads and imports (`uploadArchive`, `importArchive`), reads an archive (`tarFiles`, `manifestIn`, `rowsIn`) and writes one another WebCraft could have (`rewritten`: a manifest, rows or tables changed, checksums made to fit); `support.ts` builds a rich Site.

## The update check

`packages/core/src/updates.ts`, behind `services.updates`; table `update_checks` (one row).

- The worker's `updates.check` (05:29 UTC, at most once a day: `check({ onlyIfDue: true })`) and `webcraft admin check-update` (at once) read the release feed (`config.updateFeed`, `WEBCRAFT_UPDATE_FEED`) through the guarded fetcher, to the feed's host only, 10 seconds, 2 MB. The request carries `User-Agent: WebCraft` and `Accept: application/json`, nothing else: no Install id, no version, no query of its own.
- The feed can be GitHub's release JSON (`/releases/latest`, or the list `/releases`, skipping drafts and pre-releases) or a static file `{ "version", "url", "notes", "publishedAt" }`. The newest version (semver, `v` dropped), its `https` release URL, its notes as plain text (4,000 characters at most) and when it was published are kept; a failed read keeps the release found before and records why (`problem`: the guarded fetcher's, `status` or `format`).
- `services.updates.latest()` compares it with `WEBCRAFT_VERSION` (`updateAvailable`; a version that isn't semver never is behind). The admin shows `shell.context`'s `banners.update` on the Install's pages for the Superadmin, and `install.updates.get` on Install settings › General.
- **WebCraft's version** is the `version` of the repository's root `package.json` (`WEBCRAFT_VERSION`, `packages/core/src/version.ts`): a release sets it, then is tagged `v<version>`.

## Logs

Every process writes one JSON object per line to standard output through `createLogger` (`packages/core/src/log.ts`): `time` (ISO, UTC), `level`, `msg`, `process` (`web`, `worker`, `migrate`) and the line's own fields. `WEBCRAFT_LOG_LEVEL` drops lines below a level; `WEBCRAFT_LOG_FORMAT=pretty` (`bun run dev` only) writes a readable line each instead.

- **The log context** (`withLogContext`, `noteLogPlace`; `AsyncLocalStorage`): whoever starts work names it, and every line written meanwhile carries it, however deep the code: the request's `requestId` (the handler, see "How a request flows"), the job's `job` and `jobId` and the request id it was queued with (`runJob`, see "Jobs"), the Workspace and Site the data layer opens (`workspaceId`, `siteId`: `site()`, `workspace()`, `background()`, `visitors()` and `staging()` note them; the last one opened wins), and `module` while a Module's procedure, Site route, event or job runs. A job sent from inside a request or another job carries its request id in its data (`_requestId`), so a request's jobs log with its id.
- **Redaction** (`packages/core/src/redaction.ts`, one list for every process): every line is redacted before it is written. Fields named as secrets (`SECRET_NAMES`, compared without case, `-`, `_` or `.`, as a name or its end: passwords, passphrases, secrets, tokens, API, AI, Content and private keys, `Authorization`, cookies, signatures, one-time codes) and as people's content (`CONTENT_NAMES`: a Submission's values, an email's HTML body) are replaced with `[redacted]`, at any depth (objects, arrays, maps, `Headers`, an error's cause); in every text (messages, stacks, URLs) WebCraft's tokens and keys by their prefixes, `Bearer`/`Basic` credentials, a password in a URL and secret query or form parameters (`?token=`, `&key=`) are too. Errors are written as their name, message, stack and code. The only exceptions are marked where they are written, `printedOnPurpose(value)`: the setup token (spec story 5) and a System email written to the log while no SMTP is set (story 7). The Module log's details go through the same redaction. Tests: `tests/logs/` (`redaction.test.ts` plants one secret of each kind).

## The Error log

Unexpected failures, grouped by cause, for the Superadmin (`GLOSSARY.md`; `packages/core/src/error-log/`, behind `services.errorLog`; tables `error_groups` and `error_group_places`; procedures `errorLog.list`, `errorLog.get`, `errorLog.resolve`, all internal, `installHealth.manage`; the admin's Install › Error log).

- **What lands there**: every line logged at error level with an `error` field (`reportingFailures`, the logger `createInstallServices` hands every service): the handler's 500s, a Management API call that broke (`logUnexpectedFailure` in `packages/api/src/errors.ts` hands over the error behind oRPC's answer; refusals under 500 never), a job that threw (`runJob`), and failures caught so work goes on. Not a Module's own failures (`isModuleFailure`: `ModuleFailedError`, or what the Module host marked with `markModuleFailure`): its Module log has them.
- **By cause** (`fingerprint.ts`): the error's kind and the top 3 frames of its stack, made plain (no line numbers, folders above `packages/`/`apps/`/`modules/`/`node_modules/`, query or bundle hashes), or its message made plain when it has no stack. Each cause keeps its count, first and last seen, the newest example (message and stack, redacted, with its request id and where it happened: `request` with the procedure or method and path, `job` with its name, or `process`) and the Workspaces and Sites it happened in (from the log context; a deleted one drops out).
- **Bounded writes**: `capture` only notes a failure in memory, never waits and never throws; the failures of each cause are written together at most every 5 seconds (`FLUSH_DELAY_MS`) per process, up to 50 causes at a time (`MAX_PENDING_CAUSES`; more are dropped and the drop is logged at warn) and 20 places per cause, one upsert each. A storm of one failure costs one write per 5 seconds. Reads write what is noted first, so a process sees its own failures at once.
- **Resolving**: Mark resolved (`errorLog.resolve`, audited `errorLog.resolve`) hides a cause from the open list; it opens again (`reopenedAt`) when it happens again.
- **Retention**: `error-log.prune` (daily, 03:57 UTC) deletes causes not seen for 30 days (`ERROR_LOG_DAYS`).
- **An outside error tracker** (`WEBCRAFT_ERROR_DSN`, `tracker.ts`): each failure is also posted, best effort, as an event of the widely used open error-tracking protocol. The subset sent: the DSN `https://<public key>@<host>[:port][/<path>]/<project id>` (a secret key in it is never sent); one `POST …/api/<project id>/envelope/` per event with `Content-Type: application/x-sentry-envelope` and `X-Sentry-Auth: Sentry sentry_version=7, sentry_key=<public key>, sentry_client=webcraft/<version>`; an envelope of three lines (its header with `event_id`, `sent_at` and `dsn`, one item header `type: event`, the event: `event_id`, `timestamp`, `platform`, `level: error`, `logger`, `release: webcraft@<version>`, `transaction` (the procedure, job or request), `exception.values[0]` with `type`, `value` and the top frames as `stacktrace.frames`, `fingerprint` (the cause), `tags` (`source`, `job`, `workspace_id`, `site_id`, `module`) and `extra.request_id`). Never a request's headers, cookies, body or person. It goes through the guarded fetcher (the DSN's host only, so on a public address; no redirect; 5 seconds; 64 KB answer), never waited for by the request or job, at most one event per cause a minute per process and 5 on their way at once; what can't be sent is dropped and said at warn.

## Install health and Health alerts

`packages/core/src/health/`, behind `services.health`; table `health_rows`; procedures `installHealth.get` (`refresh` works it out now), `installHealth.queues`, `installHealth.retry` and `installHealth.discard` (all internal, `installHealth.manage`); the admin's Install › Install health (`/install/health`, `INSTALL_HEALTH_PATH`).

- **The parts** (`HEALTH_PARTS`, `checks.ts`), each OK, Warning or Failing with a reason code and its values (worded by the admin, `installHealth.reasons.<part>.<code>`): `web` (the process answering: always OK, never stored); `worker` (`services.workers`: Failing when no check-in for 2 minutes or none ever, a Warning when it runs another version); `database` (`checkSchema`: reachable and current); `jobQueue` (from pg-boss's table: Failing when a job waits 15 minutes past its time, or runs 15 minutes past its expiry, while a worker runs; a Warning for jobs failed in the last day, or waiting while no worker runs, which the worker part says); `storage` (a file written, read back and removed where new files go, `checkBucket`; on local disk the room left, through the `disk` port: a Warning under 10% or 2 GB free, Failing under 1% or 256 MB); `backups` (Failing when the last Backup failed or none was done for 50 hours, a Warning after 26 hours, with scheduled Backups off, or with none yet); `smtp` (Failing while the Install SMTP is Failing; a Warning while it isn't set, or while Sites' own servers are Failing); `domains` (from the Domain check: Failing when a verified domain is Unreachable for the Install's sake, `unreachable` or `certificate-missing`; a Warning for DNS elsewhere, another answer or a certificate running out); `errorLog` (a Warning while causes first seen in the last day aren't resolved); `moduleLog` (a Warning for Module failures in the last day). One part that can't be checked is Failing (`unchecked`), never the whole check.
- **When**: the worker's `health.check` every minute; the screen when it opens and on Check now; and the web process, every minute, when the stored check is over 2 minutes old (`checkIfStale`: the worker stopped). Each check stores the rows under an advisory lock, so pages read them (the banner reads only them and the worker's check-ins).
- **Jobs**: `installHealth.queues` lists each queue with failed (as long as pg-boss keeps them) or stuck jobs and its newest 20 failed jobs: when, how many attempts, the error's name and message (redacted), never the payload (it can hold an email or a form's answers). `retry` and `discard` take some of a queue's failed jobs (`jobIds`) or all of them (`"all"`), through the job queue port's `retry` and `discard`, audited `job.retry` and `job.discard`; the admin asks first for Discard and Retry all.
- **Health alerts** (`service.ts`): a part Failing for 5 minutes (`ALERT_AFTER_MS`) sends `health-failing` to every Superadmin, once, naming every part due in that check; an alerted part that has worked for 15 minutes (`RECOVERED_AFTER_MS`) sends `health-recovered` with how long it failed. A part that fails for less than 5 minutes sends nothing, however often it flaps; an alerted part that fails again within the 15 minutes continues the same episode, with no new email. Both are sent at once (`now: true`, see "Email") and can't be turned off.
- **The banner**: `shell.context`'s `banners.health` (`{ failing }`, null for people without `installHealth.manage`): the stored Failing parts, with the worker as its check-ins say now. `ui/shell/HealthBanner.tsx` shows it on every page, linking to Install health.
- **Tests** (`tests/health/`): `createTestInstall({ healthChecks: true })` runs the minute check with each drain (off otherwise, so other tests get no Health alerts); a drain checks in as a worker (`drainJobs({ only: [] })` only checks in); `install.disk.set({ freeBytes, totalBytes })` sets the room on the disk.

## Jobs

Jobs are defined with `defineJob({ name, payload, queue, run })` (in `packages/core/src/jobs/`, or next to their module, such as `email/outbox.ts`) and listed in `coreJobs` (`packages/core/src/jobs/index.ts`); the worker only works listed jobs. `sample-ping.ts` is the sample to copy.

- `name` is the queue name, `area.action` (`email.send`). Never rename one that has shipped.
- `payload` is a Zod schema; it is checked when the job is sent and again when it runs.
- `queue` holds retry and expiry settings (`retryLimit`, `retryDelay`, `retryBackoff`, ...).
- `run(payload, services)` gets the same `InstallServices` as the web process: use `services.clock`, `services.http` and `services.log`, never `Date.now()`, `fetch` or `console` directly. To email someone, send a System email (see "Email"); never call `services.mailer`.
- `runJob` runs every job in a log context naming it (`job`, `jobId`) and carrying the request id it was queued with (kept in its data as `_requestId`, never part of the payload). A job that throws is logged as `A job failed` at error level, so it lands in the Error log, then thrown on for the queue's retries: a job handles the failures it expects itself (an SMTP answer, a receiver that is down) and throws only for the unexpected.
- Enqueue with `services.jobs.send(job, payload, { startAfter })`.
- A job that works on one Site's data opens it with `services.data.background(siteId)` (see "The tenant data layer"); `media.process` and `media.variants` are the examples.
- **Scheduled jobs**: `defineSchedule({ job, cron, payload })`, listed in `coreSchedules` (so far the daily `email.purge-log`, `rate-limits.purge`, `modules.purge-log`, `site-files.purge`, `media.purge`, `webhooks.purge-log`, `consent.purge-records`, `modules.daily`, `modules.partitions` (Modules' partitioned tables, 02:17), `visitors.purge-salts` (00:04), `imports.purge` (04:23; besides `imports.run`, `imports.survey` and `imports.preview`, which the web process queues), `backups.scheduled` (01:11, see "Backups"), the hourly `siteTransfers.purge` (:13; besides `siteExports.run` and `siteImports.run`, which the web process queues; see "Site export and import"), `history.prune` (03:47, see "Revisions and Checkpoints"), `audit.prune` (03:27, see "The Audit log"), `error-log.prune` (03:57, see "The Error log") and `updates.check` (05:29, see "The update check"), the hourly `modules.hourly` (Modules' scheduled jobs) and `translations.resume` (:07, see "AI keys and AI translation"), `reports.due` every quarter of an hour and the daily `reports.purge` (03:41, see "Reports and Share links"), `domains.verify-due`, `search.rebuild-due`, `publishing.scheduled-due` (see "Scheduled publish") and `health.check` (see "Install health and Health alerts") every minute, the hourly `domains.check-all`, and the daily `cdn.refresh-ranges`). `cron` is read in UTC (`17 3 * * *` is every day at 03:17). The migrate step makes every job's queue (and the worker makes any still missing), then the worker registers every listed schedule when it starts (and removes ones no longer listed); when it was down at a due time, the job runs once when it is back. The test harness's `drainJobs()` sends each scheduled job whose time came by the test clock since the last drain.

## Email

Everything about email is in `packages/core/src/email/`, behind `services.email`. Nothing else calls the `Mailer`: every email goes through the System email sender, which records it in the Email log and queues it.

**Sending a System email** (an email to a person with an account, or to an address about to get one, such as an invite):

```ts
await services.email.send({
	type: "review-submitted",                       // from the catalogue
	to: [{ personId }, { email: "new@client.example", language: "it" }],
	data: { authorName, items: [{ kind: "entry", title }], siteName, note, reviewPath: `/sites/${siteId}/entries/${postTypeId}/${id}` },
	related: { kind: "entry", id },                  // what the Email log links to
});
// → [{ recipient, status: "queued" | "logged" | "turned-off", emailId }]
```

- **The catalogue** (`email/catalogue.ts`) lists every System email type with its category and a Zod schema of its data. `security` emails (invites, resets, sign-in changes) always go; each `work` and `alert` type can be turned off by each person in Your account › Emails, and is then skipped for them (`turned-off`), except an alert marked `alwaysSent` (the Health alerts, `health-failing` and `health-recovered`).
- **At once**: `services.email.send({ …, now: true })` queues the email as usual, then tries it from this process straight away (the queued attempt then finds it done; a failed try is retried by the worker). Only Health alerts use it: the worker may be what is failing. Add a type to the catalogue, its wording to `email/wording/en.ts` and `it.ts` (a missing one fails the typecheck), and its name (and, for work and alert types, a description) under `emailTypes` in the admin's message files. Never rename a type that shipped.
- **Links** in data are admin paths (`/invites/abc`), never full URLs: the sender puts the admin's origin in front, so System emails only ever link into the admin.
- **Language and layout**: each recipient gets the email in their Admin language (a bare address: the `language` given, else the Install's default), in one built-in layout (`email/layout.ts`: logo or sender name on a bar in the accent colour, heading, paragraphs, one button, footer), under **Install branding** (Install settings › Email (SMTP): sender name, logo address, accent colour, footer text). Every value is escaped. Work and alert emails link to the person's switches.
- **Which SMTP**: each queued email names its SMTP (`SmtpChoice` in `email/outbox.ts`): `{ kind: "install" }`, or `{ kind: "site", siteId }` for a Site's own server. System emails use the Install SMTP. The Install SMTP password is the Install secret `smtp.password`, a Site's the Site secret `smtp.password`, each filled in only for the send; the `email.send` job runs through `services.email.deliver`, which holds the secrets store and the data layer.
- **A Site's mail** (Notifications, Auto-replies, and the emails of Site-level Modules, SDK 1.6) goes out as the Site: `services.email.siteMail.route(scope)` reads the Site's email settings (through its scope) and `siteMail.queue` renders (`renderSiteMail`: a plain, escaped layout without Install branding) and queues one email, recorded in the Email log with its Site (`email_log.site_id`). Through the Site's own server when it has one (its From address, the sender name set, else the Site's name), else the Install SMTP under the Site's name. Its Reply-To is the email's own (a Notification: the visitor) or the Site's reply-to, never the Install SMTP's. A Site that goes back to the Install SMTP while an email waits sends it through the Install SMTP; a Site's server that fails never does.
- **Site SMTP** (`email/site-smtp.ts`, behind `services.email.siteSmtp`; table `site_smtp`; procedures `siteSmtp.get`, `siteSmtp.update`, `siteSmtp.sendTest`, all `structure.edit`; the admin's Site › Email (SMTP)): a Site setting, saved at once and logged `siteSmtp.change` (never the password), a Sensitive action (`siteSmtp.change`). It holds the sender name (null: the Site's name), the reply-to, and the server (host, port, security, username, From address; null: the Install SMTP). Leaving the server out forgets its password; new settings clear its health record. Send test sends at once through the settings in the form (the stored password unless one is typed), or through the Install SMTP under the Site's name when no server is given; it never counts towards Failing. Its health record is `site:<site id>` in `smtp_health`; the third failure in a row (Failing) sends the `site-smtp-failing` alert to the Site's Owners and Staff (`siteOwners`, `siteStaff`), through the Install SMTP, once.
- **No SMTP yet**: until the Superadmin sets the Install SMTP, a System email is written to the log (`warn`, with `to`, `subject` and `text`, printed on purpose: spec story 7) instead of being sent, and the Email log says "Written to the log". A Site's mail written to the log keeps only its subject: it can carry a visitor's form answers (an Auto-reply goes to the address they gave).
- **Delivery and retries**: the `email.send` job sends it. A failed attempt is tried again after 1 minute, then a wait that doubles up to 4 hours, until 24 hours after it was queued; then it is Failed. An address the server refuses for good (a 5xx answer to `RCPT TO`) is Failed at once. Completed `email.send` jobs, which hold the body, are deleted after an hour. Delivery is at least once: if the server takes an email but recording that fails, the job is retried and the email can go out twice. Every attempt carries the same `Message-ID` (`<Email log line id@sender's domain>`), so mail clients can drop the copy; losing an email (a password reset, an invite) would be worse than a rare duplicate.
- **Failing**: 3 failed sends in a row make the SMTP Failing: logged as an error, shown in Install settings › Email (SMTP) and as a notice on every admin page for the Superadmin (an email about a failing Install SMTP would go through it). A send that goes through, or saving new settings, clears it. Test emails don't count.
- **Test email**: `services.email.sendTest` sends at once through settings that may not be saved yet, and answers the server's reply. Without a typed password it uses the stored one, but only with the server it was saved for (the same host, port, security and user name, `sameSmtpServer`): naming another server without its password is refused (`SMTP_PASSWORD_NEEDED`, 400), so nobody who may send a test can have a stored password sent where they choose. The same holds for `siteSmtp.sendTest`.
- **The Email log** keeps time, type, recipient, subject, SMTP (`install`, `site` or `none`), the Site for a Site's mail, status (Queued, Retrying, Sent, Failed, Written to the log), attempts, the server's last answer and the related item, never the body. The `email.purge-log` job deletes lines older than 90 days every day.
- **Attachments**: a queued email may name files to send with it (`attachments`, `EmailAttachmentRef`: whose they are and which), never holding them, so a job never carries a file; `email.send` reads each when it sends (`attachment` in the email's options: a Report's PDF or a Form upload, see "Reports and Share links" and "Form uploads"), and fails an email whose file is gone rather than sending it without.
- **The Mailer** (`email/mailer.ts`) is the edge: `send(email, smtp)` over SMTP with Nodemailer (`email/smtp-mailer.ts`), rejecting with `SmtpDeliveryError` (the server's answer, and whether it refused the recipient for good).
- **Who manages it**: the Install SMTP, Install branding and the Email log procedures (`packages/api/src/procedures/email.ts`) are for the Superadmin, through one gate at the top of that file (`emailAdmin`). Changing the SMTP is a Sensitive action (`installSmtp.change`, see "Sensitive actions") and is recorded in the Audit log, never with the password.

## Secrets and the Master key

Every secret (SMTP passwords, AI keys, CDN tokens, Webhook and CAPTCHA secrets) goes through `services.secrets` (`packages/core/src/secrets/`). Secrets are write-only: code gets a value only inside a callback, and people see only "set" and its last 4 characters.

```ts
const password = { owner: { kind: "install" }, name: "smtp.password" } as const;

await services.secrets.set(password, value);                      // stores or replaces it
await services.secrets.useWith(password, (value) => smtp.login(user, value));
await services.secrets.describe(password);                        // { set: true, lastFour: "…", updatedAt } or { set: false }
await services.secrets.remove(password);
```

- **Owners**: a secret belongs to the Install (`{ kind: "install" }`: Install SMTP, shared AI keys), a Workspace (its AI key) or a Site (its SMTP, CDN token, Webhook and CAPTCHA secrets). `services.secrets` holds the Install's own; a Workspace's or Site's are reached only through the data layer, `workspace.secrets.set("ai.key", value)`, so code can't name another Workspace's secret (see "The tenant data layer"). They have foreign keys to their Workspace and Site, and go when it is deleted. A name is unique within its owner: 1 to 200 letters, digits and `.:_-`, such as `smtp.password` or `webhook:<id>:signing`.
- **No read-back**: there is no function that returns a value. `useWith` refuses a callback that returns the value (or a string holding it), so fill the secret into the call inside the callback and return the call's result. Never log a value, put it in a job payload or send it to the browser. `describe` shows no characters of a secret shorter than 16 characters, so never more than a quarter of one shows.
- **Errors**: `useWith` throws `SecretNotSetError` for a secret nobody set, and `useWith` and `describe` throw `SecretUnreadableError` for one that was changed outside WebCraft. Messages name the secret, never its value.
- **Encryption**: AES-256-GCM, with a random 12-byte nonce per value, under a key derived with HKDF-SHA256 from the Master key and the secret's key version. The authenticated data binds each ciphertext to its owner and name, so a ciphertext copied into another row can't be decrypted there. Only Web Crypto is used, so the same code runs on Bun and Node.
- **Key versions**: the table `master_key_versions` holds the fingerprint of every Master key the Install has had (never a key). The Install's first secret records its key as version 1; each rotation adds the next. Every secret stores the version it is encrypted under.
- **Fingerprint**: `masterKey.fingerprint()` (a `MasterKey` comes from `loadMasterKey(env)` or `parseMasterKey(value, source)`) is a one-way value, 64 hexadecimal characters, safe to store and show. Backup manifests carry it, and restore compares it with the running key (ADR 0032).
- **Start-up**: web and worker read the key with `loadMasterKey`, then open secrets with `openSecrets`, which refuses a key that isn't the Install's current one. An Install with no secrets yet accepts any key.

### Rotating the Master key

`webcraft admin rotate-key` decrypts every secret with the old key and encrypts it again under the new key as the next key version, in one transaction. If any secret can't be decrypted, nothing changes and the command lists it. The new key goes where web and worker will read it from afterwards, `WEBCRAFT_SECRET_KEY`; the key being replaced is passed once, in `WEBCRAFT_OLD_SECRET_KEY` or a file.

1. Make the new key: `openssl rand -base64 32`. Store a copy outside the server.
2. Stop web and worker.
3. Put the new key in `WEBCRAFT_SECRET_KEY` in the Install's environment.
4. Run the command in that environment, with the old key:

   ```sh
   WEBCRAFT_OLD_SECRET_KEY='<old key>' webcraft admin rotate-key
   # or: webcraft admin rotate-key --old-key-file /run/secrets/old-master-key
   ```

   It prints how many secrets it re-encrypted, the new key version and the new key's fingerprint.
5. Start web and worker. Then remove the old key from wherever you passed it; it opens nothing any more.

Running the command again after it finished changes nothing. It refuses a new key the Install used before, and an old key that isn't the current one. Web or worker left running with the old key can no longer read or write secrets, and both refuse to start again until they have the new key, naming the key version they need.

### When the Master key is lost

Secrets can't be recovered without their Master key, and web and worker refuse to start with any other key while secrets exist. To start again with a new key, deleting every secret:

1. Make a new key with `openssl rand -base64 32` and put it in `WEBCRAFT_SECRET_KEY`.
2. `webcraft admin rotate-key --old-key-lost` lists the secrets that would be deleted and the lost key's version and fingerprint, and changes nothing. If you still have the old key, or mistyped the new one, stop here and rotate normally.
3. `webcraft admin rotate-key --old-key-lost --confirm-fingerprint <first 12 characters of that fingerprint>` deletes them and makes the new key the next key version. Typing the fingerprint proves you read the listing for this Install; a bare "yes" would not. It refuses when `WEBCRAFT_SECRET_KEY` is still the current key.
4. Start web and worker, and set the listed secrets again.

## Outgoing calls and the guarded fetcher

`services.http` (`OutgoingHttp`) is WebCraft's one way to other servers: `(Request, { connectTo? }) => Promise<Response>`, faked in tests. Calls to hosts someone else chose (a Module's outside hosts, Domain verification and the Domain check, Library downloads, Webhooks, Import from URL, the update check's release feed, AI providers; later CAPTCHA verification) go through the **guarded fetcher** on top of it (`createGuardedFetch({ http, dns })`, `packages/core/src/guarded-fetch.ts`):

- only `http` and `https` URLs without credentials, on the hosts allowed (`allowedHosts`, or `"any"` for any host on the public internet: Import from URL, where a person gives the address);
- the host is resolved first through the `Dns` port (`services.dns`; `systemDns` in the processes, `install.dns` in tests) and refused when any address is loopback, private, link-local, shared (CGNAT), multicast, reserved, documentation or benchmarking space (`isPublicAddress`, IPv4 and IPv6, IPv4 inside IPv6 and NAT64 included); the same after every redirect;
- the call is **pinned to the address checked**: outgoing HTTP gets the first address the host resolved to as `connectTo` and connects there, never resolving the name again, so a DNS server that answers the check with a public address and the connection with an internal one (DNS rebinding) can't reach inside the network; each redirect is checked and pinned again;
- at most 3 redirects (`maxRedirects: 0` hands a redirect back without following it); 303, and 301/302 after a POST, become a GET;
- a timeout (10 s by default, 30 s at most) and a size limit (5 MB, 20 MB at most): the body is read in full, and a larger one is refused. Only the fetcher the Media library builds raises the size ceiling (`ceilings`), to the largest file an Install may take (1 GB), for Import from URL;
- the answer's `url` is the address it finally came from, as `fetch` gives it.

`installAddresses` lets one caller, the Install's checks of its own domains, also call the Install's own addresses although they aren't public (see "Domains"); nothing else passes it. `privateNetwork` lets one caller, a shared Install key's self-hosted endpoint while the Superadmin allows it, call inside the network (see "AI keys and AI translation"). Refusals throw `GuardedFetchError` with `problem` (`scheme`, `host`, `address`, `redirects`, `timeout`, `size`, `unreachable`).

Each process has its own outgoing HTTP, wired in `openInstall` (`openInstallForCommand` uses the web one):

- **Web (Bun)**: `createBunHttp()` (`apps/server/src/outgoing-http.ts`; it uses Bun's own `fetch` TLS options, so it lives with the web process, not in `packages/core`, which keeps to standard APIs, ADR 0006). A pinned call is Bun's `fetch` to the address as the URL's host, with the URL's host name in the `Host` header and as `tls.serverName`, which Bun sends for SNI and checks the certificate against. WebCraft has no outgoing proxy in v1: Bun's `fetch` would honour `HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY` while Node's never does, so `createBunHttp()` sets those variables empty for the process (Bun reads them again when they are set, but not when they are deleted), and both processes call servers directly (`tests/outgoing-http/no-proxy.test.ts`).
- **Server commands (`webcraft admin …`)** run on Bun: the ones that make calls (`check-update` through the guarded fetcher, `backup` to a bucket) hand `createBunHttp()` to `openInstallForCommand({ http })` or `openBackupForCommand`; the others' outgoing HTTP refuses a pinned call.
- **Worker (Node)**: `createNodeHttp()` (`packages/core/src/http-node.ts`, standard Node APIs only). A pinned call goes over `node:http`/`node:https` with `host` set to the address and the host name as `servername`, which Node sends for SNI and checks the certificate against (a custom `checkServerIdentity` would stop Node reusing connections); the body streams as it arrives, decoded (gzip, deflate, br) as `fetch` decodes it.

On both, the certificate must be valid for the host name, never for the address (an address in the URL is checked as an address); a kept connection is reused only for the same address and host name; a pinned call never follows a redirect itself (the guarded fetcher does, checking each hop), and its timeout and size limit are the guarded fetcher's (the call stops when its signal fires; the body is read as it streams). A call without `connectTo` (S3 storage, the leaked-password check, the sample job) is the runtime's own `fetch`, as before. Each process hands its adapter to `openInstall` (`http`); each adapter refuses to start on the other runtime. Their `ca` option (`createBunHttp({ ca })`, `createNodeHttp({ ca })`) makes pinned calls trust a certificate authority a test made instead of the usual ones.

The guarded fetcher connects to the first address only; when it doesn't answer, the call is `unreachable` even if another address of the host would have. In development the Domain check connects to the first address a `.localhost` name resolves to: `::1` on macOS, where `vite dev` (listening on `localhost`) is too. A system that answers `127.0.0.1` first for `.localhost` names but binds `localhost` to `::1` would see the development domain as unreachable.

## Webhooks
