A Site's Webhooks (spec story 139; `packages/core/src/webhooks/`, behind `services.webhooks`; tables `webhooks`, `webhook_deliveries`, `webhook_delivery_attempts`; procedures `webhooks.*`; the admin's Site › Webhooks) are Site settings: everyone who sees the Site reads them (`webhooks.list`, `webhooks.deliveries`, `site.view`), Staff change them (`structure.edit`), each change applies at once and is logged, and none is part of Publish all. How receivers check a delivery is in `docs/webhooks.md`.

- **A Webhook** is a name (its URL's host unless given), an `https` URL, the events it receives (`WEBHOOK_EVENTS`, `webhooks/events.ts`) and on or off; at most 20 per Site (`TOO_MANY_WEBHOOKS`). `webhookUrl` (`webhooks/rules.ts`) refuses at save time what could never be called (`WEBHOOK_INVALID` with `field: "url"` and `reason`): not `https` (`scheme`), credentials in it (`credentials`), `localhost`, a name without a dot, `.local`, `.internal`, `.home.arpa` or an IP address inside a network (`host`); it drops the fragment. Whether the host resolves to a public address is the guarded fetcher's to check on every call, since DNS changes.
- **The signing secret** is a Site secret, `webhook:<id>:signing` (see "Secrets and the Master key"): Staff give one (16 to 200 visible characters; `WEBHOOK_INVALID` with `field: "secret"`), or WebCraft makes one (32 random bytes, base64url) and answers it once, from `webhooks.add` and `webhooks.changeSecret`, never again; the list shows only "set" and its last 4 characters, and only to `structure.edit`. People without `structure.edit` see each URL's origin only, since a URL can carry a token in its path or query; the Audit log records hosts, never paths.
- **Sensitive action** (`webhook.change`): adding a Webhook (`webhooks.add`) and changing its name, URL and events (`webhooks.update`) or its secret (`webhooks.changeSecret`), since it sends the Site's data to an outside address, signed with a secret; API tokens and AI clients can't open such a channel. Switching on and off (`webhooks.setEnabled`), removing (`webhooks.remove`, two steps: the first answers `preview: { name, url, deliveries }`), Send test and Redeliver only use what was confirmed. Logged as `webhook.add`, `webhook.update` (with `changed`), `webhook.changeSecret`, `webhook.switchOn`, `webhook.switchOff`, `webhook.remove`.
- **Events** (`publish`, `unpublish`, `formSubmitted`): `services.webhooks.queueEvent({ siteId }, event, data, at)` queues an event once the change it is about is saved, never inside its transaction: one delivery per Webhook of the Site that is on and receives it, each recorded with its body, and one `webhooks.deliver` job. The `publish` event comes from the Audit log: `afterAudit` turns every `<item>.publish` entry (`entry.publish`, `template.publish`, `form.publish`, `loop.publish`, …; the item's id in `<item>Id`) into it (`publishedBy`), so every way something is published, and every kind of item publishing gains, fires it, and a publish never waits for a receiver or fails because of one. `unpublish` comes the same way from `entry.unpublish` (`unpublishedBy`): the Entry (`kind`, `id`, `postTypeId`), its `paths`, `by` and `via`. `formSubmitted` is queued once a Submission is kept (never spam; also when one comes out of the Spam folder), with the Form and the Submission's values (the spec sends a Submission's values, story 99), related to the Submission (`webhook_deliveries.related_kind` and `related_id`), so the Submission's own delivery log lists it.
- **The body** is small JSON, the same on every attempt: `{ id, type, occurredAt, site: { id, name, url }, data }`, where `id` is the delivery id and `site.url` where visitors find the Site (`siteAddresses().main`). `publish`'s `data` is `{ item: { kind, id, postTypeId?, path? }, pathChanges, by: { id, name } | null, via }`: what was published, never a secret nor Content values (no titles). A test send's `data` is `{ by, via }`.
- **Sending** (`webhooks/deliveries.ts`): the worker signs each attempt (`webhooks/signing.ts`: `WebCraft-Signature: t=<unix seconds>,v1=<hex HMAC-SHA256 of "<t>.<body>">`, with `WebCraft-Delivery` and `WebCraft-Event`) and posts it through the guarded fetcher: only to the Webhook's host, pinned to the address it checked, no redirect followed (the call carries a signature), 10 seconds, a 1 MB answer. The signing secret is read only inside `useWith`, from the store the data layer uses, for the Site the job names (a background scope cannot use secret values).
- **Retries**: no answer, a timeout, a host that doesn't resolve to a public address, 408, 429 and 5xx are tried again after 1, 4, 16, 64 and 256 minutes (fourfold from a minute), or as long as `Retry-After` asks when that is longer, up to 6 hours; 6 attempts in all, then the delivery is failed. A redirect, another 4xx or an answer over 1 MB fails it at once. Every attempt carries the same delivery id. Statuses: `queued`, `retrying`, `delivered`, `failed`.
- **The delivery log** (`webhooks.deliveries`, newest first, `limit` and `before` to page) keeps each delivery's body and every attempt: when, how long, the receiver's status, the first 500 characters of its answer, or what went wrong (`problem`: `status`, `redirect`, `timeout`, `unreachable`, `address`, `size`, `secret`; never an address it resolved to). `webhooks.purge-log` deletes deliveries older than 30 days, daily. **Send test** (`webhooks.test`) sends a signed `test` event at once from the web process, on or off, and answers what the receiver said; it is never retried and never counts as a failure. **Redeliver** (`webhooks.redeliver`) queues a failed delivery again with the same id and body for a new round of 6 attempts (numbered on), while the Webhook is on (`WEBHOOK_REDELIVERY_REFUSED`: `not-failed`, `test`, `switched-off`).
- **Switched off**: a Webhook switched off receives nothing, and its deliveries still waiting stop (failed, `lastProblem: "switched-off"`; redeliver them once it is on). After 10 deliveries in a row failed (`SWITCH_OFF_AFTER`; test sends don't count, a delivered one starts the count again), WebCraft switches it off (`offReason: "failing"`) and sends the Site's Owners (`siteOwners`, `tenancy/site-owners.ts`, as the Domain check does) the `webhook-switched-off` alert email once. Switching it on starts the count over.
- **Tests** (`tests/webhooks/`) answer receivers on the fake outgoing HTTP and check signatures as a receiver would (`receiverAccepts` in `tests/webhooks/support.ts`); `confirmed(browser, account, call)` there confirms who Staff are again when a test moved the clock past their identity check.

## Modules

A Module is a self-contained feature that reaches WebCraft only through the Module SDK (ADRs 0012, 0024). v1 loads first-party Modules only, in the same process as Core; the boundary is built so that a sandbox later changes the runtime, not the API.

### The SDK and the boundary

`@webcraft/module-sdk` (`packages/module-sdk`) is the public, versioned contract (`SDK_VERSION`, the same as its package version; additions raise the minor version, a change that could break a Module the major one). It holds the manifest schema, `defineModule`, `procedure`, `siteRoute`, `job`, `component`, the table helpers, the event catalogue, the context types Module code gets, `ModuleError` (with `isModuleError`, which knows one by its name, code and status, as a Module may run against another copy of the SDK) and `ModuleRefusedError`, address ranges (`isAddressRange`, `addressInRanges`), and re-exports what Modules need of Drizzle (column builders, comparison operators and the aggregates `count`, `countDistinct`, `sum`, `min`, `max`, never raw `sql`) and Zod, so every Module uses WebCraft's versions.

- **A Module imports nothing from WebCraft but the SDK**, and nothing that reaches around it: no `@webcraft/*` package, no Drizzle, Zod, `pg` or `pg-boss` of its own, no `node:` or `bun` modules, no `fetch`, `process`, `Bun`, `require` or `globalThis` globals. Biome refuses them in `modules/**` and `tests/modules/sample/**`; `tests/modules/boundary.test.ts` scans every Module's imports, globals and `package.json` too. A real Module package depends only on `@webcraft/module-sdk` (and its own outside packages), and Bun's isolated installs then can't resolve anything else of WebCraft's.
- **First-party Modules** are workspace packages, `modules/<id>`, each default-exporting a `defineModule(…)`, listed in `FIRST_PARTY_MODULES` (`packages/core/src/modules/first-party.ts`). Web, worker and the `migrate` step all load that list (`openInstall({ modules })` defaults to it).
- **The sample Module** (`tests/modules/sample/index.ts`, with the Workspace-level `board.ts`) uses every extension point up to SDK 1.3 and is the Module the host's tests drive; copy its shape. SDK 1.4's (what Site routes learn of the visitor, route limits, Site scripts before consent, monthly partitions, `aggregate`, `form.submitted`) are used by Site analytics (`modules/site-analytics`), whose tests drive them, with the definition rules in `tests/modules/sdk-rules.test.ts`. The extension points one Module of a build keeps (Form Submissions, Redirect rules and the 404 log) are shown by the Forms and Redirects Modules instead (SDK 1.5's are the Redirects Module's, SDK 1.6's the Forms Module's; the sample checks that `context.submissions` is refused to it). SDK 1.7's import sources are the WordPress import's (`modules/wordpress-import`), and its rules for Imports the Redirects Module's. SDK 1.8's (Widgets, `widgetGoals`, `context.data.values`, the sender's daily id on `form.submitted`, and `formSenderCounted`, the one rule for whether a Form Submission's sender is counted by analytics, which core and Site analytics both use) are Site analytics' again, with Widgets' definition rules in `tests/modules/sdk-rules.test.ts`.

### The manifest

```ts
export default defineModule({
	manifest: {
		id: "sample",                                    // lower-case words joined by dashes
		name: { en: "Sample notes", it: "Note di esempio" },
		description: { en: "…", it: "…" },
		version: "1.0.0",
		sdk: "^1.0.0",                                   // the SDK versions it works with
		level: "site",                                   // switched per Site, or "workspace"
		onByDefault: true,                               // on wherever Staff haven't switched it off
		adminLanguages: ["en", "it"],                    // every label in each; the first is the fallback
		permissions: [{ key: "notes.edit", label: { en: "Write notes", it: "…" }, roles: ["staff", "owner", "editor"] }],
		uses: ["email"],                                 // WebCraft services it uses
		outsideHosts: ["hooks.sample.example"],          // the only hosts it may call
		routePrefix: "sample",                           // its Site routes: /_wc/m/sample/…
		siteScript: null,                                // or the Consent category of the script it adds
	},
	// tables, migrations, procedures, siteRoutes, adminPages, events, jobs, components
});
```

The manifest is checked with Zod (`moduleManifestSchema`), and the whole definition against the SDK's rules, when the Install loads it (`loadModules` in `packages/core/src/modules/registry.ts`): the SDK range must include `SDK_VERSION`; ids and route prefixes are unique; labels come in exactly the declared languages; a procedure or admin page names a Permission the Module adds or one of WebCraft's checked at its level; tables are in the Module's own schema and follow the tenant rule; migrations sort oldest first; Site routes need a route prefix and a Site-level Module; events are in the catalogue; unknown extension points are refused. A Module that breaks a rule is listed on the Modules screen as `cannot-run`, with the reason, and never runs; the rest of the Install runs on.

### Approval (the consent screen)

Nothing a Module asks for is granted until the Superadmin approves it on Install settings › Modules (`modules.list`, `modules.approve`; Permission `module.approve`, held by the Superadmin only). Approving is a Sensitive action (`module.approve`) and is recorded in the Audit log with what it granted.

- **What a Module asks for** is a list of consent items (`consentItems`): `permission:<key>:<role>` for each role it gives one of its Permissions, `use:email`, `host:<host>`, `script:<category>`. The consent screen shows them in WebCraft's own words (the admin's message files, English and Italian), never the Module's, so a Module can't word what it asks for itself; only its Permissions' labels are its own.
- **An approval** (`module_approvals`) stores the version approved and the items granted. A later version that asks for nothing more runs on it; one that asks for more is `update-waiting`: off everywhere (its switches and data kept) until the Superadmin approves it, the new items marked. Only one version of a first-party Module's code exists in a build, so "held at the approved version" means off until approved.
- `modules.approve` names the version the screen showed: another version answers `MODULE_CHANGED`. `modules.withdraw` takes a Module back to waiting, off everywhere, data kept.
- **The setup wizard** ends with the Modules step: the Modules waiting for approval, ticked (all Modules WebCraft loads are first-party), each with what it asks for.

### Switches and removing data

Staff switch Site-level Modules on each Site and Workspace-level Modules on each Workspace (`modules.siteList`, `modules.setOnSite`, `modules.workspaceList`, `modules.setOnWorkspace`; Permission `module.manage`, held by Staff and the Superadmin). The switch is a row of `module_switches` (a Workspace table); without one, the Module's `onByDefault` applies, to later Sites too. A Module **runs** at a place when it is approved for this version and switched on there; only approved Modules can be switched on. Switching off keeps the Module's data.

**Removing a Module's data** (`modules.removeSiteData`, `modules.removeWorkspaceData`) takes two calls, through `askConfirmation`/`useConfirmation`: the first answers how many rows of each of its tables and how many of its secrets would go, and a confirmation id; the second deletes them (tables in reverse order of the definition's list, so list parents first), removes its secrets there and switches it off. It is logged as `module.removeData`.

### Extension points

- **Tables and migrations.** A Module's tables live in its own Postgres schema, `m_<id>` (dashes become underscores), declared with `moduleSchema(id).table(name, { ...siteColumns(), … })` (or `workspaceColumns()`). A Site table has `workspace_id` and `site_id` with `FOREIGN KEY (workspace_id, site_id) REFERENCES sites (workspace_id, id) ON DELETE CASCADE`; a Workspace table `workspace_id` with `REFERENCES workspaces (id) ON DELETE CASCADE`. Site-level Modules have Site tables only. Migrations are SQL in the definition (`{ name, sql }`, names sorting oldest first), run once each by the `migrate` step after WebCraft's own, with the Module's schema first on the search path. The step refuses, rolling everything back: a migration that changes anything outside the Module's schema (compared before and after through Postgres's catalog), changes or reads rows outside it (the transaction's own table statistics and locks, before and after; reading `sites` and `workspaces` and locking them, which foreign keys need, is allowed, changing them isn't), a table or view the Module doesn't declare, a table without its tenant key, a declared table or column the migrations never made (checked on every run, so the TypeScript and the SQL can't drift), and a migration that changed or disappeared after it was applied (by checksum). Never change a shipped migration: add one. The schema check counts pending Module migrations, so web and worker refuse to start until `migrate` ran them.
- **API procedures**, by name and in groups, become `m.<module id>.<name>` in the Management API (all of them public, at `/api/v1/m/<module id>/<name>`; `kind: "read"` (SDK 1.6) marks one that changes nothing, which read-only API tokens may call and which doesn't count as a write) (`adminRouter(services)` in `packages/api/src/module-router.ts`, built from the Install's Modules, served at `/api/rpc` and to the admin's own pages). Each takes `siteId` (Site-level) or `workspaceId` besides its own input, runs only where the Module runs (`MODULE_NOT_FOUND` otherwise), through `can()` with the Permission it names (`FORBIDDEN` with `data.permission`). A `ModuleError` it throws is its answer (`MODULE_ERROR` with its status and `data.code`); anything else is a failure: it goes to the Module log and the API answers `MODULE_FAILED` (500).
- **Permissions** a Module adds are named under its id (`sample.notes.edit`), checked at its level, and held by the Superadmin and the built-in roles it lists (ADR 0028).
- **Site routes** answer `/_wc/m/<route prefix>/<path>` on Sites where the Module runs: declared paths (`/notes`, `/notes/:id`) and methods only (405 with `Allow` for another method on a declared path, HEAD for GET routes), never cached as pages (`Cache-Control: no-store` unless the route sets its own), `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` on Staging addresses like every Site answer. A route never sees WebCraft's own cookies (`webcraft.*`, `isWebCraftCookie` in `packages/core/src/cookies.ts`) and can't set one: name every cookie WebCraft sets on a Site's domain (Edit mode's) `webcraft.<what>`. A route that throws answers the plain 500 and goes to the Module log.
- **Admin pages** (`adminPages: [{ path, label, permission }]`) join the sidebar of the Sites (or Workspaces) where the Module runs, for people holding their Permission (`sites.get` and `workspaces.get` answer them as `modulePages`), at `/sites/<id>/m/<module id>/<path>`. Their screens are registered in the admin's `MODULE_PAGES` (`apps/server/src/ui/modules/pages.tsx`) by the first-party Module that ships one; without one the page says so.
- **Events.** Modules react afterwards, as background jobs (ADR 0024): an Audit log entry the catalogue maps (`entry.publish` → `entry.published` with the Entry's `path` and the `pathChanges` the publish made, `entry.delete` → `entry.deleted`, `template.publish`, `globals.publish`, and, SDK 1.5, `postType.update` and `defaultLanguage.change` → `paths.changed` with `reason` (`urlPattern` or `defaultLanguage`) and their `pathChanges`, when any address moved) queues a `modules.event` job for each Module with a handler, once the change commits. The job runs only if the Module still runs there. Saving and publishing never wait for a Module and never fail because of one.
- **Jobs** of the Module's own (`jobs: { remind: job({ payload, run }) }`), queued with `context.jobs.send(name, payload, { delaySeconds })`, run as `modules.job` in the place they were queued from; payloads are JSON, checked when sent and when run. A job queued inside `context.transaction` is sent once it commits, never after a rollback.
- **Components** (SDK 1.1): `components: [component({ name, label, props, markup })]`, declarative: Props declared as Fields are (labels and help in each admin language; no Relationships, since each Site has its own Post Types) and output in Template markup reading only `props.x` (no Globals, Classes, inline styles or Components, which differ on every Site). Site-level Modules only. The Install checks them when it loads the Module (names unique across Modules); they get the id `<module id>:<Name>`, never change on a Site, and are placed like the Site's own Components, only on Sites where the Module runs (`services.modules.components`, the `ComponentSources` the content store reads). Approving, withdrawing or switching a Module that adds Components clears the page caches it changes; its Placements show nothing where it doesn't run. The consent screen lists them under what it adds. Never rename one that shipped.
- **Scheduled jobs** (SDK 1.2): `schedules: [{ job: "purgeSpam", every: "day" }]` (or `"hour"`) queues the Module's job, with an empty payload, once in every place where the Module runs, from the core jobs `modules.daily` and `modules.hourly` (`services.modules.runSchedules`). The job must take `{}`.
- **Form Submissions** (SDK 1.2): `formSubmissions: submissionReceiver({ receive(submission, context) })` makes a Site-level Module the one that keeps the Sites' Form Submissions (at most one Module of a build; a second is `cannot-run`). WebCraft checks a post itself, then hands the `FormSubmission` (Form, language, page, spam reason or null, each field's key, label, type and value) to it inline, before the visitor gets an answer, so nothing is lost; a receiver that throws fails the post and goes to the Module log. Forms render only where such a Module runs (`services.modules.submissionsKeptBy(site)`), and approving, withdrawing or switching it clears the page caches it changes. The consent screen says the Module keeps Form Submissions and what it runs on a schedule. Module form actions (run inline once a Submission is kept) are a seam in the Forms Module's receiver.
- **Site scripts** (SDK 1.3): `siteScript: { code }` adds a script to Site pages where a Site-level Module runs, at most `MODULE_SITE_SCRIPT_MAX_LENGTH` characters; the manifest's `siteScript` names its Consent category (a definition with code and no category is `cannot-run`). The Site serves it as `/_wc/js/<hash>.js` (`services.modules.siteScriptsAt`), pages load it with `defer` after the Site's own scripts, `data-wc-module="<id>"`, held back by the Consent banner until its category is accepted, and never in Edit mode (Modules' code, like outside scripts, isn't Staff's). Switching or approving such a Module clears the page caches it changes.
- **What a Site route learns of the visitor** (SDK 1.4): `context.visitor`, built by the Module host (`modules/visitors.ts`) before WebCraft's cookies are taken off the request, so a Module never sees the address or those cookies: `via` (`staging` or `domain`), `signedIn()` (an edit cookie that holds, or an unexpired admin session from the same client address: judged from what WebCraft keeps, never from what the browser says), `country()` (the countries port), `consent()` (`asks`: `banner`, `none` or `outside`, and `has(category)`, read from the `webcraft.consent` cookie by the Site's mode: Opt-in accepts nothing until answered, Opt-out everything until declined, None everything, and with an outside service WebCraft can't tell), `inRanges(ranges)`, and `dailyId(category, { acceptedInPage })`: an HMAC-SHA256 of the day, the day's salt, the Site, the address and the user agent, under a key derived from the Master key (`visitorIdKey`), only once the visitor accepted the category (with an outside consent service, as the page said). The day's salt (`visitor_salts`) is random, made by the first process that needs it, and deleted the next day (when a new day's salt is made, and by `visitors.purge-salts`), so nobody can tell afterwards whose an id was.
- **Site route limits** (SDK 1.4): `siteRoute({ …, limit: { requests, perSeconds }, maxBodyBytes })`: the host answers 429 with `Retry-After` past the limit, per client address and Site (counted through `rateLimits.count`), and 413 for a bigger body (by its `Content-Length`, then by what comes), before the route runs.
- **Site scripts before consent** (SDK 1.4): `siteScript: { code, beforeConsent: true }` isn't held back by the Consent banner; the page still asks for its category, and the script holds back itself what needs it. The Superadmin approves it as its own consent item (`script:<category>:before-consent`), which the consent screen words as such. A Necessary script can't say it (`cannot-run`).
- **Monthly partitions** (SDK 1.4): `partitions: [{ table, column, label, keepMonths }]` keeps a Module table in monthly partitions by a `timestamp with time zone` column. Its migrations make it `PARTITION BY RANGE (<column>)` with the column in its primary key (the `migrate` step refuses it otherwise, and skips its partitions when it checks the Module's tables); WebCraft makes the partitions (`<table>_<yyyy>_<mm>`, in the Module's schema): the current month and the next two every day (`modules.partitions`), and the month a new row needs at once (`partitions.ts`); and drops a month once all of it is older than the Install keeps it, writing both to the Module log. How many months is an Install setting per table (`module_partition_settings`; the Module's `keepMonths` until changed): `modules.list` answers `partitions`, `modules.setKeepMonths` changes it (`module.approve`, logged `module.changeKeep`), on Install settings › Modules.
- **Counting in Postgres** (SDK 1.4): `context.data.aggregate(table, { where, groupBy, values, orderBy, limit })`, values built from the SDK's aggregates, groups from the table's own columns, limited to the place like every query.
- **SDK 1.6** (the Forms Module's): the `FormSubmission` handed to the receiver carries its `sender` (`FormSender`), which the Module keeps; `context.submissions` (only for the Module keeping Submissions, at a Site; `ModuleRefusedError` with `submissions` otherwise) has `kept(submission)`, which sets off what follows a kept Submission (for one taken out of spam) and `deliveries(id)`, its delivery log; procedures get `context.confirmations` (`ask`, `use`: two-step confirmation, see "Two-step confirmation"; a wrong id answers `CONFIRMATION_INVALID`); selects take an `offset`; and a Site-level Module's `context.email.send` is the Site's mail (through the Site SMTP when it has one, else the Install SMTP under the Site's name; see "Email").
- **`form.submitted`** (SDK 1.4): once a Submission is kept (never spam), Modules reacting to it get the Form's id and key, the page and the language, as a background job, nothing the visitor typed; and `sender` (`FormSender`), what WebCraft knew of the visitor then, worked out from the post (`formSenderOf`, `modules/visitors.ts`), since the request is gone by the time the event runs: `via` (Staging address or domain), `signedIn`, `doNotTrack` (Do Not Track or Global Privacy Control) and `consent` (`asks`, and the categories `accepted`; only `necessary` where an outside consent service asks). Never the address.
- **Redirect rules** (SDK 1.5): `redirectRules: redirectRules({ tables, find(request, context) })` makes a Site-level Module the one that keeps the Sites' Redirect rules (at most one Module of a build; a second is `cannot-run`, as is one listing a table that isn't its own). ADR 0024 lets Modules act inline only for form actions and their own routes; a Redirect rule must answer before Entry routing, so this is a third inline place, kept as narrow as the Submission receiver: the Site pipeline asks `find` with a **Site path** (the path as the Staging address writes it, every language but the Default language under its prefix: `fr.example.com/x` asks `/fr/x`) and its language, and gets `{ to, status: 301 | 302, whereNoPage? }` or null. WebCraft turns `to` (a Site path, or an `https://` address; anything else is left out and logged) into the address it has on the visitor's hostname, follows a Site path through the rules again (at most `MAX_REDIRECT_HOPS`, 5, answered in one hop; a loop is never followed: the Site's page answers and the Module log says so once), and skips a `whereNoPage` rule where a page answers. A `find` that throws or takes longer than `FIND_TIMEOUT_MS` (500 ms) is "no rule", in the Module log. Answers are kept per web process with the Site's page cache generation, so each path is asked once until the Site's pages change: any `insert`, `update` or `delete` of the Module on one of `tables` clears the Site's page cache in the same transaction (`moduleData`), and approving, withdrawing or switching the Module clears the page caches it changes. The consent screen says the Module keeps Redirect rules.
- **The 404 log** (SDK 1.5): `notFoundLog: notFoundLog({ record(misses, context) })` makes a Site-level Module the one the Site pipeline hands its misses (at most one of a build). The web process gathers them in memory (`services.modules.noteMiss`, one entry per Site path, language and referring host, with hits and first and last times; at most 5,000 held) and hands them over every 5 seconds (a timer in `web-services.ts`), at once past 500 (`MISS_BATCH_SIZE`), as `modules.notFound` jobs, one per Site where the Module runs (`flushMisses`); `record` runs in the worker like any Module job. A miss on its way when a web process stops is lost: the log is a guide. The consent screen says the Module keeps the 404 log.
- **Import sources** (SDK 1.7): `importSources: [importSource({ key, label, description, permission, file: { maxBytes, extensions }, read(file, context) })]` (Site-level Modules only) adds a source to the Import pipeline, `<module id>.<key>`, offered on the Source step to people holding `import.run` and the source's `permission`, where the Module runs. The Module only reads its file: `read` gets a `SourceFile` whose `text()` streams the file as UTF-8 from the start (as often as it is called: a long run reads it again in each job) and yields `SourceItem`s in the order they are to be written: a `type` before its first item, `entry` items (a Source id, title, slug, parent, old path and values by key, each `text`, `html`, `date`, `file` or `items`, and what it knows of the files its HTML names) and `media` items (a file to fetch with its texts). A `ModuleError` it throws refuses the file. WebCraft does the rest (see "Imports"). The consent screen says what the Module imports from.
- **Redirect rules for Imports** (SDK 1.7): the Module keeping Redirect rules may add `add(rules, context)` and `remove(rules, context)` (both or neither) to its `redirectRules`, for the rules an Import run makes and Undo removes; a rule's target may also be `media:<id>` (see "Redirects and the 404 log").
- **Widgets** (SDK 1.8): `widgets: [widget({ kind, label, description, shape, filters, settings, query })]` adds kinds to the catalog Dashboards are built from (Site-level Modules only: a Widget counts one Site at a time; WebCraft adds Sites up for Workspace Dashboards). `shape` is one the admin draws with its own pieces: `number` (named counts, a rate as `count` and `of`), `series` (lines of counts by hour or day), `table` (columns of a kind: `text`, `path`, `source`, `country`, `device`, `count`, `rate`; rows keyed across Sites) or `funnel` (steps' counts). `filters` lists those it takes (`page`, `source`, `country`, `device`, `goal`; `goal` needs `widgetGoals(context)`, the Site's Goals' names), `settings` its choices (fixed options, or options worked out per Site; values naming things Staff made by name, so templates carry them). `query(request, context)` gets the range, interval, filters, settings and row limit and answers counts only, never ratios, plus `countedFrom` when its counts start later than the range; it runs as the Module acting by itself on a Site where it runs, and a `ModuleError` it throws is shown in the Widget. The registry checks kinds (named once, lower-case words), labels in every language, shapes, filters and settings (defaults it offers, none for options worked out per Site).
- **SDK 1.8 also**: `context.data.values(table, column, where)`, a subquery of one column's values in the place, for `inArray` (Postgres reads the ids, they never travel to the Module); and `form.submitted`'s `visitor`, the sender's daily id when they accepted Analytics on the Site's banner and analytics may count them (null otherwise, and for a Submission taken out of spam later), never kept with the Submission.
- **SDK 1.9**: a procedure names the Toolset of WebCraft's it belongs to for API tokens and AI clients (`toolset: "analytics"`, one of `TOOLSETS`; see "The MCP server and AI clients"), else it is in the Module's own Toolset (`m.<module id>`), which people switch on by the Module's name. Procedures reading what visitors sent belong in `submissions`, off unless chosen. The Forms Module's Submissions procedures are in `submissions` and its retention in `forms`, Site analytics' in `analytics`, the Redirects Module's in `content`.
- **SDK 1.10**: the Module keeping Redirect rules may add `findMany(requests, context)` to its `redirectRules`: the rules for many Site paths at once, in order, in one or two reads, as `find` would answer each. Lists of a Site's pages (its sitemaps, `llms.txt`) ask it a batch at a time through `services.modules.pathsWithRules` (`FIND_MANY_TIMEOUT_MS`, 5 s; `find` for each path without it) and follow each path a rule answers with `redirectFor`, so listing a big Site never asks per page. The Redirects Module answers it.
- **Declarative Block types** (SDK 1.12): `blockTypes: [blockType({ name, label, props, markup })]` reuses Component Fields, markup and the lossless Component Block representation. Staff allow them per Post Type through its Body field’s `components` list. `siteScript.whenUsed` loads the Module script only after one of its declarative types actually renders. See [Extra blocks](extra-blocks.md) for translation, Markdown, accessibility, Consent and Site transfer.
- **Live Import sources** (SDK 1.13): an Import source can declare `live: { test, read }` instead of `file` and `read`. Its `LiveConnection` supplies the chosen visibility and a GET port bound to the source's REST root; Core keeps the saved login. The manifest must request `importSourceHttp`, requiring fresh Superadmin consent when added. See "WordPress from a live site" above for capture, retention and transfer rules.
- **Reserved**: `structurePresets` stays unavailable until its SDK version.

### What Module code gets

Procedures, Site routes, events and jobs get a `ModuleContext` (`packages/core/src/modules/runtime.ts`), limited to one place and to what the manifest declares; anything else throws `ModuleRefusedError` with a `reason` (`permission`, `table`, `host`, `use`, `job`, `secret`, `email`):

- `data`: its own tables in the place, through the data layer (`select`, `first`, `count`, `insert`, `update`, `delete`, `aggregate`, and `values` for a subquery, SDK 1.8); the Workspace and Site are filled in, and any other table is refused. `transaction(work)` runs table changes and Audit log entries together.
- `audit(action, details)`: recorded under the Module's id. In a procedure the person acted (`via: admin`); elsewhere the Module did (`via: module`, no person).
- `email.send(message)` (needs `uses: ["email"]`): the `module-message` System email (a work email people can turn off), in the Module's words for each recipient's Admin language (else its first), in the built-in layout, recorded in the Email log.
- `http(url, options)`: the guarded fetcher, to its declared hosts only. When a call fails the Module gets WebCraft's own error (`GuardedFetchError` with its `problem`, or `ModuleRefusedError`), never the error behind it, which could name the address.
- `secrets`: `set`, `describe`, `remove` its own secrets in the place (stored as `module:<id>:<name>`), and `placeholder(name)`, which WebCraft replaces with the value inside an outgoing call (URL, header or body) to a declared host, percent-encoded in the URL (its path and query) so no character of it can change the address; Module code never sees a value, and a call carrying a secret never follows a redirect.
- `jobs.send`, `log.info/warn/error` (the Module log), `now()`, `module`, `place`, `submissions` (SDK 1.6, the Module keeping Submissions only); a procedure also gets `person`, `can(permission)` and `confirmations` (SDK 1.6).

When a Module acts by itself it is a `ModuleActor` for `can()`, opening its place through the data layer like anyone.

### The Module log

`module_log` keeps what Modules wrote and every failure while they ran (a procedure, Site route, event or job that threw, an event that couldn't be queued), with the Workspace, Site and details (never a stack or a secret), 90 days (`modules.purge-log`, daily). The Superadmin reads it on Install settings › Modules (`modules.log`). A failed event or job is tried twice more; a refusal is logged and never retried, since the Module must change.

### Testing Modules

`createTestInstall({ modules: [sample, sampleBoard] })` loads Modules (this build's first-party Modules unless given); every test database has had `TEST_MODULES`' migrations (`tests/harness/modules.ts`: the first-party Modules and `SAMPLE_MODULES`). `tests/modules/first-party.test.ts` fails when a first-party Module can't run or its migrations break the step's rules. The isolation guard (`tests/isolation/procedures.test.ts`) lists every test Module's procedures too: add a line for each procedure a first-party Module adds. `moduleApi<typeof sample>(browser, "sample")` (`tests/harness/modules.ts`) calls a Module's procedures, typed from its definition (`ModuleApi` in the SDK). `install.restart({ modules })` runs another version; `install.migrate()` runs the `migrate` step. Tests are in `tests/modules/`.

## The Management API and API tokens

The Management API (ADR 0029, spec › Management API) is every procedure of `packages/api/src/router.ts` and the Install's Modules' (`adminRouter(services)`). The admin calls them all at `/api/rpc`; the public ones are also served to API tokens at `/api/v1` (`createPublicApi`, `packages/api/src/public.ts`), which the MCP server (#106) and the CLI (#107) are generated from (ADR 0030).

- **Paths** (ADR 0034): a public procedure answers `POST /api/v1/<router path with slashes>` (`/api/v1/entries/publish`, `/api/v1/media/folders/list`), through oRPC's OpenAPI handler: the input as a JSON body (multipart form data when it holds a file: `media.upload`, `media.replace`, `imports.uploadCsv`), dates as ISO strings (read back through the input's schema), the answer as plain JSON, a refusal as `{ code, status, message, data }` with its status. Module procedures are all public, at `/api/v1/m/<module id>/<name>`, and say whether they read or write (`kind`, SDK 1.6; a write unless `read`).
- **Which are public**: `PROCEDURES` in `packages/api/src/catalogue.ts` lists every procedure, `internal()` or a public `read(summary)` / `write(summary)`; the typecheck fails while one is missing. Internal: the setup wizard, signing in, the shell, a person's own account and API tokens (`me.*`, `apiTokens.*`), Install settings, `workspaces.create`, Staff, invite links, the Audit log and Impersonation (a security record and a person's session: never a token's to read or start), approving Modules and their log, Edit mode's link, the visual builder's own procedures (`builder.*`: scripts use the Template markup procedures), shared Install AI keys (`sharedAiKeys.*`), `media.suggestAltText` and `history.leave` (an editor closing). Everything Install-level is internal, since a token acts within one Workspace.
- **The OpenAPI document**: `GET /api/v1/openapi.json` (open to anyone; OpenAPI 3.1, `servers: [{ url: "/api/v1" }]`, bearer auth). Each operation's id is the dotted path (`entries.publish`), tagged by its group (`m.<id>` for a Module's), with its summary and input schema, and WebCraft's marks: `x-webcraft-access` (`read`, `write`), `x-webcraft-sensitive` (the Sensitive action, never callable with a token; read from the procedure's `sensitive(…)`), `x-webcraft-confirmation` (it takes a `confirmation`: two steps). The document of a build with its first-party Modules is checked in at `docs/api/openapi.json`; `bun run api:document` writes it again, so every change to the public API shows in review. How scripts call it: `docs/api/README.md`.
- **API tokens** (`packages/core/src/api-tokens.ts`, `services.apiTokens`; tables `api_tokens`, `api_token_sites`; procedures `apiTokens.list`, `create`, `revoke`; the admin's Your account › API tokens): a person makes one for one Workspace they reach, every Site of it (later ones too) or chosen ones they see, optionally read-only, lasting 30, 90 (the default) or 365 days or never. Creating is a Sensitive action (`apiToken.create`), logged in the Workspace. The token, `webcraft_` and 43 random base64url characters, is answered once; only an HMAC-SHA256 of it is kept (a key derived from the Master key, `apiTokenKey`), with its last 4 characters as a hint, so a request finds it with one indexed lookup and a new Master key ends every token, as it signs everyone out. Revoking (`apiToken.revoke`) stops it at once; rows stay, so a script is told its token was revoked.
- **A request** carries `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (cookies are never read at `/api/v1`): an API token, or the access token of a device sign-in (`webcraft login`, an OAuth grant made for `<admin origin>/api/v1`; see "The CLI"), checked by `services.oauth.use(secret, services.oauth.apiResource)` and counted under its grant (`oauth.count`). `services.apiTokens.use` checks it (401 `TOKEN_INVALID`, `data.problem`: `missing`, `unknown`, `expired`, `revoked`, or `locked` with `Retry-After` while its person's account is locked) and answers `access`: a `TokenActor`, the person's access read fresh, limited to the token's Workspace and Sites, `via: { kind: "token", name }`. Procedures then run as for anyone (`signedIn` takes `context.token`); `context.person` is the token's person and `context.session` null.
- **Losing access**: removing a Member, changing their Sites and changing Staff's Workspaces revoke, in the same transaction, the person's tokens whose Workspace (or every listed Site) they no longer reach (`revokeTokensOutOfReach`, logged `apiToken.revoke` with `reason: "access"`); a later Membership never brings one back. `use` checks the same on every request and revokes a token there too. A role change or fewer Sites take effect on the next request through `can()`. Deleting a person or a Workspace deletes their tokens.
- **Limits**: per token, 300 requests a minute and, of them, 30 writes (`API_TOKEN_LIMITS`, counted with `rateLimits.tally` under `api-token:<id>:requests` and `:writes`, so the counters hold a keyed hash, never the token); past either, 429 `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` with `Retry-After` and `data.limit`. Every answer carries `RateLimit-Limit`, `RateLimit-Remaining` and `RateLimit-Reset` (seconds) of the limit closest to running out. A read-only token calling a write gets 403 `TOKEN_READ_ONLY` before it runs; an internal or unknown path 404 `NOT_FOUND`.
- **Never for a token**: Sensitive actions (`SENSITIVE_ACTION_REFUSED`, ADR 0030) and Install-level procedures (internal). Publishing and deleting Structure take two steps (see "Two-step confirmation").
- **Toolsets** (`GLOSSARY.md`, ADR 0030): a token calls only the procedures of the Toolsets switched on for it (`api_tokens.toolsets`), else 403 `TOOLSET_OFF` (`data.toolsets`) before it runs. A new token has every Toolset this Install offers but Submissions (`defaultToolsets`); tokens made before Toolsets have WebCraft's own but Submissions. The admin's dialog offers them (`apiTokens.list` answers `toolsets`, each with `optIn`). See "The MCP server and AI clients" for where each procedure stands.
- **The Install's version**: every answer to a client whose token works carries `WebCraft-Version` (`VERSION_HEADER`, `WEBCRAFT_VERSION`), never sent to anyone else; the CLI warns once per run when it is older. `GET /api/v1/openapi.json` answers with an `ETag` (a hash of the document) and 304 to a matching `If-None-Match`, so the CLI keeps its copy between runs.
- **Who is calling**: `access.current` (public, `always`, read) answers the caller's person, the Install's address and version, and for an API client its kind (`apiToken` or `oauthGrant`), name, Workspace, Sites (`"all"` or those it reaches), read-only, May publish and Toolsets; `client` is null in the admin. `webcraft whoami` reads it, and AI clients get it as the tool `access_current`.
- **One caller for every API client**: `createProcedureCaller` (`packages/api/src/public.ts`) is how `/api/v1` and the MCP server call a public procedure for a token or grant (`ApiClientUse`, in `context.client`): it refuses what `clientRefusal` refuses (read-only, Toolsets, May publish), keeps uploads to their limit, runs the procedure, and records every read of Submissions in the Audit log (`submissions.read`).

## The Content API

A Site's read-only API for frontends built elsewhere (ADR 0029, `GLOSSARY.md` › Content API, Content key; spec story 146). Core: `packages/core/src/content-api/` (`services.contentApi`: the settings and keys, `manage.ts` and `keys.ts`; the Site side, `read.ts`, `present.ts`, `params.ts`, `cursor.ts`, `exposure.ts`; its OpenAPI document, `openapi.ts`); the web side: `apps/server/src/site/content-api.ts`; procedures `contentApi.*` (`packages/api/src/procedures/content-api.ts`); tables `content_api_settings`, `content_keys`, `content_key_usage` (Site tables); the admin's Site settings › Content API (`ui/content-api/ContentApi.tsx`). It is separate from the Management API in every way: its own paths, its own credential (a Content key, never an API token), its own limits and its own OpenAPI document.

- **Where**: `GET /_content/v1/…` on every hostname of the Site (its Primary domain, its languages' domains and its Staging address; a Redirect domain sends it on to the Primary domain, as it does every request), answered in the Site pipeline right after the hostname is resolved, before the Staging address's checks (`isContentApiPath`). It shows published content only, so the Staging address's sign-in and password don't apply to it: a Content key is what opens it there too (frontends can't carry the Staging cookies, and nothing the Primary domain wouldn't show is shown). Anything under `/_content` other than `/_content/v1/…` is `NOT_FOUND`; a v2 would be served beside v1.
- **Off by default** (`content_api_settings.enabled`): every request answers 404 `CONTENT_API_OFF` until Staff switch it on (Site settings › Content API, `contentApi.update({ siteId, enabled })`), and keys stop working while it is off.
- **Content keys** (`keys.ts`): `webcraft-ck_` and 43 random base64url characters, answered once by `contentApi.keys.create({ siteId, name, origins })`; only an HMAC-SHA256 of the key is kept (`contentKeyKey`, derived from the Master key, so a new Master key ends every Content key), with its last 4 characters as `hint`. Named (1 to 100 characters), revocable at once (`contentApi.keys.revoke`, rows stay, so a frontend is told its key was revoked), renamed and given new origins at once (`contentApi.keys.update`). At most `CONTENT_KEY_LIMITS.keys` (50) working keys per Site. Keys are always required: there is no public mode, since a key is what the limits, the origins and the use are counted by, and ADR 0029 opens the API with one. A key travels in `Authorization: Bearer <key>`; a request with `key`, `access_token` (and the like) in its query string is refused with `CONTENT_KEY_IN_QUERY` before anything else, so a key never sits in a log. A key is looked up among the Site's own keys only, so another Site's key is `unknown` here.
- **Origins and CORS** (each key's `origins`, at most 20): exact origins as a browser sends them, saved in their normal form (`HTTPS://App.Example.org:443/` is `https://app.example.org`): http or https, a host and a port when it isn't the scheme's own, nothing after. No wildcards: an allow-list names each origin it trusts. A request without `Origin` (a server) and one from the Site's own origin are answered whatever a key lists; one from another origin must be listed by its key, else 403 `ORIGIN_NOT_ALLOWED`. A preflight (`OPTIONS`) carries no key, so it is answered for the origins any working key of the Site lists (`Access-Control-Allow-Methods: GET, HEAD`, `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Authorization, If-None-Match`, `Access-Control-Max-Age: 600`); the request that follows is checked against its own key's. Refusals before the key is known let pages at those origins read why. Every answer says `Vary: Origin, Authorization` and exposes `ETag`, `RateLimit-*` and `Retry-After` to the page.
- **Limits** (`CONTENT_API_LIMITS`, counted in Postgres with `rateLimits.tally` like every limit): 120 requests a minute per client address and Site (`content-api:<site id>:<address>`, every request but a preflight, keyless ones too), then 600 a minute per key (`content-key:<id>`), each past it 429 `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` with `data.limit` (`address` or `key`) and `Retry-After` until the window ends. Every answer carries `RateLimit-Limit`, `RateLimit-Remaining` and `RateLimit-Reset` of the limit closest to running out.
- **Use** (`content_key_usage`): every request a working key makes counts in its day (UTC), one upsert per request; `lastUsedAt` is written at most once a minute. A key's days older than 90 are deleted when it is first used on a new day. `contentApi.get` answers each key's `usage` (today, the last 7 and 30 days, and each of the last 30 days) and `lastUsedAt`.
- **What it shows** (`exposure.ts`): every Post Type and Field Staff didn't hide (`hidden`: Post Type ids, Field paths by Post Type id, `address.city` inside a Group, `steps.text` inside a Repeater, and the Globals' Field paths; new ones are shown until hidden). Everything the API answers reads Post Types and Fields from the exposure only, so hidden ones never appear and can't be told apart from what doesn't exist: lists and single Entries (`POST_TYPE_NOT_FOUND`), values, filters, sorts and `fields` (`fieldUnknown`, exactly as an unknown Field), Relationships (a Relationship pointing only at hidden Post Types is left out; ids of Entries of hidden Post Types are dropped), the schema and search. Search leaves out a Post Type whose Searched fields include a hidden Field (`searchesIn`), since a hit and its snippet would show it.
- **Published only**: Entries' published versions (`entries.published`), never a Draft, never a Scheduled publish's version before its time; an Entry unpublished since answers 410 `ENTRY_GONE` (by id, by slug through `gone_paths`, by path); one hidden in the language asked answers 404 and is left out of lists and Relationships (`publishedVisibleIn`, `isVisibleIn`); the Globals' published values (null while never published); Media that is ready and not in the Trash.
- **Paths** (`readContent`): `/entries/<post type key>` (a list), `/entries/<key>/<id>`, `/entries/<key>/slug/<slug>` (not for Pages: `notForPages`), `/entry?path=/fr/blog/pain-frais` (the Entry at a path as answered, its prefix naming the language; `/` is the home page), `/globals`, `/media/<id>`, `/search?q=`, `/languages`, `/schema` and `/openapi.json` (no key needed; the API must be on).
- **An Entry** (`present.ts`): `id`, `postType`, `language`, `title`, `slug`, `path` (under the language's prefix), `url` (absolute, on the language's own domain when it has one, from `SiteHost.visitorAddresses`), `parent` (Pages), `publishedAt`, `lastPublishedAt`, `seo` (the SEO fields, the image as Media details), `fields` and `fallbacks`. Values: Media as their details (`ContentMedia`: kind, type, file name, size, width, height, the original's and each variant's absolute address, title, alt text and caption in the language, Focal point), Relationships as ids of published Entries of exposed Post Types shown in the language, Groups by key, Repeaters as rows `{ id, … }`, Body fields as `body` asks: `html` (the default, `renderBody`, Media at absolute addresses), `blocks` (as stored, with their ids, without translations' `source`) or `markdown` (`bodyMarkdown`, the Markdown copies' converter). In HTML and Markdown alike, links stay as written (a frontend has the Site's paths or completes them) and Media images are at their absolute addresses on the Site's domain.
- **Languages**: `lang` reads one of the Site's languages (any case and form of the tag; on a language's own domain, that language unless given); a value with none of its own falls back one Field at a time (ADR 0003; `readFieldsThrough` in `content/field-values.ts`) through `fallback` (comma-separated languages in order; the Default language unless given; `none` or empty for none). Shared values are the Default language's whatever the fallbacks. `fallbacks` names each value read from another language, by path (`summary`, `origin.city`, `steps[1].text`). Filters and sorts read the same values (`LoopPlace.chain` in the Loop engine's `QuerySql`).
- **Lists** (`/entries/<key>`): `fields`, `filter.<field>[.<op>]`, `sort`, `limit` (20 unless asked, up to 100, `CONTENT_API_PAGE`) and `cursor`; answered as `{ items, total, next }`. Filters, on top-level exposed Fields: equality (`filter.price=6`) for text, email, numbers, true/false, choices (has the option), days (the whole day, UTC) and Relationships (links to the Entry, by id; ids of Entries the API doesn't show match nothing); `in` (one of, up to 50, comma-separated) for text, email, numbers, choices and Relationships; `gt`, `gte`, `lt`, `lte` for numbers, days and times (a day counts whole), and for `publishedAt` and `lastPublishedAt`. Time-only date Fields, rich text, Bodies, Media, links, Groups and Repeaters aren't filtered (`filterUnsupported`). One sort: `sort=<field>`, `-<field>` descending (`title`, `publishedAt`, `lastPublishedAt`, and top-level text, email, number, true/false, one-choice and date Fields); `-publishedAt` unless given; Entries without a value last, then the id, so ties are stable. Each Field's `filters` and `sortable` are in the schema.
- **Cursors** (`cursor.ts`): keyset pagination, so a page never repeats or skips an item as Entries are published meanwhile: a cursor holds the last item's sort value (as text) and id and a fingerprint of the query (its Post Type, filters, sort and language chain), signed with HMAC-SHA256 under `contentCursorKey` (from the Master key). One changed, made up or sent with another query is `cursorInvalid`. Search pages by offset the same way, through its first `CONTENT_SEARCH_DEPTH` (1,000) hits.
- **`fields`**: the Fields to answer, comma-separated (built-in values always come); `<relationship>.<field>` answers the Entries a Relationship points at as Entries (their built-in values and those Fields), one level deep (`tooDeep` past it).
- **Search** (`/search?q=&postType=`): through Site search (ADR 0021, `queryRows` with `Narrowing.search` and `indexableOnly`): searchable, exposed Post Types (`searchesIn`), in the language, noindex Entries left out, best match first, each hit an Entry's built-in values and `snippet` (`searchSnippets`). Never kept in the page cache.
- **Refusals** are JSON `{ code, status, message, data }` with stable codes (`CONTENT_API_ERRORS`): `CONTENT_API_OFF`, `NOT_FOUND`, `METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED` (only `GET`, `HEAD` and `OPTIONS`), `CONTENT_KEY_INVALID` (401, `WWW-Authenticate`, `data.problem`: `missing`, `unknown`, `revoked`), `CONTENT_KEY_IN_QUERY`, `ORIGIN_NOT_ALLOWED`, `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS`, `QUERY_INVALID` (400, `data.problems`: each `{ parameter, problem, message }`, every problem found at once; the `problem` codes are `CONTENT_API_QUERY_PROBLEMS`: an unknown or repeated parameter, an unknown language, Field or operator, a value of the wrong kind, a bad limit or cursor…), `POST_TYPE_NOT_FOUND`, `ENTRY_NOT_FOUND`, `ENTRY_GONE` (410) and `MEDIA_NOT_FOUND`. Refusals are `no-store`.
- **Caching**: answers carry a strong ETag (the SHA-256 of their JSON) and `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, so browsers and CDNs keep them but check each time (the key is checked then too); `If-None-Match` gets 304. They are kept in the web process's page cache under the Site's page cache generation (`content-api <origin><path>?<sorted query>`, a variant when it has a query; `X-Cache: HIT` or `MISS`), so every publish, Publish all, Scheduled publish and unpublish, AI translation's publishes, Media changes and the Content API's own settings (`contentApi.update` clears the page cache) give new answers at once. Search is never kept. Every answer says `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`.
- **Who** (`structure.edit`, so Staff only; no new Permission): `contentApi.get` (the switch, what is hidden as far as it still exists, the keys with their use, and `addresses`: the main address's and the Staging address's `/_content/v1`), `contentApi.update`, `contentApi.keys.create`, `update` and `revoke`. Public procedures (Toolset Site settings), so Staff's API tokens and AI clients call them too: making a key isn't a Sensitive action, since a key reads only what the Site publishes and is meant for browser code. Refusals: `CONTENT_API_INVALID` (`data.problems`, each `at` with a `rule`: `contentKeyName`, `contentKeyOrigin`, `contentKeyOrigins`, `contentKeys`, `postTypeUnknown`, `fieldUnknown`) and `CONTENT_KEY_NOT_FOUND`.
- **Audit log**: `contentApi.change` (what changed: `enabled` `{ from, to }`, and each hidden list given), `contentKey.create` (`keyId`, `name`, `origins`), `contentKey.update` (`changed`: `name`, `origins` `{ from, to }`) and `contentKey.revoke`; never a key.
- **Its OpenAPI document** (`contentApiDocument`, `openapi.ts`): OpenAPI 3.1, the same for every Site, served at `/_content/v1/openapi.json` and checked in at `docs/api/content-api.openapi.json` (`bun run content-api:document` writes it again; `tests/content-api/openapi.test.ts` fails while they differ). Its error codes, query problems, limits and body formats are read from the constants the API applies.
- **Tests**: `tests/content-api/` (`support.ts`: `contentClient(install, origin, key, { address })` is a frontend from an address of its own; `bakery(install, slug)` makes a live Site in English and French with every kind of Field, its Content API on and a key), `tests/browser/content-api.browser.ts`.
- **Not in v1** (ADR 0029, spec › Out of Scope): Draft preview and GraphQL.

## The MCP server and AI clients

AI clients (`GLOSSARY.md`) work through the MCP server at `/mcp` on the admin host (ADR 0030; `packages/mcp`, `createMcpEndpoint`), built on the official MCP TypeScript SDK for servers (`@modelcontextprotocol/server`, Apache-2.0), Streamable HTTP, stateless: a client of the 2026-07-28 revision sends no handshake, and one of the 2025 revisions (`initialize`) is served one request at a time. Every request carries a bearer token: an AI client's access token (an OAuth grant) or an API token.

- **Tools** (`tools.ts`, `generateTools`): one per operation of the Install's OpenAPI document (`openApiDocument`, its Modules' included), named after its operation id in snake case (`toolName`: `entries.saveDraft` is `entries_save_draft`, `m.site-analytics.stats.totals` is `site_analytics_stats_totals`), with its summary and input schema (a file input becomes `{ name, type, base64 }`, sent to the procedure as multipart form data; a request to `/mcp` holds at most 4 MiB, so larger files come in by address, `media_import_url`). Operations marked `x-webcraft-sensitive` never become tools. `_meta` names each tool's operation (`webcraft/operation`) and Toolsets (`webcraft/toolsets`); annotations say whether it reads and whether it takes two steps (`destructiveHint`). One tool is hand-written: `guide_get` (below). `tests/mcp/generation.test.ts` checks the generation against the document.
- **Toolsets** (`TOOLSETS` in `packages/core/src/toolsets.ts`): Content, Media, Structure, Forms, Publishing, Translation, Site settings, Analytics, Submissions, and a Module's own (`m.<id>`) for Module procedures that name none of WebCraft's (SDK 1.9 `toolset`; Forms' Submissions procedures are in Submissions, Site analytics' in Analytics, Redirects' in Content). Each public procedure of WebCraft's has a line in `TOOL_PLACES` (`packages/api/src/toolsets.ts`): its Toolsets (`always` for finding the Workspace and its Sites), the Permissions that let a person call it (any one) and whether it publishes; the document carries them (`x-webcraft-toolsets`, `x-webcraft-permissions`, `x-webcraft-publishes`). Submissions are opt-in (`OPT_IN_TOOLSETS`): off unless the person ticks them, with a warning, and every read is logged (`submissions.read`).
- **A session's tools** (`listedTools`, `session.ts`): those whose procedure `clientRefusal` allows (read-only clients: reads only; Toolsets; "May publish" for what publishes), whose Permission the person holds on the Workspace or a Site the client reaches, and, for a Module's, where the Module runs. A call to a tool the session doesn't list is refused (`-32602`). A tool runs its procedure through `createProcedureCaller`, the same checks as `/api/v1`, as the client's person, recorded as `aiClient` (or `token`); its answer is the result's JSON (`structuredContent` for objects), a refusal an `isError` result holding `{ code, message, data }`.
- **Two steps**: a tool marked `x-webcraft-confirmation` answers its preview and confirmation id first, which the client calls again with. A client of the 2026-07-28 revision that declares elicitation is asked instead: the first call answers `input_required` with a question (the preview) and the confirmation id in its `requestState`, signed with `services.mcpRequestStateKey` (from the Master key) and bound to the client, so it comes back only with the person's answer; ticked, the procedure goes ahead with the id; declined, nothing changes (`{ confirmed: false }`).
- **Limits**: each `/mcp` request counts against the client's 300 requests a minute, each write tool against its 30 writes (an API token's counters are the same as at `/api/v1`; a grant's are `oauth-grant:<id>`).
- **Guides** (`guides.ts`): the Template markup reference (`templateMarkupReference`, its commands and formatters taken from the renderer), a Site's schema (its languages, Post Types with their Fields, the Globals' Fields, Components, Loops and Forms, read through the procedures as the client, each part left out and named in `unavailable` when the client may not read it) and its Protected terms (through `protectedTerms.list` once the Install has it). Resources `webcraft://guides/template-markup`, `webcraft://sites/<id>/schema` and `webcraft://sites/<id>/protected-terms` (templates for any Site), and the `guide_get` tool (`topic`, `siteId`).
- **OAuth** (`packages/core/src/oauth.ts`, `services.oauth`; endpoints in `packages/mcp/src/oauth-endpoints.ts`): WebCraft is the authorization server of its own MCP server, not Better Auth's OAuth plugin, so codes and tokens are kept as keyed hashes like API tokens and the approval is WebCraft's own page. The MCP server's metadata (RFC 9728) is at `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp` (and the root), named in every 401 from `/mcp` (`resource_metadata`); the authorization server's (RFC 8414) at `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server`. Clients register at `/oauth/register` (RFC 7591; redirect addresses https, or http on the loopback interface, compared exactly; `none` or a secret, kept as a hash; 20 registrations an hour per address; an unapproved client is forgotten after a week). `/oauth/authorize` is the admin's approval page (`routes/oauth.authorize.tsx`, never framed): signed in, the person sees the client's name and the host it sends them back to (with a warning when that is their own device) and approves it for one Workspace, its Sites, Toolsets, read-only and "May publish" (`oauth.approve`, a Sensitive action, `aiClient.approve`) or denies it; an unknown client or an unregistered address is refused there and nobody is sent anywhere. PKCE is S256 only; the code lasts a minute and works once (used again, it ends the grant); the answer carries `iss` (RFC 9207). `/oauth/token` gives an access token (`webcraft-at_`, 1 hour) and a refresh token (`webcraft-rt_`, 30 days, replaced on use; one used again ends the grant); tokens are for `/mcp` only (`resource`, RFC 8707). `/oauth/revoke` (RFC 7009) lets a client end its grant. CORS lets browser-based clients call these and `/mcp` from any origin (no cookies).
- **Device sign-ins** (the OAuth 2.0 device authorization grant, RFC 8628; table `oauth_device_codes`): a client registered for it (`grant_types` with `urn:ietf:params:oauth:grant-type:device_code`, `DEVICE_CODE_GRANT`, and no redirect addresses, kept in `oauth_clients.grant_types`; a client registered before it has the code and refresh grants) asks `/oauth/device_authorization` (`client_id`, optionally `resource`: the public API `<admin origin>/api/v1` unless it names the MCP server; 30 an hour per address, `OAUTH_DEVICE_LIMIT`) for a device code (`webcraft-dc_`) and a user code of 8 consonants (`BCDF-GHJK`, 20^8 values), valid 10 minutes, `interval` 5 seconds. The person enters or confirms the code on `/oauth/device` (`routes/oauth.device.tsx`, never framed): `oauth.checkDevice` looks it up (at most 20 a person in 15 minutes, `OAUTH_USER_CODE_LIMIT`, else `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS`) and answers the client to approve or why it can't be (`unknown`, `expired`, `decided`); `oauth.approveDevice` takes the same choices as `oauth.approve` (a Sensitive action, `aiClient.approve` with `device: true`) and `oauth.denyDevice` refuses it (`DEVICE_CODE_INVALID`, 400, `data.problem`, for a code that can't be decided). Meanwhile the client polls `/oauth/token` with `grant_type=urn:ietf:params:oauth:grant-type:device_code`: `authorization_pending`, `slow_down` (a poll sooner than its interval adds 5 seconds to it), `access_denied`, `expired_token`, then the tokens, once (a device code used again ends its grant, as a code does). Both codes are kept as keyed hashes only; `oauth.purge` deletes expired device codes. The metadata names `device_authorization_endpoint`.
- **A grant's resource** (`oauth_grants.resource`, RFC 8707): its tokens are for one resource only, the MCP server (AI clients; null for grants made before) or the public API (device sign-ins asking for it): `services.oauth.use(secret, resource)` refuses an access token for the other (`TOKEN_INVALID`, `unknown`). A device grant sends nobody anywhere: its `redirect_uri` is empty and `OAuthGrantView.client.redirectHost` null (Your account › AI clients says "Signed in with a device code").
- **Grants** (tables `oauth_clients`, `oauth_grants`, `oauth_grant_sites`, `oauth_codes`, `oauth_tokens`): a grant acts as its person now (`GrantActor`), within its Workspace and Sites; it waits while its person's account is locked, and ends when they revoke it (Your account › AI clients: `aiClients.list`, `aiClients.revoke`), when its client revokes it, when its person loses access (`revokeGrantsOutOfReach`, beside `revokeTokensOutOfReach`, and again on its next use), or on reuse. `oauth.purge` deletes ended codes and tokens daily. A new Master key ends every grant's tokens.
- **History**: a change through a token or AI client is recorded as "<person> via <client>": the Audit log's `via`, and the Drafts' `updated_via` (Publish all's `changedBy.via`).

## The CLI

`webcraft` (`apps/cli`; how people use it: `docs/cli.md`) is one Bun program with three parts, kept apart so the remote commands never load the Install's code:

- **`src/cli.ts`** (`runCli(args, io)`) takes the options every command has (`--install`, `--json`, `--yes`, `--help`, `--version`) and dispatches. Everything it touches outside itself is `CliIo` (`src/io.ts`): environment, output, terminal questions, fetch, the config folder, the credential store, sleep and the clock. `src/main.ts` runs it with `processIo()`; tests with their own (`tests/harness/cli.ts`).
- **Remote commands** (`src/remote/`): `catalogueOf(document)` turns the Install's OpenAPI document into commands (one per operation, Sensitive ones left out and named in `sensitive`, a Module's under its id; `m <module> …` always works, and WebCraft's own procedure wins should a Module's take its words), `inputOf` reads options through each input schema, `runRemote` calls `POST /api/v1/<path>` and handles two steps, `render.ts` prints answers for people. `connection.ts` resolves the Install and credential (`WEBCRAFT_TOKEN`, else a profile's sign-in, refreshed under a lock file so two processes never reuse a refresh token), keeps the document by its ETag in the config folder's `cache/`, waits out a `Retry-After` of a minute or less, and warns once per run when `WebCraft-Version` is newer than its own (`CLI_VERSION`, the root `package.json`). `known.ts` is the document this version was built with (`docs/api/openapi.json`, embedded in the compiled file): help falls back to it, and a command it knows that the Install lacks is refused with exit code 7. Exit codes are `EXIT` in `src/exit-codes.ts` (documented in `docs/cli.md`; never renumber one).
- **Accounts** (`src/account/`): `login` registers the CLI once per Install as a public client for device codes (`webcraft CLI on <hostname>`), runs the device flow and keeps the tokens with `credentialStore` (Bun's `secrets`: the system keychain; else `credentials.json`, mode 600); profiles are `config.json` (no secret). `whoami` reads `access.current`; `logout` revokes the grant at `/oauth/revoke`.
- **Structure** (`src/structure/`): `readStructure` reads a Site's Structure through public procedures and renders each file; `pull` writes them and `webcraft.json`; `push` compares the files with the manifest (unchanged, changed, new; stale when the Install's render of the item moved since the pull), previews, then saves item by item in dependency order (Post Types, the Design system, Loops, Components, Templates, Forms, Filters), Template markup and Components with `basedOn`, and writes back what the Install stored. The CSS files are read with `topLevelBlocks` (`css-blocks.ts`), which only splits blocks: the Install checks their contents.
- **Server commands** (`src/admin/`), loaded only for `webcraft admin …`: the migrate step, Backups, restore, `rotate-key`, `create-superadmin`, `reset-2fa`, `check-update`, and `setup` (`services.setup.newSetupLink()`), `doctor`, `health` and `media move` (`checks.ts`). They write to the process's own output and read `process.env`, as their tests run them as real processes.
- **The worker checks in** every 30 seconds (`startWorkerHeartbeat`, `services.workers`, table `worker_heartbeats`, its row deleted when it stops): `health --worker` and `doctor` count a worker as running when one checked in within 2 minutes (`WORKER_FRESH_MS`). Install health (#86) can read the same.
- **Distribution** (`scripts/build-cli.ts`): `bun build --compile --minify` with `process.env.WEBCRAFT_BUILD` defined as `standalone`, which drops the server commands from the file (they need the migrations and the Install's files); for each platform target, `SHA256SUMS`, and with `--npm` the packages `@webcraft/cli` (a Node launcher that runs `@webcraft/cli-<os>-<cpu>`'s binary) and one per platform. The web image runs the CLI from source (`/usr/local/bin/webcraft`), server commands included; it keeps `docs/api` for `known.ts`.

## Signing in and the setup wizard

- **`@webcraft/auth`** is the only way into sign-in: `services.auth.signIn`, `session(headers)`, `signOut`, `createPerson` (inside the caller's transaction). Better Auth 1.7.18 (pinned in the root `package.json` catalog) sits behind it in `packages/auth/src/auth.ts`; no other package imports it. Its own HTTP endpoints are not served: WebCraft's procedures call it. When you upgrade it, read its security advisories and keep the version exact.
- **People** are the `people` table; their password hash is a row in `credentials`. Install-level roles are text keys in `install_roles` (`superadmin` and `staff`, ADR 0028; see "Tenancy and permissions"). Emails are stored in lower case.
- **Passwords** need at least 12 characters (`assertNewPasswordAllowed`). Extra checks plug in as `passwordChecks` in `createAuth`; `createInstallServices` plugs in the leaked-password check.
- **The leaked-password check** (`leakedPasswordCheck` in `packages/auth/src/leaked-passwords.ts`) checks every new password (new accounts, password changes) with a partial-hash lookup through `services.http`: only the first 5 characters of the password's SHA-1 hash leave the server (`GET <range service>/range/<prefix>` with `Add-Padding: true`), and the password is refused (`PASSWORD_REFUSED`, `leaked`) when the answer lists the rest of its hash as seen at least once. It fails open: when the service can't be reached, times out (5 seconds) or answers with an error, the password is accepted and a warning is logged. The Superadmin switches it off in Install settings › Security (`install.security.update`, a Sensitive action), for an Install that is always offline.
- **Sessions** end after 7 days unused and 30 days after sign-in, by WebCraft's clock (`services.clock`), so tests move them with `install.clock.advance`. `auth.activeSession(id)` reads one by its id, without renewing it, for checks away from the admin's hostname (Edit mode). A session in use is renewed at most every 10 minutes, and records the client address it was used from. The cookie is `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax` and `Secure` (except on a `.localhost` development host), signed with a key derived from the Master key: a new Master key signs everyone out.
- **Your sessions** (Your account › Sessions): `me.sessions.list` shows each session's browser and system (from its `User-Agent`), its network (the client address with the end hidden, `203.0.113.*`; no outside location lookup, which would send people's addresses to another company) and when it was last used. `me.sessions.revoke` signs one out at once (this browser's own one too, clearing its cookie), `me.sessions.revokeOthers` every other one. `me.changePassword` (a Sensitive action) signs out every other session in the same transaction; changing 2FA does the same with `auth.revokeOtherSessions`.
- **Lockout** (`packages/auth/src/lockout.ts`): 10 wrong passwords in a row, signing in or confirming who you are, lock the account for 15 minutes. While it is locked nothing is checked, not even the right password, and trying doesn't make the lock longer; a right password, or the lock, starts the count again. Addresses nobody signs in with never lock. The answer is `ACCOUNT_LOCKED` (423) with `retryAfterSeconds` and a `Retry-After` header. When an account locks, `onAccountLocked` sends the `account-locked` security email and writes `account.lock` to the Audit log with the address of the last attempt.
- **Rate limits** on the auth endpoints (signing in, confirming who you are, setup tokens, invite links): at most 10 requests a minute from one client address, counted in Postgres (`services.rateLimits`, `RATE_LIMITS` in `packages/core/src/rate-limits.ts`) so every web process shares the count; then `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` (429) with `Retry-After` until the minute is over, and one warning in the log. Requests whose address the web process can't tell share one count. Features with limits of their own count under keys they make with `rateLimits.count(key, rule)`, which answers whether the request is within the limit (Forms' per-address limits, Consent records, Modules' Site routes' `limit`), or `rateLimits.tally(key, rule)`, which also answers what is left and when the window ends (API tokens' limits, the Content API's). A key names a client address, which is never kept: `rate_limits.key` holds an HMAC-SHA256 of it under a key derived from the Master key (`rateLimitKey`). The `rate-limits.purge` job deletes ended counters every day. Better Auth's own rate limiter stays off: its endpoints aren't served.
- **The setup token**: while the Install has no Superadmin, every start of the web process prints a new one-time token and a `setupUrl` in the log (`warn`, fields `setupToken` and `setupUrl`). Only its SHA-256 hash is stored; the last 10 printed stay valid. Claiming the Install with one creates the Superadmin and deletes every token. Creating the Superadmin is serialised with a Postgres advisory lock, across processes.
- **Two-factor authentication**: see the next section.
- **`webcraft admin create-superadmin <email> [--name <name>] [--language <en|it>]`** does the same headless: the password is asked twice on a terminal, or read from the first line of standard input (`printf '%s\n' "$PASSWORD" | webcraft admin create-superadmin ops@agency.example`); it is refused on the command line. It needs the Install's environment (`DATABASE_URL`, `WEBCRAFT_ADMIN_HOST`, `WEBCRAFT_SECRET_KEY`) and finishes setup, so the wizard never opens.
- **Setup stages** (`services.setup.stage()`): `claim` (no Superadmin: the wizard asks for the token), `continue` (the Superadmin exists and goes through the wizard's remaining steps, signed in), `done` (the wizard answers 404 for good).
- **The Master key banner** shows to the Superadmin until they confirm the running Master key is stored outside the server. The confirmation stores who, when and the key's fingerprint, and is logged; with another key the banner returns.

### Two-factor authentication

Second factors live in `@webcraft/auth` (`packages/auth/src/two-factor/`), behind `Auth`; what they mean for the Install (the Audit log, security emails, who must use it, resets) is `services.twoFactor` (`packages/core/src/two-factor/`). Better Auth's own two-factor and passkey plugins aren't used: they keep backup codes readable, keep their own lockout and sign-in cookies, and serve endpoints WebCraft never serves. WebAuthn is checked with Web Crypto only (`webauthn.ts`): asking for no attestation leaves only CBOR, authenticator data and three signature algorithms to read, which a library would bring certificate parsing for. The admin draws the QR code with `uqr` (MIT, no dependencies), as SVG in the page.

- **Authenticator apps** (TOTP, RFC 6238: HMAC-SHA1, 30-second steps, 6 digits): one per person (table `authenticators`). `me.twoFactor.startAuthenticator` answers a new 160-bit secret (base32) and its `otpauth://` address (the admin draws it as a QR code with the key as text beside it); `confirmAuthenticator({ code })` adds the app once a code from it is right. The secret is an Install secret of the secret store, `person:<person id>:authenticator` (`…-pending` while being added), so it is encrypted under the Master key and `rotate-key` carries it over. A code is accepted for the step before or after now, compared in constant time, and only for a step after the last one used (`last_used_step`), so no code works twice.
- **Backup codes**: 10, `abcde-23456` (about 50 bits), made with a person's first second factor and shown once (Download gives a text file); `me.twoFactor.regenerateBackupCodes` replaces them. Only an HMAC-SHA256 of each is kept (`backup_codes`), under a random key that is itself an Install secret (`auth.backup-code-key`, made the first time anyone gets codes): a key derived from the Master key would end every backup code at a rotation. A used code keeps its row with `used_at`. Removing the last second factor removes them.
- **Passkeys** (WebAuthn, table `passkeys`): the relying party is the admin's hostname (`WEBCRAFT_ADMIN_HOST`) and only the admin's origin may use it; discoverable credentials, user verification required, no attestation asked for (WebCraft keeps the key the device gives, never a statement about the device), ES256, EdDSA and RS256. Challenges are random, work once and for 5 minutes, and are kept as hashes in `verifications` with what they were given for and to whom. A signature count that doesn't go up is refused. Edit mode and the Staging address never need one: they live on other hostnames, reached through a single-use link from the admin session, and they end when that session does.
