- **Signing in**: `session.signIn` answers `secondStep: { methods }` instead of a session for a person with a second factor, and sets an `HttpOnly` cookie (`__Host-webcraft-second-step`) tying the second step to that browser for 5 minutes (a hash of it in `verifications`). `session.secondStep({ method: "authenticator" | "backupCode", code })` finishes it; `session.passkeyOptions` + `session.signInWithPasskey` sign in with a passkey, alone (it counts as both factors) or as the second step (then only that person's passkeys). Wrong codes count towards the lockout like wrong passwords, and a right password doesn't start the count again while its second step waits, so a guesser who knows the password gets 10 codes, not 10 per sign-in. All of them are auth endpoints (rate-limited per address). The session itself is started by a server-only endpoint of WebCraft's in the library (`startSessionFor`), with the library's own cookie.
- **Changes**: adding or removing an authenticator app or passkey and making new backup codes are Sensitive actions (`twoFactor.change`); adding or removing ends the person's other sessions in the same transaction, sends the `two-factor-changed` security email and is logged. A person who must use it can't remove their last second factor (`TWO_FACTOR_REQUIRED`).
- **Requiring it**: Install settings › Security (`install.security.requireTwoFactor`, the Superadmin) for everyone, and the Members screen (`members.requireTwoFactor`, `twoFactor.require`) for a Workspace's Members: Staff and the Superadmin follow the Install's switch only, since their sign-in is the agency's to decide and a client's Owner shouldn't change it. Switching it on needs your own second factor first (`TWO_FACTOR_OFF`), and is a Sensitive action (`twoFactorRequirement.change`). A person who must use it and has none is refused everything (`TWO_FACTOR_SETUP_REQUIRED`) but what setting it up needs: procedures starting from `signedInBeforeTwoFactor` / `inAdminSessionBeforeTwoFactor` (`me.twoFactor.*` but removals, `session.confirmIdentity`, `me.updatePreferences`), the shell and signing out. The admin sends them to `/two-factor` right after signing in. Their Edit mode and Staging address cookies stop working too (`mustSetUpTwoFactor`), and so do the admin's Draft sockets (refused with `TWO_FACTOR_SETUP_REQUIRED`, or ended at the next change). API tokens and AI clients they made before keep working: the switch is about signing in, and a token is a credential of its own.
- **The reminder**: `shell.context` answers `twoFactor: { on, methods, required, mustSetUp, reminder }`; everyone without a second factor who isn't required to have one sees a banner until they add it. "Remind me later" (`me.twoFactor.remindLater`) hides it for 7 days (`people.two_factor_reminder_until`), never for good.
- **Resets**: the Superadmin resets anyone's (a Member's from the Members screen, `members.resetTwoFactor`; Staff's from the Staff screen, `staff.resetTwoFactor`), Staff reset their Workspaces' Members' (`twoFactor.reset`), never other Staff or the Superadmin. A reset removes the authenticator app, passkeys and backup codes, ends every session of the person, lifts a lock on the account, sends `two-factor-reset` (with who reset it) and is logged. It is a Sensitive action (`twoFactor.reset`).
- **`webcraft admin reset-2fa <email>`** does the same from the server, for a Superadmin who lost their second factors (nobody on the Install can reset theirs). It needs the Install's environment, sends the email through the job queue (`openInstallForCommandSendingJobs`) and is logged as the command (`via: command`, `reset-2fa`). Losing the Master key (`rotate-key --old-key-lost`) deletes authenticator secrets and the backup-code key with the other secrets: people then sign in with a passkey, or have their two-factor authentication reset.
- **Everything is internal**: no procedure about two-factor authentication is public, so API tokens and AI clients never see or change it.

### Impersonation

The Superadmin, or a Staff member, acts in the admin as another person to see exactly what they see (ADR 0008, `GLOSSARY.md` › Impersonation). Core: `packages/core/src/impersonation.ts` (`services.impersonation`); table `impersonations`; procedures `impersonation.start` and `impersonation.stop` (internal).

- **Who**: the Superadmin impersonates any Staff member or Member; Staff only the Members of the Workspaces they are assigned to (`impersonation.start` there), and then only within those Workspaces: a Member who belongs to another Workspace too is impersonated without it (the person's access is limited to `impersonations.workspace_ids`, read fresh on every request with the impersonator's own). Nobody impersonates the Superadmin, Staff never another Staff member, Members never anyone, nobody themselves (`IMPERSONATION_REFUSED`, `data.reason`: `superadmin`, `staff`, `self`); a person outside the impersonator's Workspaces is `PERSON_NOT_FOUND` (404), an Owner trying is `FORBIDDEN`.
- **Starting** (`impersonation.start({ personId, reason?, returnTo? })`): the rules and the impersonator's own second factor are checked first (`services.impersonation.check`: `TWO_FACTOR_OFF` with `data.for: "impersonation"` without one), then the recent identity check (the Sensitive action `impersonation.start`). It lasts an hour (`IMPERSONATION_MAX_MS`); `returnTo` (a path of the admin) is where Stop goes back to; `reason` is kept in the Audit log. One at a time: not while impersonating (`NOT_WHILE_IMPERSONATING`).
- **It rides on the impersonator's own admin session**: a row in `impersonations` names the session (`session_id`); while it goes on (not ended, before `ends_at`), `services.impersonation.actingAs(session)` answers the person's access with `via: { kind: "impersonation", name, by }` and `identityCheckedAt: null`, and every place that reads an admin session asks it: the procedures (`withPerson` in `packages/api/src/context.ts` gives `context.acting`, then `context.person`, `context.access` and `context.impersonation`; `context.session` stays the impersonator's), the Draft sockets (`apps/server/src/co-editing/sockets.ts`: a Draft opened while impersonating ends when the impersonation does) and Media previews. The two-factor requirement is checked against whoever signed in. Stop (`impersonation.stop`, answering `returnTo`), the hour running out (requests act as the impersonator again at once; the `impersonation.end` job, sent for `ends_at`, records it) and signing out (`session.signOut` stops it first) end it; so does the impersonator losing the right to (`access`).
- **Nothing about the person's account changes** while it goes on: `notWhileImpersonating(what)` refuses with `NOT_WHILE_IMPERSONATING` (403, `data.what`) changing their password (`password`), second factors and the reminder (`twoFactor`), sessions (`sessions`), API tokens (`apiTokens`), AI clients and approvals (`aiClients`), preferences and email switches (`preferences`), confirming who they are (`identityCheck`), every Sensitive action (`sensitiveAction`, in `assertSensitiveActionAllowed`), Edit mode (`editMode`), the Staging address (`staging`) and another impersonation (`impersonation`). Reading their account works (`me.sessions.list` with none of them this browser's, `apiTokens.list`, `aiClients.list` and `me.activity` limited to the Workspaces it reaches). Edit mode and the Staging address never open while impersonating: they would run on other hostnames under cookies lasting up to 8 hours, past the hour and the banner; the admin hides "Staging" and "Edit on site" then.
- **Recorded under both names**: every entry while it goes on, and its start and stop, has the impersonated person as its person and `via: impersonation` with the impersonator (see "The Audit log"); the person sees them in Your account › Activity, the Workspace's Owners in its Audit log. Presence and history show "<Staff> as <Member>". Nobody is emailed: the person sees every entry in their activity.
- **In the admin**: Members' row menus and the Staff screen offer Impersonate (`ui/impersonation/ImpersonateDialog.tsx`: what it means, an optional reason, then Start through `useSensitiveAction`); `shell.context` answers `impersonation` (`person`, `impersonator`, `startedAt`, `endsAt`, `secondsLeft`), and `ImpersonationBanner` shows it on every page with the time left and Stop, said to screen readers when it appears and with 5 minutes left. Your account says the person's account can't change.
- **Not yet recorded under both names**: background work an impersonator starts runs later as the person alone (an Import run, Translate now, a Scheduled publish): the start is recorded "<Staff> as <Member>", what the worker writes after as the person (`admin`, `translation`, `scheduledPublish`). A Draft's `updated_via` (Publish all's "changed by") names API and AI clients only, so Publish all shows the Member alone.

## The admin

The admin is a TanStack Start app in `apps/server/src/routes` (file routes) and `apps/server/src/ui` (everything else). It reads and changes data only through the Management API: `getApi()` (`ui/api.ts`) calls the procedures in-process while a page renders on the server, and `/api/rpc` in the browser.

- **Shell context**: the root route loads `shell.context` on every navigation (who is signed in, Admin language, theme, setup stage, banners); routes read it with `Route.useRouteContext()`. After a change that affects it, call `router.invalidate()` or navigate.
- **Text**: every word people see comes from `apps/server/src/messages/en.json` and `it.json`, through `useT()` (`t("shell.nav.overview")`, with ICU placeholders such as `{name}`). Add each key to both files; a missing key fails the typecheck, and `tests/i18n/admin-messages.test.ts` checks that every language has every key with the same placeholders. Page titles come from `pageTitle(language, key)` in the route's `head`.
- **Right to left**: `<html lang dir>` follows the Admin language (`ADMIN_LANGUAGES` in `@webcraft/core/admin-languages`). Use logical classes (`ms-`, `me-`, `ps-`, `pe-`, `start-`, `end-`, `text-start`, `border-s`) and flip direction icons with `rtl:rotate-180`.
- **Light and dark**: `<html class="light|dark">` when a person chose, no class to follow the device (`prefers-color-scheme`); the `dark:` variant and the colour variables in `packages/ui/src/styles/globals.css` handle all three.
- **The top bar**: on a Site's pages, "Staging" (opens the Staging address in a new tab through `staging.open`, `ui/shell/StagingButton.tsx`), "Edit on site" (`entry.edit`) and Publish all with how many Drafts the person may publish (`entry.publish` or `structure.edit`; the count is `place.drafts`, loaded with the Site; `ui/publishing/PublishAll.tsx`, opened at once by `?publishAll=1`). The Staging address's "Sign in" goes to `/staging/<site id>?path=…`, which opens it at that page once the person is signed in.
- **Levels and the sidebar**: `ui/shell/levels.ts` holds each level's sidebar groups and items (`label`, `icon`, `to`, `requires`: the Permission that shows it). A group can carry `requires` too: the Site level's Structure group requires `structure.edit`, so Members see Content items only (and its `note` says "Staff only"). The Site level's groups are Content (Overview, Dashboards where the person sees one (`place.dashboards`, as at the Workspace level), Pages, each of the Site's Post Types, Media, Globals, Translations for `entry.edit`, Imports for `import.run`: the Post Types come from `place.postTypes`), Structure (Post Types & Fields, Libraries) and Site settings (Domains & Staging, Languages, AI translation for `translation.manage`, SEO, Consent banner, Email (SMTP), Webhooks, Outside scripts, Modules); the Workspace level has Protected terms and AI keys (`aiKey.manage`), and Install settings AI (shared AI keys); each Form's item follows Media in Content (see "Forms"); Module pages follow. The Site's `place` also carries `modulePermissions`: the Permissions Modules running there add that the person holds (`sites.get` answers them), for screens a Module's Permission opens, such as a Form's Submissions. The levels are the Install, a Workspace and a Site: pages under `routes/_admin/workspaces.$workspaceId.tsx` are at the Workspace level and pages under `routes/_admin/sites.$siteId.tsx` at the Site level. Those two routes load the Workspace or Site and the person's Permissions there, and set `place` in the route context; the sidebar, the breadcrumbs (the Workspace's and Site's names first) and the context switcher read it. A page reads `Route.useRouteContext().place.permissions` to decide what to offer, never a role name. A page sets its breadcrumb with `staticData: { crumb: "<message key>" }`, or, when its name is data (a Post Type's name, an Entry's title), with `crumb` in what its loader answers; `pageTitleOf` makes such a page's `<title>`.
- **Pages** inside the shell are routes under `routes/_admin/` (signed in only). Start a page with `PageHeader`; use the shadcn components from `@webcraft/ui/components/*`.
- **The setup wizard's steps** are the list in `ui/setup/steps.tsx`: each has a title, a hint, a stage and a panel. After `two-factor` comes `email` (the Install SMTP, which can be skipped), then `modules` (approving the first-party Modules, ticked). The last step finishes setup.
- **Site › Design system** (`sites.$siteId.design-system.tsx`, Structure): see "The Design system".
- **Structure screens for the builder**: Site › Templates (`sites.$siteId.templates.tsx`) and Site › Components (`sites.$siteId.components.tsx`) open the visual builder, which is full-screen, outside the shell (`routes/builder.$siteId.$.tsx`, see "The visual builder").
- **The Filter builder** (`routes/_admin/sites.$siteId.filters.index.tsx` and `…filters.$filterId.tsx`, `ui/filters/`; Structure, in the shell, wide): Site › Filters lists the Filters (their markup, status and where they are placed) and makes one for a query Loop without a Filter. A Filter's builder keeps its Draft in the browser (`model.ts`) until Save draft and Publish Filter; the header has the Live / On submit switch (`Segmented`: toggle buttons in a fieldset). Its tabs (`?tab=`, and `?control=` for the selected control) sit beside the live preview (`Tabs` with `aside`, beside them from the `2xl` breakpoint): **Controls** (`ControlsTab.tsx`: the list, each control selected to edit, moved by its handle, by Alt with the up and down arrows or from its menu, every move and addition said in a live region (`Announcer`); the Add control menu with the eight kinds, the one-of kinds disabled once there; the selected control's settings, `ControlSettings.tsx`, with WebCraft's own words as placeholders from `@webcraft/renderer/filters/words`, each problem under its setting, and the markup placing it on its own with Copy), **Layout** (`LayoutTab.tsx`: the presets as radio cards, the phone choice, where it is placed, each with "Open in visual builder"), **Settings** and **Translations** (Staff's own words per language). The preview (`Preview.tsx`) asks `filters.preview` a moment after the Draft or the previewed choices change and writes the answer into the visual builder's canvas frame (`/api/builder/canvas`, sandboxed, no script, out of the tab order), at a desktop's width (1024 px, scaled to fit) or a phone's, emulating the Filter script (Live mode's Apply hidden, sliders shown), the selected control outlined; choices are made in its address box (the keyboard's way) or with the pointer in the frame. The preview is a server-rendered fragment in that frame rather than an iframe of the Site: it shows unsaved changes, needs no Staging address or Site cookie, and runs none of the Site's scripts in the admin.
- **Content screens** (`ui/content/`): Post Types & Fields (`routes/_admin/sites.$siteId.post-types*.tsx`, Structure: `structure.edit`; a Post Type other than Pages and the Globals have a Data tab), Loops (`sites.$siteId.loops*.tsx`, Structure: `LoopEditor.tsx` with the query builder, the Loop items' Fields and values, and the Data tab), each Post Type's Entries and the Entry editor (`sites.$siteId.entries.$postTypeId*.tsx`) and the Globals editor (`sites.$siteId.globals.tsx`).
  - **Tabs** (`ui/Tabs.tsx`): the ARIA tabs pattern (one tab stop, the arrow keys, Home and End), panels kept mounted once shown; screens keep the open tab in the address (`?tab=data`), so a check can open it.
  - **The Data tab** (`DataTab.tsx`): a real item's values from the Draft, as JSON in a read-only text area (reached, scrolled and selected from the keyboard), and the bindings that read each, each with a Copy button that says what it copied in a live region (the Clipboard API, else the copy command).
  - **Field rules in the browser**: the Field types, their traits (translatable by default, can be required, can have a default) and limits come from `@webcraft/core/field-rules`, which imports nothing, so the admin reads the same rules the content store checks. Never import `@webcraft/core` itself into browser code (types only).
  - **One control per Field type** in `FieldValues.tsx` (`FieldValue`, used by the Entry editor, the Globals editor and a Field's default), and one set of settings per type in `FieldSettingsPanel.tsx`. A new Field type adds its control and its settings there; `offeredTypes` (`FieldBuilder.tsx`) decides which types a new Field can be where it is (a Body field only on a Post Type that has none, at the top). Media Fields choose files through `MediaPicker` (`ui/media/`, built on the Media library's preview pieces in `ui/media/preview.ts`); rich text is edited in `RichTextEditor`, which cleans pasted HTML with the same cleaner the server uses (`@webcraft/renderer/rich-text`).
  - **The Field builder** (`FieldBuilder.tsx`, `StructureEditor.tsx`) keeps Fields as drafts (`field-drafts.ts`) until the screen saves the whole list (`postTypes.update`, `globals.setFields`); a problem the server answers (`fields[2].fields[0].key`) is located on its Field and setting, and shown there (in the server's words) and in a list above the Fields.
  - **Entries and the Globals are co-edited**: their editors keep the Draft in its shared document (`useCoEditedValues`, see "Co-editing"), with who else is editing and where. Other Drafts save by themselves through `useAutosave` (`autosave.ts`): a moment after typing pauses, the changes (merged by value) go to their save, one save at a time; a refused change is kept and sent again with the next one, leaving the page saves what is waiting, and the browser asks before a tab with unsaved changes closes. A save that loses a race shows `CHANGED_MEANWHILE`'s words with Try again and Reload.
  - **Problems in words**: `problemText` (`problems.ts`) words every rule code (`VALUE_RULES`) from the Field's settings, and values refused as the wrong kind by what they are; `errorMessage` (`ui/errors.ts`) words the content error codes (`ENTRY_*`, `GLOBALS_*`, `PATH_TAKEN`, `PAGE_*`, `POST_TYPE_*`, `CONFIRMATION_INVALID` by its `problem`); `settingProblemText` words a refused setting from its `SETTING_RULES` code (`content.settingProblems.*`, with Field and Block types by their names), falling back to the server's English for a code it doesn't know.
  - **The block editor** (`ui/content/body/`): a Body field's Blocks, each edited in place, in our own small editor over the Block JSON (no editor library: the same contenteditable approach as `RichTextEditor`). `InlineEditor` is one piece of formatted text (the browser types into it; `inline-dom.ts` reads it back as runs and places the caret by character); `BodyEditor` holds the Blocks and the keys between them (Enter splits, Backspace at the start joins or turns a Block back into a paragraph, the arrow keys cross Blocks, Ctrl/⌘ with B, I, E, K and Shift+X format, Alt+Shift with the arrows moves a Block, Ctrl/⌘ with `]` and `[` nest list items; Tab always leaves); `BlockMenu` is the `/` menu and Add Block (a combobox over a list box, filtered by what the Field allows, `choices.ts`). Markdown shortcuts turn a paragraph into a Block as it is typed (`## `, `### `, `#### `, `- `, `* `, `1. `, `> `, then ```` ``` ```` and `---` on their own). Checks are worked out in the browser with the renderer's `bodyChecks` and shown, with the server's problems, under their Block.
  - **Every drag has a keyboard way**: lists that drag by a handle (`useDragOrder` in `reorder.tsx`) also offer Move up and Move down in each item's menu, and say where the item went (`Announcer`).
  - **Languages**: `sites.get` answers the Site's `languages` (`{ default, all }`, the Default language first), carried in the Site's `place`; the language tabs of the editors and the chips of the Entries list show them all.
- **Times** are shown in UTC with `formatUtc`, and days with `formatUtcDate` (`ui/format.ts`), in one fixed form: the server renders pages first, and its date formatting differs from the browser's in small ways, which breaks hydration.
- **shadcn components** come from preset `b1Fk3bmIS` (`style: base-vega`, zinc, RTL on, see `packages/ui/components.json`). Add one with `bunx shadcn add <name>` in `packages/ui`, then run `bun run lint`: generated code sometimes fails the accessibility rules; fix the component (several are marked `WebCraft:`) rather than switching a rule off, and give every visible or screen-reader string a prop so it can be translated.

## Testing

Tests live in `tests/`, grouped by area, and run with `bun run test` from the repo root. They test at the spec's seams and check what a person, visitor or client would observe: HTTP responses, emails that went out, outgoing calls, job outcomes and later responses. They never import internal functions or read tables directly; refactoring without changing behaviour must not break a test.

**Seam 1** is the web process's handler, booted in-process against a real Postgres. `createTestInstall()` from `tests/harness/index.ts` gives each test file its own Install:

| Part | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `install.secrets` | Every secret of the Install (`set`, `useWith`, `describe`, `remove`), for any owner, encrypted under its Master key: for tests of the secret store. WebCraft's own code reaches a Workspace's or Site's secrets through the data layer. |
| `install.env` | The environment a real process needs to run as this Install: `DATABASE_URL`, `WEBCRAFT_ADMIN_HOST` and `WEBCRAFT_SECRET_KEY`. |
| `install.upgrade(request)`, `install.webProcess()` | A WebSocket upgrade sent through the handler as it is (answering its refusal or the socket to open), and another web process on the same database (its own handler, documents and LISTEN connection) whose `upgrade` a browser's `openSocket(…, { through })` can use. |
| `install.request(url, init)` | Sends a standard `Request` through the handler, in process; a `Request` given keeps the client address `fromAddress` gave it. Always use a full URL: the hostname decides where it goes. `install.adminUrl("/healthz")` builds admin-host URLs (`https://admin.test/...`); a Site's Staging address is `https://<slug>.sites.test` (`sites.get` answers it as `stagingUrl`), which shows Drafts to the people reviewing it only: for what visitors see, put the Site live first (`goLive`, below). |
| `install.browser()` | A person's browser on the admin: it keeps cookies and sends the admin's origin. `browser.api` is the typed Management API client (`await browser.api.session.current()`); errors reject with their API `code`. `install.browser({ origin })` sends requests from another site's pages. Each browser comes from its own client address (`browser.address`, which a test can change); give `{ address }` to choose one (null: one the web process can't tell), `{ userAgent }` for the browser it says it is, and `{ headers }` for headers on every request (a proxy's `X-Forwarded-For`, a CDN's visitor header, or a client's forgery of them). |
| `install.createSuperadmin(account?)` | Creates the Superadmin as `webcraft admin create-superadmin` does and answers the account (email, password `TEST_PASSWORD`). |
| `install.superadmin()` | A browser signed in as the Superadmin (made first when there is none). |
| `install.createWorkspace(name?)`, `install.createSite(workspaceId, { name?, slug? })` | A Workspace or Site, created by the Superadmin through the API. |
| `install.createStaff({ workspaces })` | A Staff member, the way people become one: invited, then accepting the link in the email. Answers `{ account, personId, browser }`. |
| `install.createMember({ workspaceId, role, sites? })` | A Member the way people become one: the Superadmin invites them and they accept the link with a new account, signed in. `install.addMembership` gives an existing person a Membership directly, for tests of what a Membership may never be. |
| `install.signIn(account, browser?)` | A new browser, signed in; `browser` takes the same options as `install.browser()`. |
| `install.setupToken()` | The setup token the web process printed when it last started (the harness starts like the web process, printing one while there's no Superadmin). |
| `install.jobs.send(job, payload)` | Enqueues a job as the web process would. |
| `install.drainJobs()` | First hands the 404 log's misses the Site pipeline gathered to the job queue (`services.modules.flushMisses`), then runs every job that is due by the test clock, including jobs those jobs enqueue and scheduled jobs whose time came, and returns `{ completed, failed }`. It works the queue as the worker does, so it checks in as one first (Install health's worker part). `drainJobs({ only: ["media.process"] })` runs only those jobs, leaving the rest queued (to see what visitors get between two steps); `batchSize: 1` makes two drains running at once share a queue's jobs, as two workers do. |
| `install.lastEmailTo(address)` | Runs the due jobs, then answers the newest email to the address: sent through the Install SMTP or, while it isn't set, written to the log (`{ to, subject, text }`). `inviteLinkIn(email)` finds an invite link in one. |
| `install.sendSystemEmail(email)` | Sends a System email as WebCraft's features do (`services.email.send`); drain the jobs to send it. |
| `install.mail` | The fake Mailer: `sent` holds every email the server took (with the `smtp` settings it went through, password included), `attempts` every try; `fail(response)` makes every send fail with that answer until `accept()`; `rejectRecipient(address)` refuses one address for good; `clear()` forgets them. |
| `install.storageDir` | The Install's local-disk storage folder (`WEBCRAFT_STORAGE_DIR`): a new temporary folder per test Install, removed by `close()`. Its layout (`<workspace id>/<site id>/media/…`) is what Superadmins back up and mount, so tests may look at it. |
| `createFakeS3(install.http, settings)` | A fake S3-compatible bucket on the fake outgoing HTTP (`tests/harness/fake-s3.ts`): it keeps objects, answers ranges, takes multipart uploads (`openUploads` counts those neither finished nor aborted), and checks every request's Signature Version 4 with its own implementation, refusing a wrongly signed one like a real bucket. |
| `install.http` | The fake outgoing HTTP: `respond(method, url or RegExp, { status, body, headers } or a function)`; bodies may be text or bytes, and each recorded request has `body` (text), `bytes` and `address`: the address it connected to, the one the caller pinned it to (`connectTo`) or else the first `install.dns` answers at that moment, as a runtime's `fetch` resolves the name again; `requests` holds every call. A request nobody prepared an answer for fails like an unreachable server. The leaked-password range service (`LEAKED_PASSWORD_RANGE`) answers that no password is leaked unless a test answers it otherwise; `reset()` forgets that answer too, so the lookup is then unreachable. |
| `install.clock` | Time stands still until `advance(ms)` or `set(date)`. The job queue reads the same time inside Postgres, so a job sent with `startAfter` in an hour runs only after `advance` passes it, and retries wait for their delay. `advance` jumps like a process that slept, so moving days ahead is quick. Sessions expire by this clock too. Starts at `2026-03-02T09:00:00Z` unless you pass `{ now }`. |
| `install.logs` | Every log entry the Install wrote, redacted as a process writes it. |
| `install.disk` | The room on the disk local storage is on, for Install health: plenty unless a test calls `set({ freeBytes, totalBytes })` (null: it can't be read). |
| `install.tablesHolding(text)` | The tables anywhere in the Install's database (WebCraft's, Modules', the job queue's) holding `text` in a row: for what must never be kept, such as a visitor's address. It reads no table's layout. |
| `install.dns` | The fake DNS the guarded fetcher asks: `answer(name, addresses)`; any other name doesn't resolve. `answerInTurn(name, [first, second, …])` gives one answer per lookup, then keeps giving the last (DNS rebinding). `lookups` holds every name asked. The admin host resolves to the Install's addresses (`TEST_INSTALL_ADDRESSES`, documentation addresses `203.0.113.10` and `2001:db8::10`, also its `WEBCRAFT_PUBLIC_ADDRESSES`). |
| `install.ai` | The fake AI providers (`InstallPorts.ai`): `answer(text or a function of the request)` for image descriptions, `translateWith((text, request) => translation, usage?)` for translations (`taggedTranslation` in `tests/harness/fake-ai.ts`), `reportWith(request => object or text, usage?)` for Reports' text (`reportRequests()` lists what was sent), `fail(problem?)`; `requests` holds every call, key included, `translations()` the translation calls. Unavailable until a test answers. `createTestInstall({ aiProviders: "http" })` uses the real providers on `install.http` instead. |
| `install.pdf` | The fake PDF renderer (`tests/harness/fake-pdf.ts`): each PDF holds the page's text after a PDF header, so tests read what a Report says from its email's attachment with `pdfText`; `rendered` holds every page handed to it, `fail(problem)` makes renders fail until `work()`. `tests/reports/chromium.test.ts` runs the worker's real headless Chromium (Playwright's, or `WEBCRAFT_CHROMIUM`; skipped where none is installed). |
| `install.tls` | The fake TLS the Domain check asks: `answer(name, { expiresAt, trusted? })`, or `null` for none; any other name answers with no certificate. `probes` holds every name asked. |
| `install.connectDomain(name, { certificate? })` | Points a domain at the Install, as its DNS records would: it resolves to the Install's first address, every `http(s)://<name>/…` request the Install makes reaches its own handler, and it answers HTTPS with a certificate valid for 90 more days (or the one given; null for none). Move the clock a minute and drain the jobs to have it verified (`tests/domains/`). |
| `install.restart({ masterKey?, modules? })` | Starts the Install again, as web and worker do (with another Master key, or other Modules, such as a new version of one, when given), running their start-up checks and printing a new setup token while there's no Superadmin. |
| `install.migrate({ modules?, backup? })` | Runs the `migrate` step on the Install's database as `webcraft admin migrate` does, with the Install's Modules (or the ones given), and answers what it did (`appliedMigrations`, `createdQueues`, `backup`). Without `backup: true` it skips the Backup, as `--no-backup` does. |
| `install.backup({ to? })` | Takes a Backup as `webcraft admin backup` does: where Install settings › Backups says, or into the file or folder `to`. |
| `install.restore(archive, { replace? })` | Restores a Backup into the Install's database as `webcraft admin restore` does (with the Install's Master key, storage and the test suite's Modules), then starts the Install on it with a job queue, as web and worker would after it. `replace: { confirm }` is `--force` with the database's name. |
| `install.checkUpdate()` | Reads the release feed now, as `webcraft admin check-update` does. |
| `install.moveMedia({ to, keepSource? })` | Moves the stored Media files to the bucket or to local disk, as `webcraft admin media move` does. |
| `install.backupDir` | The Install's Backups folder (`WEBCRAFT_BACKUP_DIR`): a new temporary folder per test Install, removed by `close()`. |
| `install.close()` | Stops the Install and drops its database. |

`createTestInstall({ database: "empty" })` gives an Install whose database nobody migrated, and `{ database: "unreachable" }` one whose database doesn't answer. Every test Install starts with the Master key `TEST_MASTER_KEY` unless you pass `{ masterKey }`; tests never use the key in `.env`. Its sites domain is `sites.test` (`TEST_SITES_DOMAIN`) unless you pass `{ sitesDomain }` (`null` for none), its admin host `admin.test` unless you pass `{ adminHost }`, its edge `caddy` unless you pass `{ edge }`, its public addresses `TEST_INSTALL_ADDRESSES` unless you pass `{ publicAddresses }`, and `WEBCRAFT_TRUSTED_PROXIES` unset (the edge's default: test browsers' own addresses, `10.x.y.z`, are private, so they count as trusted proxies, which matters only when a test sends `X-Forwarded-For`) unless you pass `{ trustedProxies }`. Import from URL waits 30 seconds unless you pass `{ mediaImportTimeoutMs }` (to test a slow server without waiting). The page cache keeps the web process's number of filtered pages per Site unless you pass `{ filterVariants }` (to test the limit), and a sitemap holds the protocol's limits unless you pass `{ sitemapLimits: { urls, bytes } }` (to test splitting). A Site export reads 500 rows at a time and keeps its archive in 32 MiB parts unless you pass `{ siteTransfer: { batchRows, partBytes } }` (to test streaming with a small Site; see "Site export and import"). The update check reads `DEFAULT_UPDATE_FEED` unless you pass `{ updateFeed }` (`null` for `off`); nothing answers it unless a test does (`install.dns.answer("api.github.com", […])` and `install.http.respond("GET", DEFAULT_UPDATE_FEED, …)`). Unexpected failures are also sent to an error tracker when you pass `{ errorDsn }`, and drains run the minute Install health check (and send Health alerts) only with `{ healthChecks: true }`. It loads no Modules unless you pass `{ modules }` (this build has no first-party Modules yet); every test database has had the migrations of the test suite's Modules (`TEST_MODULES` in `tests/harness/modules.ts`).

`tests/harness/images.ts` makes small images inside the tests (never files copied from anywhere): `photo({ width, height, format, orientation, camera })` (red left half, blue right half, so turning it shows; `camera: true` adds a camera make and GPS data), `heicPhoto` (through `sips` on macOS or `heif-enc` where libheif's tools are installed, as CI does; `canMakeHeic` says whether this machine can), `pdf()`, `upload(bytes, name, type)` for a browser-like `File`, and `imageInfo` / `pixel` to look at what the Install serves.

`tests/harness/staging.ts` is the Staging address as the people reviewing a Site use it, and a Site's visitors: `goLive(install, siteId)` puts a Site on its Primary domain `<slug>.live.test`, verified at once (as "Check now" does), and answers its origin; tests of what visitors see (published pages, the page cache, files, Form posts, Consent records, Modules' routes) request it there, since the Staging address shows Drafts to reviewers only. `openStaging(install, adminBrowser, siteId, path)` opens the Staging address from the admin (`staging.open`) in a new browser holding the Staging cookie (`page(path)` loads its pages); `stagingVisitor(install, slug)` is a browser it doesn't know yet, and `givePassword(visitor, password, path)` gives the Site's staging password on its page. The Staging address's own tests are in `tests/staging/`, Publish all's in `tests/publishing/`.

`tests/harness/code-mode.ts` turns Code mode on in an Edit mode browser (`openCodeMode`, then `edit("code/open", …)` and the other `code/…` requests) and reads Code mode's tags in a page (`codeTagsIn`). `tests/harness/edit-mode.ts` opens Edit mode as a browser does: `openEditMode(install, adminBrowser, siteId, path)` asks for the link with `editMode.open`, follows it in a new browser on the Site's origin and loads the page; `page(path)` loads pages with the edit cookie, `edit(action, body, options)` sends edit requests as the editing bar does (`options` drop the header token or send another origin, to test refusals). A test browser sends `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` for requests to its own origin. `openEditMode(…, path, { safeMode: true })` follows an "Edit in Safe mode" link.

`tests/harness/xml.ts` reads XML as a crawler does, strictly (`parseXml`), and checks sitemaps and sitemap indexes against the protocol (`readSitemap`, `readSitemapIndex`); the discovery files' tests are in `tests/discovery/`. `tests/harness/seo.ts` reads what a page tells search engines as a crawler does (`titleIn`, `metaName`, `metaProperty`, `canonicalIn`, `jsonLdNode`), and `withoutStructuredData` sets a page's JSON-LD block aside for checks that a page holds no `<script>` at all; the SEO tests are in `tests/seo/`. `tests/harness/site-safety.ts` holds the one check every Site test uses on pages: `expectSafePage(response, policy)` checks the fixed security headers, the policy the page is under (`strict`, `relaxed`, `reportOnly` or `editMode`) and that the HTML carries no inline code (`expectNoInlineCode`: no script without `src` or with code (a JSON-LD data block must be JSON with no `<` or `>`), no `<style>`, `style` or `on…` attribute, no `javascript:` address; attack text escaped into values doesn't count), and answers the HTML. `expectSiteHeaders` checks any other answer (files, redirects, refusals). Use them for every new built-in feature's output. `tests/harness/two-factor.ts` is a person's authenticator app: `totpCode(secret, at, stepOffset)` makes the code an app shows, from the key the admin answered; `addAuthenticator(install, browser)` adds one as a person does and answers the key and backup codes; `signInWithAuthenticator` signs in with password and code (moving the clock a step first, so the code is new). `tests/harness/passkeys.ts` is a software authenticator: `softwareAuthenticator({ algorithm })` makes passkeys (`create(options)`) and signs in with them (`get(options)`), answering with the JSON a browser gives, and can be made to misbehave (`tamper`: another origin or relying party, no user verification, another key, a count going back). Their tests are in `tests/auth/` (`authenticator.test.ts`, `passkeys.test.ts`, `two-factor-required.test.ts`, `two-factor-resets.test.ts`, `reset-2fa-command.test.ts`). `tests/harness/impersonation.ts` starts Impersonation as the Superadmin and Staff do: `withSecondFactor(install, person)` adds an authenticator app to a signed-in person (their browser stays signed in) and `impersonate(browser, personId, input)` starts it, confirming the password when asked; its tests are in `tests/impersonation/` (`rules.test.ts`, `account.test.ts`, `recorded.test.ts`) and the Audit log's in `tests/audit/` (`reading.test.ts`, `filters.test.ts`, `retention.test.ts`). `tests/harness/api-tokens.ts` is a script with an API token: `createApiToken(browser, input)` makes one as the browser's person (confirming their password when asked) and `tokenClient(install, secret)` calls `/api/v1` with it (`api`, typed like the admin's client, its answers plain JSON; `request(path, init)` for anything else). The tests of tokens, their limits and the OpenAPI document are in `tests/api/`. `tests/harness/fake-npm.ts` puts a fake package on the fake outgoing HTTP (`fakeNpmPackage(install, name, versions)`, real gzipped archives with the registry's integrity hashes; `publish` adds a version, `tampered` serves one that doesn't match its hash), for Library tests.

The Consent banner's tests are in `tests/consent/`: the setting, what pages hold back in each mode, the words as Content, `{{@consentSettings}}` and Consent records (a visitor's `POST /_wc/consent` is a standard `Request` with `fromAddress`). Tests about something else that render outside scripts or videos set their Site to `none`.

Every test Install reads the real countries file format from a small file of its own (`tests/harness/countries.ts`): `203.0.113.0/24` is in Ireland, `198.51.100.0/24` in Italy, `192.0.2.0/24` in France and `2001:db8::/32` in Germany; the test browsers' `10.x.x.x` addresses are in no country it knows. `tests/harness/analytics.ts` is a visitor's browser sending what the collection script sends (`analyticsVisitor(install, origin, { address, userAgent })`: `view(path)`, `send(event)`, `answer(choices)` on the Consent banner), `liveSite` puts a Site on a verified domain with Site analytics on (Staging addresses are left out), and `analyticsApi(browser)` calls the Module's procedures. Site analytics' tests are in `tests/analytics/`, the served collection script run in a stand-in page among them (`script.test.ts`).

`tests/harness/forms.ts` is a visitor's browser filling in Forms without JavaScript: `formVisitor(install)` loads a page (`open`), reads a Form's hidden inputs (`form(n)`, `formIn(html, n)`) and sends it as a browser posts it (`send`: the Site's `Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Site`, the page as `Referer`, its own client address; options leave out or forge what a test needs). The time trap needs time to pass: `await install.clock.advance(5000)` between loading and sending.

`tests/harness/co-editing.ts` is Co-editing as editors do it: `openDraft(install, browser, siteId, item)` opens a Draft's shared document through the handler's WebSocket upgrade, in process (`browser.openSocket`: the browser's cookies and page origin), with WebCraft's own client (`createDraftClient`, the one the admin uses); the editor `change`s the Draft (the JSON it should become), `place`s its person, reads `draft()`, `participants()`, `messages` and `refusals`, waits with `until`, `untilDraft` and `saved()`, and can `disconnect()` and `reconnect()` (changes made between go when it is back). `openDraftInEditMode(edit, item)` opens one from a page in Edit mode; `upgradeDraft` and `upgradeDraftInEditMode` answer the handler's answer as it is, for refusals. Co-editing's tests are in `tests/co-editing/` (`support.ts` makes a Site in English and French with Staff, an Editor and an Author), the editors in `tests/browser/co-editing.browser.ts` (presence, offline then read-only with Playwright's clock and a routed socket, Edit mode, the Globals).

`tests/harness/cli.ts` is a developer's computer with `webcraft` on it: `cliUser(install)` runs the CLI in process (`runCli` from `@webcraft/cli/run`) with its fetch pointed at the Install's handler, the Install's clock (its waits move the clock on), a config folder of its own and sign-ins in a file (never this computer's keychain); `run(args, { env, stdin, answers, whileWaiting, version })` answers `{ code, stdout, stderr }` (`answers` runs it as on a terminal, `version` as another `webcraft` version). `signInCli` runs `webcraft login` and approves its code in the admin while it waits (`approveDevice`, `userCodeIn`). Its tests are in `tests/cli/`; the server commands are run as real processes there too (`admin-commands.test.ts`), and `compiled.test.ts` compiles this computer's `webcraft` and runs it against the test Install through a local port.

`tests/harness/mcp.ts` is an AI client through the official MCP client: `connectMcp(install, token, { era, answer })` connects with a bearer token (an API token, or an access token), `era: "modern"` speaking the 2026-07-28 revision and `answer` answering the server's questions; `approveAiClient(install, browser, approval)` registers a client, has the person approve it in the admin and exchanges the code for tokens; `connectMcpWithOAuth` lets the client find its way in by itself (discovery, registration, PKCE); `answerOf` and `refusalOf` read a tool's result. MCP tests are in `tests/mcp/`.

`tests/harness/trees.ts` writes element trees briefly: `tree(element("article", [element("h1", [value("title")])]))`, `text("…")`, and attributes as `element("a", [text("More")], { href: [{ type: "text", text: "/blog/" }, value("slug")] })`; comments and Conditions are written as their stored shapes, and `outlet()` is the Site template's `{{@outlet}}`, and `placement(componentId, { heading: "Hi" })` places a Component. `tests/sites/first-site.test.ts` shows a whole Site: Post Type, Entry template, Entry, publish, the rendered page and the page cache. Most Template tests write Template markup instead (`templates.saveEntryTemplateMarkup`), as `tests/sites/template-markup.test.ts` does; `tests/sites/loops.test.ts` and `tests/sites/archives.test.ts` show Loops, pagination and Archive templates on pages; `tests/filters/` shows Filters (`shop.ts` builds a shop with every kind of control, live on its domain). Property-based tests use fast-check (`tests/markup/round-trip.test.ts`): generate inputs, drive them through the API, and check what comes back.

A seam-1 test:

```ts
import { afterAll, beforeAll, expect, test } from "bun:test";
import { samplePing } from "@webcraft/core";

import { createTestInstall, type TestInstall } from "../harness/index.ts";

let install: TestInstall;
beforeAll(async () => {
	install = await createTestInstall();
});
afterAll(async () => {
	await install.close();
});

test("the sample job emails the status of a URL", async () => {
	install.http.respond("GET", "https://status.example.org/ping", { status: 204 });

	await install.jobs.send(samplePing, { url: "https://status.example.org/ping", notify: "ops@agency.example" });
	const report = await install.drainJobs();

	expect(report.failed).toEqual([]);
	expect(install.mail.sent.map((email) => email.subject)).toEqual(["https://status.example.org/ping answered 204"]);
});
```

`tests/worker/sample-job.test.ts` shows the job drain, the fake outgoing HTTP and the clock; `tests/email/` shows the fake mailer and `install.sendSystemEmail`; `tests/web/health.test.ts` shows the database modes. Tests in one file share their Install, so give each test its own data (its own Workspace, its own email address) or create an Install per test when they must not see each other. `tests/worker/node-worker.test.ts` starts the real worker with `node`, the way to check something runs on Node; give it `install.env`. `tests/outgoing-http/pinned-address.test.ts` calls real servers on 127.0.0.1 and ::1 with both processes' outgoing HTTP, Bun's in the test and Node's in a `node` process (`node-calls.ts`, one JSON call per line); its HTTPS servers use certificates from `createTestCertificateAuthority()` (`tests/harness/certificates.ts`), which issues certificates for made-up names and addresses, trusted only by a client given its `cert` as `ca`. It also sends a System email with the real Mailer to `startSmtpServer()` (`tests/harness/smtp-server.ts`), a real SMTP server in the test process, which the browser tests use too (the production web process sends test emails with the real Mailer). Start a real `bun` process (the CLI, the web entry) in an empty directory when a test leaves a setting out on purpose: Bun fills missing variables from the `.env` in the directory it starts in (see `tests/secrets/start-refusal.test.ts`).

**Workspace isolation**: every procedure that takes a `workspaceId` or `siteId` gets one line in `tests/isolation/procedures.test.ts`, proving it works on the caller's own Workspace and refuses another's (404 or 403). The last test there fails when a procedure is missing:

```ts
isolation.refuses("sites.list", (api, { workspaceId }) => api.sites.list({ workspaceId }), { as: ["staff", "editor"] });
```

`as` names who calls it (Staff assigned to that one Workspace by default, or a Member of it). Each public procedure's line also runs through `/api/v1` with a token the Superadmin made for that one Workspace (refused there for a Sensitive procedure), so a token never reaches another Workspace even when its person does. The helper is `workspaceIsolation` in `tests/harness/isolation.ts`.

**Seam 2** is a real browser, for accessibility (and later the editors). `tests/browser/harness.ts` starts a test Install served by the production web process (`bun run build` first; started through `tests/browser/web.ts`, which hands it the test suite's Modules) on `http://admin.localhost:<port>`, with Sites' Staging addresses on `http://<site slug>.sites.localhost:<port>` (`browserInstall.siteUrl(slug, path)`, for Edit mode and the Staging address's own checks) and their Primary domains on `http://<site slug>.live.localhost:<port>` (`browserInstall.goLive(siteId)`, then `liveUrl(slug, path)`, for what visitors see), and Chromium through Playwright; `tests/browser/checks.ts` holds the checks; files are named `*.browser.ts` so `bun test` leaves them out, and `bun run test:browser` runs them. Every check runs with the device set to light and to dark (the admin follows the device).

- `expectAccessibleScreen(page, where)`: axe-core finds no violation of WCAG 2.0, 2.1 or 2.2 at A or AA, nor of its best practices; the first Tab reaches "Skip to main content" and following it moves focus into the `<main>` it points at; Tab visits every link, button and field in page order (a date or time box once, though the browser stops on each of its parts), each with a visible focus indicator; no positive `tabindex`; Tab always moves on (no traps); the page logged no errors.
- `expectOverlayUsable(page, trigger, "menu" | "dialog", where)`: opens a menu or dialog from the keyboard, checks it with axe, and Escape closes it with focus back on the trigger.

`browserInstall.signIn(page, account)` signs in through the sign-in page the first time for an account and hands later pages the same session: the web process limits auth requests per client address, and every page comes from this machine. `browserInstall.signInWithOldIdentityCheck(page, account, minutesAgo)` signs a page in with a session whose identity check is old, so the next Sensitive action opens "Confirm it's you" (the web process runs on the real clock, so the test Install makes that session with its own clock set back). Real processes started by tests (the CLI, the web process here) do the real leaked-password lookup, or accept the password when offline, so their test passwords must not be on any leaked list.

`startBrowserInstall()` also gives the test Install behind the web process (`browserInstall.install`), to prepare data through the Management API, send System emails and drain jobs before the page opens (see `tests/browser/email.browser.ts`).

**Adding a screen** is one line in `tests/browser/screens.ts`: its name, its path and who looks at it (`superadmin`, `staff`, `owner` or `signed-out`). A screen inside a Workspace or Site gives its path as a function of the shared fixture (`({ siteId }) => `/sites/${siteId}``); prepare more data in `admin-screens.browser.ts` through `install.install`, the same Install in the test process. For a state a path can't reach (a wizard step, an error shown, an open menu), write a test like `tests/browser/setup-wizard.browser.ts`: drive the page there with Playwright, then call `expectAccessibleScreen` or `expectOverlayUsable`. Don't retest behaviour seam 1 covers.

**The visual builder** is checked in `tests/browser/builder.browser.ts`: its top bar, Layers panel, style panel, insert menu and Template markup view in light and dark, every Layers panel move from the keyboard (checked by what is saved), the insert menu, a Media image, the canvas, the style panel, "Make it a Field" and Publish. The canvas shows Staff's own Template (Checks look at it) and runs no script, so the checks leave it out (`expectAccessibleScreen(page, where, { exclude })`; axe can't look inside a frame without scripts). Playwright's mouse runs a script in every frame to watch for native drags, so a drag on the canvas is tested as the pointer events a drag makes, sent to the canvas page.

**Code mode** is checked in `tests/browser/code-mode.browser.ts`: the panels in light and dark, choosing a suggestion and moving between the panels from the keyboard, the preview while typing (markup and CSS, under the strict policy), Format, selecting an element, Run and the console, and the conflict prompt. **Edit mode** is checked in `tests/browser/edit-mode.browser.ts`: the page with the editing bar, its menus, editing text and rich text from the keyboard, and focus never under the bar. The checks see inside open shadow roots (the bar draws itself in one), count text edited in place as a tab stop, and ignore the inline-style reports axe itself causes on a page under a strict CSP.

**Dashboards** are checked in `tests/browser/dashboards.browser.ts`: a Dashboard with every shape of Widget and a chart shown as a table, the Widget picker, a Widget's menu and editor, moving and resizing a Widget from the keyboard (checked by what is saved, and said in the live region), a Workspace Dashboard, Site › Goals with its Goal editor and Site › Funnels with its Funnel editor (a step moved from the keyboard), in light and dark. The web process counts by the real clock, so the test sends its visits at the real time. Their lists are lines of `screens.ts`. Seam-1 tests are in `tests/dashboards/` and `tests/analytics/` (`goals.test.ts`, `funnels.test.ts`).

**Reports and Share links** are checked in `tests/browser/reports.browser.ts`: a Dashboard's Reports page with a history (as Staff and as an Owner), the Report dialog and a Report's menu, the review dialog, the Share dialog and making a link from the keyboard, and a Share link's page at desktop and phone widths and its password page (also after a wrong password, then given from the keyboard), in light and dark. Install settings › Report branding, Install › Services list, Workspace › Report branding and Site › Report branding are lines of `screens.ts`. Seam-1 tests are in `tests/reports/`.

**Site › Redirects** is checked in `tests/browser/redirects.browser.ts`: the rules (one added when a path changed), the 404 log and its ignored paths, the rule dialog and a rule's menu from the keyboard, Add redirect from the log starting at the target, and a refused target said beside its field, in light and dark.

**Forms** are checked in `tests/browser/forms.browser.ts`: a Form on a Site's page, the same page sent back with errors (the error summary takes focus, its links move focus to their fields) and the message once sent, in light and dark; the Forms list and each tab of the Form builder; a Form's Submissions tab (a Submission open, the Spam folder) and Emails tab as an Owner sees them, and Site › Email (SMTP), in light and dark; building a Form, reading Submissions (opening one, marking it unread, the delete dialog) and routing a Notification from the keyboard. Their seam-1 tests are in `tests/forms/` (`emails.test.ts`, `notifications.test.ts`, `submissions.test.ts`) and `tests/email/site-smtp.test.ts`.

**Imports** are checked in `tests/browser/imports.browser.ts`: the Import list, the steps of a new Import (Source, Map and Preview from the keyboard), a run in progress, an Import report with its tabs and the Undo dialog, and the CSV side sheet an Owner opens from a Post Type's Entries. **The WordPress import** is checked in `tests/browser/wordpress-import.browser.ts`: the Source step with the export file uploaded in parts and surveyed, Map variant B with the live sample Entry and Preview from the keyboard, and a run's report with its leftovers and Redirect rules (the web process queues on the real clock, so the test catches the test Install's clock up before it drains). Their seam-1 tests are in `tests/imports/` (`support.ts` uploads a CSV file, starts a run and drains the worker; `wordpress.ts` uploads an export file in parts and runs a WordPress Import, with the made-up fixture `fixtures/old-mill-export.xml`; `wordpress-upload.test.ts`, `wordpress-run.test.ts` and `wordpress-again.test.ts`).

**The Media library** is checked in `tests/browser/media.browser.ts`: its dialogs (New folder, a folder's menu, Import from URL, Delete with where the file is used, Empty Trash, the Workspace's quota) from the keyboard, and setting a Focal point without a mouse; the Trash and a file's details are admin screens.

**SEO** is checked in `tests/browser/seo.browser.ts`: the Entry editor's SEO & sharing card with the SEO check's warnings, writing SEO fields from the keyboard, a Post Type's SEO tab (tokens, its Summary, the sitemap switch, the Schema type, a refusal's summary taking focus), Site › SEO, and Site › Crawlers (an Owner choosing the AI crawler setting from the keyboard, Staff's refused robots.txt line said beside the field), in light and dark.

**The Consent banner** is checked in `tests/browser/consent.browser.ts`: each layout in light and dark (axe and the keyboard, the dialog's focus kept inside, Cookie settings opening it again), answering it, a held-back video's placeholder, reduced motion, a page without JavaScript (`install.newPage(theme, { javaScript: false })`) and an outside consent service's answers.

**Site script safety** is checked in `tests/browser/site-scripts.browser.ts`: Site › Libraries and Site › Outside scripts with their menus and dialogs (a snippet's problems too), the "Edit on site" menu, and in Edit mode the notice a failing Site script shows, Safe mode opened from it and turned off again, and outside scripts' placeholders.

**History** is checked in `tests/browser/history.browser.ts`: the Entry editor's History card, a Revision compared with the Draft (what differs, then every part, then another language from the keyboard), a Component's markup line by line, the Restore dialog from the keyboard and a restore made without a mouse, Install settings › Revisions and a Post Type's Revisions tab, in light and dark. Seam-1 tests are in `tests/history/` (`support.ts` makes a Site in English and French with Staff, an Editor and an Author).

**Two-factor authentication** is checked in `tests/browser/two-factor.browser.ts`: the second sign-in step (a refused code, a backup code), Your account adding an authenticator app (QR code and key, a refused code, the backup codes shown and downloaded) and a passkey (Chromium's virtual authenticator through the DevTools protocol, then signing in with it), the screen a person who must use it lands on, the reminder, and the reset dialogs on the Members and Staff screens, in light and dark. The web process limits sign-ins per address and every page comes from this machine, so most of its pages are signed in with a session the test Install makes at the real time.

**Impersonation** is checked in `tests/browser/impersonation.browser.ts`: the start dialog from a Member's row menu and the Staff screen, started from the keyboard, the banner on a page (axe and the keyboard, said in a live region) and Stop, in light and dark. The Audit log screens and Your account › Activity are lines of `screens.ts`.

**Review** is checked in `tests/browser/review.browser.ts`: the Author's Submit for review and its dialog, an item In review for its Author (Withdraw) and for a reviewer (Send back and its dialog), a sent-back comment, the Site overview's Waiting for review with the sidebar's count, the Entries list's chips and Edit mode's Publish menu for an Author, in light and dark; and submitting, sending back (the comment required) and submitting a page in Edit mode without a mouse. Seam-1 tests are `tests/publishing/review.test.ts` and `tests/edit-mode/review.test.ts`.

**Publish all** is checked in `tests/browser/publish-all.browser.ts`: the list for Staff and for an Owner (Structure shown, never theirs to tick), in light and dark, opened and closed from the keyboard, and an item unticked and the rest published without a mouse. **The Staging address**'s page for people it doesn't know (with the password form, and after a wrong password) is checked in `tests/browser/staging.browser.ts`, in light and dark, and giving the password from the keyboard.

**Filters** are checked in `tests/browser/filters.browser.ts`: a Filter on a Site's page in light and dark (axe and the keyboard), Live mode updating the Loop in place and announcing the count, On submit mode applying with Apply, focus kept, Back, and the page without JavaScript sending the form. **The Filter builder and the layout presets** are checked in `tests/browser/filter-builder.browser.ts`: Site › Filters and each tab of the builder in light and dark (the preview's frame left out of axe), its menus, the control list's keyboard moves (said and kept when saved), adding a control; each preset on the Site in light and dark at a desktop's and a phone's width, a top bar's dropdown and the phone drawer from the keyboard (the drawer holding its choices until Show), the slider from the keyboard, a search box placed on its own in the header; and the visual builder opened with the Filter selected and placing a Filter's control from its insert menu. Their seam-1 tests are `tests/filters/layouts.test.ts` and `tests/filters/builder.test.ts`.

**Install health and the Error log** are lines of `screens.ts` (the fixture has a job failed for good, also in the Error log), and `tests/browser/install-health.browser.ts` checks the banner across the admin, the Discard, Discard all and Retry all dialogs from the keyboard, and a cause's details opened from the keyboard, in light and dark.

**Richer Forms** are checked in `tests/browser/form-steps.browser.ts`: steps, progress, conditional fields/sections/steps, Back/Next, client and server errors, an upload and success focus, and all steps without the script, in light and dark; the builder's step, file and condition controls, and private download links. Seam-1 tests are `tests/forms/steps.test.ts` and `uploads.test.ts`, including hidden values in spam, hidden file parts, multipart caps, quota separation, cross-Site/Workspace isolation, deletion/retention, storage failures and Notification attachment limits.

**Site pages** are checked in `tests/browser/site-pages.browser.ts`: an Entry's page and the 404 page, inside a Site template with a header, navigation and footer, prepared through `install.install` and opened with `install.siteUrl`. Built-in Site output that later tickets add (Components, Forms, Popups, the Consent banner) gets its line there.

```ts
export const ADMIN_SCREENS: readonly Screen[] = [
	// ...
	{ name: "Site › Overview", path: ({ siteId }) => `/sites/${siteId}`, as: "staff" },
];
```

## Conventions

- **Words**: use `GLOSSARY.md` terms exactly, in code, tests, UI text and commits. British spelling in prose ("licence", "normalise").
- **TypeScript**: strict, with explicit `.ts`/`.tsx` extensions on relative imports and only erasable syntax (no `enum`, `namespace` or parameter properties). That lets Node run shared code and the worker without a build step.
- **Runtimes** (ADR 0006): `packages/*` and `apps/worker` use standard Web and Node APIs only; Biome rejects the `Bun` global and `bun:*` imports there, and their tsconfigs don't load Bun's types. `apps/server`, `apps/cli`, `tests` and `scripts` may use Bun.
- **Edges go through ports**: time through `Clock`, email through `Mailer` (only the email sender calls it; features send System emails with `services.email.send`), calls to other servers through `services.http`. Tests replace them with fakes; nothing else is faked.
- **Secrets** go through `services.secrets` only: never in another table, a log line, a job payload or a response (see "Secrets and the Master key").
- **Workspace data** goes through the data layer (`services.data`), which runs `can()` first (ADR 0007); no query on tenant data without a Workspace, and no check of a role name: check a Permission.
- **Errors**: Site visitors and unknown hostnames see plain, unbranded answers. Unexpected errors are logged and answered with a plain 500.
- **Logs**: JSON lines through `Logger` (`services.log`), with fields rather than values pasted into the message (see "Logs"). A line at error level with an `error` is an unexpected failure and lands in the Error log; log failures WebCraft expects (an outside server that doesn't answer) at warn.
- **Licences**: every workspace's `package.json` says `"license": "AGPL-3.0-or-later"`; new dependencies must pass `bun run check:licences`.
- **Commits**: Gitmoji + Conventional Commits with the ticket number, such as `✨ feat(auth): add setup wizard (#50)`.
- **Before you finish**: `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck` and `bun run test` all pass.

## Reference-material denylist

Names of third-party products that informed WebCraft must never appear in anything tracked (ADR 0013, `docs/reference-material.md`). `scripts/check-denylist.sh` enforces it: it reads one case-insensitive extended regular expression per line from `WEBCRAFT_DENYLIST` or from the file named by `WEBCRAFT_DENYLIST_FILE`, and checks tracked files, file names, the messages of the commits in `--range` and a `--branch` name. It prints only where a match is (file and line, commit, branch), never the list or the matching text.

```sh
WEBCRAFT_DENYLIST_FILE=~/.config/webcraft/reference-denylist.txt bun run check:denylist -- --range main..HEAD
```

To check every commit locally, turn on the opt-in hooks (staged files before a commit, then the commit message):

```sh
git config core.hooksPath .githooks
export WEBCRAFT_DENYLIST_FILE=~/.config/webcraft/reference-denylist.txt   # in your shell profile
```

Without a list the check is skipped locally with a warning and fails in CI.

## CI

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on pull requests and on pushes to `main`:

- **Lint, typecheck, build and licences**.
- **Tests** against a Postgres 18 service, with the current Node LTS installed for the worker test.
- **Accessibility (browser)**: builds the admin, installs Playwright's Chromium and runs `bun run test:browser`.
- **Deploy files (Compose smoke test)**: `bun run test:deploy`.

`.github/workflows/cli.yml` compiles `webcraft` for the five platforms (`bun run build:cli -- --npm`) on pull requests that touch it and on pushes to `main`, runs the Linux and macOS binaries, and only on a release tag `v1.2.3` (matching `package.json`) attaches the binaries and `SHA256SUMS` to the GitHub release and publishes the npm packages (`@webcraft/cli` and `@webcraft/cli-<os>-<cpu>`, with provenance) with the `NPM_TOKEN` secret.

`.github/workflows/images.yml` publishes the web and worker images to the GitHub Container Registry for amd64 and arm64 (each built on a runner of its own architecture, since Bun can't run the admin's build under QEMU emulation; pushed by digest, then joined into one multi-platform tag) on pushes to `main` (`main`, `sha-<commit>`) and on release tags `v1.2.3` (`1.2.3`, `1.2`, `1`, `latest`), with OCI labels and the AGPL licence. Check workflow changes with `actionlint`.
- **Reference-material denylist** over tracked files, the commit messages being added and the branch name, with the list from the `REFERENCE_DENYLIST` repository secret. It fails when the secret is missing, as it is for pull requests from forks.

### Checks and Decorative images

Checks (`core/checks`, `renderer/checks.ts`) are advisory warnings, separate from the problems that prevent saving or publishing. `checks.list` collects a Site's Drafts in every Site language; `checks.entry` reads one Entry. Content warnings are available to people who can view the Site; Structure and Design system items are Staff-only. Editor links carry the language and Field/Block location or select the affected element in the builder. The Entry editor refreshes after Draft saves; Builder and Code mode preview unsaved changes.

Document rules (one H1, one main landmark and document language) run on a composed page. Reusable fragments have no missing-H1/main requirement. Resolved Template/Component values, Loops, Forms and Media are rendered before checking. Body warnings and Design system contrast calculations reuse their existing rules; headings are checked across the composition, including Component Blocks. SEO warnings come from the existing Entry report. These automated Checks do not certify WCAG compliance and do not cover a manual accessibility audit.

Decorative belongs to a placed image, never a Media record. An Image Block or `img` element can store `decorative: true`; false/absent means meaningful. Template markup represents it as `data-wc-decorative="true"`; exchange Markdown uses `::image{media="<id>" decorative="true"}`. Both formats retain the setting through normal saves and translations, including Block translation provenance. Visitors receive `alt=""` for a Decorative image; meaningful images use their usual alt text and otherwise raise a Check. A shared Media alt update does not alter a placement's choice.

### Accessibility statement

Staff can use Site settings › Accessibility statement (`accessibilityStatement.get/generate/setEnabled`) to generate an optional ordinary Page Draft. It follows Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 2018/1523, Annex section 1 ([official EU model](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/dec_impl/2018/1523/oj)): scope and commitment, the assessed status and relevant standard, limitations/exemptions/alternatives, preparation and review information, feedback and enforcement contacts. Facts must be supplied by Staff. The initial choice states that the website aims for WCAG 2.2 AA, a manual audit is pending and compliance has not been established. The form and generated text distinguish this public-sector model from an EAA declaration of conformity.

Generation creates an optional Group Field on the built-in Page Post Type and a normal editable Component whose Props read that Page's translated text and headings. Both are Drafts and use normal publish controls. Until general Page layouts (#75), `statementLayout` supplies only the binding Placement; no statement text is hardcoded in visitor rendering. The Component's actual stored tree controls its layout. The form supplies English and Italian boilerplate. The Page can be edited, translated manually or through the normal translation jobs into any Site language, hidden or deleted like other Content. Generating again changes only the chosen language's text/title Draft. It does not republish anything or overwrite the Component layout.

`accessibility_statements` stores a Site-scoped association (`pageId`, `componentId`, `fieldKey`), an enabled footer link and the last form in each language. Stored forms are Staff-only convenience data, never the source of rendered text. The footer link appears only when the Page and Component are published and the Page is visible in that language; its label and path follow the Page's normal translated title and address. An existing Site footer receives the link; a Site without one gets a footer. Disabling the link keeps the Page. Deleting the Page removes the association; an associated Component cannot be deleted while used.

Site transfer (#109) automatically includes that association and remaps its Site/Workspace, Page and Component references through the ordinary table discovery and UUID remapping. A round-trip test covers the association, translated content, footer behavior and Decorative placements. Include the generated Page Post Type Field and all ordinary Page/Component Drafts, published versions and translations. Keep `fieldKey` consistent with any Field-key remapping. Include the saved per-language forms and `enabled`, and preserve `decorative` on element/Block placements and inside translated Blocks' `source.was`. Checks are derived and need no export.

## Popups

`@webcraft/module-popups` is the first-party Popups Module. It uses only the Module SDK and declares its Site script Necessary. The core owns the Template command and tenant data, as it does Form definitions (ADR 0024). Approval and per-Site switches are the ordinary Module controls. Switching off keeps data and makes old Popup commands render nothing; removing Module data deletes that Site's Popups and switches the Module off.

Site › Popups separates Content from Structure. Staff create a Popup and build its dedicated Component with the existing builder or Component markup API. Its `title`, `text` (rich text) and `image` Props receive that Popup's Content. The Component owns the element tree, CSS and nested Components, so Classes, tokens, Media usage, Forms, history and live editing use the normal registries. Its initial name is `Popup` followed by the Popup key in PascalCase, shortened to the normal Component name limit and suffixed when that name is taken, including names reserved by retained Module source declarations. Deleting a Popup retains its Component, which can be reused elsewhere.

Layout, Trigger, Display rules and frequency are Structure (`structure.edit`). Title, text, image, on/off and start/end instants are Content (`entry.edit`, including Authors); publishing Content requires `entry.publish`. Translatable values follow per-Field fallback. Shared on/off and dates stay shared when the Default language changes. The Structure publish and its history snapshot contain both the Component tree and settings atomically. Both layers have Drafts, publishing, history and Scheduled publish; Popup Content also participates in Review, Co-editing and AI translation. The backing Component cannot be published or scheduled independently: `POPUP_PUBLISH_REQUIRED` names its Popup action, including API-client previews. The builder saves its tree and opens the Popup review with **Review and publish Popup**. Publish all orders the compound Popup in the same dependency graph as ordinary Components, including reuse of the backing tree. Deleting the Popup leaves the retained Component free to publish normally. Publishing a Popup publishes its Component in the same transaction; Scheduled publish freezes both, and Publish all lists the Popup as one Structure item. Draft Content never changes the live promotion.

The seven Triggers are **On load**, **After a delay** (seconds), **Scroll depth** (percentage), **Element in view** (an element's `#id`), **Exit intent** (desktop pointer leaving the top of the document), **Idle** (seconds without interaction) and **Click**. These follow the seven types in the [Popup decision (#27)](https://github.com/flyingwebie/WebCraft/issues/27). A Popup can have more than one; the first to fire requests its slot. No referrer, UTM, country, time-of-day or A/B targeting is added.

Display rules intersect: path, Post Type, Entry, language, phone/tablet/desktop, and new/returning visitor. An empty include list means all; exclusion lists for paths, Post Types and Entries override includes. A path is the actual URL path including its language prefix, exact or ending with `*` for a prefix. Phone is below the smallest named Design-system breakpoint, or below 768 px if none; tablet ends at the next breakpoint, defaulting to 1024 px. Start is inclusive and end exclusive; both are instants. Cached output expires at the next date boundary.

Frequency is every page view, once per visit (a new visit after 30 minutes without activity), every N days, never again after closing, or never again after submitting its Form or clicking its configured main button. Necessary local storage is scoped by Site: `webcraft.popups.<siteId>.visited` marks a previous visit, and `webcraft.popups.<siteId>.<popupId>` holds the last opening time in milliseconds and closed/submitted flags. There is no visitor identifier or network collection. Corrupt, unavailable or full storage falls back to the current page's in-memory coordination; it never blocks the page. Clearing browser storage resets this state.

The renderer sends inert `<template>` markup and the Module serves a hashed, same-origin script under the strict CSP. `{{@popup key}}` attaches a Popup to that Template or Component regardless of Display rules, and renders its Click link (`#popup-<key>`); on/off and dates still apply. Attachments inside a Popup’s Component follow the same rule; cyclic links produce each dialog once. It is a core registry command; parsing and printing preserve the Popup id in element-tree JSON. With JavaScript disabled the link reaches a visible `<noscript>` section with its Content and Forms. Popups run only on ordinary Site pages, never in Edit/Code/Safe mode. A Form POST's validation or success response reveals its Popup Content so the visitor can finish without JavaScript; private uploads remain in the normal Forms pipeline.

Trigger timers begin after consent is answered. The runtime waits for `window.webcraft.consent.whenAnswered()` and listens to `webcraft:consent`. A page with no Consent API asks nothing, so it can proceed. It queues simultaneous Triggers, with at most one Modal/Slide-in/Full screen plus one Bar. Each native dialog has a translated close button and an accessible name; Escape, close and unmount free the slot, and closing returns focus. Page teardown clears queues and listeners; fragment links and same-document history preserve pending Triggers. Bars can sit at the top or bottom, either overlaying the page or reserving space at that edge. Bottom overlay is the default, including older saved versions and archives without these options. Push space is created only when the Bar opens after Consent, follows its size, and is released on close, unmount or page teardown. Animation may be none, fade, slide or zoom; reduced motion disables it. Modal and Full screen trap focus. Slide-ins and Bars do not trap focus or dim the page. Optional backdrop closing and timed closing (including after Form success) are Structure settings. When Site analytics permits it, the existing `webcraft.analytics.track()` records `popup-shown:<key>`, `popup-closed:<key>` and `popup-clicked:<key>`. When the Analytics script is still loading, eligible Popup events wait for its load event and are sent once, only if Analytics consent still permits them. This does not delay the Popup; missing or failed Analytics scripts collect nothing. No popup data is sent when its analytics script or consent is absent. Phone Popups opening on load or before five seconds produce the non-blocking `popupPhoneEarly` Check in the shared Site collection and the Popup editor. Structure Checks are Staff-only.

Site transfer includes Popup SQL rows through ordinary schema discovery. The shared typed reference remapper handles rule Entry/Post Type IDs, Content Media/provenance, Popup commands, compound Structure and Content revisions, and Popup Audit IDs. Text that happens to contain a UUID stays literal. Import clears both published layers, retaining former live versions as restorable revisions. Scheduled publishes and live shared-editing state follow the normal transfer exclusions.

Hidden Popup trees contribute to the shared rendered-Component collection used by `siteScript.whenUsed`. Once a dialog is inserted, the Popup runtime emits the bubbling `webcraft:mounted` event. Forms and Extra blocks initialize newly mounted content through that hook without initializing existing controls twice; their normal Module Consent categories still govern when their scripts run.
